「或者说得更确切些，」吉米接着说，「肯不肯去撬门入宅，我在一旁跟着你？严格说来，我是来度假的，可这么点小事算不得正经活儿。是这么回事，」他解释道，「我看上你了，斯派克，不愿意瞧着你把光阴糟蹋在粗活上。你身上有这行的根苗，稍加调教，我就能给你抛光上蜡。这份抬举不是谁都够格领受的，可我就见不得一个人明明能干得好偏在那里瞎糊弄！我要看看你的身手。这就动身，咱们上城北去，你便可以开工了。别紧张。只当我不在场，该怎么干怎么干。我不会苛求。罗马又不是一天建成的。完事之后，我挑你几处错，点评点评。你看这个章程可好？」

「哎呀，老大！好极了！还有啊，哪儿的门路合适，我一清二楚。有位朋友给我透过信儿。起码吧，先前我不知道他算我朋友，打今儿起我可认下了。那是一——」

===Original===

“Very well, then. One moment, though.”

He went to the telephone. Before he had left New York on his travels, Arthur Mifflin had been living at an hotel near Washington Square. It was probable that he was still there. He called up the number. The night clerk was an old acquaintance of his.

“Hullo, Dixon!” said Jimmy, “is that you? I’m Pitt. Pitt. Yes. I’m back. How did you guess? Yes, very pleasant, thanks. Has Mr. Mifflin come in yet? Gone to bed? Never mind, ring him up, will you? Thanks.” Presently the sleepy and outraged voice of Mr. Mifflin spoke at the other end of the line.

“What’s wrong? Who the devil’s that?”

===Chinese===

「很好。不过且慢。」

他走到电话机前。当初离纽约出游之前，亚瑟·米夫林一直住在华盛顿广场附近一家旅馆里。多半人还没挪窝。他拨了号码。夜班接待员是他的旧相识。

「喂，狄克逊（Dixon）！」吉米说，「是你吗？我是皮特。皮特。对。我回来了。你怎么猜着的？是啊，一路很开心，多谢。米夫林先生回来了吗？睡下了？不要紧，替我摇个电话，行吗？多谢。」不多时，电话线那头传来米夫林先生睡意惺忪、怒气冲冲的声音。

「出了什么事？见鬼，是哪个？」

===Original===

“My dear Arthur! Where you pick up such expressions I can’t think. Not from me.”

“Is that you, Jimmy? What in the name of—”

“Heavens! what are you kicking about? The night’s yet young. Arthur, touching that little arrangement we made—cracking that crib, you know. Are you listening? Have you any objection to my taking an assistant along with me? I don’t want to do anything contrary to our agreement, but there’s a young fellow here who’s anxious that I should let him come along and pick up a few hints. He’s a professional all right. Not in our class, of course, but quite a fair rough workman. He—Arthur! Arthur! These are harsh words! Then am I to understand you have no objection? Very well. Only don’t say later on that I didn’t play fair. Good night.”

He hung up the receiver and turned to Spike.

===Chinese===

「我亲爱的亚瑟！真想不出你这些字眼儿是打哪儿学来的。反正不是跟我。」

「是你吗，吉米？你到底安的什么——」

「天哪！你嚷嚷什么？夜还年轻着呢。亚瑟，说起我们订下的那桩小小约定——撬门入宅那桩，你知道的。在听吗？我带个助手同去，你有异议吗？我不想做半点违背协议的事，可这儿有个小伙子，一心求我带他同去，好学上几手。他倒是地道业内人士。当然，跟我们不是一个级别，不过作为粗活匠人，手艺颇像样。他——亚瑟！亚瑟！这话说得可伤人了！那么，我是否可以理解为阁下并无异议？很好。只是往后可别说我不讲公平。晚安。」

他挂上听筒，转身面向斯派克。

===Original===

“Ready?”

“Ain’t youse goin’ to put on your gumshoes, boss?”

Jimmy frowned reflectively, as if there was something in what this novice suggested. He went into the bedroom, and returned wearing a pair of thin patent leather shoes.

Spike coughed tentatively.

===Chinese===

「准备好了吗？」

「老大不换上胶底鞋吗？」

吉米若有所思地皱起眉头，仿佛这位新手的话不无道理。他走进卧室，再出来时，脚上已经换了一双薄薄的漆皮鞋。

斯派克试探性地咳嗽了一声。

===Original===

“Won’t youse need your gun?” he hazarded.

Jimmy gave a short laugh.

“I work with my brains, not guns,” he said. “Let us be going.”

===Chinese===

「老大不要带枪吗？」他冒昧地问。

吉米短促地一笑。

「我凭脑子吃饭，不凭枪，」他说，「走吧。」

===Original===

There was a taxicab near by, as there always is in New York. Jimmy pushed Spike in.

The luxury of riding in a taxicab kept Spike dumb for several miles. At One Hundred and Fiftieth Street Jimmy stopped the cab and paid the driver, who took the money with that magnificently aloof air which characterizes the taxi-chauffeur. A lesser man might have displayed some curiosity about the ill-matched pair. The chauffeur, having lit a cigarette, drove off without any display of interest whatsoever. It might have been part of his ordinary duties to drive gentlemen in evening clothes and shock-headed youths in parti-coloured sweaters about the city at three o’clock in the morning.

“We will now,” said Jimmy, “stroll on and prospect. It might excite comment if we drove up to the door. It is up to you, Spike. Lead me to this house you mentioned.”

===Chinese===

附近恰好停着一辆出租车——在纽约，出租车向来不缺。吉米把斯派克推上车。

出租车的这份阔气，叫斯派克好几个街口都说不出话来。到了第一百五十街，吉米停车付账；司机接过钱，端着一副出租车司机特有的、气派十足的冷然架势。换个涵养浅些的，兴许还要对这不般配的一对儿露出几分好奇。这位司机点上一支烟，兴味索然地开车走了，仿佛凌晨三点，载着一位穿晚礼服的绅士和一个顶着刺猬乱发、穿杂色毛衣的小伙子满城转悠，本就是他日常分内的差事。

「眼下，」吉米说，「我们且信步走走，踏勘踏勘。车一直开到门口，怕要惹人议论。全看你的了，斯派克。带我去你说的那所房子。」

===Original===

They walked on, striking eastward out of Broadway. It caused Jimmy some surprise to find that much-enduring thoroughfare extended as far as this. It had never occurred to him before to ascertain what Broadway did with itself beyond Times Square. He had spent much of his time abroad, in cities where a street changes its name every hundred yards or so without any apparent reason.

It was darker now that they had moved from the centre of things, but it was still far too light for Jimmy’s tastes. He was content, however, to leave matters entirely in his companion’s charge. Spike probably had his method for evading publicity on these occasions.

Spike, meanwhile, plodded steadily onwards. Block after block he passed, until finally the houses began to be more scattered.

===Chinese===

两人接着往前走，朝东拐出了百老汇大街。吉米颇感意外：这条吃苦耐劳的大街，居然一直延伸到这么远的地方。他从前从没想过要弄明白，过了时代广场，百老汇都拿自己怎么办。他在国外消磨了大量光阴，那些城市里，一条街每隔百来码就换个名字，全无道理可言。

离开了闹市中心，天色暗了下来，可照吉米的口味，还是太亮。不过他乐得把事情全权交给同伴打理。这种场合如何避开众人的耳目，斯派克想必自有门道。

这边，斯派克只顾闷头稳稳往前。走过一个又一个街区，末了，房屋渐渐稀疏起来。

===Original===

At length he stopped outside a fair-sized detached house. As he did so a single raindrop descended with a splash on the nape of Jimmy’s neck. In another moment the shower had begun—jerkily at first, then, as if warming to its work, with the quiet persistence of a shower-bath.

“Dis is de place, boss,” said Spike.

From a burglar’s point of view it was an admirable house. It had no porch, but there was a handy window only a few feet from the ground. Spike pulled from his pocket a small bottle and a piece of coarse paper.

===Chinese===

他终于在一所规模可观的独立宅子门前停住。恰在此时，一粒雨点啪嗒一声，正落在吉米的后颈窝。转眼间阵雨已经来了——起初断断续续，随后像渐渐上了劲，带着淋浴喷头那种不动声色的执着。

「就是这儿，老大。」斯派克说。

从窃贼的眼光看，这是一所妙极了的宅子。没有门廊，却有一扇得用的窗，离地不过几英尺。斯派克从兜里掏出一个小瓶和一张粗纸。

===Original===

“What’s that?” inquired Jimmy.

“Treacle, boss,” said Spike deferentially.

He poured the contents of the bottle onto the paper, which he pressed firmly against the windowpane. Then, drawing out a short steel instrument, he gave the paper a sharp tap. The glass beneath broke, though the sound was almost inaudible. The paper came away with the glass attached, then Spike inserting his hand in the opening, shot back the catch and softly pushed up the window.

===Chinese===

「那是什么？」吉米问。

「糖浆，老大。」斯派克恭恭敬敬地说。

他把瓶里的东西倒在纸上，把纸牢牢按在窗玻璃上。随后抽出一小截钢钎，朝纸上干脆地一敲。底下的玻璃碎了，声音却轻得几乎听不见。纸带着粘住的玻璃揭了下来；接着斯派克把手伸进豁口，拨回插销，轻轻把窗推了上去。

===Original===

“Elementary,” said Jimmy; “elementary, but quite neat.”

There was now a shutter to be negotiated. This took longer, but in the end Spike’s persuasive methods prevailed.

Jimmy became quite cordial.

“You have been well grounded, Spike,” he said. “And, after all, that is half the battle. The advice I give to every novice is ‘Learn to walk before you try to run.’ Master the A.B.C. of the craft first. With a little careful coaching you will do. Just so. Pop in.”

===Chinese===

「入门功夫，」吉米说，「入门是入门，倒也满利落。」

接下来还有一道护窗板要交涉。这一道费时久些，但末了，斯派克那套「劝说法」终究占了上风。

吉米顿时亲热起来。

「你的根底打得扎实，斯派克，」他说，「说到底，这就成功了一半。我送给每个新手的话都是：‘先学走，再学跑。’先掌握这门手艺的 ABC。稍加细心调教，你准成。就是这样。钻进去吧。」

===Original===

Spike climbed cautiously over the sill, followed by Jimmy. The latter struck a match and found the electric light switch. They were in a parlour furnished and decorated with surprising taste. Jimmy had expected the usual hideousness, but here everything, from the wallpaper to the smallest ornaments, was wonderfully well selected.

Business, however, was business. This was no time to stand admiring artistic efforts in room furnishing. There was that big J to be carved on the front door. If ’twere done, then ’twere well ’twere done quickly.

He was just moving to the door, when from some distant part of the house came the bark of a dog. Another joined in. The solo became a duet. The air was filled with their clamour.

===Chinese===

斯派克小心翼翼爬过窗台，吉米随后跟进。后者划亮一根火柴，找到了电灯开关。他们进的是一间客厅，陈设装潢之雅，出人意料。吉米原以为照例要饱览一番俗气，可这里从墙纸到最小的摆设，样样都挑得出奇地好。

然而，正事总是正事。眼下可不是站着欣赏室内装潢艺术的时候。大门上还等着刻那个大大的 J 呢。若是当真要干，那便干得越快越好。

他正要朝门口挪步，忽听得宅子深处远远传来一声犬吠。另一只随即加入。独唱成了二重唱。空气里满是它们的喧嚣。

===Original===

“Gee!” cried Spike.

The remark seemed more or less to sum up the situation.

“ ’Tis sweet,” says Byron, “to hear the watchdog’s honest bark.” Jimmy and Spike found two watchdogs’ honest barks cloying. Spike intimated this by making a feverish dash for the open window. Unfortunately for the success of this manoeuvre, the floor of the room was covered, not with a carpet, but with tastefully scattered rugs, and underneath these rugs it was very highly polished. Spike, treading on one of these islands, was instantly undone. No power of will or muscle can save a man in such a case. Spike skidded. His feet flew from under him. There was a momentary flash of red hair, as of a passing meteor. The next moment he had fallen on his back with a thud which shook the house, and probably the rest of Manhattan Island as well. Even in that crisis the thought flashed across Jimmy’s mind that this was not Spike’s lucky night.

===Chinese===

「哎呀！」斯派克叫道。

这句话多多少少把眼下的局面概括尽了。

拜伦说：「听那看家狗诚实的吠声，何等甜美。」吉米和斯派克却发现，两条看家狗诚实的吠声，就甜得叫人发腻了。斯派克用行动表示了这一点——他发疯似的朝敞开的窗子冲去。不巧这一着未能奏效：屋里地上铺的不是整幅地毯，而是雅致错落的小块地毯，地毯底下的地板打过蜡，亮得厉害。斯派克一脚踩上其中一座孤岛，登时完蛋大吉。这等关头，天大的意志、再壮的筋肉也救不了人。斯派克打滑了。双脚腾空飞起。一头红发倏地一闪，好似流星掠过。下一瞬，他已经仰面朝天砸在地上，那一声闷响震得整所房子直颤，曼哈顿岛其余的部分多半也跟着颤了颤。便是在这等危急关头，吉米脑中仍闪过一个念头：今晚实在不是斯派克的幸运夜。

===Original===

Upstairs the efforts of the canine choir had begun to resemble the “A che la morte” duet in *Il Trovatore*. Particularly good work was being done by the baritone dog.

Spike sat up, groaning. Equipped though he was by nature with a skull of the purest and most solid ivory, the fall had disconcerted him. His eyes, like those of Shakespeare’s poet, rolling a fine frenzy, did glance from heaven to earth, from earth to heaven. He passed his fingers tenderly through his vermilion hair.

Heavy footsteps were descending the stairs. In the distance the soprano dog had reached A in alto and was holding it, while his fellow artiste executed runs in the lower register.

===Chinese===

楼上，犬类唱诗班的演出已渐渐有几分《游吟诗人》（Il Trovatore）里「A che la morte」那段二重唱的意思。男中音那只狗唱得格外出色。

斯派克哼哼着坐起来。他虽天生顶着一颗最纯粹、最结实的象牙脑壳，这一跤也摔得他发了懵。他那双眼睛，正像莎士比亚笔下那位诗人，在美妙的狂放中骨碌碌转着，上窥苍天，下瞰大地，从大地又望回苍天。他满心疼惜地用手指梳了梳自己朱红的头发。

沉重的脚步声正下楼梯。远处，女高音那只狗已经唱到了高音 A，还稳稳拖住不放，它那位同行艺人则在低音区跑着花腔。

===Original===

“Get up!” hissed Jimmy. “There’s somebody coming! Get up, you idiot, can’t you?”

It was characteristic of Jimmy that it never even occurred to him to desert the fallen one and depart alone. There was once an Italian convict who, in planning a jail-breaking, assigned to his brother felons such duties as shooting the governor and strangling the warders, reserving for himself the task of making “da gran’ escape.” Jimmy was the exact opposite of this strategist. Spike was his brother-in-arms. He would as soon have thought of deserting him as a sea captain would have abandoned his ship.

Consequently, as Spike, despite all exhortations, continued to remain on the floor, rubbing his head and uttering “Gee!” at intervals in a melancholy voice, Jimmy resigned himself to fate, and stood where he was, waiting for the door to open.

It opened the next moment as if a cyclone had been behind it.

===Chinese===

「起来！」吉米压着嗓子急促地喝道，「有人来了！起来呀，你这笨蛋，起得来吗？」

吉米的为人正在于此：撇下倒下的伙伴独自开溜——这念头他压根儿不曾有过。从前有个意大利囚犯，谋划越狱时，把枪击典狱长、勒死狱卒之类的活计都派给狱中弟兄，只把「来一记大逃亡」这出压轴戏留给自己。吉米恰是这位战略家的反面。斯派克是他的战友。要他丢下斯派克，无异于要一位船长弃船而去。

于是，斯派克任凭怎样催促，兀自赖在地板上，揉着脑袋，隔一阵便没精打采地哼一声「哎呀」，吉米只得听天由命，站在原地，等那扇门开。

下一刻，门开了，那架势就像门后刮着一股旋风。

## VII Getting Acquainted / 结识

===Original===
A cyclone entering the room is apt to alter the position of things. This one shifted a footstool, a small chair, a rug, and Spike. The chair struck by a massive boot, whirled against the wall. The footstool rolled away. The rug crumpled up and slid. Spike, with a yell, leaped to his feet, slipped again, fell, and finally compromised on an all-fours position, in which attitude he remained, blinking.

While these stirring acts were in progress there was the sound of a door opening upstairs, followed by a scuttering of feet and an appalling increase in the canine contribution to the current noises. The duet had now taken on quite a Wagnerian effect.

There raced into the room first a white bull terrier, he of the soprano voice, and—a bad second—his fellow artiste, the baritone, a massive bulldog, bearing a striking resemblance to the big man with the revolver.

And then, in theatrical parlance, the entire company "held the picture." Upstage, with his hand still on the door, stood the large householder; downstage Jimmy. Centre, Spike and the bulldog, their noses, a couple of inches apart, inspected each other with mutual disfavour. On the extreme O.P. side the bull terrier, who had fallen foul of a wickerwork table, was crouching with extended tongue and rolling eyes, waiting for the next move.

===Chinese===
一股龙卷风闯进屋来，往往会把屋里各物的位置变动一番。这一股挪动了一只脚凳、一把小椅、一块地毯，外加斯派克。挨了大靴子一击的小椅滴溜溜转着撞向墙壁。脚凳骨碌碌滚开。地毯皱作一团，滑出老远。斯派克怪叫一声，蹦了起来，脚底一滑，又摔倒在地，末了折中成一副四肢着地的架势，便以这副姿势定住，眨巴着眼睛。

这一连串惊天动地的动作正演到紧处，楼上响起开门声，跟着是一阵慌乱的脚步，而犬科对眼前这场喧腾的贡献也骇人地加码。这出二重唱，如今已然很有些瓦格纳派的气势。

率先冲进屋里的是一头白色牛头梗，正是那位女高音；随后是位很不济的二路同行——男中音：一头壮硕的斗牛犬，同那位端着左轮枪的大汉有着惊人的相似。

接着，用舞台行话说，全班人马「定格住了」。舞台里侧，大块头的宅主立在门边，手还搭在门上；台前，是吉米。正中，斯派克与斗牛犬鼻尖相距不过两英寸，彼此端详着，各怀不满。最靠台口外侧的地方，那头牛头梗——方才同一张柳条桌结了梁子——正伏低身子，舌头伸得老长，眼珠乱转，静候下一步动静。

===Original===
The householder looked at Jimmy. Jimmy looked at the householder. Spike and the bulldog looked at each other. The bull terrier distributed his gaze impartially around the company.

"A typical scene of quiet American home life," murmured Jimmy.

The man with the pistol glowered.

"Hands up, you devils!" he roared, pointing a mammoth revolver.

The two marauders humoured his whim.

===Chinese===
宅主看着吉米。吉米看着宅主。斯派克和斗牛犬互相看着。牛头梗的目光倒是不偏不倚，在满场人马里雨露均沾。

「一幅典型的美国式宁静家庭生活图景，」吉米低声说。

持枪大汉怒目而视。

「举起手来，你们这两个恶魔！」他吼道，一支硕大无朋的左轮枪直指过来。

两位劫掠客便顺从了他这个怪念头。

===Original===
"Let me explain," said Jimmy pacifically, shuffling warily round in order to face the bull terrier, who was now strolling in his direction with an ill-assumed carelessness.

"Keep still, you blackguard!"

Jimmy kept still. The bull terrier, with the same abstracted air, was beginning a casual inspection of his right trouser leg.

===Chinese===
「容我解释，」吉米息事宁人地说，一边拖着脚小心转身，好面对那头牛头梗——它正装出一副漫不经心的架势朝他这边踱来，只可惜装得太不到家。

「站住别动，你这恶棍！」

吉米站住不动。牛头梗带着同样一副心不在焉的神情，开始随意检视他的右裤脚。

===Original===
Relations between Spike and the bulldog, meanwhile, had become more strained. The sudden flinging up of the former's arms had had the worst effect on the animal's nerves. Spike, the croucher on all-fours, he might have tolerated; but Spike, the semaphore, inspired him with thoughts of battle. He was growling in a moody, reflective manner. His eye was full of purpose.

It was probably this that caused Spike to look at the householder. Till then he had been too busy to gaze elsewhere, but now the bulldog's eye had become so unpleasant that he cast a pathetic glance up at the man by the door.

"Gee!" he cried, as he did so. "It's de boss! Say, boss, call off de dawg. It's sure goin' to nip de old head of me."

The other lowered his revolver in surprise.

"So it's you, is it, you limb of Satan?" he remarked. "I thought I had seen that damned red head of yours before. What are you doing in my house?"

===Chinese===
与此同时，斯派克同斗牛犬之间的关系愈发紧张。前者猛然高举的双臂，对这只狗的神经产生了极坏的影响。四肢着地伏着的斯派克，它兴许还能容忍；可摆成信号机的斯派克，勾起了它决一死战的念头。它阴沉而若有所思地低吼着。它眼里满是杀机。

多半正是这个，才叫斯派克朝宅主望了过去。在那之前他一直自顾不暇，没工夫分神他顾；可这会儿斗牛犬的眼神已经凶得不像话，他便抬起头，朝门口那人投去可怜巴巴的一瞥。

「哎呀！」他一面望一面嚷。「这不是老大嘛！喂，老大，快把狗唤开吧。它准要咬我这颗老脑袋了。」

对方一惊，放低了左轮枪。

「原来是你呀，你这撒旦的枝条？」他说。「我说呢，你那颗倒霉红毛脑袋怎么那么眼熟。你跑我家里来干什么？」

===Original===
Spike uttered a moan of self-pity.

"Boss," he cried, "I've had a raw deal. Dere's bin coarse work goin' on. Listen! It's dis way. Honest, I didn't know dis was where you lived. A fat Swede—Ole Larsen his monaker is—tells me dis house belongs to a widder-loidy what lives here all alone, and has all kinds of silver and all dat, and she's down Sout' visiting, so dat de house is empty. Gee, I'm onto his curves now. I'm wise. Listen, boss. Him and me starts a scrappin' last week over somet'in, and I t'inks he's got it in bad for me, because I puts it all over him. But t'ree days ago up he comes and says, 'Let's be fren's,' and puts me wise on dis joint. I'll soak it to dat Swede! Dis was what he was woikin' for. He knows you lives here, and he t'inks to put me in bad wit youse. It's a raw deal, boss!"

The big man listened to this sad tale of Grecian gifts in silence. Not so the bulldog, which growled ominously from start to finish. Spike glanced nervously in its direction.

"De dawg," he persisted uneasily. "Won't you call on de dawg, boss?"

===Chinese===
斯派克发出一声自怜的呻吟。

「老大，」他嚷道，「我吃了大亏。这里头有卑鄙勾当。您听我说！事情是这样的。天地良心，我压根不知道这是您的府上。一个肥瑞典佬——名叫奥勒·拉森（Ole Larsen）——跟我说这房子是个寡妇太太的，就她一个人住，家里各式银器应有尽有，眼下她上南边串门去了，屋里空着。哎呀，如今我算摸清他的花招了。我门儿清。您听我说，老大。上礼拜他为点事跟我干了一架，我寻思他记恨上我了，因为我把他收拾得服服帖帖。可三天前他倒寻上门来说，『咱们和好吧』，还把这处宅子的底细透给了我。我非痛揍那瑞典佬不可！敢情他打的就是这个主意。他知道您住在这儿，想借您的手来整我、让我在您跟前背黑锅。这不是明摆着坑我吗，老大！」

大汉默默听完了这套「希腊人的礼物」式的苦情故事。斗牛犬可没这份涵养，从头到尾吼得凶兆毕露。斯派克战战兢兢地朝它那边瞟了一眼。

「那狗，」他惴惴不安地追问。「您把狗唤开行不行，老大？」

===Original===
The big man stooped and grasped the animal's collar, jerking him away.

"The same treatment," suggested Jimmy, with approval, "would also do a world of good to this playful and affectionate animal—unless he is a vegetarian, in which case don't bother."

The householder glowered at him.

"Who are you?" he demanded.

===Chinese===
大汉弯下腰，攥住那畜生的项圈，一把将它拽开。

「同样的处置，」吉米赞许地建议，「对这位爱嬉闹、重感情的仁兄也一定大有裨益——除非它吃素，那就不必劳驾了。」

宅主对他怒目而视。

「你是什么人？」他喝问。

===Original===
"My name," began Jimmy, "is—"

"Say," said Spike, "he's a champion burglar, boss—"

"Eh!" he said.

"He's a champion burglar from de odder side. He sure is. From Lunnon. Gee, he's de guy! Tell him about the bank you opened, and de jools you swiped from de duchess, and de what-d'ye-call-it blowpipe."

===Chinese===
「敝人姓名，」吉米开口，「是——」

「喂，」斯派克说，「他是位顶尖的窃贼高手，老大——」

「嗯？」他说。

「他是打另一头来的顶尖窃贼高手。错不了。伦敦来的。哎呀，他就是那位爷！您跟他讲讲您撬开的那家银行，从公爵夫人那儿顺来的珠宝，还有那个什么来着的吹管。」

===Original===
It seemed to Jimmy that Spike was showing a certain want of tact. When you are discovered by a householder—with revolver—in his parlour at half past three in the morning, it is surely an injudicious move to lay stress on your proficiency as a burglar. The householder may be supposed to take it for granted. The side of your character which should be advertised in such a crisis is the non-burglarious. Allusion should be made to the fact that as a child you attended Sunday-school regularly, and to what the curate said when you took the Divinity prize. The idea should be conveyed to the householder's mind that, if let off with a caution, your innate goodness of heart will lead you to reform and avoid such scenes in future.

With some astonishment, therefore, Jimmy found that these revelations, so far from prejudicing the man with the revolver against him, had apparently told in his favour. The man behind the gun was regarding him rather with interest than disapproval.

"So you're a crook from London, are you?"

===Chinese===
吉米觉得，斯派克这番话未免缺了几分机变。凌晨三点半，你让一位宅主——还端着左轮枪——在自己客厅里逮个正着，这时候大肆标榜你行窃的手艺如何精湛，无疑是一步昏棋。这一层，宅主想必视为理所当然，无须你来强调。这种危急关头，该大做广告的是你性格里非盗窃的那一面。你应当提及你小时候主日学校回回不落，还应当提及你领神学奖时副牧师说了些什么。要叫宅主心里装着这样一个念头：只要他肯高抬贵手、训诫放人，你天性纯良，自会痛改前非，往后决不再演这种场面。

所以，当吉米发现这一番揭底非但没有叫持枪人对他心生恶感，反而显然替他加了分时，不免有些诧异。枪口后面那位瞧着他的神情，与其说是嫌恶，不如说来了兴致。

「这么说，你是位伦敦来的窃贼？」

===Original===
Jimmy did not hesitate. If being a crook from London was a passport into citizens' parlours in the small hours, and, more particularly, if it carried with it also a safe-conduct out of them, Jimmy was not the man to refuse the role. He bowed.

"Well, you'll have to come across now you're in New York. Understand that. And come across good."

"Sure, he will," said Spike, charmed that the tension had been relieved and matters placed upon a pleasant and businesslike footing. "He'll be good. He's next to de game, sure."

"Sure," echoed Jimmy courteously. He did not understand; but things seemed to be taking a turn for the better, so why disturb the harmony?

===Chinese===
吉米毫不迟疑。倘若「伦敦来的窃贼」这身份是凌晨时分出入市民客厅的通行证，尤其倘若它还附赠一条安然脱身的安全通道，那吉米绝不是会推辞这个角色的人。他欠了欠身。

「哼，既然到了纽约，你就得照规矩上贡。把这点记牢。而且要贡得像样。」

「他准上贡，」斯派克说。剑拔弩张化为乌有、事情摆上了愉快而合乎生意的轨道，他心花怒放。「他会守规矩的。他精通这行门道，错不了。」

「那是一定，」吉米客气地随声附和。他不明所以；可事情似乎正在好转，又何必搅了这一团和气？

===Original===
"Dis gent," said Spike respectfully, "is boss of de cops. A police captain," he corrected himself.

A light broke upon Jimmy's darkness. He wondered he had not understood before. He had not been a newspaperman in New York for a year without finding out something of the inner workings of the police force. He saw now why the other's manner had changed.

"Pleased to meet you," he said. "We must have a talk together one of these days."

"We must," said the police captain significantly.

===Chinese===
「这位老爷，」斯派克恭恭敬敬地说，「是全体警察的老大。是位警察局长，」他改口纠正自己。

吉米心头的黑暗里豁然透进一道光。他奇怪自己先前怎么没悟出来。他在纽约当新闻记者当了一年，警队内部的运作门道，总归打听到过一些。他此刻明白对方的口气为什么变了。

「幸会幸会，」他说。「过几天咱们非得好好叙谈叙谈。」

「非叙谈不可，」警察局长意味深长地说。

===Original===
"Of course, I don't know your methods on this side, but anything that's usual—"

"I'll see you at my office. Spike Mullins will show you where it is."

"Very well. You must forgive this preliminary informal call. We came in more to shelter from the rain than anything."

"You did, did you?"

===Chinese===
「当然，贵方这边的规矩我不懂，不过凡是惯例之内的事——」

「你上我局子里来见吧。斯派克·马林斯会告诉你地方。」

「很好。这一回先行非正式拜访，不揣冒昧，还望海涵。我们进来主要是避雨，别的倒在其次。」

「是避雨来的，嗯？」

===Original===
Jimmy felt that it behoved him to stand on his dignity. The situation demanded it.

"Why," he said, with some hauteur, "in the ordinary course of business I should hardly waste time over a small crib like—"

"It's banks for his," murmured Spike rapturously. "He eats dem alive. And jools from duchesses."

"I admit a partiality for jewels and duchesses," said Jimmy. "And now, as it's a little late, perhaps we had better—Ready, Spike? Good night, then. Pleased to have met you."

===Chinese===
吉米觉得，自己理当端起架子来。眼下这个场合有此要求。

「唉，」他说，带几分傲气，「照平常的行市，我犯不上在这么个小窝上浪费时间——」

「他专做大银行，」斯派克如醉如痴地咕哝。「银行他生吞活剥。还有公爵夫人的珠宝。」

「珠宝和公爵夫人，我承认确有偏爱，」吉米说。「好了，时候不早，咱们还是——准备好了吗，斯派克？那么，晚安。认识你很荣幸。」

===Original===
"I'll see you at my office."

"I may possibly look in. I shall be doing very little work in New York, I fancy. I am here merely on a vacation."

"If you do any work at all," said the policeman coldly, "you'll look in at my office, or you'll wish you had when it's too late."

"Of course, of course. I shouldn't dream of omitting any formality that may be usual. But I don't fancy I shall break my vacation. By the way, one little thing. Have you any objection to my carving a J on your front door?"

===Chinese===
「你上我局子里来见。」

「我或许会顺路一顾。我想我在纽约没多少活儿好干。我不过是来度个假。」

「只要你干上一丁点儿活，」警察冷冷地说，「你就得到我局子里来报到，不然等一切太迟，你会巴不得当初来过。」

「那是自然，那是自然。凡属惯例的礼数，我做梦也不会漏掉一样。不过我想我的假期不至于中断。顺便说一句，有件小事。我在你前门上刻个 J 字，你可有异议？」

===Original===
The policeman stared.

"On the inside. It won't show. It's just a whim of mine. If you have no objection."

"I don't want any of your—" began the policeman.

"You misunderstand me. It's only that it means paying for a dinner. I wouldn't for the world—"

===Chinese===
警察瞪眼直瞧。

「刻在里面。瞧不见的。只是我的一点怪癖。你要是没意见的话。」

「我不稀罕你那一套——」警察开口道。

「你误会了。这不过关系一顿晚饭的输赢。我万万不敢——」

===Original===
The policeman pointed to the window.

"Out you get," he said abruptly. "I've had enough of you. And don't you forget to come to my office."

Spike, still deeply mistrustful of the bulldog Rastus, jumped at the invitation. He was through the window and out of sight in the friendly darkness almost before the policeman had finished speaking. Jimmy remained.

"I shall be delighted—" he had begun, when he stopped. In the doorway was standing a girl—a girl whom he recognized. Her startled look told him that she too had recognized him.

===Chinese===
警察朝窗户一指。

「滚出去，」他没好气地说。「我受够你了。还有，可别忘了上我局子里来。」

斯派克对斗牛犬拉斯图斯仍深怀疑惧，一听这道恩旨，立刻照办。警察话音未落，他已翻出窗口，隐没在友好的黑暗里。吉米留在原地。

「那我可太乐意——」他才起了个头，忽然顿住了。门口立着一位姑娘——一位他认得的姑娘。她惊惶的神色告诉他，她也认出了他。

===Original===
Now for the first time since he had set out from his flat that night in Spike's company Jimmy was conscious of a sense of the unreality of things. It was all so exactly as it would have happened in a dream. He had gone to sleep thinking of this girl, and here she was. But a glance at McEachern brought him back to earth. There was nothing of the dreamworld about the police captain.

The policeman, whose back was towards the door, had not observed the addition to the company. Molly had turned the handle quietly and her slippered feet made no sound. It was the amazed expression on Jimmy's face that caused him to look towards the door.

"Molly!"

She smiled, though her face was still white. Jimmy's evening clothes had reassured her. She did not understand how he came to be there, but evidently there was nothing wrong. She had interrupted a conversation, not a conflict.

===Chinese===
直到此刻——自从那天夜里他带着斯派克离开公寓以来——吉米头一回生出世事如梦之感。一切同梦里的情形分毫不差。他上床时想的正是这位姑娘，而她现在就在眼前。可是朝麦克伊彻恩一瞥，又把他拽回了实地。这位警察局长身上，没有半点梦境的成分。

警察背对着门，没察觉班底里添了新角。莫莉轻轻转动了门把，脚上那双便鞋落地无声。是吉米脸上那副惊愕相，才叫他朝门口望去。

「莫莉！」

她微笑起来，虽然脸色还发白。吉米那一身晚礼服叫她放了心。她不明白他怎么会在这儿，但显然没什么不对劲。她打断的是一场谈话，不是一场厮打。

===Original===
"I heard the noise and you going downstairs, and I sent the dogs down to help you, father," she said. "And then, after a little while, I came down to see if you were all right."

Mr. McEachern was perplexed. Molly's arrival had put him in an awkward position. To denounce him as a cracksman was impossible. Jimmy knew too much about him. The only real fear of the policeman's life was that some word of his moneymaking methods might come to his daughter's ears.

Quite a brilliant idea came to him.

"A man broke in, my dear," he said. "This gentleman was passing and saw him."

===Chinese===
「我听见响动，又听见您下楼，就把狗放下去帮您了，爸爸，」她说。「过了一小会儿，我才下来看看您没事吧。」

麦克伊彻恩先生犯了难。莫莉的驾临叫他处境尴尬。把这位当撬贼告发是万万不能的，吉米知道他太多的底细。警察先生这辈子唯一真正的恐惧，就是他生财之道的只言片语传进女儿耳朵。

一个妙极了的主意油然而生。

「方才有贼闯了进来，亲爱的，」他说。「这位先生路过，亲眼撞见的。」

===Original===
"Distinctly," said Jimmy. "An ugly-looking customer!"

"But he slipped out of the window and got away," concluded the policeman.

"He was very quick," said Jimmy. "I think he may have been a professional acrobat."

"He didn't hurt you, father?"

===Chinese===
「看得一清二楚，」吉米说。「一副凶神恶煞的长相！」

「不过他从窗口溜出去跑了，」警察下了结论。

「他手脚非常麻利，」吉米说。「我看他没准是个职业杂技演员。」

「他没伤着您吧，爸爸？」

===Original===
"No, no, my dear."

"Perhaps I frightened him," said Jimmy airily.

Mr. McEachern scowled furtively at him.

"We mustn't detain you, Mr.—"

===Chinese===
「没有，没有，亲爱的。」

「也许是我把他给吓跑了，」吉米轻盈地说。

麦克伊彻恩先生偷偷朝他皱了皱眉。

「不敢再耽搁您了，先生——」

===Original===
"Pitt," said Jimmy. "My name is Pitt." He turned to Molly. "I hope you enjoyed the voyage."

The policeman started.

"You know my daughter?"

"By sight only, I'm afraid. We were fellow passengers on the Mauretania. Unfortunately I was in the second cabin. I used to see your daughter walking the deck sometimes."

===Chinese===
「皮特，」吉米说。「我叫皮特。」他转向莫莉。「希望这趟越洋航行还算尽兴。」

警察猛地一震。

「你认识我女儿？」

「恐怕只是眼熟。我们同乘『毛里塔尼亚号』。不巧我坐的是二等舱。时常望见您女儿在甲板上散步。」

===Original===
Molly smiled.

"I remember seeing you—sometimes."

McEachern burst out:

"Then you—"

===Chinese===
莫莉微微一笑。

「我也记得见过你——有时候。」

麦克伊彻恩脱口而出：

「那么你——」

===Original===
He stopped and looked at Molly. Molly was bending over Rastus, tickling him under the ear.

"Let me show you the way out, Mr. Pitt," said the policeman shortly. His manner was abrupt, but when one is speaking to a man whom one would dearly love to throw out of the window abruptness is almost unavoidable.

"Perhaps I should be going," said Jimmy.

"Good night, Mr. Pitt," said Molly.

===Chinese===
他咽住话头，去看莫莉。莫莉正俯身逗着拉斯图斯，在它耳朵根底下挠痒。

「我送您出去，皮特先生，」警察简慢地说。他口气生硬，可当你在跟一个恨不得一把扔出窗外的人讲话时，生硬几乎在所难免。

「我也该告辞了，」吉米说。

「晚安，皮特先生，」莫莉说。

===Original===
"I hope we shall meet again," said Jimmy.

"This way, Mr. Pitt," growled McEachern, holding the door.

"Please don't trouble," said Jimmy. He went to the window, and, flinging his leg over the sill, dropped noiselessly to the ground.

He turned and put his head in at the window again.

"I did that rather well," he said pleasantly. "I think I must take up this sort of thing as a profession. Good night."

===Chinese===
「希望后会有期，」吉米说。

「这边走，皮特先生，」麦克伊彻恩扶着门，低吼道。

「不敢劳驾，」吉米说。他走到窗前，一条腿甩过窗台，悄无声息地落到地上。

他又转过身来，把头探回窗内。

「这一手干得相当漂亮，」他愉快地说。「我看我得把这一行领受成正经营生才行。晚安。」

## VIII At Dreever / 在德里弗

===Original===
In the days before the Welshman began to expend his surplus energy in playing rugby football he was accustomed, whenever the monotony of his everyday life began to oppress him, to collect a few friends and make raids across the border into England, to the huge discomfort of the dwellers on the other side. It was to cope with this habit that Dreever Castle, in Shropshire, came into existence. It met a long-felt want. In time of trouble it became a haven of refuge. From all sides people poured into it, emerging cautiously when the marauders had disappeared. In the whole history of the castle there is but one instance recorded of a bandit attempting to take the place by storm, and the attack was an emphatic failure. On receipt of a ladleful of molten lead, aimed to a nicety by one John, the Chaplain—evidently one of those sporting parsons—this warrior retired, done to a turn, to his mountain fastnesses, and is never heard of again. He would seem, however, to have passed the word round among his friends, for subsequent raiding parties studiously avoided the castle, and a peasant who had succeeded in crossing its threshold was for the future considered to be "home" and out of the game.

Such was Dreever in the olden times. Today the Welshman having calmed down considerably, it had lost its militant character. The old walls still stood, grey, menacing, and unchanged, but they were the only link with the past. The castle was now a very comfortable country house, nominally ruled over by Hildebrand Spencer Poyns de Burgh John Hannasyde Coombe-Crombie, twelfth Earl of Dreever ("Spennie" to his relatives and intimates), but in reality the possession of his uncle and aunt, Sir Thomas and Lady Julia Blunt.

===Chinese===
在威尔士人还没把过剩精力拿去打橄榄球的那些年月，但凡平常日子单调得叫人发闷，他便惯于纠集几个同伙，越境杀进英格兰打草谷，搅得对岸居民苦不堪言。什罗普郡（Shropshire）的德里弗城堡（Dreever Castle），正是为了对付这桩勾当才应运而生，可谓补上了一项久悬未决的需求。一有风吹草动，它便成了避难的港汊：四方百姓涌进堡来，待劫掠的人马散尽，再小心翼翼地探头出去。纵观城堡全史，巨寇硬攻此地的记载仅得一例，而那一遭败得干干净净——这位好汉吃了一勺滚热的铅水，出手者是位名叫约翰的牧师，瞄得分毫不差，显见属于那类讲究玩耍之道的豪侠牧师——于是鸣金收兵，被拾掇得恰到好处，缩回山中老巢，从此音信全无。不过他想必在江湖朋友中间把话递到了：此后各路打劫的队伍都对这座城堡敬而远之，但凡有个农夫得以跨进门槛，往后便算“到了家”，退出这场游戏了。

当年的德里弗便是这般光景。如今威尔士人的火气消停大半，城堡也就失了那股烽火之气。老墙依然矗立，灰沉沉，恶狠狠，一成未改，却成了它与往昔仅存的一线牵连。城堡如今是一处十分惬意的乡间宅邸，名义上由希尔德布兰德·斯宾塞·波因斯·德·伯格·约翰·汉纳赛德·库姆-克龙比（Hildebrand Spencer Poyns de Burgh John Hannasyde Coombe-Crombie）——第十二代德里弗伯爵，亲戚和亲近之人唤他“斯宾尼”（Spennie）——执掌，实际上却握在他叔父叔母托马斯·布伦特爵士（Sir Thomas Blunt）与朱莉娅·布伦特夫人（Lady Julia Blunt）手中。

===Original===
Spennie's position was one of some embarrassment. At no point in their history had the Dreevers been what one might call a parsimonious family. If a chance presented itself of losing money in a particularly wild and futile manner, the Dreever of the period had invariably sprung at it with the vim of an energetic bloodhound. The South Sea Bubble absorbed £200,000 of good Dreever money, and the remainder of the family fortune was squandered to the ultimate farthing by the sportive gentleman who had held the title in the days of the Regency, when Watier's and the Cocoa Tree were in their prime, and fortunes had a habit of disappearing in a single evening. When Spennie became Earl of Dreever there was about eighteenpence in the old oak chest.

This is the point at which Sir Thomas Blunt breaks into Dreever history. Sir Thomas was a small, pink, fussy, obstinate man, with a genius for trade and the ambition of a Napoleon, probably one of the finest and most complete specimens of the came-over-Waterloo-Bridge-with-half-a-crown-in-my-pocket-and-now-look-at-me class of millionaire in existence. He had started almost literally with nothing. By carefully excluding from his mind every thought except that of making money, he had risen in the world with a gruesome persistence which nothing could check. At the age of fifty-one he was chairman of Blunt's Stores, Ltd., a member of Parliament, silent as a wax figure, but a great comfort to the party by virtue of liberal contributions to its funds, and a knight. This was good, but he aimed still higher; and, meeting Spennie's aunt, Lady Julia Coombe-Crombie, just at the moment when, financially, the Dreevers were at their lowest ebb, he had effected a very satisfactory deal by marrying her, thereby becoming, as one might say, the chairman of Dreever, Ltd. Until Spennie should marry money, an act on which his chairman vehemently insisted, Sir Thomas held the purse, and, except in minor matters ordered by his wife, of whom he stood in uneasy awe, had things entirely his own way.

===Chinese===
斯宾尼的处境颇为尴尬。德里弗家自有史以来，从没算得上我们所谓俭省的人家。但凡冒出一个机会，可以让人以一种格外豪放而徒劳的方式把钱赔掉，当朝的德里弗伯爵必定一跃而上，劲头不输一条生龙活虎的猎犬。南海泡沫（South Sea Bubble）一役，吞掉了德利弗家足足 200,000 英镑的清白家财；余下的祖产，又被摄政时代（the Regency）坐拥爵位的那位浪荡爷挥霍到最后一枚铜板——那正是沃蒂耶俱乐部（Watier's）与可可树俱乐部（the Cocoa Tree）如日中天的年月，家财素有一夜之间化为乌有的雅兴。等斯宾尼袭了德利弗伯爵之位，老橡木柜里统共剩着十八便士上下。

布伦特爵士正是在这里闯进德里弗家历史的。托马斯爵士是个矮小、粉团脸、絮叨而固执的人，天生一块做买卖的料，外加一颗拿破仑的雄心，大概是当世“兜里揣着半克朗走过滑铁卢桥——且看我如今”这一流派百万富翁当中最纯粹、最完备的活标本。他的起家几乎当真是从一无所有起步。他小心翼翼地把挣钱以外的念头一概摒诸脑外，便以一股无可阻遏的吓人韧劲在世路上步步高升。五十一岁上，他已是布伦特百货有限公司（Blunt's Stores, Ltd.）的董事长、下院议员，沉默得像一尊蜡人，却因慷慨捐助党务而深为本党上下倚为慰藉，外加持有一顶骑士头衔。这已经不错了，可他胃口犹在更高处；恰逢他结识斯宾尼的姑母朱莉娅·库姆-克龙比夫人（Lady Julia Coombe-Crombie），又恰逢德里弗家的财路落到最低水位，他便通过娶她做成了桩十分满意的买卖，从而摇身一变，当上了姑且可以这么称呼的“德里弗有限公司”董事长。直到斯宾尼娶来一门有钱的亲事为止——这门亲事正是他的董事长竭力催逼的——钱袋子都攥在托马斯爵士手里；除了妻子吩咐的些小琐事（他对妻子又敬又畏，颇不自在），一切都由他说了算。

===Original===
One afternoon, a year after the events recorded in the preceding chapter, he was in his private room, looking out of the window. The view from that window was very beautiful. The castle stood on a hill, the lower portion of which, between the house and the lake, had been cut into broad terraces. The lake itself, with its island with the little boathouse in the centre, was a glimpse of Fairyland.

But it was not altogether the beauty of the view that had drawn Sir Thomas to the window. He was looking at it more because the position enabled him to avoid his wife's eye; and, just at the moment, he was rather anxious to avoid his wife's eye. A somewhat stormy board meeting was in progress, and Lady Julia, who constituted the board of directors, had been heckling the chairman. The point under discussion was one of etiquette, and in matters of etiquette Sir Thomas felt himself at a disadvantage.

"I tell you, my dear," he said to the window, "I am not easy in my mind."

"Nonsense!" snapped Lady Julia. "Absurd! Ridiculous!"

Lady Julia Blunt, when conversing, resembled a Maxim gun more than anything else.

===Chinese===
前一章所记诸事过去一年，一个午后，他在自己的私室里凭窗而望。窗外的景致美极了。城堡坐落山冈，山下房屋与湖之间的一段坡地，修剪成宽阔的层层台地。那一泓湖水，湖心浮着小岛，岛上立着小小的船屋，望去恍如仙境一角。

不过，把托马斯爵士引到窗前的，倒也不全是景致之美。他望着窗外，多半是因为这个姿势便于躲开太太的眼睛；而眼下他正巴不得躲开太太的眼睛。一场火药味颇为浓烈的董事会正在进行，身为董事会全体班底的朱莉娅夫人，正向董事长连珠炮似的发难。争的是一桩礼数问题，而在礼数问题上，托马斯爵士自觉底气不足。

“我跟你说，亲爱的，”他对着窗户说，“我心里不安生。”

“胡说！”朱莉娅夫人厉声道，“荒唐！可笑！”

朱莉娅·布伦特夫人说起话来，最像的一样东西是马克沁机枪（Maxim gun）。

===Original===
"But your diamonds, my dear?"

"I can take care of them."

"But why should you have the trouble? Now, if we—"

"It's no trouble."

"When we were married there was a detective—"

"Don't be childish, Thomas. Detectives at weddings are quite customary."

===Chinese===
“可是你的钻石呢，亲爱的？”

“我自己会保管。”

“可何苦让你操这份心？瞧，要是我们——”

“谈不上费心。”

“咱们结婚那年，就雇了一名侦探——”

“别孩子气了，托马斯。婚礼上用侦探，是再寻常不过的事。”

===Original===
"But—"

"Bah—"

"I paid twenty thousand pounds for that rope of pearls," said Sir Thomas obstinately. Switch things onto a cash basis and he was more himself.

===Chinese===
“可是——”

“呸——”

“那串珍珠，我花了整整两万英镑，”托马斯爵士执拗地说。只要把话头换算成现钱，他就又成了原来的他。

===Original===
"May I ask if you suspect any of our guests of being criminals?" inquired Lady Julia, frostily.

Sir Thomas looked out of the window. At the moment the sternest censor could have found nothing to cavil at in the movements of such of the house party as were in sight. Some were playing tennis, some clock golf, and others were smoking.

"Why not," he began.

"Of course. Absurd! Quite absurd!"

"But the servants. We have engaged a number of new servants lately."

"With excellent recommendations."

===Chinese===
“敢问一句，你是不是疑心咱们哪位客人是作奸犯科之徒？”朱莉娅夫人冷冰冰地问。

托马斯爵士朝窗外望去。此时此刻，凡在视野之内的府上宾客，行止无可指摘，最严苛的检查官也挑不出半点毛病。有的在打网球，有的在玩时钟高尔夫（clock golf），还有的在那儿吞云吐雾。

“怎么就不，”他刚起了个头。

“就是嘛。荒唐！实在荒唐！”

“可还有仆人。最近咱们雇了好几个新仆人。”

“个个带着顶呱呱的推荐信。”

===Original===
Sir Thomas was on the point of suggesting that the recommendations might be forged, but his courage failed him. Julia was sometimes so abrupt in these little discussions. She did not enter into his point of view. He was always a trifle inclined to treat the castle as a branch of Blunt's Stores. As proprietor of the stores he had made a point of suspecting everybody, and the results had been excellent. In Blunt's Stores you could hardly move in any direction without bumping into a gentlemanly detective efficiently disguised. For the life of him Sir Thomas could not see why the same principle should not obtain at Dreever. Guests at a country house do not as a rule steal their host's possessions, but then it is only an occasional customer at a store who goes in for shoplifting. It was the principle of the thing, he thought. Be prepared against every emergency. With Sir Thomas Blunt suspiciousness was almost a mania. He was forced to admit that the chances were against any of his guests exhibiting larcenous tendencies, but, as for the servants, he thoroughly mistrusted them all except Saunders, the butler. It had seemed to him the merest prudence that a detective from a private inquiry agency should be installed at the castle while the house was full. Somewhat rashly he had mentioned this to his wife, and Lady Julia's critique of the scheme had been terse and unflattering.

===Chinese===
托马斯爵士险些便要出口，说那推荐信没准是伪造的，终究勇气不济。朱莉娅在这类小小交锋里，有时着实叫人没有转圜的余地。她体察不到他的立场。他素来隐隐倾向把这座城堡当作布伦特百货的一家分号。当年执掌百货公司，他便奉行见谁都疑的方针，而收效极佳。在布伦特百货，你无论朝哪个方向挪动，都保不齐一头撞上一名伪装得滴水不漏、绅士派头十足的侦探。托马斯爵士死活想不通，同一套方针何以到德里弗就行不通。乡间宅邸的客人照理不会偷主家的东西，可话又说回来，百货公司里也不过是偶有顾客顺手牵羊罢了。要的是这个原则，他想——有备无患，以防万一场。在托马斯·布伦特爵士身上，疑心几乎成了一种癖。他不得不承认，客人当中谁要露出行窃的苗头，多半轮不上；至于仆人，除了管家桑德斯（Saunders），他一概彻头彻尾信不过。府上宾客盈门之际，在城堡里安插一名私家侦探社的侦探，在他看来不过是再审慎不过的本分。他曾有些冒失地把这层意思对太太提过一句，朱莉娅夫人对这项方略的评语，简短而不留情面。

===Original===
"I suppose," said Lady Julia sarcastically, "you will jump to the conclusion that this man whom Spennie is bringing down with him today is a criminal of some sort?"

"Eh? Is Spennie bringing a friend?"

There was not a great deal of enthusiasm in Sir Thomas's voice. His nephew was not a young man whom he respected very highly. Spennie regarded his uncle with nervous apprehension, as one who would deal with his shortcomings with vigour and severity. Sir Thomas, for his part, looked on Spennie as a youth who would get into mischief unless he had an eye fixed on him. So he proceeded to fix that eye.

"I had a wire from him just now."

===Chinese===
“我猜，”朱莉娅夫人讥诮道，“你会立马断定，斯宾尼今天带下乡来的这位朋友，是个什么作奸犯科之徒吧？”

“嗯？斯宾尼要带个朋友来？”

托马斯爵士的话音里谈不上多少热乎气。他这位侄儿，不是他怎么敬重得起来的青年。斯宾尼望向叔父，总是战战兢兢，只当他是位会雷厉风行、从严发落自己种种毛病的尊神。托马斯爵士这边，则把斯宾尼看成个不盯紧点儿就要闯祸的少年郎。于是他当下就把那只眼睛盯了上去。

“我刚收到他一封电报。”

===Original===
"Who is his friend?"

"He doesn't say. He just says he's a man he met in London."

"H'm!"

"And what does 'H'm!' mean?" demanded Lady Julia.

"A man can pick up strange people in London," said Sir Thomas judicially.

===Chinese===
“他那朋友是谁？”

“没说。只说是他在伦敦结识的一个人。”

“唔！”

“这声‘唔！’是什么意思？”朱莉娅夫人追问。

“人在伦敦，什么古怪的人都结识得来，”托马斯爵士慢条斯理地说，俨然法官下判词。

===Original===
"Nonsense."

"Just as you say, my dear."

Lady Julia rose.

"As for what you suggest about the detective, it is, of course, absolutely absurd."

"Quite so, my dear."

===Chinese===
“胡说。”

“全凭你说，亲爱的。”

朱莉娅夫人站起身来。

“至于你那个安插侦探的主意，当然是十足的荒唐。”

“一点不错，亲爱的。”

===Original===
"You mustn't think of it."

"Just as you say, my dear."

Lady Julia left the room.

What followed may afford some slight clue to the secret of Sir Thomas Blunt's rise in the world. It certainly suggests singleness of purpose, which is one of the essentials of success.

===Chinese===
“你可不许动这个念头。”

“全凭你说，亲爱的。”

朱莉娅夫人走出房去。

接下来这一幕，或可为托马斯·布伦特爵士的发迹之谜提供一点小小的线索。它至少足资证明：目标专一，此乃成功的要素之一。

===Original===
No sooner had the door closed behind Lady Julia than he went to his writing-table, took pen and paper, and wrote the following letter:

> **Sir**—With reference to my last of the 28th ult., I should be glad if you would send down immediately one of your best men. Am making arrangements to receive him. Kindly instruct him to present himself at Dreever Castle as applicant for position of valet to myself. I will see and engage him on his arrival, and further instruct him in his duties.
> Yours faithfully,
> Thos. Blunt
> P.S. I shall expect him tomorrow evening. There is a good train leaving Paddington at 2:15.

He read it over and put in a couple of commas, then placed it in an envelope, and lit a cigar with the air of one who can be checked—yes, but vanquished, never.

===Chinese===
朱莉娅夫人身后的房门刚一合拢，他便走到写字台前，提起纸笔，写下了这样一封信：

> **敬启者**——关于本人上月28日去函一节，如蒙贵社即刻遣派最得力干员一名南下，不胜感荷。接待事宜已在布置。请嘱其以应聘本人贴身男仆（valet）为名，径赴德里弗城堡报到。渠到之日，本人当亲自接见、予以录用，并再行面授职务。
> 谨启
> 托·布伦特（Thos. Blunt）
> 又及：候其明晚到埠。2:15 有一班好车自帕丁顿（Paddington）开出。

他把信通读一遍，添上两处逗号，随即装入信封，点起一支雪茄，那副神情俨然在说：拦他一拦，可以；叫他服输，万无此理。

## IX A New Friend and an Old One / 新友与故交

===Original===
On the night of the day on which Sir Thomas Blunt wrote his letter to Wragge’s, Jimmy Pitt was supping at the Savoy.

If you have the money and the clothes, and do not object to being turned out into the night just as you are beginning to enjoy yourself, there are few things pleasanter than supper at the Savoy Hotel, London. But as Jimmy sat there, eyeing the multitude through the smoke of his cigarette, he felt, despite all the brightness and glitter, that this was a flat world and that he was very much alone in it.

A little over a year had passed since the merry evening at Police Captain McEachern’s. During that time he had covered a good deal of new ground. His restlessness had reasserted itself. Somebody had mentioned Morocco in his hearing, and a fortnight later he was in Fez.

Of the principals in that night’s drama he had seen nothing more. It was only when walking home on air, rejoicing over the strange chance which had led to his finding and having speech with the lady of the *Mauretania*—he had reached Fifty-Ninth Street—that he realised that he had also lost her. It suddenly came home to him that not only did he not know her address, but was also ignorant of her name. Spike had called the man with the revolver “boss” throughout—only that and nothing more. Except that he was a police captain, Jimmy knew as little about him as he had done before their meeting. And Spike, who held the key to the mystery, had vanished. His acquaintances of that night had passed out of his life like figures in a waking dream. As far as the big man with the pistol was concerned, this did not distress him. He had only known that massive person for about a quarter of an hour, but to his thinking that was ample. Spike he would have liked to have met again, but he bore the separation with fortitude. There remained the girl of the ship, and she had haunted him with unfailing persistence during every one of the three hundred and eighty-four days which had passed since their meeting.

===Chinese===
就在托马斯·布伦特爵士致函 Wragge 私家侦探社的那个夜晚，吉米·皮特正在萨伏伊大饭店（the Savoy）用夜宵。

只要你有钱，有像样的行头，又不嫌弃正当你刚尝到乐子的时候就被请出大门、抛进夜色里去，那么伦敦萨伏伊大饭店的一顿夜宵，可算人间数一数二的乐事。可是吉米坐在那里，隔着香烟的青烟打量满堂宾客，只觉得纵然满目光华璀璨，这个世界依然乏味得很，而他在里头孤孤单单。

距离警察局长麦克伊彻恩家那个快活的夜晚，一年多的时间过去了。这期间他跑了不少新地方。他那颗躁动的心又故态复萌。有人在他耳边提了一句摩洛哥，不出两个礼拜，他人已经在非斯（Fez）了。

那一夜这出戏里的各位主角，此后他再没见过。那天他一路脚下生风、飘飘然往家走，正为那桩奇缘得意——居然撞见了「毛里塔尼亚号」上的姑娘，还同她讲了话——走到第五十九街的时候，才猛然醒悟自己也把她丢了。他倏地想到，自己不但不知道她的住址，连她的名字也不知道。斯派克自始至终只管那个持枪的人叫「老大」，除这一声之外什么也没漏。除去知道他是位警察局长，吉米对他的了解不比见面之前多分毫。而握着谜底的斯派克，也踪影全无。那一晚结识的人，一个个都像白日梦里的人影，从他生活中退了场。就那位持枪大汉而言，这倒并不叫他难受。那位大块头他统共结识了约莫一刻钟，可在吉米看来，一刻钟尽够了。斯派克他倒真想再见一面，不过分手分得也算坚忍。剩下的是船上那位姑娘：自打相遇那天起，三百八十四天里，她一天不落地萦绕在他心头。

===Original===
It was the thought of her that had made New York seem cramped. For weeks he had patrolled the more likely streets, the Park, and Riverside Drive in the hope of meeting her. He had gone to the theatres and restaurants, but with no success. Sometimes he had wandered through the Bowery on the chance of meeting Spike. He had seen red heads in profusion, but none that belonged to his young disciple in the art of burglary. In the end he had wearied of the search, and, to the disgust of Arthur Mifflin and his other friends of the Strollers’, had gone out again on his wanderings. He was greatly missed, especially by that large section of his circle which was in a perpetual state of wanting a little to see it through till Saturday. For years Jimmy had been to these unfortunates a human bank on which they could draw at will. It offended them that one of those rare natures which are always good for two dollars at any hour of the day should be allowed to waste itself on places like Morocco and Spain—especially Morocco, where, by all accounts, there were brigands with almost a New York sense of touch.

They argued earnestly with Jimmy. They spoke of Raisuli and Kaid Maclean. But Jimmy was not to be stopped. The gadfly was vexing him, and he had to move.

For a year he had wandered, realising every day the truth of Horace’s philosophy for those who travel—that a man cannot change his feelings with his climate, until finally he had found himself, as every wanderer does, at Charing Cross.

===Chinese===
正是这份牵挂，让纽约显得逼仄起来。一连几个礼拜，他在那些最可能碰见她的街上来回巡逻，逛中央公园，沿着里弗赛德大道（Riverside Drive）走了一趟又一趟。戏园子、馆子也都去了，一无所获。有时候他还在鲍里街（Bowery）一带转悠，盼着撞见斯派克。红头发的人他见了个够，可没有一颗脑袋是他在行窃这门手艺上的小徒弟的。到后来，他搜得腻了，不顾亚瑟·米夫林和「闲逛者」俱乐部里一班朋友的败兴，又出门过他的漂泊日子去了。他这一走，叫大家着实惦念，尤其叫朋友圈里那一大批人怅然若失——他们永远处在缺一小笔钱、好撑到礼拜六的状态。多年来，对这些倒霉朋友来说，吉米就是一座随取随用的人形银行。这样一位一天到晚都掏得出两美元的稀世宝货，竟由着他把自己浪费在摩洛哥、西班牙那样的地方，他们替他不值——尤其是摩洛哥：据传闻，那儿的强盗手指头上的功夫，几乎赶得上纽约的水准。

他们恳切地跟吉米辩理，抬出拉伊苏利（Raisuli）和凯德·麦克莱恩（Kaid Maclean）来吓他。可吉米是拦不住的。牛虻叮得他心神不宁，他非走不可。

他漂了一年，日复一日地体会到贺拉斯（Horace）写给出门人的那番道理——人换得了气候，换不了心境——最后，像每一个漂泊者一样，他发现自己到了查令十字车站（Charing Cross）。

===Original===
At this point he had tried to rally. This running away, he told himself, was futile. He would stand still and fight the fever in him.

He had been fighting it now for a matter of two weeks, and already he was contemplating retreat. A man at lunch had been talking about Japan—

Watching the crowd, Jimmy had found his attention attracted chiefly by a party of three a few tables away. The party consisted of a girl, rather pretty; a lady of middle age and stately demeanour, plainly her mother; and a light-haired, weedy young man in the twenties. It had been the almost incessant prattle of this youth and the peculiarly high-pitched, gurgling laugh which shot from him at short intervals which had drawn Jimmy’s notice upon them. And it was the curious cessation of both prattle and laugh which now made him look again in their direction.

The young man faced Jimmy; and Jimmy, looking at him, could see that all was not well. He was pale. He talked at random.

===Chinese===
到这一步，他试着振作。他对自己说，这样一味逃来逃去是徒劳的。他要站定不动，跟心里那股热病较量较量。

这一仗打到如今，约莫两个星期，退兵的念头却已经在肚里盘算开了。吃午饭的时候，有位先生聊起了日本——

望着满堂人影，吉米的注意力主要让隔了几张桌子的一行三人勾了去。这三位当中，有一位姑娘，模样相当标致；一位气派庄重的中年太太，明摆着是她母亲；还有一个头发浅淡、单瘦得像根豆芽菜的年轻人，二十来岁年纪。把吉米的目光引过去的，原是这位小哥几乎不住口的絮叨，还有他隔一小阵便迸出来一通的、格外尖细的咯咯笑。而此刻，絮叨和笑声双双蹊跷地停了，这才让他又朝那一桌望去。

那年轻人正对着吉米坐。吉米端详他，看得出情形不妙。他脸色发白，说话前言不搭后语。

===Original===
Jimmy caught his eye. There was a hunted look in it.

Given the time and the place, there were only two things which could have caused that look. Either the light-haired young man had seen a ghost, or he had suddenly realised that he had not enough money to pay the bill.

Jimmy’s heart went out to the sufferer. He took a card from his case, scribbled the words “Can I help?” on it, and gave it to a waiter to take to the young man, who was now in a state bordering on collapse.

The next moment the light-haired one was at his table, talking in a feverish whisper.

===Chinese===
吉米碰上了他的目光。那目光里有一副被追猎的神气。

论此时此地，能造成那种神气的只有两桩事：要么这位浅发年轻人撞见了鬼，要么他猛然发觉身上的钱不够付账。

吉米对这位受难者满心怜惜。他从名片夹里抽出一张，抹上「能帮上忙吗？」几个字，交给侍者送去给那年轻人——后者此刻已经濒临瘫软。

转眼之间，那位浅发先生已经站到了他的桌边，压着嗓子急煎煎地低声说话。

===Original===
“I say,” he said, “it’s frightfully good of you, old chap; it’s frightfully awkward. I’ve come out with too little money. I hardly like to—. You’ve never seen me before—”

“Don’t rub in my misfortunes,” pleaded Jimmy. “It wasn’t my fault.”

He placed a £5 note on the table.

“Say when,” he said, producing another.

===Chinese===
「我说，」他说，「您真是太好心了，老兄；这事儿实在狼狈。我出门带的钱太少。实在不好意思开这个口——咱俩素昧平生——」

「别哪壶不开提哪壶，」吉米央告道，「这可不是我的错。」

他把一张五英镑的钞票放到桌上。

「不够就言语一声。」他说着又抽出一张。

===Original===
“I say, thanks fearfully,” the young man said. “I don’t know what I’d have done.” He grabbed at the note. “I’ll let you have it back tomorrow. Here’s my card. Is your address on your card? I can’t remember. Oh, by Jove, I’ve got it in my hand all the time.” The gurgling laugh came into action again, freshened and strengthened by its rest. “Savoy Mansions, eh? I’ll come round tomorrow. Thanks frightfully again, old chap. I don’t know what I should have done.”

“It’s been a treat,” said Jimmy deprecatingly.

The young man flitted back to his table, bearing the spoil. Jimmy looked at the card he had left. “Lord Dreever,” it read, and in the corner the name of a well-known club. The name Dreever was familiar to Jimmy. Everyone knew of Dreever Castle, partly because it was one of the oldest houses in England, but principally because for centuries it had been advertised by a particularly gruesome ghost story. Everyone had heard of the secret of Dreever, which was known only to the Earl and the family lawyer, and confided to the heir at midnight on his twenty-first birthday. Jimmy had come across the story in corners of the papers all over the States, from New York to Onehorseville, Iowa. He looked with interest at the light-haired young man—the latest depository of the awful secret. It was popularly supposed that the heir, after hearing it, never smiled again: but it did not seem to have affected the present Lord Dreever to any great extent. His gurgling laugh was drowning the orchestra. Probably, Jimmy thought, when the family lawyer had told the light-haired young man the secret, the latter’s comment had been, “No, really? By Jove, I say, you know!”

Jimmy paid his bill and got up to go.

===Chinese===
「我说，真是万分感谢，」年轻人说，「真不知道方才可怎么办才好。」他一把攥住钞票。「明天一准儿还您。这是我的名片。您名片上印着住址没有？我记不清了。哦，天哪，这不是一直在我手里攥着嘛。」那咕嘟咕嘟的笑声重新开动，歇过一阵，愈发清亮有力。「萨伏伊公寓（Savoy Mansions），是吧？我明天就过去。再次万分感谢，老兄。真不知道方才可怎么办才好。」

「这可真是桩乐事，」吉米谦辞道。

年轻人揣着战利品，飘然回到自己桌上。吉米端详他留下的名片，上面印着「德利弗勋爵（Lord Dreever）」，角上是一家知名俱乐部的名字。德利弗这个姓氏，吉米不陌生。德里弗城堡（Dreever Castle）无人不晓：一半因为它是英格兰最古老的宅第之一，但主要还是因为几百年来，一段格外骇人的鬼故事一直在替它扬名。「德里弗的秘密」人人听过：知情者唯有伯爵本人和家族律师，传给继承人的时刻，是他二十一岁生日那天的午夜。这故事，吉米在全美国各地的报纸角落里都撞见过——从纽约一直到爱荷华州的「独马镇」（Onehorseville, Iowa）。他饶有兴致地打量这位浅发年轻人——那桩可怕秘密的最新保管人。按世俗的说法，继承人听完秘密，从此再没笑过；可这话在如今这位德利弗勋爵身上，似乎没有多大效力。他那咕嘟咕嘟的笑声，简直要把乐队都给淹了。吉米想，当年家族律师把秘密说给这位浅发年轻人听的时候，后者的评语八成是：「不会吧？天哪，我说，真的吗！」

吉米会了账，起身要走。

===Original===
It was a perfect summer night—too perfect for bed. Jimmy strolled on to the Embankment, and stood leaning over the balustrade, looking across the river at the vague, mysterious mass of buildings on the Surrey side.

He must have been standing there for some time, his thoughts far away, when a voice spoke at his elbow.

“I say. Excuse me, have you—Halloa!”

It was his light-haired lordship of Dreever.

===Chinese===
那是个十全十美的夏夜——十全十美得叫人舍不得上床。吉米踱到泰晤士河堤岸（the Embankment）上，倚着石栏，隔河眺望萨里那一边影影绰绰、神秘莫测的楼影。

他想必在那儿站了好一阵子，神思早飘到了天边，忽然一个声音在胳膊肘旁开了腔。

「我说，劳驾，您有没有——咦！」

原来是他那位浅发的德利弗勋爵大人。

===Original===
“I say, by Jove! Why, we’re always meeting!”

A tramp on the bench close by stirred uneasily in his sleep as the gurgling laughter ripped the air.

“Been looking at the water?” inquired Lord Dreever. “I have. I often do. Don’t you think it sort of makes a chap feel—oh, you know. Sort of—I don’t know how to put it.”

“Mushy?” said Jimmy.

===Chinese===
「我说，天哪！咦，咱们老碰面嘛！」

咕嘟咕嘟的笑声撕开夜空，长椅上挨着的流浪汉在睡梦里不安地动了动。

「在看河水？」德利弗勋爵问。「我也在看。老这么看。你不觉得它叫人心里有种——哦，您懂的。就是那种——我不知道怎么说才好。」

「酸溜溜的？」吉米说。

===Original===
“I was going to say poetical. Suppose there’s a girl—”

He paused and looked down at the water. Jimmy was with him there. There was a girl.

“I saw my party off in a taxi,” continued Lord Dreever, “and came down here for a smoke. Only I hadn’t a match. Have you?”

Jimmy handed over his matchbox. Lord Dreever lit a cigar, and fixed his gaze once more on the river.

“Ripping it looks,” he said.

===Chinese===
「我本来要说的是『诗意』。假如有一位姑娘——」

他顿住，低头望着河水。这一点上，吉米跟他同感。是有一位姑娘。

「我刚用出租车把我们的那班人马送走，」德利弗勋爵接着说，「就下来抽口烟。偏偏身上没带火柴。您带了吗？」

吉米把火柴盒递过去。德利弗勋爵点上一支雪茄，目光又落回河面。

「真好看呐。」他说。

===Original===
Jimmy nodded.

“Funny thing,” said Lord Dreever. “In the daytime the water here looks all muddy and beastly. Damn depressing, I call it. But at night—” He paused. “I say,” he went on, after a moment, “did you see the girl I was with at the Savoy?”

“Yes,” said Jimmy.

“She’s a ripper,” said Lord Dreever devoutly.

On the Thames Embankment, in the small hours of a summer morning, there is no such thing as a stranger. The man you talk with is a friend, and if he will listen—as, by the etiquette of the place, he must—you may pour out your heart to him without restraint. It is expected of you.

===Chinese===
吉米点点头。

「怪就怪在这儿，」德利弗勋爵说。「白天这水瞧着浑浊不堪，面目可憎。依我说，丧气透顶。可到了晚上——」他顿了顿。「我说，」过了一会儿，他接着道，「在萨伏伊跟我同桌的那位姑娘，您瞧见了吗？」

「瞧见了。」吉米说。

「她真是百里挑一。」德利弗勋爵满脸虔诚地说。

在泰晤士河堤上，夏日凌晨的时分，没有陌生人这一说。你与之交谈的那个人便是朋友；只要他肯听——按此地的礼数，他也非听不可——你尽可以毫无顾忌地把心掏给他。这是大家对你的指望。

===Original===
“I’m fearfully in love with her,” said his lordship.

“She looked a charming girl,” said Jimmy.

They examined the water in silence. From somewhere out in the night came the sound of oars, where the police boat moved on its patrol.

“Does she make you want to go to Japan?” asked Jimmy suddenly.

===Chinese===
「我爱她爱得要死要活。」勋爵大人说。

「她看上去是位可爱的姑娘。」吉米说。

两人默默检阅起河水来。夜里不知何处传来桨声——警察的巡逻艇正巡着它那一趟。

「她是不是也弄得你想上日本去？」吉米冷不丁问。

===Original===
“Eh?” said Lord Dreever, startled. “Japan?”

Jimmy adroitly abandoned the position of confidant and seized that of confider.

“I met a girl a year ago. Only really met her once, and even then—oh, well. Anyway, it’s made me so restless that I haven’t been able to stay in one place for more than a month on end. I tried Morocco, and had to quit. I tried Spain, and that wasn’t any good either. The other day I heard a fellow say that Japan was a pretty interesting sort of country. I was wondering whether I wouldn’t give it a trial.”

Lord Dreever regarded this travelled man with interest.

===Chinese===
「嗯？」德利弗勋爵吓了一跳。「日本？」

吉米手腕利落，一转身便交出了知心人的位子，抢下了倾诉人的位子。

「一年前，我遇见过一位姑娘。正经见面只那么一回，就连那一回——唉，也罢。总之，这事儿搅得我六神无主，一个地方连着待上一个月都待不住。摩洛哥我试过，没撑住，撤了。西班牙也试了，一样不成。前几天听一位先生说，日本是个相当有意思的国家。我正琢磨着要不要去试一试。」

德利弗勋爵兴致勃勃地端详起这位周游列国的人物。

===Original===
“It beats me,” he said wonderingly. “What do you want to leg it about the world like that for? What’s the trouble? Why don’t you stay where the girl is?”

“I don’t know where she is.”

“Don’t know?”

“She disappeared.”

“Where did you see her last?” asked his lordship, as if Molly were a mislaid penknife.

===Chinese===
「我真想不透，」他纳罕道。「你要这么满世界撒开腿跑，图的什么呀？到底是什么毛病？姑娘在哪儿，你干嘛不就在哪儿待着？」

「我不知道她在哪儿。」

「不知道？」

「她失踪了。」

「你末一回在哪儿见着她？」勋爵大人问，口气仿佛莫莉是件搁丢了的小折刀。

===Original===
“New York.”

“But how do you mean, disappeared? Don’t you know her address?”

“I don’t even know her name.”

“But dash it all, I say, I mean! Have you ever spoken to her?”

“Only once. It’s rather a complicated story. At any rate, she’s gone.”

Lord Dreever said that it was a rum business. Jimmy conceded the point.

===Chinese===
「纽约。」

「可你这失踪是什么意思？你连她的住址都不知道？」

「我连她叫什么名字都不知道。」

「可这也太不像话了，我说，我说真的！你跟她讲过话没有？」

「就一回。这故事相当复杂。反正，人是没影儿了。」

德利弗勋爵说这真是一桩邪门事。吉米承认此言不虚。

===Original===
“Seems to me,” said his lordship, “we’re both in the cart.”

“What’s your trouble?”

“Oh, well, it’s only that I want to marry one girl, and my uncle’s dead set on my marrying another.”

“Are you afraid of hurting your uncle’s feelings?”

“It’s not so much hurting his feelings. It’s—oh, well, it’s too long to tell now. I think I’ll be getting home. I’m staying at our place in Eaton Square.”

===Chinese===
「依我看，」勋爵大人说，「咱们俩是一对难兄难弟。」

「你的烦难又是什么？」

「哦，这个嘛，无非是我想娶一位姑娘，我叔父却铁了心要我娶另一位。」

「你是怕伤你叔父的心？」

「倒不光是伤不伤心的事。是——唉，说来话长，这会儿讲不完。我看我得回家了。我这阵子住在我们家伊顿广场（Eaton Square）的宅子里。」

===Original===
“How are you going? If you’ll walk I’ll come some of the way with you.”

“Right you are. Let’s be pushing along, shall we?”

They turned up into the Strand, and through Trafalgar Square into Piccadilly. Piccadilly has a restful aspect in the small hours. Some men were cleaning the road with water from a long hose. The swishing of the torrent on the parched wood was musical.

Just beyond the gate of Hyde Park, to the right of the road, stands a cabman’s shelter. Conversation and emotion had made Lord Dreever thirsty. He suggested coffee as a suitable conclusion to the night’s revels.

===Chinese===
「你怎么个走法？要是走着回去，我陪你走一段。」

「好嘞。那咱们这就动身，如何？」

两人拐进河岸街，穿过特拉法尔加广场，上了皮卡迪利大街（Piccadilly）。凌晨时分的皮卡迪利，一派安谧气象。几名工人正用长水管冲洗路面，大水泼在焦渴的木块路面上，唰唰之声有如乐音。

过了海德公园的大门，路右侧立着一座马车夫歇脚棚（cabman’s shelter）。倾谈与抒情使得德利弗勋爵口中生渴。他提议喝杯咖啡，给这一夜的欢会收个得体的尾。

===Original===
“I often go in here when I’m up in town,” he said. “The cabbies don’t mind. They’re sportsmen.”

The shelter was nearly full when they opened the door. It was very warm inside. A cabman gets so much fresh air in the exercise of his professional duties that he is apt to avoid it in private life. The air was heavy with conflicting scents. Fried onions seemed to be having the best of the struggle for the moment, though plug tobacco competed gallantly. A keenly analytical nose might also have detected the presence of steak and coffee.

A dispute seemed to be in progress as they entered.

“You don’t wish you was in Russher,” said a voice.

===Chinese===
「我进城时常上这儿来，」他说。「马车夫们不介意。都是爽快人。」

推门进去，歇脚棚里几乎客满。屋里暖烘烘的。马车夫当差时呼吸的新鲜空气太多，私底下便往往要避着它走。棚中空气沉甸甸的，诸般气味各争高下。当前占上风的似乎是煎洋葱，板烟则奋勇相抗。嗅觉灵敏、善于剖析的鼻子，兴许还能从中嗅出牛排与咖啡的踪迹。

他们进门的时候，一场争论似乎正酣。

「你才不盼着去俄国呢。」有个声音说。

===Original===
“Yus, I do wish I was in Russher,” retorted a shrivelled mummy of a cabman who was blowing patiently at a saucerful of coffee.

“Why do you wish you was in Russher?” asked the interlocutor, introducing a Massa Bones and Massa Johnsing touch into the dialogue.

“Because you can wade over yer knees in bla-a-ad there,” said the mummy.

“In wot?”

“In bla-a-ad—ruddy bla-a-ad. That’s why I wish I wos in Russher.”

===Chinese===
「盼，我就盼着去俄国，」一个干瘪得活像木乃伊的老马车夫回敬道，正耐着性子吹一碟咖啡。

「你干吗盼着去俄国？」问话的那位接口道，这一声里带上了几分黑脸滑稽戏（minstrel show）中博恩斯老爷、约翰逊老爷们的腔调。

「因为在那儿，蹚血能蹚过你的膝盖，」木乃伊说。

「在啥里头？」

「在血——红彤彤的血里头。所以我才盼着去俄国嘛。」

===Original===
“Cheery cove that,” said Lord Dreever. “I say, can you give us some coffee?”

“I might try Russia after Japan,” said Jimmy meditatively.

The lethal liquid was brought. Conversation began again. Other experts gave their views on the internal affairs of Russia. Jimmy would have enjoyed it more if he had been less sleepy. His back was wedged comfortably against the wall of the shelter, and the heat of the room stole into his brain. The voices of the disputants grew fainter and fainter.

He had almost dozed off when a new voice cut through the murmur and woke him. It was a voice he knew, and the accent was a familiar accent.

“Gents, excuse me.”

===Chinese===
「那位老哥真欢实。」德利弗勋爵说。「我说，给我们来点咖啡行吗？」

「日本过后，我兴许再试试俄国。」吉米沉思着说。

那致命的流质端了上来。谈话重新开张。别的行家纷纷就俄国内政各抒高见。吉米若不是困成那样，本可以听得更津津有味。他的后背舒舒服服抵着棚壁，屋里的热气悄悄爬进脑子。争论诸公的声音，一层淡似一层。

他眼瞅着就要打起盹来，忽然一个新嗓音划破嗡嗡的低语，把他惊醒。那嗓音他认得，那口音也是熟得不能再熟。

「各位爷，借光。」

===Original===
He looked up. The mists of sleep shredded away. A ragged youth with a crop of fiery red hair was standing in the doorway, regarding the occupants of the shelter with a grin, half whimsical, half defiant.

Jimmy recognized him. It was Spike Mullins.

“Excuse me,” said Spike Mullins, “is dere any gent in dis bunch of professional beauts wants to give a poor orphan dat suffers from a painful toist something to drink? Gents is courteously requested not to speak all in a crowd.”

“Shet that blanky door,” said the mummy cabman sourly.

“And ’op it,” added his late opponent. “We don’t want none of your sort ’ere.”

===Chinese===
他抬头一看。睡意的迷雾倏然散去。门口立着一个衣衫褴褛的年轻人，满头火红的乱发，咧着嘴打量棚中众人，那笑一半顽皮，一半挑衅。

吉米认出来了。是斯派克·马林斯。

「借光，」斯派克·马林斯说，「这一大堆专业美人里头，有没有哪位爷肯赏一个害着要命渴症的可怜孤儿一口喝的？请诸位爷雅静些，别大伙儿一齐抢着开腔。」

「把那扇破门给我带上。」木乃伊马车夫酸溜溜地说。

「带上再滚。」方才的论敌补上一句。「咱这儿不收你这种货色。」

===Original===
“Den you ain’t my long-lost brudders, after all,” said the newcomer regretfully. “I t’ought youse didn’t look handsome enough for dat. Good night to youse, gents.”

“Shet that door, can’t yer, when I’m tellin’ yer!” said the mummy, with increased asperity.

Spike was reluctantly withdrawing when Jimmy rose.

“One moment,” he said.

Never in his life had Jimmy failed to stand by a friend in need. Spike was not, perhaps, exactly a friend, but even an acquaintance could rely on Jimmy when down in the world. And Spike was manifestly in that condition.

===Chinese===
「这么着，你们到底不是我失散多年的亲兄弟喽，」来人一脸惋惜。「我早寻思，你们几位长得不够俊，配不上这份亲。回见啦，各位爷。」

「叫你关门你就关，听见没有！」木乃伊愈发火冒三丈。

斯派克正一步三回头地往外退，吉米站起身来。

「且慢。」他说。

吉米生平从没有过见朋友有难而袖手的时候。斯派克也许算不得正经朋友，可就算是萍水之交，倒了霉也一样可以指望吉米。而斯派克分明是倒了霉。

===Original===
A look of surprise came into the Bowery boy’s face, followed by one of stolid woodenness. He took the sovereign which Jimmy held out to him with a muttered word of thanks, and shuffled out of the room.

“Can’t see what you wanted to give him anything for,” said Lord Dreever. “Chap’ll only spend it getting tight.”

“Oh, he reminded me of a man I used to know.”

“Did he? Barnum’s what-is-it, I should think,” said his lordship. “Shall we be moving?”

===Chinese===
那鲍里街小子脸上先掠过一丝讶异，跟着换上一副死木头疙瘩似的呆相。他接过吉米递来的一枚一镑金币（sovereign），含混道了声谢，拖着脚蹭出了屋子。

「真不明白你给他钱做什么，」德利弗勋爵说。「这号人转头就得拿去灌黄汤。」

「哦，他让我想起从前认识的一个家伙。」

「是吗？我瞧是巴纳姆（Barnum）那个叫什么来着的展览里出来的吧，」勋爵大人说。「咱们是不是该挪窝了？」

## X Jimmy Adopts a Lame Dog / 吉米收留一条瘸腿狗

===Original===
A black figure detached itself from the blacker shadows and shuffled stealthily to where Jimmy stood on the doorstep.

“That you, Spike?” asked Jimmy, in a low voice.

“Dat’s right, boss.”

“Come on in.”

===Chinese===
一条黑影从更黑的暗处脱了出来，拖着脚，蹑手蹑脚地蹭到吉米伫立的门阶前。

「是你吗，斯派克？」吉米低声问。

「没错，是我，老大。」

「进来吧。」

===Original===
He led the way up to his rooms, switched on the electric light, and shut the door. Spike stood blinking at the sudden glare. He twirled his battered hat in his hands. His red hair shone fiercely.

Jimmy inspected him out of the corner of his eye, and came to the conclusion that the Mullins finances must be at a low ebb. Spike’s costume differed in several important details from that of the ordinary well-groomed man about town. There was nothing of the flaneur about the Bowery boy. His hat was of the soft black felt fashionable on the East Side of New York. It was in poor condition, and looked as if it had been up too late the night before. A black tailcoat, burst at the elbows and stained with mud, was tightly buttoned across his chest, this evidently with the idea of concealing the fact that he wore no shirt—an attempt which was not wholly successful. A pair of grey flannel trousers and boots, out of which two toes peeped coyly, completed the picture.

Even Spike himself seemed to be aware that there were points in his appearance which would have distressed the editor of the *Tailor and Cutter*.

“ ’Scuse these duds,” he said. “Me man’s bin an’ mislaid de trunk wit me best suit in. Dis is me number two.”

“Don’t mention it, Spike,” said Jimmy. “You look a perfect matinee idol. Have a drink?”

===Chinese===
他头前带路，回到自己那套房间，啪的一声拧亮电灯，关上了门。斯派克站在原地，给突如其来的强光耀得直眨眼。两只手捻着他那顶破帽子团团转。一头红发亮得灼灼逼人。

吉米用眼角把他打量了一番，得出结论：马林斯家的财源，想必正闹着枯水。斯派克这身行头，同寻常衣冠楚楚的城中阔少比起来，在好几个要紧的细节上大有出入。这位鲍里街小子，浑身上下寻不出半点「闲逛者」的气息。他那顶帽子，是纽约东区时兴的软黑呢货色，成色已经很糟，那副模样，倒像它自个儿前一夜在外头熬夜熬过了头。一件黑燕尾服，肘部绽着口子，又沾了泥渍，紧紧扣在胸前——用意显然是遮掩他没穿衬衫这一事实，不过遮得并不算成功。再添一条灰法兰绒裤子、一双靴子——两根脚趾头正忸怩地探出头来——整幅画像至此便告完成。

连斯派克自己似乎也心中有数：他这副尊容，有几处足以叫《裁缝与剪裁》（Tailor and Cutter）杂志的编辑痛心疾首。

「这身行头您多包涵，」他说。「我的贴身男仆一不留神，把装着我顶好西装的那口箱子给弄丢了。这身是二号。」

「不必客气，斯派克，」吉米说。「你这副模样，活脱脱一位十全十美的日场偶像（matinee idol）。来一杯？」

===Original===
Spike’s eyes gleamed as he reached for the decanter. He took a seat.

“Cigar, Spike?”

“Sure. T’anks, boss.”

Jimmy lit his pipe. Spike, after a few genteel sips, threw off his restraint and finished the rest of his glass at a gulp.

“Try another?” suggested Jimmy.

Spike’s grin showed that the idea had been well received.

===Chinese===
斯派克伸手去够玻璃酒瓶，双眼放光。他落了座。

「来支雪茄吗，斯派克？」

「那敢情好。多谢，老大。」

吉米点上了烟斗。斯派克斯斯文文地啜了几口，随即再按捺不住，一仰脖把杯里剩下的灌了个干净。

「再来一杯？」吉米提议。

斯派克咧嘴一笑，表示这项提议大受欢迎。

===Original===
Jimmy sat and smoked in silence for a while. He was thinking the thing over. He felt like a detective who has found a clue. At last he would be able to discover the name of the *Mauretania* girl. The discovery would not take him very far, certainly, but it would be something. Possibly Spike might even be able to fix the position of the house they had broken into that night.

Spike was looking at him over his glass with silent admiration. This flat, which Jimmy had rented for a year, in the hope that the possession of a fixed abode might help to tie him down to one spot, was handsomely, even luxuriously, furnished. To Spike every chair and table in the room had a romance of its own as having been purchased out of the proceeds of that New Asiatic Bank robbery, or from the revenue accruing from the Duchess of Havent’s jewels. He was dumb with reverence for one who could make burglary pay to this extent. In his own case the profession had rarely provided anything more than bread and butter and an occasional trip to Coney Island.

Jimmy caught his eye, and spoke.

“Well, Spike,” he said. “Curious that we should meet like this?”

“De limit,” agreed Spike.

===Chinese===
吉米坐着闷头抽烟，抽了一阵。他在心里盘算这桩事。他觉得自己像个寻获了线索的侦探。「毛里塔尼亚号」姑娘的名字，他终于有指望查明了。这个发现当然带不了他走多远，可总归是桩收获。兴许连他们那晚撬进去的那栋房子坐落何处，斯派克都还指得出方位来。

斯派克端着杯子，眼巴巴地望着他，满心仰慕，说不出话来。这套公寓，吉米租下了一年，指望有个固定住处，或能把自己拴在一处地方。屋里陈设得漂漂亮亮，甚至可称奢华。在斯派克眼里，屋中每把椅子、每张桌子都自有一段罗曼史：不是用那桩「新亚细亚银行」（the new Asiatic Bank）劫案的赃款置办的，便是拿哈文特公爵夫人（Duchess of Havent）那批珠宝生出的进项添置的。入室行窃这门营生，竟能让人做出这等进项，他敬慕得哑口无言。轮到他自己干这一行，进项向来少得可怜，除了一口面包黄油，顶多偶尔逛一趟康尼岛（Coney Island）。

吉米迎上他的目光，开了口。

「我说，斯派克，」他说。「咱们居然这么巧碰上了，怪事，嗯？」

「邪门到家了。」斯派克附和道。

===Original===
“I can’t imagine you three thousand miles from New York. How do you know the cars still run both ways on Broadway?”

A wistful look came into Spike’s eyes.

“I t’ought it was time I give old Lunnon a call. T’ings was getting too fierce in Noo York. De cops was layin’ for me. Dey didn’t seem like as if they had any use for me. So I beat it.”

“Bad luck,” said Jimmy.

“Fierce,” agreed Spike.

“Do you know, Spike,” said Jimmy, “I spent a great deal of time before I left New York looking for you.”

===Chinese===
「真难想象，你如今离纽约有三千英里。你怎么知道百老汇的街车还两头对开？」

斯派克眼里泛起一抹怅惘。

「我寻思着，也该来拜会拜会老伦敦了。纽约那阵子风头太紧。警察到处设伏候着我。瞧他们那意思，是再不用得着我了。我就脚底抹油了。」

「背运。」吉米说。

「紧得很。」斯派克附和。

「跟你说，斯派克，」吉米说，「我离开纽约之前，花了老大的工夫找你来着。」

===Original===
“Gee, I wish you’d found me. And did youse want me to help on some lay, boss?”

“Well, no, not that. Do you remember that night we broke into that house uptown—the police captain’s house?”

“Sure.”

“What was his name?”

“What, de cop’s? Why, McEachern, boss.”

“Mac what? How do you spell it?”

===Chinese===
「哎呀，您找着我就好了。怎么，老大，是有桩买卖要我搭把手？」

「呃，不，不是为这个。还记得咱们那天夜里撬进城里上城那栋宅子的吗——就是那位警察局长的府上？」

「记得。」

「他叫什么来着？」

「哪个，那位局长？嗨，麦克伊彻恩（McEachern）嘛，老大。」

「麦克什么？怎么个拼法？」

===Original===
“Search me,” said Spike, simply.

“Say it again. Fill your lungs and enunciate slowly and clearly. Be bell-like. Now.”

“McEachern.”

“Ah! And where was the house? Can you remember that?”

Spike’s forehead wrinkled.

“It’s gone,” he said at last. “It was somewheres up some street up de town.”

===Chinese===
「这可问倒我了。」斯派克老老实实地说。

「再说一遍。深吸一口气，放慢速度，吐字清楚，要敲得像口钟。来。」

「麦克伊彻恩。」

「啊！那栋房子在哪儿？这个还记得吗？」

斯派克的脑门拧了起来。

「忘了，」他末了说。「就在城里头，上城哪条街上的什么地方。」

===Original===
“That’s a lot of help,” said Jimmy. “Try again.”

“It’ll come back some time, boss, sure.”

“Then I’m going to keep an eye on you till it does. Just for the moment you’re the most important man in the world to me. Where are you living?”

“Me? Why, in de Park. Dat’s right. One of dem swell detached benches wit a southern exposure.”

“Well, unless you prefer it, you needn’t sleep in the Park any more. You can pitch your moving tent with me.”

“What, here, boss?”

===Chinese===
「这可真是帮了大忙了，」吉米说。「再想想。」

「早晚会想起来，老大，包管。」

「那我就看住你，直到你想起来为止。至少眼下这一刻，你是天底下顶要紧的人。你如今住哪儿？」

「我吗？嗨，公园里。没错。挑的是那种带南向采光、气派十足的独立式长椅。」

「那好，只要你不嫌，往后不必再睡公园了。把你的流动营帐挪到我这儿来扎吧。」

「什么，就这儿，老大？」

===Original===
“Unless we move.”

“Me fer dis,” said Spike, rolling luxuriously in his chair.

“You’ll want some clothes,” said Jimmy. “We’ll get those tomorrow. You’re the sort of figure they can fit off the peg. You’re not too tall, which is a good thing.”

“Bad t’ing for me, boss. If I’d bin taller I’d have stood for being a cop, and bin buying a brownstone house on Fifth Avenue by this. It’s de cops makes de big money in little old Manhattan, dat’s who it is.”

“The man who knows!” said Jimmy. “Tell me more, Spike. I suppose a good many of the New York force do get rich by graft?”

“Sure. Look at old man McEachern.”

===Chinese===
「除非咱们搬家。」

「这买卖我入了。」斯派克说，在椅子里奢侈地滚作一团。

「你得添几身衣裳，」吉米说。「明天去置办。你这副身板，拿现成的号就能配。个子不算高，这是桩好事。」

「对我可是坏事，老大。我要是长得高些，早就报名投考警察去了，这会儿第五大道上的褐石宅子都置办下了。在咱们小小的老曼哈顿，挣大钱的是警察，正是警察这一号。」

「不愧是行家！」吉米说。「再多讲讲，斯派克。想必纽约警队里，靠捞油水（graft）发家的很有一批？」

「那可不。瞧瞧麦克伊彻恩那老家伙。」

===Original===
“I wish I could. Tell me about him, Spike. You seemed to know him pretty well.”

“Me? Sure. Dere wasn’t a worse grafter dan him in de bunch. He was out for the dough all the time. But, say, did youse ever see his girl?”

“What’s that?” said Jimmy sharply.

“I seen her once.” Spike became almost lyrical in his enthusiasm. “Gee, she was a bird. A peach for fair. I’d have left me happy home for her. Molly was her monaker. She—”

Jimmy was glaring at him.

“Drop it!” he cried.

===Chinese===
「我倒真想瞧瞧。给我讲讲他，斯派克。你跟他仿佛混得挺熟。」

「我？那可不。一帮人里头，捞油水的本事没一个比得上他。他成天惦记的就是搂票子。不过，喂，您见过他家那位姑娘没有？」

「你说什么？」吉米厉声问。

「我见过一回。」斯派克来了兴致，简直诗兴大发。「哎呀，她真叫俊。水灵灵一颗蜜桃，顶呱呱的成色。为了她，我连快活老家都舍得离。她芳名叫莫莉。她——」

吉米瞪着他。

「打住！」他喝道。

===Original===
“What’s dat, boss?” said Spike.

“Cut it out!” said Jimmy savagely.

Spike looked at him amazed.

“Sure,” he said, puzzled, but realising that his words had not pleased the great man.

Jimmy chewed the stem of his pipe irritably, while Spike, full of excellent intentions, sat on the edge of his chair drawing sorrowfully at his cigar and wondering what he had done to give offence.

“Boss?” said Spike.

===Chinese===
「怎么啦，老大？」斯派克说。

「闭嘴！」吉米恶声恶气地说。

斯派克愕然望着他。

「好嘞，」他说，满腹狐疑，但明白自己的话惹得这位大人物不痛快了。

吉米烦躁地咬着烟斗柄。斯派克呢，一腔天大的好意，在椅子边上坐着，苦着脸吧嗒雪茄，直纳闷自己方才哪句话闯下了祸。

「老大？」斯派克唤道。

===Original===
“Halloa!”

“Boss, what’s doin’ here? Put me next to de game. Is it de old lay? Banks and jools from duchesses! You’ll be able to let me sit in on de game, won’t you?”

“I’d quite forgotten I hadn’t told you about myself, Spike. I’ve retired.”

The horrid truth sank slowly into the other’s mind.

“Say! What’s dat, boss? You’re cuttin’ it out?”

“That’s it. Absolutely.”

===Chinese===
「嗯？」

「老大，这儿的行情是个什么章程？给我交个底。还是老买卖吗？银行，外加公爵夫人的珠宝！这回您总肯让我入伙了吧？」

「我倒忘了还没跟你交代我自己的事，斯派克。我金盆洗手了。」

这可怕的事实，缓缓沉入了对方的脑子。

「喂！这话什么意思，老大？您收山啦？」

「正是。彻底收山。」

===Original===
“Ain’t youse swiping no more jools?”

“Not me.”

“Nor usin’ de what’s-its-name blowpipe?”

“I have sold my oxyacetylene blowpipe, given away my anaesthetics, and am going to turn over a new leaf and settle down as a respectable citizen.”

Spike gasped. His world had fallen about his ears. His excursion with Jimmy, the master cracksman, in New York had been the highest and proudest memory of his life, and now that he had met him again in London, he had looked forward to a long and prosperous partnership in crime. He was content that his own share in the partnership should be humble. It was enough for him to be connected, however humbly, with such a master. He had looked upon the richness of London, and he had said with Blucher, “What a city to loot!”

And here was his idol shattering his visions with a word.

===Chinese===
「珠子也不摸啦？」

「我不摸了。」

「那个叫什么来着的吹管也不使啦？」

「我的氧乙炔吹管已经卖掉，麻药也送了人，往后要翻开新的一页，安安分分做个规规矩矩的良民。」

斯派克倒抽一口冷气。他的天塌了。当年在纽约跟着盗术宗师吉米走的那一遭，是他一生顶风光、顶得意的记忆；如今在伦敦重逢，他满心指望的，便是一段又长久又兴旺的犯罪合伙生涯。他自己在这份合伙里份额再卑微也不要紧；能同这样一位宗师沾上边，无论沾得多么低微，于愿已足。他瞻仰过伦敦的富庶，还学布吕歇尔（Blücher）元帅赞叹过一句：「好一座劫掠的城！」

而他的偶像，一句话便把他的黄粱美梦砸了个粉碎。

===Original===
“Have another drink, Spike,” said the Lost Leader sympathetically. “It’s a shock to you, I expect.”

“I t’ought, boss—”

“I know, I know. These are life’s tragedies. I’m very sorry for you; but it can’t be helped.”

Spike sat silent, with a long face. Jimmy slapped him on the shoulder.

“Cheer up,” he said. “How do you know that living honestly may not be splendid fun? Numbers of people do it, you know, and enjoy themselves tremendously. You must give it a trial, Spike.”

“Me, boss? What, me too?”

===Chinese===
「再喝一杯，斯派克，」这位「迷失的领袖」（the Lost Leader）满怀同情地说。「我料想，这对你是个不小的打击。」

「我原本还当，老大——」

「我知道，我知道。这些都是人生的悲剧。我很替你难过；可这事没法子。」

斯派克拉长了脸，闷声坐着。吉米拍拍他的肩膀。

「打起精神来，」他说。「你怎么就知道，规规矩矩做人不见得是桩天大的乐事？这么过日子的人可不少，不瞒你说，还过得痛快之至。你总得试一试，斯派克。」

「我吗，老大？怎么，连我也算上？」

===Original===
“Rather. You’re my link with—I don’t want to have you remembering that address in the second month of a ten-year spell at Dartmoor. I’m going to look after you, Spike, my son, like a lynx. We’ll go out together and see life. Buck up, Spike! Be cheerful! Grin!”

After a moment’s reflection the other grinned, howbeit faintly.

“That’s right,” said Jimmy. “We’ll go into society, Spike, hand in hand. You’ll be a terrific success in society. All you have to do is to look cheerful, brush your hair, and keep your hands off the spoons, for in the best circles they invariably count them after the departure of the last guest.”

“Sure,” said Spike, as one who thoroughly understood this sensible precaution.

“And now,” said Jimmy, “we’ll be turning in. Can you manage sleeping on the sofa one night? Some fellows would give their bed up to you. However, I’ll have a bed made up for you tomorrow.”

“Me!” said Spike. “Gee! I’ve been sleepin’ in de Park all de last week. Dis is to de good, boss.”

===Chinese===
「那还用说。你是我与——之间的一条纽带。我可不想叫你在达特穆尔（Dartmoor）监狱十年刑期的第二个月里，还惦记着那处门牌。我会像猞猁似的看牢你，斯派克，我的孩子。咱们一块儿出去见见世面。振作点，斯派克！高兴起来！笑一个！」

对方寻思了片刻，咧嘴笑了，虽然笑得颇为勉强。

「这就对了，」吉米说。「咱们要携手并肩，一同跻身上流社会，斯派克。你在上流社会准能大放异彩。你只需做到三条：面带喜色，头发梳齐，手别去碰汤匙——顶体面的人家，末一位客人前脚出门，后脚便要一根根清点汤匙的。」

「明白，」斯派克说，一副把这番稳妥防备摸得透透的样子。

「好了，」吉米说，「咱们该歇息了。今晚在沙发上凑合一宿，成吗？换了旁人，兴许把床让给你。不过，明天我就叫人给你铺张床。」

「还让我睡沙发！」斯派克说。「哎呀！上个礼拜我可是夜夜睡公园。这可是净赚，老大。」

## XI At the Turn of the Road / 路之转折处

===Original===
On the morning after the meeting at the Savoy when Jimmy, having sent Spike off to the tailor’s, was dealing with a combination of breakfast and lunch at his flat, Lord Dreever called.

“Thought I should find you in,” observed his lordship. “Well, laddie, how goes it? Having breakfast? Eggs *and* bacon! Great Scot, I couldn’t touch a thing!”

The statement was borne out by his looks. The son of a hundred earls was pale, and his eyes were markedly fishlike.

“A fellow I’ve got stopping with me—taking him down to Dreever with me today—man I met at the club—fellow named Hargate. Don’t know if you know him? No? Well, he was still up when I got back last night, and we stayed up playing pills—he’s rotten at pills; something frightful; I give him thirty—till five this morning. I feel frightfully cheap. Wouldn’t have got up at all, only I’m due to catch the two-fifteen down to Dreever. It’s the only good train.”

He dropped into a chair.

===Chinese===
萨伏伊那场会面的第二天上午，吉米把斯派克打发去裁缝店，正在自己的公寓里对付一顿早饭午饭二合一的饭食，德利弗勋爵来访。

「想着你总该在家，」勋爵大人开腔道。「怎么样，老弟，近来如何？正吃早饭呢？鸡蛋**加**培根！我的老天，我现在什么都咽不下！」

这番话从他脸色上得到了印证。这位「一百位伯爵之子」脸色苍白，眼睛里透着一股鲜明的呆鱼神气。

「我家里如今住着一位客人——今天就要带他一道下德里弗去——俱乐部里认识的人——姓哈盖特。不知道你认不认识他？不认识？呃，我昨儿夜里回去的时候他还没睡，我们就一块熬夜打台球——他的台球臭得要命；臭得惊天动地；我还让他三十分——一直打到今天早上五点。我这会儿浑身发飘，难受极了。本来压根儿起不来床，只因两点一刻下德里弗的火车误不得。那是唯一一班像样的车。」

他一屁股陷进椅子里。

===Original===
“Sorry you don’t feel up to breakfast,” said Jimmy, helping himself to marmalade. “I am generally to be found among those lining up when the gong goes. I’ve breakfasted on a glass of water and a bag of birdseed in my time. That sort of thing makes you ready to take whatever you can get. Seen the papers?”

“Thanks.”

Jimmy finished his breakfast and lit a pipe. Lord Dreever laid down the paper.

“I say,” he said, “what I came round about was this. What have you got on just now?”

===Chinese===
「可惜你吃不下早饭，」吉米说着，给自己抹了一匙橘子酱。「铜锣一响，排队的行列里向来找得着我。想当年，一杯清水、一袋鸟食，我也当早饭吃过。受过那种训练，人就成了有什么抓什么的脾气。报纸看了没有？」

「多谢。」

吉米吃完早饭，点上一斗烟。德利弗勋爵放下了报纸。

「我说，」他开口道，「我这一趟登门，为的是这么件事。你眼下有什么安排？」

===Original===
Jimmy had imagined that his friend had dropped in to return the five-pound note he had borrowed, but his lordship maintained a complete reserve on the subject. Jimmy was to discover later that this weakness of memory where financial obligations were concerned was a leading trait in Lord Dreever’s character.

“Today, do you mean?” said Jimmy.

“Well, in the near future. What I mean is, why not put off that Japan trip you spoke about and come down to Dreever with me?”

Jimmy reflected. After all, Japan or Dreever, it made very little difference. And it would be interesting seeing a place about which he had read so much.

“That’s very good of you,” he said. “You’re sure it will be all right? It won’t be upsetting your arrangements?”

===Chinese===
吉米原以为朋友是专程上门来还那笔五英镑借款的，可勋爵大人对这个话题讳莫如深。吉米日后才会明白：一碰到钱财债务，记性就靠不住了，这是德利弗勋爵性格里的一桩头号特征。

「你是说今天？」吉米问。

「呃，是最近。我的意思是，你何不把说起过的日本之行往后推一推，跟我一道下德里弗去？」

吉米沉吟。说到底，日本也好，德里弗也好，区别并不大。再说，亲眼去看一个他在书里读过那么多回的地方，也是桩有意思的事。

「你真是太好了，」他说。「你确定没问题吧？不会打乱你的安排？」

===Original===
“Not a bit. The more the merrier. Can’t you catch the two-fifteen? It’s fearfully short notice.”

“Heavens, yes. I can pack in ten minutes. Thanks very much.”

“Stout fellow. There’ll be shooting and all that sort of rot. Oh! by the way, are you any good at acting? I mean, I believe there are going to be private theatricals of sorts. A man called Charteris is getting them up. Cambridge man; belongs to the Footlights. Always getting up theatricals. Rot, I call it; but you can’t stop him. Do you do anything in that line?”

“Put me down for what you like, from Emperor of Morocco to Confused Noise Without. I was on the stage once. I’m particularly good at shifting scenery.”

===Chinese===
「一点也不。人越多越热闹。两点一刻你赶得上吧？这么急就说走，实在抱歉。」

「天哪，赶得上。十分钟我就能收拾好行李。多谢多谢。」

「好样的。到时候有打猎啦，诸如此类的无聊名目。哦！对了，你会演戏吗？我是说，我听说要搞个什么业余演剧。一个叫查特里斯（Charteris）的人在张罗。剑桥出身；『脚灯社』（the Footlights）的。老爱张罗演剧。依我说全是瞎胡闹，可谁也拦不住他。这一行你来得来？」

「随便把我报上什么角色都行，从摩洛哥皇帝到『幕外喧哗』都成。我上过一回台。搬布景尤其拿手。」

===Original===
“Good for you. Well, so long. Two-fifteen from Paddington, remember. I’ll meet you there. I’ve got to go and see a fellow now.”

“I’ll look out for you.”

A sudden thought occurred to Jimmy. Spike! He had forgotten Spike for the moment. It was vital that the Bowery boy should not be lost sight of again. He was the one link with the little house somewhere beyond One Hundred and Fiftieth Street. He could not leave the Bowery boy at the flat. A vision rose in his mind of Spike alone in London with Savoy Mansions as a base for his operations. No; Spike must be transplanted to the country. He could not seem to see Spike in the country. His boredom would probably be pathetic. But it was the only way.

===Chinese===
「有你的。那好，回头见。记着，帕丁顿车站两点一刻。我在那儿等你。这会儿我得去见个人。」

「我到站里找你。」

吉米忽然想起一件事。斯派克！他一时把斯派克忘了个干净。绝不能叫这个鲍里街小子再度失去踪影。他是通向第一百五十街外某处那所小房子的唯一一条纽带。不能把鲍里街小子撇在公寓里。一幅图景在他脑中升起：斯派克孤零零地留在伦敦，把萨伏伊公寓立作大本营，开展他那一行的事业。不行；得把斯派克连根移栽到乡下去。他实在想象不出斯派克待在乡下的模样。那份百无聊赖，恐怕要叫人心酸。可也只有这么一条路。

===Original===
Lord Dreever facilitated matters.

“By the way, Pitt,” he said, “you’ve got a man of sorts, of course? One of those frightful fellows who forget to pack your collars! Bring him along, of course.”

“Thanks,” said Jimmy. “I will.”

The matter had scarcely been settled when the door opened and revealed the subject of discussion.
