Wearing a broad grin of mingled pride and bashfulness, and looking very stiff and awkward in one of the brightest tweed suits ever seen off the stage, Spike stood for a moment in the doorway to let his appearance sink into the spectator; then advanced into the room.

===Chinese===
德利弗勋爵倒是替他把事情办顺了。

「对了，皮特，」他说，「你总有那么个男仆吧？就是那种收拾行李忘了给你装箱衣领的可怕家伙！当然得把他带上。」

「多谢，」吉米说。「我带上。」

这事刚刚议妥，门一开，讨论的正主儿到了。斯派克咧着一张大嘴，得意与腼腆各掺一半，身穿一身戏台底下难得一见的鲜亮花呢套装，浑身上下僵直而别扭，先在门口站定片刻，好让观者把他的行头领略个够；然后才迈进屋来。

===Original===
“How do dese strike you, boss?” he inquired genially, as Lord Dreever gaped in astonishment at this bright being.

“Pretty nearly blind, Spike,” said Jimmy. “What made you get those? We use electric light here.”

Spike was full of news.

“Say, boss, dat clothing-store’s a willy wonder, sure. De old mug what showed me round give me de frozen face when I came in foist. ‘What’s doin’?’ he says. ‘To de woods wit you! git de hook!’ But I hands out de plunks you give me, an’ tells him how I’m here to get a dude suit, an’ gee! if he don’t haul out suits by de mile. Give me a toist, it did, watching him. ‘It’s up to youse,’ says de mug. ‘Choose somet’ing. You pays de money, an’ we does de rest.’ So I says dis is de one, and I put down de plunks, an’ here I am, boss.”

===Chinese===
「这身行头您瞧着怎么样，老大？」他和和气气地问。德利弗勋爵正张着嘴，惊愕地打量这个亮闪闪的人儿。

「差一点就瞧瞎了，斯派克，」吉米说。「你怎么买了这么一身？我们这儿使的是电灯。」

斯派克满肚子都是新闻。

「喂，老大，那家衣庄可真是个神妙地方，没跑。我头一脚跨进去，领着我转的那个老家伙就赏了我一张冰脸。『干啥的？』他说。『上树林子去吧！快滚蛋！』可我把您给的大洋一掏，告诉他我是来置办少爷行头的，好家伙！他就一里一里地往外拖西装。瞧着他折腾，真叫人心里一乐。『看您的了，』老家伙说。『拣一件。您出钱，剩下的归我们。』我就说这件成了，大洋一放，这不，我就成了眼下这副模样，老大。」

===Original===
“I noticed that, Spike,” said Jimmy. “I could see you in the dark.”

“Don’t you like de duds, boss?” inquired Spike anxiously.

“They’re the last word,” said Jimmy. “You’d make Solomon in all his glory look like a tramp cyclist.”

“Dat’s right,” agreed Spike. “Dey’se de limit.”

===Chinese===
「我注意到了，斯派克，」吉米说。「黑灯瞎火我也瞧得见你。」

「您不喜欢这身行头吗，老大？」斯派克不安地问。

「时髦到了顶了，」吉米说。「你往这儿一站，所罗门王盛装华服，也不过像个骑自行车的流浪汉。」

「可不是嘛，」斯派克点头称是。「绝了不是。」

===Original===
And, apparently oblivious to the presence of Lord Dreever, who had been watching him in blank silence since his entrance, the Bowery boy proceeded to execute a mysterious shuffling dance on the carpet.

This was too much for the overwrought brain of his lordship.

“Goodbye, Pitt,” he said; “I’m off. Got to see a man.”

Jimmy saw him to the door.

===Chinese===
这位鲍里街小子显然压根没留意德利弗勋爵在场——后者自他进屋起就一言不发、呆若木鸡地望着他——径自在地毯上跳起一种神秘的曳步舞来。

勋爵大人那颗超负荷的脑子再也吃不消了。

「再会，皮特，」他说；「我走了。得去见个人。」

吉米送他到门口。

===Original===
Outside, Lord Dreever placed the palm of his right hand on his forehead.

“I say, Pitt,” he said.

“Halloa!”

“Who the devil’s that?”

“Who? Spike? Oh, that’s my man.”

“Your man! Is he always like that?—I mean going on like a frightful music-hall comedian, dancing, you know? And, I say, what on earth language was that he was talking? I couldn’t understand one word in ten.”

===Chinese===
到了外头，德利弗勋爵把右手掌心贴在前额上。

「我说，皮特，」他说。

「喂！」

「方才那家伙到底是谁？」

「谁？斯派克？哦，那是我的男仆。」

「你的男仆！他一向都这样吗？——我是说，像一出要命的音乐厅滑稽戏似的闹腾，又蹦又跳的，你知道吧？还有，我说，他说的那到底是哪国话？十个词里我只能听懂一个。」
===Original===
“Oh, that’s American—the Bowery variety.”

“Oh! Well, I suppose it’s all right if you understand it. I can’t. By Gad!” he broke off, with a chuckle, “I’d give something to see him talking to old Saunders, our butler at home. He’s got the manners of a duke.”

“Spike should revise those,” said Jimmy.

“What do you call him?”

“Spike.”

===Chinese===
「哦，那是美国话——鲍里街那一品种。」

「哦！呃，要是你听得懂，想必无妨。我可听不懂。天哪！」他噗嗤一笑，岔开了话头，「要是能瞧见他跟我们家老管家桑德斯讲话，我情愿出点血。桑德斯那派头，跟公爵一个样。」

「那派头斯派克真该修订修订，」吉米说。

「你管他叫什么？」

「斯派克。」

===Original===
“Rummy name, isn’t it?”

“Fashionable in the States; short for Algernon.”

“He seemed pretty chummy.”

“That’s his independent bringing-up. They’re all like that in America.”

“Jolly country.”

===Chinese===
「怪名字，不是吗？」

「在美国时兴这个；是『阿尔杰农』（Algernon）的简称。」

「他看着倒是怪热络的。」

「那是他自小独立养成的。美国人个个这样。」

「真是个好地方。」

===Original===
“You’d love it.”

“Well, so long.”

“So long.”

===Chinese===
「你会迷上的。」

「那好，回头见。」

「回头见。」

===Original===
On the bottom step Lord Dreever halted.

“I say, I’ve got it!”

“Good for you; got what?”

“Why, I knew I’d seen that chap’s face somewhere before, only I couldn’t place him. I’ve got him now. He’s the Johnny who came into the shelter last night—chap you gave a quid to.”

Spike’s was one of those faces which, without being essentially beautiful, stamp themselves on the memory.

===Chinese===
走到最底下一级台阶，德利弗勋爵站住了。

「我说，我想起来了！」

「有你的；想起什么了？」

「呃，我就知道那张脸我从前在哪儿见过，只是对不上号。这会儿对上了。他就是昨儿晚上进歇脚棚的那位老兄——你赏了一镑钱的那个。」

斯派克那张脸属于这样一类：说不上本体俊美，却能给自己在人的记忆里盖下印戳。

===Original===
“You’re quite right,” said Jimmy. “I was wondering if you would recognise him. Would you prefer a cigar or a coconut? The fact is, he’s a man I once employed over in New York, and when I came across him over here he was so evidently wanting a bit of help that I took him on again. As a matter of fact I needed somebody to look after my things, and Spike can do it as well as anybody else.”

“I see. Not bad my spotting him, was it? Well, I must be off. Goodbye. Two-fifteen at Paddington. Meet you there. Book for Dreever if you’re there before me.”

“Right. Goodbye.”

Jimmy returned to the dining-room. Spike, who was examining as much as he could of himself in the glass, turned round with his wonted grin.

“Say, who’s de gazebo, boss? Ain’t he de mug youse was wit last night?”

===Chinese===
「你一点没错，」吉米说。「我还正琢磨你认不认得出来呢。赏你支雪茄，还是赏你个椰子？实情是这样，他是我在纽约那会儿雇过的一个人，在这儿碰上了，眼见分明缺人搭把手，我就又把他雇下了。说起来，我正需要个人替我照管东西，斯派克干这个，不比谁差。」

「原来如此。我这一眼认出来，不赖吧？好，我该走了。再会。帕丁顿两点一刻。那儿见。你要先到了，就替我买好去德里弗的票。」

「好。再会。」

吉米回到餐室。斯派克正对着镜子，把身上照得见的部分逐一检阅，见他进来，照老样子咧嘴一笑，转过身来。

「喂，老大，那家伙是哪位？不就是昨儿晚上跟您在一块的那位吗？」

===Original===
“That’s the man. We’re going down with him to the country today, Spike, so be ready.”

“On your way, boss. What’s dat?”

“He has invited us to his country house, and we’re going.”

“What? Bote of us?”

“Yes. I told him you were my servant. I hope you aren’t offended.”

===Chinese===
「正是他。今天我们就跟他一道下乡去，斯派克，预备着吧。」

「得嘞，老大。这是啥章程？」

「他请我们上他的乡间宅子去，我们这就动身。」

「啥？咱俩一齐去？」

「对。我告诉他你是我仆人。希望你不介意。」

===Original===
“Nit. What’s dere to be offended at, boss?”

“That’s all right. Well, we’d better be packing. We have to be at the station at a quarter to two.”

“Sure.”

“And, Spike.”

“Yes, boss?”

===Chinese===
「不碍。这有啥好介意的，老大？」

「那就成了。好，咱们最好收拾行李去。一点三刻得到车站。」

「没跑。」

「还有，斯派克。」

「啥事，老大？」

===Original===
“Did you get any other clothes besides what you’ve got on?”

“Nit. What do I want wit more dan one dude suit?”

“I approve of your rugged simplicity,” said Jimmy, “but what you’re wearing is a town suit, excellent for the Park or the Marchioness’s Thursday crush, but essentially metropolitan. You must get something else for the country, something dark and quiet. I’ll come and help you choose it, now.”

“Why, won’t dis go in de country?”

“Not on your life, Spike. It would unsettle the rustic mind. They’re fearfully particular about that sort of thing in England.”

“Dey’s to de bad,” said the baffled disciple of Beau Brummell, with deep discontent.

===Chinese===
「除了身上这身，还置了别的衣裳没有？」

「没置。有了少爷行头还要多的干啥？」

「你这粗犷的俭朴，我很赞成，」吉米说，「可你身上这身是城里装，上公园、赴侯爵夫人礼拜四那种摩肩接踵的盛会，再好没有，骨子里却是都市货。乡下得另备一身，颜色深、样式静的。这就走，我帮你去挑。」

「咦，这身在乡下穿不得？」

「万万穿不得，斯派克。它会搅得乡下人心神不宁。英国人对这种事讲究得吓人。」

「白瞎了，」这位博·布鲁梅尔（Beau Brummell）的门徒碰了钉子，好不郁郁地说。

===Original===
“And there’s just one thing more, Spike. I know you’ll excuse me mentioning it. When we’re at Dreever Castle you will find yourself within reach of a good deal of silver and other things. Would it be too much to ask you to forget your professional instincts? I mentioned this before in a general sort of way, but this is a particular case.”

“Ain’t I to get busy at all, den?” queried Spike.

“Not so much as a salt spoon,” said Jimmy firmly. “Now we’ll whistle a cab and go and choose you some more clothes.”

===Chinese===
「还有末了一件，斯派克。我知道你会恕我直说。到了德里弗城堡，你手边就够得着大把的银器和别的物件。要你把职业本能忘掉，这要求不算过分吧？这话我先前笼统提过一回，可这回是具体场合。」

「那我就一单买卖都开不了张了？」斯派克问。

「连把盐勺也不许动，」吉米斩钉截铁地说。「走，咱们这就喊一辆马车，再去给你挑几身衣裳。」

===Original===
Accompanied by Spike, who came within an ace of looking almost respectable in new blue serge (“small gent’s”—off the peg), Jimmy arrived at Paddington with a quarter of an hour to spare. Lord Dreever appeared ten minutes later, accompanied by a man of about Jimmy’s age. He was tall and thin, with cold eyes and tight, thin lips. His clothes fitted him in the way clothes do fit one man in a thousand. They were the best part of him. His general appearance gave one the idea that his meals did him little good, and his meditations rather less. He had practically no conversation.

This was Lord Dreever’s friend Hargate—the Hon. Louis Hargate. Lord Dreever made the two acquainted; but even as they shook hands Jimmy had an impression that he had seen the man before, but where or in what circumstances he could not remember. Hargate appeared to have no recollection of him, so he did not mention the matter. A man who has led a wandering life often sees faces which come back to him later on, absolutely detached from their context. He might merely have passed Lord Dreever’s friend in the street. But Jimmy had an idea that the other had figured in some episode which at the moment had had an importance.

What that episode was had escaped him. He dismissed the thing from his mind. It was not worth harrying his memory about.

Judicious tipping had secured them a compartment to themselves. Hargate, having read the evening paper, went to sleep in the far corner. Jimmy and Lord Dreever, who sat opposite one another, fell into a desultory conversation.

===Chinese===
斯派克换了身新的蓝哔叽，眼看着有几分近乎体面了（「小号绅士装」——现成衣买的），一路相随，吉米提早一刻钟到了帕丁顿。十分钟后德利弗勋爵也到了，身边带着一个跟吉米年纪相仿的人。那人又高又瘦，目光冷冷的，薄嘴唇抿得紧紧的。他那身衣服的合身程度，便是千人之中才有一人消受得起的那种。那身衣服也是他全身上下最好的部分。他这副通相给人的印象是：饭菜没给他带来多少好处，冥思苦想带来的更少。他压根儿不具备谈话这一功能。

这位便是德利弗勋爵的朋友哈盖特——路易斯·哈盖特（the Hon. Louis Hargate，荣誉头衔）。德利弗勋爵给两人引见了；可就在握手的一瞬，吉米模模糊糊觉得此人从前见过，在哪里、何种场合，却记不起来。哈盖特似乎对他毫无印象，他也就没提。过惯漂泊日子的人，常常会遇见一些面孔，日后又浮上心头，却同当初的场合全然脱了节。他兴许不过在大街上同勋爵的这位朋友擦肩而过。可吉米隐隐觉得，此人曾在某件当时颇关紧要的事情里露过面。

那件事究竟是什么，他想不起来了。他把这事从心里撵了出去。犯不上为它折腾自己的记性。

小费使得合宜，他们包下一节独用的车厢。哈盖特看完晚报，到最远的角落里睡觉去了。吉米和德利弗勋爵面对面坐着，东拉西扯地聊开了。
===Original===
At Reading Lord Dreever’s remarks took a somewhat intimate turn. Jimmy was one of those men whose manner invites confidences. His lordship began to unburden his soul of certain facts relating to the family.

“Have you ever met my Uncle Thomas?” he inquired. “You know Blunt’s Stores? Well, he’s Blunt. It’s a company now, but he still runs it. He married my aunt. You’ll meet him at Dreever.”

Jimmy said he would be delighted.

“I bet you won’t,” said the last of the Dreevers, with candour. “He’s a frightful man—the limit. Always fussing round like a hen. Gives me a fearful time, I can tell you. Look here, I don’t mind telling you—we’re pals—he’s dead set on my marrying a rich girl.”

“Well, that sounds all right. There are worse hobbies. Any particular rich girl?”

===Chinese===
行到雷丁，德利弗勋爵的话头转得亲昵起来。吉米是那种神色之间便引人倾诉的人。勋爵大人开始向他和盘托出家里的一些实情。

「你见过我叔父托马斯没有？」他问。「布伦特百货你总知道吧？他本人就是布伦特。如今改成了公司，可还是他当家。他娶了我叔母。到了德里弗你就能见着他。」

吉米说他一定非常乐意。

「我赌你乐不起来，」这位德利弗家的末代子孙坦言道。「他这人可怕得很——无以复加。一天到晚像只老母鸡似的团团转。我跟你说，他可叫我够受。跟你交个底——咱们是哥们儿——他一门心思要我娶个有钱姑娘。」

「这个嘛，听着倒也不坏。比这糟的爱好有的是。有具体哪一位有钱姑娘吗？」

===Original===
“There’s always one. He sticks me onto one after another. Quite nice girls, you know, some of them, only I want to marry somebody else—that girl you saw me with at the Savoy.”

“Why don’t you tell your uncle?”

“He’d have a fit. She hasn’t a penny. Nor have I, except what I get from him. Of course, this is strictly between ourselves.”

“Of course.”

“I know everybody thinks there’s money attached to the title; but there isn’t—not a penny. When my Aunt Julia married Sir Thomas the whole frightful show was pretty well in pawn. So you see how it is.”

“Ever think of work?” asked Jimmy.

===Chinese===
「总有一号。他一个接一个往我身上粘。有几位，你知道，人还挺不错，只是我想娶的是别人——你在萨伏伊瞧见跟我在一起的那位姑娘。」

「那你干不告诉你叔父？」

「他会气得背过气去。她一个子儿也没有。我也没有，除了从他那儿领的。当然，这话咱俩知道就行。」

「那自然。」

「我知道人人都当这爵位连着家财；其实连着的一分钱也没有。我叔母朱莉娅嫁给托马斯爵士的时候，我们那份吓人的家当差不多整个儿都当出去了。所以你瞧，情形就是这样。」

「想过找份工作吗？」吉米问。

===Original===
“Work?” said Lord Dreever reflectively. “Well, you know, I shouldn’t mind work, only I’m dashed if I can see what I could do. I shouldn’t know how. Nowadays you want a fearful specialised education, and so on. Tell you what, though, I shouldn’t mind the Diplomatic Service. One of these days I shall have a dash at asking my uncle to put up the money. I believe I shouldn’t be half-bad at that. I’m rather a quick sort of chap at times you know. Lots of fellows have said so.”

He cleared his throat modestly, and proceeded.

“It isn’t only my Uncle Thomas,” he said; “there’s Aunt Julia too. She’s about as much the limit as he is. I remember when I was a kid she was always sitting on me. She does still. Wait till you see her. Sort of woman who makes you feel that your hands are the colour of tomatoes and the size of legs of mutton, if you know what I mean. And talks as if she were biting at you. Frightful!”

Having unburdened himself of which criticism, he yawned, leaned back, and was presently asleep.

It was about an hour later that the train, which had been taking itself less seriously for some time, stopping at all stations of quite minor importance, and generally showing a tendency to dawdle, halted again. A board with the legend “Dreever” in large letters showed that they had reached their destination.

===Chinese===
「工作？」德利弗勋爵若有所思。「呃，你知道，工作我倒不反对，只是我实在想不出我能干什么。压根儿不知道怎么入手。如今干什么都得先受一种怕人的专门教育，诸如此类。不过跟你说，外交官这行我倒不介意。改天我要斗胆跟我叔父提提，请他出这笔钱。我觉着干那个我不会太赖。你知道，我这个人有时候是相当机灵的。好多人都这样说过。」

他谦虚地清了清嗓子，接着说了下去。

「还不光是我叔父托马斯，」他说；「还有朱莉娅叔母呢。她跟他一样无以复加。记得小时候，她老是把我压得死死的。现在还那样。等你见着她就知道了。那一路的女人，叫你觉得自己两只手红得赛番茄，大得赛羊腿，你明白我的意思吧。说起话来，就像要咬你一口。可怕！」

这条批评卸下了担子，他打了个哈欠，身子往椅背上一靠，不多时便睡着了。

约莫一个钟头之后，那趟有一阵子越来越不拿自己当回事的火车——逢站就停，还净是些无关紧要的小站，总之一副磨磨蹭蹭的做派——又停了下来。一块大字写着「德里弗」的牌子昭告他们：目的地到了。

===Original===
The stationmaster informed Lord Dreever that her ladyship had come to meet the train in the motorcar, and was now waiting in the road outside.

Lord Dreever’s jaw fell.

“Oh, Lord!” he said. “She’s probably motored in to get the afternoon letters. That means she’s come in the runabout, and there’s only room for two of us in that. I forgot to write that you were coming, Pitt. I only wired about Hargate. Dash it, I shall have to walk.”

His fears proved correct. The car at the station door was small. It was obviously designed to seat four only.

Lord Dreever introduced Hargate and Jimmy to the statuesque lady in the tonneau, and then there was an awkward silence.

At this point Spike came up, chuckling amiably, with a magazine in his hand.

===Chinese===
站长告诉德利弗勋爵，夫人已经坐汽车来接这趟车了，这会儿正在外头的路上等着。

德利弗勋爵的下巴掉了下来。

「哦，天哪！」他说。「她八成是开车进来取下午的信件的。那就是说，她开的是那辆小敞车；那车只坐得下两个人。我忘了写信说你也要来，皮特。我只就哈盖特一个人拍了电报。该死，那我只好走去了。」

他的担心果然应验。车站门口那辆汽车很小。显而易见，它是照着只坐四个人的规格设计的。

德利弗勋爵把哈盖特和吉米引见给车斗里那位雕像般的夫人，跟着便是一阵尴尬的沉默。

正在这时，斯派克走上前来，手里拿着一本杂志，和和气气地咯咯直乐。

===Original===
“Gee!” said Spike. “Say, boss, de mug what wrote dis piece must have bin livin’ out in de woods. Say, dere’s a gazebo who wants to swipe de heroine’s jools what’s locked in a drawer. So dis mug—what do you t’ink he does?” Spike laughed shortly, in professional scorn. “Why—”

“Is this gentleman a friend of yours, Spennie?” inquired Lady Julia politely, eyeing the red-haired speaker coldly.

“It’s—”

He looked appealingly at Jimmy.

“It’s my man,” said Jimmy. “Spike,” he added, in an undertone, “to the woods. Chase yourself. Fade away.”

===Chinese===
「哎呀！」斯派克说。「喂，老大，写这篇玩意儿的家伙，准是在深山老林里住的。喏，有个家伙想偷女主角锁在抽屉里的珠宝。那这位老兄——你猜他怎么着？」斯派克短促地一笑，笑里带着行家看外行的轻蔑。「他呀——」

「这位先生是你的朋友吗，斯宾尼？」朱莉娅夫人客客气气地问，一双眼睛冷冷地睃着这位红头发的发言者。

「他——」

他用求助的目光望着吉米。

「是我的男仆，」吉米说。「斯派克，」他压低嗓门补了一句，「上树林子去。自己撤。闪人。」

===Original===
“Sure,” said the abashed Spike. “Dat’s right. It ain’t up to me to come buttin’ in. Sorry, boss. Sorry, gents. Sorry, loidy. Me for the tall grass.”

“There’s a luggage cart of sorts,” said Lord Dreever, pointing.

“Sure,” said Spike, affably. He trotted away.

“Jump in, Pitt,” said Lord Dreever. “I’m going to walk.”

“No, I’ll walk,” said Jimmy. “I’d rather. I want a bit of exercise. Which way do I go?”

===Chinese===
「成，」讪讪的斯派克说。「没错。本来就轮不到我乱插嘴。对不住，老大。对不住，各位爷。对不住，夫人。我这就钻高草棵子去。」

「那边有辆行李车什么的，」德利弗勋爵指了指说。

「成。」斯派克和和气气地应道。他一路小跑去了。

「上车吧，皮特，」德利弗勋爵说。「我走着去。」

「不，我走着去，」吉米说。「我乐意。想活动活动腿脚。往哪边走？」

===Original===
“Frightfully good of you, old chap,” said Lord Dreever. “Sure you don’t mind? I do bar walking. Right-O! You keep straight on.”

Jimmy watched them out of sight and started to follow at a leisurely pace. It certainly was an ideal afternoon for a country walk. The sun was just hesitating whether to treat the time as afternoon or evening. Eventually it decided that it was evening and moderated its beams. After London the country was deliciously fresh and cool. Jimmy felt an unwonted content. It seemed to him just then that the only thing worth doing in the world was to settle down somewhere with three acres and a cow and become pastoral.

There was a marked lack of traffic on the road. Once he met a cart and once a flock of sheep with a friendly dog. Sometimes a rabbit would dash out into the road, stop to listen, and dart into the opposite hedge, all hind legs and white scut. But except for these he was alone in the world.

And gradually there began to be borne in upon him the conviction that he had lost his way.

===Chinese===
「你真是太够意思了，老兄，」德利弗勋爵说。「真的不介意？我最怕走路。好嘞！你一直往前走就是。」

吉米望着他们走得没了影，才迈开悠然的步子跟上。这确然是个乡间散步的绝好下午。太阳正踌躇着，不知该把这光景算作下午还是傍晚。末了它判定已是傍晚，便收敛了光焰。出了伦敦，乡间清新凉爽，叫人通体舒泰。吉米感到一种少有的满足。他觉得此时此刻，世上唯一值得干的事，就是寻个地方安顿下来，置上三英亩地和一头奶牛，做起田园生活来。

路上车马稀少得可观。他遇上过一辆大车，还有一回是一群羊，带着一条和气的狗。有时一只兔子会窜上大路，停住听听动静，又一头扎进对面的树篱，只见后腿蹬，一团白尾翘。除此之外，天地之间只剩他一人。

渐渐地，一个念头越来越沉地压上他的心头：他迷路了。

===Original===
It is difficult to judge distance when one is walking but it certainly seemed to Jimmy that he must have covered five miles by this time. He must have mistaken the way. He had certainly come straight; he could not have come straighter. On the other hand, it would be quite in keeping with the cheap substitute which served the Earl of Dreever in place of a mind that he should have forgotten to mention some important turning. Jimmy sat down by the roadside.

As he sat there came to him from down the road the sound of a horse’s feet trotting. He got up. Here was somebody at last who would direct him.

“Halloa!” he said. “Accident? And, by Jove, a sidesaddle!”

Jimmy stopped the horse, and led it back the way it had come. As he turned the bend in the road he saw a girl in a riding habit running towards him. She stopped running when she caught sight of him, and slowed down to walk.

===Chinese===
走着路，距离是难以判断的，可吉米分明觉得，到这会儿他少说也走出了五英里。准是走岔了道。他分明是一直照直走的；没有比这更直的了。可话说回来，倘若是给德利弗伯爵权充头脑的那件廉价代用品忘了交代某个要紧的岔口，那也正是这种代用品干得出来的事。吉米在路边坐了下来。

他正坐着，大路那头传来了马蹄嘚嘚小跑的声音。他站起身。终于有人可以给他指路了。

「喂！」他喊道。「出了事故？还有，老天，还是副女式侧鞍！」

吉米拦住了马，牵着它顺来路往回走。转过路的弯角，他看见一位穿骑装的姑娘朝他奔来。瞧见他，姑娘停下不跑了，放慢脚步走了过来。

===Original===
“Thank you ever so much,” she said, taking the reins from him. “Dandy, you naughty old thing!”

Jimmy looked at her flushed, smiling face, and stood staring. It was Molly McEachern.

===Chinese===
「太感谢您了，」她说着从他手里接过缰绳。「丹迪，你这个淘气的老东西！」

吉米望着她那张红扑扑、笑盈盈的脸，呆呆地立在那里。正是莫莉·麦克伊彻恩。

## XII Making a Start / 开了个头

===Original===
Self-possession was one of Jimmy’s leading characteristics, but for the moment he found himself speechless. This girl had been occupying his thoughts for so long that—in his mind—he had grown very intimate with her. It was something of a shock to come suddenly out of his dreams and face the fact that she was in reality practically a stranger. He felt as one might with a friend whose memory has been wiped out. It went against the grain to have to begin again from the beginning after all the time they had been together.

A curious constraint fell upon him.

“Why, how do you do, Mr. Pitt?” she said, holding out her hand.

Jimmy began to feel better. It was something that she remembered his name.

“It’s like meeting somebody out of a dream,” said Molly. “I have sometimes wondered if you were real. Everything that happened that night was so like a dream.”

===Chinese===
沉着镇定向来是吉米的看家本领，可这一刻，他发觉自己竟说不出话来。这位姑娘在他心里盘踞得太久了——在他的想象之中，他早已同她熟稔至深。猛地从梦里跌出来，直面一个事实：她实际上近乎是个陌生人——这着实是桩震动。那种滋味，就像面对一位被抹去了全部记忆的朋友。相与了这许久，到头来却得从头再熟起，实在叫他别扭。

一种说不出的局促罩住了他。

「哎呀，您好呀，皮特先生？」她伸出手来。

吉米觉得好受多了。她还记得他的名字，这就不简单。

「简直像打梦里走出来个人，」莫莉说，「我有时候都疑心您到底是不是真有其人。那天夜里发生的事，桩桩件件都像一场梦。」

===Original===
Jimmy found his tongue.

“You haven’t altered,” he said; “you look just the same.”

“Well,” she laughed, “after all, it’s not so long ago, is it?”

He was conscious of a dull hurt. To him it had seemed years. But he was nothing to her—just an acquaintance, one of a hundred. But what more, he asked himself, could he have expected? And with the thought came consolation. The painful sense of having lost ground left him. He saw he had been allowing things to get out of proportion. He had not lost ground. He had gained it. He had met her again and she remembered him. What more had he any right to ask?

“I’ve crammed a good deal into the time,” he explained. “I’ve been travelling about a bit since we met.”

===Chinese===
吉米找回了舌头。

「您一点儿没变，」他说，「看上去同当年一模一样。」

「嗳，」她笑道，「再说也没有多久哇，是不是？」

他心头掠过一丝隐隐的刺痛。对他说来，那仿佛隔了经年累月。可在她眼里，他算不得什么——不过是个相识，一百个里的一个。但他扪心自问：除此之外，他还能指望什么？这么一想，倒也就得了安慰。那种吃了败仗、失了地盘似的痛感消散了。他看明白，是自己由着事情失了分寸。他没有失地，反倒得了地。他重新见到了她，而她还记得他。他还有什么权利讨要更多呢？

「这段日子我可没让它闲着，」他解释道，「打咱们上回见面起，我就在外头跑了些地方。」

===Original===
“Do you live in Shropshire?” asked Molly.

“No. I’m on a visit—at least, I’m supposed to be; but I’ve lost the way to the place, and I am beginning to doubt if I shall ever get there. I was told to go straight on. I’ve gone straight on, and here I am, lost in the snow. Do you happen to know whereabouts Dreever Castle is?”

She laughed.

“Why,” she said, “I’m staying at Dreever Castle myself.”

“What?”

===Chinese===
「您住在什罗普郡（Shropshire）吗？」莫莉问。

「不是。我是来做客的——至少按说该是；可去那儿的路叫我走丢了，渐渐都疑心自己未必到得了。人家叮嘱我一直往前走。我一直往前走了，结果呢，就落得在这雪地里晕头转向。您可知道德里弗城堡大致在哪个方向？」

她笑了。

「哟，」她说，「我自己就住在德里弗城堡呀。」

「什么？」

===Original===
“So the first person you meet turns out to be an experienced guide. You’re lucky, Mr. Pitt.”

“You’re right,” said Jimmy slowly; “I am.”

“Did you come down with Lord Dreever? He passed me in the car just as I was starting out. He was with another man and Lady Julia Blunt. Surely he didn’t make you walk?”

“I offered to walk. Somebody had to. Apparently he had forgotten to let them know he was bringing me.”

“And then he misdirected you! He’s very casual, I’m afraid.”

===Chinese===
「这么着，您撞见的头一个人，恰恰是位经验丰富的向导。您运气不坏，皮特先生。」

「您说得对，」吉米缓缓说道，「我运气不坏。」

「您是同德利弗勋爵一道下来的？我正要出门的当口，他坐汽车打我身边过去的。车上还有另外一位先生和朱莉娅·布伦特夫人。他总不至于让您步行吧？」

「是我自己提出走路的。总得有一个人走。看来他忘了跟府上说他捎了位客人来。」

「结果他还给您指错了路！这位爷未免也太马虎了。」

===Original===
“Inclined that way, perhaps.”

“Have you known Lord Dreever long?”

“Since a quarter-past twelve last night.”

“Last night!”

“We met at the Savoy, and later on the Embankment. We looked at the river together and told each other the painful stories of our lives, and this morning he called and invited me down here.”

===Chinese===
「兴许是有点儿那个倾向。」

「您认识德利弗勋爵很久了吗？」

「打昨晚十二点一刻起。」

「昨晚！」

「我们在萨伏伊碰上的，后来又相逢在河堤上。两人一道望了望河，把各自一生里伤心的故事讲给对方听，今天早上他就登门来访，请我上这儿来了。」

===Original===
Molly looked at him with frank amusement.

“You must be a very restless sort of person,” she said. “You seem to do a great deal of moving about.”

“I do,” said Jimmy. “I can’t keep still. I’ve got the go-fever, like the man in Kipling’s book.”

“But he was in love.”

“Yes,” said Jimmy; “he was. That’s the bacillus, you know.”

===Chinese===
莫莉直率而饶有兴味地打量着他。

「您一定是个安分不下来的人，」她说，「瞧您老是这样搬来搬去、跑来跑去。」

「正是，」吉米说，「我静不下来。我害上了『躁进热』，跟吉卜林（Kipling）书里那个人一个样。」

「可他当时是在恋爱呀。」

「可不，」吉米说，「他正是在恋爱。您要知道，那就是病根——杆菌。」

===Original===
She shot a quick glance at him. He became suddenly interesting to her. She was at the age of dreams and speculations. From being merely an ordinary young man with rather more ease of manner than the majority of the young men she had met, he developed in an instant into something worthy of closer attention. He took on a certain mystery and romance. She wondered what sort of a girl it was that he loved. Examining him in the light of this new discovery, she found him attractive. Something seemed to have happened to put her in sympathy with him. She noticed for the first time a latent forcefulness behind the pleasantness of his manner. His self-possession was the self-possession of the man who had been tried and has found himself.

At the bottom of her consciousness, too, there was a faint stirring of some emotion, which she could not analyse, not unlike pain. It was vaguely reminiscent of the agony of loneliness which she had experienced as a small child on the rare occasions when her father had been busy and distrait and had shown her by his manner that she was outside his thoughts. This was but a pale suggestion of that misery, but nevertheless there was a resemblance. It was a rather desolate, shutout sensation, half-resentful.

It was gone in a moment. But it had been there. It had passed over her heart as the shadow of a cloud moves across a meadow in the summertime.

===Chinese===
她飞快地瞟了他一眼。他霎时变得有味道起来。她正处在耽于梦想、爱作揣度的年纪。本来，他不过是个平平常常的青年，只是举止比她见过的多数青年从容些罢了；转眼之间，他却成了值得细细端详的人物。他身上平添了几分神秘，几分罗曼蒂克。她纳闷：他爱的究竟是怎样一位姑娘？就着这桩新发现重新打量他，她觉得他动人了。仿佛有什么事情忽然发生，把她同他调到了同一根心弦上。她头一回注意到，他那和悦的风度背后藏着一股潜在的力。他那份沉着，不是寻常的沉着，而是受过考验、寻见了自己的人才有的沉着。

而在她意识的最底层，也有某种她理不出头绪的情绪在微微搅动，近乎作痛。它隐隐唤起小时候那种孤苦的滋味：难得的几回，父亲忙得心不在焉，那副神气分明告诉她，她不在他的心思里。眼下不过是那番苦楚的淡淡一痕，然而毕竟相像。那是一种相当荒凉、被关在门外的感觉，还掺着几分委屈。

转瞬它便消散了。可它确曾来过。它掠过她的心头，一如夏日的云影掠过草地。

===Original===
For some moments she stood without speaking. Jimmy did not break the silence. He was looking at her with an appeal in his eyes. Why could she not understand? She must understand.

But the eyes that met his were those of a child.

As they stood there, the horse, which had been cropping in a perfunctory manner at the short grass by the roadside, raised his head and neighed impatiently. There was something so human about the performance that Jimmy and the girl laughed simultaneously. The utter materialism of the neigh broke the spell. It was a noisy demand for food.

“Poor Dandy!” said Molly. “He knows he’s near home, and he knows it’s his dinnertime.”

===Chinese===
好一阵子她站着没言语。吉米也不去打破沉默。他望着她，眼里含着恳求。她怎么就不能明白呢？她一定得明白。

然而迎着他的那双眼睛，是一双孩子的眼睛。

两人正站着，那匹马——它一路都在敷衍了事地啃着路边的短草——抬起头来，不耐烦地嘶鸣了一声。这番做派通身透着人情味，吉米和姑娘不由得同时笑出声来。那一声嘶鸣里赤裸裸的功利主义，把魔咒给破了。那分明是一顿闹哄哄的讨食。

「可怜的『丹迪』（Dandy）！」莫莉说，「它知道到家了，也知道到饭点了。」

===Original===
“Are we near the castle, then?”

“It’s a long way round by the road, but we can cut across the fields. Aren’t these English fields and hedges just perfect? I love them! Of course I loved America, but—”

“Have you left New York long?” asked Jimmy.

“We came over here about a month after you were at our house.”

“You didn’t spend much time there, then?”

===Chinese===
「这么说，咱们离城堡不远了？」

「顺大路走可就绕远了，咱们打田里抄过去。这些英国的田亩和树篱，是不是妙透了？我爱极了！当然，美国我也爱，不过——」

「你们离开纽约很久了吗？」吉米问。

「您上我们家去过后一个月光景，我们就渡海过来了。」

「这么说，你们在那儿没怎么待？」

===Original===
“Father had just made a good deal of money in Wall Street. He must have been making it when I was on the *Mauretania*. He wanted to leave New York, so we didn’t wait. We were in London all the winter. Then we went over to Paris. It was there we met Sir Thomas Blunt and Lady Julia. Have you met them? They are Lord Dreever’s uncle and aunt.”

“I’ve met Lady Julia.”

“Do you like her?”

Jimmy hesitated.

“Well, you see—”

===Chinese===
「父亲刚在华尔街（Wall Street）挣了一大笔。想必我还在『毛里塔尼亚号』上的时候，他就已经在挣这笔钱了。他要离开纽约，我们便没多等。整个冬天我们都待在伦敦。后来又去了巴黎。就是在那儿认识的托马斯·布伦特爵士和朱莉娅夫人。您见过他们吗？他们是德利弗勋爵的叔父和姑母。」

「朱莉娅夫人我倒是见过。」

「您喜欢她吗？」

吉米迟疑了一下。

「这个嘛，您瞧——」

===Original===
“I know. She’s your hostess, but you haven’t started your visit yet, so you’ve just got time to say what you really think of her before you have to pretend she’s perfect.”

“Well—”

“I detest her,” said Molly crisply. “I think she’s hard and hateful.”

“Well, I can’t say she struck me as a sort of female Cheeryble Brother. Lord Dreever introduced me to her at the station. She seemed to bear it pluckily, but with some difficulty.”

“She’s hateful,” repeated Molly. “So is he—Sir Thomas, I mean. He’s one of those fussy, bullying little men. They both bully poor Lord Dreever till I wonder he doesn’t rebel. They treat him like a schoolboy. It makes me wild. It’s such a shame. He’s so nice and good-natured. I am so sorry for him.”

===Chinese===
「我懂。她是您的女主人，可您这客还没正经开始做，所以还赶得上说说您对她的真心话，过后您就不得不装着她十全十美了。」

「呃——」

「我讨厌她，」莫莉干脆利落地说，「我看她又冷又可恶。」

「这个，我可不能说她给我的印象像位女版的切里布尔兄弟（Cheeryble Brothers，狄更斯笔下的行善兄弟）。德利弗勋爵是在车站上把我引见给她的。她仿佛硬撑着受了这一场，只是颇为吃力。」

「她可恶，」莫莉又说一遍，「他也一样——我是说托马斯爵士。他属于那种小题大做、逞威风的小个子男人。他们两个作践可怜的德利弗勋爵，作践得我都纳闷他怎么还不反。他们把他当小学生看待。气死我了。真是造孽。他人那么好，脾气那么厚道。我真替他委屈。」

===Original===
Jimmy listened to this outburst with mixed feelings. It was sweet of her to be so sympathetic; but was it merely sympathy? There had been a ring in her voice and a flush on her cheek which had suggested to Jimmy’s sensitive mind a personal interest in the downtrodden peer. Reason told him that it was foolish to be jealous of Lord Dreever. A good fellow, of course, but not to be taken seriously. The primitive man in him, on the other hand, made him hate all Molly’s male friends with an unreasoning hatred. Not that he hated Lord Dreever. He liked him. But he doubted if he could go on liking him for long if Molly were to continue in this sympathetic strain.

His affection for the absent one was not put to the test. Molly’s next remark had to do with Sir Thomas.

“The worst of it is,” she said, “father and Sir Thomas are such friends. In Paris they were always together. Father did him a very good turn.”

“How was that?”

===Chinese===
吉米听着这番激烈的陈词，百感交集。她这样富于同情，自然甜得很；可这就仅仅是同情吗？她的嗓音里有过一种异样的调子，她的颊上泛过一层红晕，传到吉米敏感的心里，暗示的分明是：她对那位受欺压的勋爵别有情愫。理智告诉他，吃德利弗勋爵的醋是愚蠢的。自然是个好小子，可犯不上当真。然而他身上那个原始人，却叫他没来由地憎恨莫莉的一切男性朋友。倒不是他恨德利弗勋爵。他喜欢他。只是他疑心，倘若莫莉把这副同情的腔调再继续下去，这份喜欢还撑得了多久。

他对那位不在场者的情谊没有受到考验。莫莉接下来的话，转到了托马斯爵士身上。

「顶糟的是，」她说，「我父亲跟托马斯爵士交情好得很。在巴黎，他们成天泡在一块儿。父亲还帮过他一个大忙。」

「这是怎么一回事？」

===Original===
“It was one afternoon just after we arrived. A man got into Lady Julia’s room while we were all out except father. Father saw him go into the room, and suspecting something was wrong, went in after him. The man was trying to steal Lady Julia’s jewels. He had opened the box where they were kept, and was actually holding her rope of diamonds in his hand when father found him. It’s the most magnificent thing I ever saw. Sir Thomas told father he gave a hundred thousand dollars for it.”

“But surely,” said Jimmy, “hadn’t the management of the hotel a safe for valuables?”

“Of course they had; but you don’t know Sir Thomas. He wasn’t going to trust any hotel safe. He’s the sort of man who insists on doing everything in his own way, and who always imagines he can do things better for himself than anyone else can do them for him. He had had this special box made, and would never keep the diamonds anywhere else. Naturally the thief opened it in a minute. A clever thief would have no difficulty with a thing like that.”

“What happened?”

“Oh, the man saw father and dropped the jewels, and ran off down the corridor. Father chased him a little way, but, of course, it was no good; so he went back and shouted and rang every bell he could see and gave the alarm, but the man was never found. Still, he left the diamonds. That was the great thing, after all. You must look at them tonight at dinner. They really are wonderful. Are you a judge of precious stones at all?”

===Chinese===
「是我们刚到的那个下午。除了父亲，我们都出了门，一个男人溜进了朱莉娅夫人的房间。父亲瞧见他进去，疑心不妙，跟着也进了屋。那家伙正要偷夫人的珠宝。藏珠宝的盒子已经叫他打开了，父亲撞见他的时候，那条钻石项链正在他手里攥着。那是我生平见过的最气派的宝贝。托马斯爵士告诉父亲，这条项链他花的是十万美元。」

「可是，」吉米说，「旅馆方面难道没有保管贵重物品的保险柜吗？」

「当然有；可您不了解托马斯爵士。旅馆的保险柜他一个也信不过。他是那种凡事非按自己的章程办不可的人，总觉得自己亲手办事，比托谁代劳都强。那只特制的盒子是他定做的，钻石从来不肯放在别处。小偷自然一眨眼的工夫就把它打开了。像那样一只盒子，手高明的贼毫不费事。」

「后来呢？」

「哦，那家伙一见父亲，撒手扔了珠宝，顺着走廊就跑。父亲追了那么一小截，自然无济于事；只好回去，又是嚷，又是见着铃就按，把警报都放了，可那人始终没有抓着。不过，钻石他留下了。说到底，这才是顶要紧的。今晚吃饭您务必瞧瞧那串钻石。真是了不得。您懂不懂点儿宝石？」

===Original===
“I am, rather,” said Jimmy; “in fact, a jeweller I once knew told me I had a natural gift in that direction. And so, of course, Sir Thomas was pretty grateful to your father?”

“He simply gushed. He couldn’t do enough for him. You see, if the diamonds had been stolen I’m sure Lady Julia would have made Sir Thomas buy her another rope just as good. He’s terrified of her, I’m certain. He tries not to show it; but he is. And besides having to pay another hundred thousand dollars, he would never have heard the last of it. It would have ruined his reputation for being infallible and doing everything better than anybody else.”

“But didn’t the mere fact that the thief got the jewels and was only stopped by a fluke from getting away with them do that?”

Molly bubbled with laughter.

===Chinese===
「还算懂一点，」吉米说，「说真的，我从前认识的一位珠宝商对我讲过，我在这方面有天分。那么，托马斯爵士自然对令尊感激不尽了？」

「他感激得简直决了堤。替父亲办事，怎么也办不够。您想，钻石要是丢了，我敢说朱莉娅夫人准会逼着托马斯爵士给她再买一串，一样地好，一点儿不许差。我敢断定他怕她怕得要命。他竭力不露出来；可他就是怕。何况再掏十万美元且不说，这事也休想有个完。他那副『永不出错、干什么都比旁人强』的名声，非砸了不可。」

「可小偷已经把珠宝弄到了手，只因走了霉运才没能得逞——单凭这一条，那名声不就已经砸了？」

莫莉咯咯地笑成一串。

===Original===
“She never knew. Sir Thomas got back to the hotel an hour before she did. I’ve never seen such a busy hour. He had the manager up and harangued him, and swore him to secrecy—which the poor manager was only too glad to agree to, because it wouldn’t have done the hotel any good to have it known. And the manager harangued the servants, and the servants harangued each other, and everybody talked at the same time, and father and I promised not to tell a soul; so Lady Julia doesn’t know a word about it to this day. And I don’t see why she ever should; though one of these days I’ve a good mind to tell Lord Dreever! Think what a hold he would have over them! They’d never be able to bully him again.”

“I shouldn’t,” said Jimmy, trying to keep a touch of coldness out of his voice. This championship of Lord Dreever, however sweet and admirable, was a little distressing.

She looked up quickly.

“You don’t think I really meant to, do you?”

“No, no,” said Jimmy hastily. “Of course not.”

===Chinese===
「她压根儿不知道。托马斯爵士比她早一个钟头回到旅馆。那样手忙脚乱的一个钟头，我生平没见过。他把经理叫了来，训了个体无完肤，又逼他起誓保密——可怜的经理求之不得，这事传扬出去，旅馆脸上也无光。经理又去训那些仆人，仆人们又互相训，人人同时开口，七嘴八舌；父亲和我答应守口如瓶；所以直到今天，朱莉娅夫人对这事还一字不知。我看她也永远用不着知道；不过哪天兴起，我倒真有心讲给德利弗勋爵听！您想想，他手里攥着这样的把柄！他们往后再也不能作践他了。」

「依我说，别讲。」吉米说，竭力不让话音里带出寒气。这番为德利弗勋爵仗义执言，无论多么甜蜜可敬，总叫他有点儿不好受。

她飞快地抬起头来。

「您不会当真以为我要去讲吧？」

「不，不，」吉米忙说，「当然不会。」

===Original===
“Well, I should think so!” said Molly indignantly. “After I promised not to tell a soul about it.”

Jimmy chuckled.

“It’s nothing,” he said, in answer to her look of inquiry.

“You laughed at something.”

“Well,” said Jimmy apologetically, “it’s only—it’s nothing really—only what I meant is, you have just told one soul a good deal about it, haven’t you?”

===Chinese===
「哼，这才像话！」莫莉忿忿地说，「我是发过誓不对任何人讲的。」

吉米噗嗤一声笑了。

「没什么，」他答道，回应她探询的目光。

「您分明是在笑什么。」

「唉，」吉米带着歉意说，「没什么——实在没什么——我是想说，您方才这不已经把这件事对一个『人』讲了一大堆吗？」

===Original===
Molly turned pink. Then she smiled.

“I don’t know how I came to do it,” she declared. “It rushed out of its own accord. I suppose it is because I know I can trust you.”

Jimmy flushed with pleasure. He turned to her and half halted, but she continued to walk on.

“You can,” he said; “but how do you know you can?”

“Why,” she said—she stopped for a moment, and then went on hurriedly, with a touch of embarrassment—“why, how absurd! Of course I know. Can’t you read faces? I can. Look,” she said, pointing, “now you can see the castle. How do you like it?”

===Chinese===
莫莉涨红了脸。跟着又笑了。

「我也不明白自己怎么会说出去的，」她声明，「话是自己冲出来的。想来是因为我知道，您这个人信得过。」

吉米喜得满脸放光。他转身朝她，脚下也停了一半，她却只顾往前走。

「我是信得过，」他说，「可您怎么知道我信得过呢？」

「这个嘛，」她说——她停了一停，接着又慌慌张张地往下讲，带着一丝窘——「哎呀，多么荒唐！我当然知道。您看不出人的脸色吗？我看得出。瞧，」她抬手一指，「这下能望见城堡了。您觉得它怎么样？」

===Original===
They had reached a point where the fields sloped sharply downward. A few hundred yards away, backed by woods, stood the grey mass of stone which proved such a killjoy of old to the Welsh sportsman during the peasant season. Even now it had a certain air of defiance. The setting sun lit up the waters of the lake. No figures were to be seen moving in the grounds. The place resembled a palace of sleep.

“Well!” said Molly.

“It’s wonderful!”

“Isn’t it? I’m so glad it strikes you like that. I always feel as if I had invented everything round here. It hurts me if people don’t appreciate it.”

===Chinese===
他们走到了一处田亩陡然下斜的坡头。几百码开外，灰石的一大团庞然巨物倚着树林矗立——古时候在庄稼人闹事的年月里，这座堡子没少给威尔士来的「打猎先生」们大扫其兴。直到今天，它周身还带着一股挑衅的神气。落日照亮了湖水。园林里不见一个人影走动。这地方宛如一座睡眠的宫殿。

「怎么样！」莫莉说。

「美极了！」

「可不是？您这么看，我高兴极了。我一向觉得，这周围的一切都仿佛是我发明的。人家不赏识，我心里就疼。」

===Original===
They went down the hill.

“By the way,” said Jimmy, “are you acting in these theatricals they are getting up?”

“Yes. Are you the other man they were going to get? That’s why Lord Dreever went up to London, to see if he couldn’t find somebody. The man who was going to play one of the parts had to go back to London on business.”

“Poor brute!” said Jimmy. It seemed to him at that moment that there was only one place in the world where a man might be even reasonably happy. “What sort of part is it? Lord Dreever said I should be wanted to act. What do I do?”

“If you’re Lord Herbert, which is the part they wanted a man for, you talk to me most of the time.”

===Chinese===
他们下坡而去。

「对了，」吉米说，「他们正张罗的这出业余演剧，您上台吗？」

「上。您莫不就是他们打算另找的那一位？德利弗勋爵就是为了这个上伦敦去的，看看能不能物色个把人来。原先派定演其中一个角色的那位先生，为着事务回伦敦去了。」

「那位老兄好不命苦！」吉米说。那一刻他觉得，天底下能让一个人过得还算快活的地方，只有一处。「是什么样的角色？德利弗勋爵说用得着我上台。我都干些什么？」

「要是派您演赫伯特勋爵（Lord Herbert）——他们缺男演员的就是这个角色——那您大部分工夫都在同我讲话。」

===Original===
Jimmy decided that the piece had been well cast.

The dressing gong sounded just as they entered the hall. From a door on the left there emerged two men—a big one and a little one—in friendly conversation. The big man’s back struck Jimmy as familiar.

“Oh, father!” Molly called. And Jimmy knew where he had seen the back before.

“Sir Thomas,” said Molly, “this is Mr. Pitt.”

===Chinese===
吉米断定，这出戏的角色分派得再公道不过。

两人走进门厅，更衣的锣声恰好敲响。左边一扇门里出来两个月人——一高一矮——正谈得亲热。那高个子的背影，吉米瞧着眼熟。

「哦，爸爸！」莫莉喊了一声。吉米也随即记起从前在哪里见过这个背影。

「托马斯爵士，」莫莉说，「这位是皮特先生。」

===Original===
The little man gave Jimmy a rapid glance—possibly with the object of detecting his more immediately obvious criminal points; then, as if satisfied as to his honesty, became genial.

“I am very glad to meet you, Mr. Pitt—very glad,” he said. “We have been expecting you for some time.”

Jimmy explained that he had lost his way.

“Exactly. It was ridiculous that you should be compelled to walk—perfectly ridiculous. It was gross carelessness of my nephew not to let us know that you were coming. My wife told him so in the car.”

“I bet she did,” said Jimmy to himself. “Really,” he said aloud, by way of lending a helping hand to a friend in trouble, “I preferred to walk. I have not been on a country road since I landed in England.” He turned to the big man and held out his hand. “I don’t suppose you remember me, Mr. McEachern. We met in New York.”

===Chinese===
小个子飞快地瞟了吉米一眼——许是想查一查他身上那些一望而知的罪犯特征；随即，仿佛验明他的清白无误，变得和蔼起来。

「幸会，皮特先生，幸会，」他说，「我们等候您多时了。」

吉米解释说，他迷了路。

「正是。居然弄得您不得不步行，岂有此理——岂有此理透了。舍侄不通知我们您要来，粗心大意到了极点。内子已经在汽车里把他数落过了。」

「这还用说。」吉米肚里暗道。「说真的，」他朗声开口，算是拉危难中的朋友一把，「走路是我自己乐意的。打上了英国的地界，这还是我头一回走乡间的路。」他转向那位大个子，伸出手去。「想必您记不得我了，麦克伊彻恩先生。我们在纽约见过。」

===Original===
“You remember the night Mr. Pitt scared away our burglar, father?” said Molly.

Mr. McEachern was momentarily silent. On his native asphalt there are few situations capable of throwing the New York policeman off his balance. In that favoured clime savoir faire is represented by a shrewd blow of the fist, and a masterful stroke with the truncheon amounts to a satisfactory repartee. Thus shall you never take a policeman of Manhattan without his answer. In other surroundings Mr. McEachern would have known how to deal with the young man whom with such good reason he believed to be an expert criminal. But another plan of action was needed here. First and foremost of all the hints on etiquette which he had imbibed since he entered this more reposeful life came this maxim, “Never make a scene.” Scenes, he had gathered, were of all things what polite society most resolutely abhorred. The natural man in him must be bound in chains. The sturdy blow must give way to the honeyed word. A cold “Really!” was the most vigorous retort that the best circles would countenance.

It had cost Mr. McEachern some pains to learn this lesson, but he had done it.

He shook hands and gruffly acknowledged the acquaintanceship.

===Chinese===
「爸爸，您还记得皮特先生替咱们吓跑窃贼的那个晚上吗？」莫莉说。

麦克伊彻恩先生一时语塞。在他家乡的柏油马路上，能把纽约警察搅得心神失据的场面寥寥无几。在那片得天独厚的水土里，所谓机敏干练，就体现在一记干净利落的拳头上；警棍抡得漂亮，便抵得上一句叫人心满意足的妙答。因此，曼哈顿的警察，你休想叫他被问得哑口无言。换个环境，对付这个青年——他有十足的把握认定此人是个行窃的老手——麦克伊彻恩先生原是自有办法的。可这会儿得换一套章程。自从过起这较为安闲的日子以来，他汲取的种种礼仪要诀里，头一条便是这句箴言：『切莫当众闹出事来。』据他领会，体面社会顶顶深恶痛绝的东西，莫过于「闹场面」。他身上那个自然人，必须捆进锁链。结实的拳头，必须让位于抹了蜜的言语。一声冷冰冰的「真的吗！」，便是上流圈子肯于首肯的最猛烈的还击。

这门功课，麦克伊彻恩先生学得着实费了些力气，但他终究学到了手。

他握了握手，粗声粗气地认下了这层旧交。

===Original===
“Really, really!” chirped Sir Thomas amiably. “So you find yourself among old friends, Mr. Pitt.”

“Old friends,” echoed Jimmy, painfully conscious of the ex-policeman’s eyes, which were boring holes in him.

“Excellent, excellent! Let me take you to your room. It is just opposite my own. This way.”

They parted from Mr. McEachern on the first landing, but Jimmy could still feel those eyes. The policeman’s stare had been of the sort which turns corners, goes upstairs, and pierces walls.

===Chinese===
「真的吗，真的吗！」托马斯爵士和和气气地尖着嗓子凑趣。「这么说，皮特先生，您这是落到故人堆里来了。」

「故人，」吉米应声道，痛切地意识到那双前警察的眼睛，正一寸一寸往他身上钻洞。

「好极了，好极了！我领您回房去。就在我房间的正对面。这边走。」

他们在第一道楼梯口便同麦克伊彻恩先生分了手，可吉米仍旧感觉得到那双眼睛。警察的盯视是会拐弯、会上楼、能穿墙的一种。

## XIII Spike’s Views / 斯派克的高见

===Original===
Nevertheless, it was in a very exalted frame of mind that he dressed for dinner. It seemed to him that he had awakened from a sort of stupor. Life, so grey yesterday, now appeared full of colour and possibilities. Most men who, either from choice or necessity, have knocked about the world for any length of time are more or less fatalists. Jimmy was an optimistic fatalist. He had always looked on fate not as a blind dispenser at random of gifts good and bad, but rather as a benevolent being with a pleasing bias in his favour. He had almost a Napoleonic faith in his star. At various periods of his life—notably at the time when, as he had told Lord Dreever, he had breakfasted on birdseed—he had been in uncommonly tight corners, but his luck had always extricated him. It struck him that it would be an unthinkable piece of bad sportsmanship on Fate’s part to see him through so much and then to abandon him just as he had arrived in sight of what was by far the biggest thing of his life. Of course, his view of what constituted the biggest thing in life had changed with the years. Every ridge of the Hill of Supreme Moments in turn had been mistaken by him for the summit; but this last, he felt instinctively, was genuine. For good or bad, Molly was woven into the texture of his life. In the stormy period of the early twenties he had thought the same of other girls, who were now mere memories as dim as those of figures in a half-forgotten play. In their case his convalescence had been temporarily painful, but brief. Force of will and an active life had worked the cure. He had merely braced himself up and firmly ejected them from his mind. A week or two of aching emptiness, and his heart had been once more in readiness—all nicely swept and done up—for the next lodger.

But in the case of Molly it was different. He had passed the age of instantaneous susceptibility. Like a landlord who had been cheated by previous tenants, he had become wary. He mistrusted his powers of recuperation in case of disaster. The will in these matters, just like the mundane “bouncer,” gets past his work. For some years now Jimmy had had a feeling that the next arrival would come to stay, and he had adopted, in consequence, a gently defensive attitude towards the other sex. Molly had broken through this, and he saw that his estimate of his willpower had been just. Methods which had proved excellent in the past were useless now. There was no trace here of that dimly consoling feeling of earlier years that there were other girls in the world. He did not try to deceive himself. He knew that he had passed the age when a man can fall in love with any one of a number of types.

This was the finish, one way or the other. There was no second throw. She had him. However it might end, he belonged to her.

There are few moments in a man’s day when his brain is more contemplative than during that brief space when he is lathering his face preparatory to shaving. Flying the brush, Jimmy reviewed the situation. He was perhaps a little too optimistic. Not unnaturally he was inclined to look upon his luck as a sort of special train which would convey him without effort to Paradise. Fate had behaved so exceedingly handsomely up till now. By a series of the most workmanlike miracles it had brought him to the point of being Molly’s fellow guest at a country house. This, as Reason coldly pointed out a few moments later, was merely the beginning; but to Jimmy, thoughtfully lathering, it seemed the end. It was only when he had finished shaving and was arranging his tie that he began to perceive that there were obstacles in his way—and sufficiently big obstacles at that.

===Chinese===
话虽如此，他去换晚礼服的时候，心头却兴高采烈。他觉得自己仿佛从一场昏沉中醒了过来。昨天还灰蒙蒙的人生，此刻忽然五彩斑斓、处处生机。世上大多数人，无论出于自愿还是无奈，只要在世间闯荡得久了，多少都成了宿命论者。吉米是个乐观的宿命论者。他从不把命运看作闭着眼睛胡乱派发善恶祸福的散财官，倒把它看作一位慈眉善目的老爷，偏偏偏心，处处向着他。他对自己的「命星」几乎怀着拿破仑式的信心。他生平好几个阶段——尤其是他对德利弗勋爵讲起过的那段靠鸟食当早饭的日子——都曾困窘到无以复加，可运气总能把他捞出来。他心想，命运既然陪他闯过了这么多关口，偏偏在他望见平生头等大事的当口撒手不管，那未免太不讲体育道德，简直匪夷所思。当然，什么算得上人生头等大事，这个标准也随着年岁变过几茬。「至高时刻之山」上的每一道山梁，他都先后误认作过顶峰；可这最后一道，他本能地觉得，是真的。无论吉凶祸福，莫莉已经织进了他生命的经纬。二十出头那段风风雨雨的岁月里，他对别的姑娘也这么想过；那些姑娘如今只剩些淡淡的记忆，模糊得像一出半已忘却的戏里的人影。那几回，他的病愈虽然一时痛苦，倒也短暂。意志加上活跃的生活，药到病除。他只需打起精神，把她们一个个坚决请出心头。难受上一两个星期的空落，他的心便又收拾得干干净净、焕然一新，恭候下一位房客了。

可莫莉这一回不同。他已经过了那种谁来了都能立刻着道的年纪。就像一个被先前房客坑过的房东，他起了戒心。他信不过自己万一再栽跟头时的复原力。意志这东西在这类事情上，也同尘世里把门的「保安」一样，有失职的时候，放人溜了进去。这几年，吉米一直有种预感：下一位来的，是要长住的。于是他对女性便摆出了一副和风细雨的守势。莫莉冲破了这道防线，他这才明白，自己当年对意志力的估计分毫不差。从前屡试不爽的法子，如今全不管用了。这里没有半点早年那种隐约的自我安慰——世上还有别的姑娘。他不骗自己。他知道，一个人见着什么类型都来得个爱的年纪，自己已经过了。

这一次，不是成，就是败，再没有第二把骰子。她赢了他。无论结局如何，他这个人已经是她的了。

一天当中，人最耽于沉思的时刻，莫过于刮脸之前往脸上抹肥皂沫的那一小段光阴。剃刷翻飞之间，吉米把局势检阅了一遍。他也许乐观得稍稍过了头。无怪乎他倾向于把自己的运气看作一列专车，会毫不费力地把他直送到天堂。命运至今表现得着实慷慨漂亮。它靠一连串手法干净利落的奇迹，把他送到了与莫莉同为一座乡间宅第座上客的地步。正如理智稍后冷冷指出的，这不过是开头；可对着肥皂沫出神的吉米，却当它就是终点。直到刮完了脸、整理领带的时候，他才开始发觉，前路上还有障碍——而且是足够大的障碍。

===Original===
In the first place, Molly did not love him. And, he was bound to admit, there was no earthly reason why she ever should. A man in love is seldom vain about his personal attractions. Also, her father firmly believed him to be a master burglar.

“Otherwise,” said Jimmy, scowling at his reflection in the glass, “everything’s splendid.”

He brushed his hair sadly.

There was a furtive rap at the door.

“Halloa?” said Jimmy. “Yes?”

===Chinese===
首先，莫莉不爱他。而且他不得不承认，她也绝没有任何理由该爱上他。恋爱中的男人，很少为自己的仪表自负。再者，她父亲死死认定他是个惯窃大王。

「除此之外，」吉米冲着镜子里自己的影子皱皱眉，「样样都好极了。」

他闷闷地刷了刷头发。

门上响起一声鬼鬼祟祟的轻叩。

「喂？」吉米说。「哪位？」

===Original===
The door opened slowly. A grin, surmounted by a mop of red hair, appeared round the edge of it.

“Halloa, Spike! Come in. What’s the matter?”

The rest of Mr. Mullins entered the room.

“Gee, boss, I wasn’t sure dis was your room. Say, who do you t’ink I nearly bumped me coco against out in de corridor downstairs? Why, old man McEachern, de cop. Dat’s right!”

===Chinese===
门慢慢地开了。一副咧开的笑，顶着一蓬红发，从门缝边上探了出来。

「哟，斯派克！进来。怎么了？」

马林斯先生的其余部分走进了房间。

「哎呀，老大，我原先拿不准这是不是您的房间。喂，您猜我在楼下走廊里差点儿把脑袋瓜撞上谁？嘿，麦克伊彻恩老头儿，那个当警察的。一点不假！」

===Original===
“Yes?”

“Sure. Say, what’s he doin’ on dis beat? I pretty near went down and out when I seen him. Dat’s right. Me breath ain’t got back home yet.”

“Did he recognise you?”

“Did he! He starts like an actor on top de stoige when he sees he’s up against de plot to ruin him, an’ he gives me de fierce eye.”

“Well?”

“I was wondering was I on Third Avenue, or was I standing on me coco, or what was I doin’ anyhow. Den I slips off and chases meself up here. Say, boss, what’s de game? What’s old man McEachern doin’ stunts dis side for?”

===Chinese===
「是吗？」

「可不。喂，他怎么巡上这条道儿来了？我见着他，差点儿当场被人一拳撂倒。一点不假。我那口气这会儿还没喘匀呢。」

「他认出你了吗？」

「怎么没认出！他猛地一震，活像戏台上的角儿看出这台戏的剧情存心要毁掉自己，跟着就狠狠瞪了我一眼。」

「后来呢？」

「我都闹不清自己是站在第三大道（Third Avenue）上，还是脑袋瓜倒栽在地上，反正不知道自己在干什么。跟着我脚底抹油，一路把自己撵上了楼。喂，老大，这是唱的哪一出？麦克伊彻恩老头儿跑到这一头耍把戏，图什么呀？」

===Original===
“It’s all right, Spike. Keep calm. I can explain. He has retired—like me. He’s one of the handsome guests here.”

“On your way, boss! What’s dat?”

“He left the Force just after that merry meeting of ours when you frolicked with the bulldog. He came over here and butted into society. So here we are again, all gathered together under the same roof, like a jolly little family party.”

Spike’s open mouth bore witness to his amazement.

===Chinese===
「没什么，斯派克。沉住气。我能解释。他退休了——跟我一样。他如今也是这儿的贵客之一，体面着呢。」

「您可拉倒吧，老大！您说啥？」

「就在咱们那回快活的聚会之后——你跟那条斗牛犬戏耍的那一回——他随即就离开了警界。漂到这边来，一头扎进了社交界。于是咱们又凑到一块儿了，全在同一座屋顶底下，像一场和和美美的家庭小聚会。」

斯派克张大的嘴巴，足见他的惊愕。

===Original===
“Den—” he stammered.

“Yes?”

“Den what’s he goin’ to do?”

“I couldn’t say. I’m expecting to hear shortly. But we needn’t worry ourselves. The next move’s with him. If he wants to comment on the situation he won’t be backward. He’ll come and do it.”

“Sure. It’s up to him,” agreed Spike.

===Chinese===
「那——」他结结巴巴地说。

「怎么？」

「那他打算怎么办？」

「我说不好。估计很快就有消息。不过咱们犯不着操心。下一步棋在他手里。他要是想对时局发表评论，绝不会客气，会亲自登门来讲的。」

「那可不。全瞧他的了。」斯派克附和道。

===Original===
“I’m quite comfortable. Speaking for myself, I’m having a good time. How are you getting along downstairs?”

“De limit, boss. Honest, it’s to de velvet. Dere’s old gazebo, de butler, Saunders his name is, dat’s de best ever at handing out long woids. I sits and listens. Dey calls me Mr. Mullins down dere,” said Spike with pride.

“Good. I’m glad you’re all right. There’s no reason why we shouldn’t have an excellent time here. I don’t think that Mr. McEachern will try to have us turned out, after he’s heard one or two little things I have to say to him—just a few reminiscences of the past which may interest him. I have the greatest affection for Mr. McEachern—I wish it was mutual—but nothing he can say is going to make me stir from here.”

“Not on your life,” agreed Spike. “Say, boss, he must have got a lot of plunks to be able to butt in here. And I know how he got dem, too. Dat’s right. I comes from little old New York meself.”

“Hush, Spike; this is scandal!”

===Chinese===
「我自在得很。拿我自己来说，日子过得挺美。你在楼下混得怎么样？」

「绝了，老大。说真格的，那叫一个滋润。底下有个摆谱的老家伙，是管家，名叫桑德斯，甩起长词儿来天下第一。我就坐在那儿听。底下那帮人都管我叫马林斯先生。」斯派克不无得意地说。

「好。你没出事我就放心。咱们在这儿没有道理不痛痛快快玩一场。等他听我跟他说的一两件小事——就是几段他听了兴许会感兴趣的旧日回忆——我想麦克伊彻恩先生不会想把咱们撵走的。我对麦克伊彻恩先生怀着最深的情谊——但愿这份情谊是双向的——可无论他说什么，都休想叫我挪窝。」

「打死也不挪。」斯派克附和道。「喂，老大，他准是捞了一大把大洋，才挤得进这种地方。他这钱怎么来的，我也门儿清。一点不假。我自己就是从小小的老纽约来的。」

「嘘，斯派克；这可是丑闻哪！」

===Original===
“Sure!” said the Bowery boy, doggedly, safely started now on his favourite subject. “I knows, and youse knows, boss. Gee! I wish I’d bin a cop. But I wasn’t tall enough. Dey’s de fellers wit de big bankrolls! Look at dis old McEachern. Money to boin a wet dog wit he’s got, and never a bit of woik for it from de start to de finish. An’ look at me, boss.”

“I do, Spike; I do.”

“Look at me. Getting busy all de year round, woiking to beat de band—”

“In prisons oft,” said Jimmy.

===Chinese===
「可不就是这么回事！」鲍里街小子执拗地说，心爱的题目一开了头，就稳稳当当刹不住车了。「我心里有数，您心里也有数，老大。哎呀！我真悔当初没去当警察。就怨我个头不够高。那号人才是揣着大卷子票子的主儿！瞧瞧麦克伊彻恩老头儿。他的钱多得能拿去煮一条落水狗，可从头到尾没为一个子儿干过半点活儿。再瞧瞧我，老大。」

「我正瞧着呢，斯派克；正瞧着呢。」

「瞧瞧我。一年到头忙得脚打后脑勺，活儿干得赛过铜管乐队——」

「多下监牢，」吉米说。

===Original===
“Sure t’ing. And chased all roun’ de town. And den what? Why, to de bad at de end of it all. Say, it’s enough to make a feller—”

“Turn honest!” said Jimmy. “That’s it, Spike—reform. You’ll be glad some day.”

Spike seemed to be doubtful. He was silent for a moment; then, as if following up a train of thought, he said:

“Boss, dis is a fine big house.”

“I’ve seen worse.”

===Chinese===
「那可不。还满城让人追着跑。到头来又怎么样？嘿，末了儿反倒落得净亏。喂，这真够让一个家伙——」

「改行学好！」吉米说。「这就对了，斯派克——重新做人。有朝一日你会庆幸的。」

斯派克似乎将信将疑。他沉默了片刻，跟着，像顺着一条思路摸下去似的，说：

「老大，这房子又大又气派。」

「更糟的我也见过。」

===Original===
“Say, couldn’t we—?”

“Spike!” said Jimmy warningly.

“Well, couldn’t we?” said Spike doggedly. “It ain’t often youse butts into a dead easy proposition like dis one. We shouldn’t have to do a t’ing excep’ git busy. De stuff’s just lying about, boss.”

“I shouldn’t wonder.”

“Aw, it’s a waste to leave it.”

===Chinese===
「喂，咱们能不能——？」

「斯派克！」吉米警告道。

「哎，到底能不能嘛？」斯派克执拗地说。「您撞上这么一桩轻省到家的买卖，可不是常有的事。咱们啥都不用干，只消动起手来。东西就四下里明摆着搁着呢，老大。」

「多半错不了。」

「哎呀，白白撂着多可惜。」

===Original===
“Spike,” said Jimmy. “I warned you of this. I begged you to be on your guard, to fight against your professional instincts. Be a man! Crush them. Try to occupy your mind. Collect butterflies.”

Spike shuffled in gloomy silence.

“ ’Member dose jools you swiped from de Duchess?” he said, musingly.

“The dear Duchess!” murmured Jimmy. “Ah, me!”

===Chinese===
「斯派克，」吉米说，「我早警告过你会有这一天。我求过你提高警惕，跟你那职业本能作斗争。拿出点男子汉气概来！把它们碾下去。想法儿让脑子有点寄托。收收蝴蝶标本吧。」

斯派克阴沉着脸，一声不响地蹭了蹭脚。

「还记得您从公爵夫人那儿顺来的那批珠宝吗？」他若有所思地说。

「亲爱的公爵夫人哪！」吉米喃喃道。「唉！」

===Original===
“And de bank you busted?”

“Those were happy days, Spike.”

“Gee!” said the Bowery boy.

He paused. “Dat was to de good,” he said wistfully.

Jimmy arranged his tie at the mirror.

===Chinese===
「还有您干掉的那家银行呢？」

「那些日子真快活，斯派克。」

「哎呀！」鲍里街小子说。

他顿了顿。「那才叫真格的好日子，」他怅然道。

吉米对着镜子理了理领带。

===Original===
“Dere’s a loidy here,” continued Spike, addressing the chest of drawers, “dat’s got a necklace of jools what’s worth a hundred t’ousand plunks. Honest, boss—a hundred t’ousand plunks. Saunders told me that—de old gazebo dat hands out de long woids. I says to him ‘Gee!’ and he says, ‘Surest t’ing you know.’ A hundred t’ousand plunks!”

“So I understand,” said Jimmy.

“Shall I rubber around and find out where is dey kept, boss?”

“Spike,” said Jimmy, “ask me no more. All this is in direct contravention of our treaty respecting keeping our fingers off the spoons. You pain me. Desist.”

“Sorry, boss. But dey’ll be willy-wonders, dem jools. A hundred t’ousand plunks! Dat’s going some, ain’t it? What’s dat dis side?”

===Chinese===
「这儿有位太太，」斯派克对着五斗橱继续说，「她有一条珠宝项链，值十万大洋。说真格的，老大——十万大洋。是桑德斯告诉我的——就是甩长词儿的那个老家伙。我跟他说『哎呀！』，他说，『这还能有假。』十万大洋哪！」

「听说如此。」吉米说。

「要不要我去四处瞅瞅，打听到东西搁在哪儿，老大？」

「斯派克，」吉米说，「别再问我了。这一切公然违反了咱们订下的『手指不碰汤匙』条约。你叫我很痛心。就此打住。」

「对不住，老大。可那串珠宝准是宝贝得不得了的玩意儿。十万大洋！这笔数目可真够瞧的，啊？折成这边儿的钱是多少？」

===Original===
“Twenty thousand pounds.”

“Gee! Can I help you wit de duds, boss?”

“No, thanks, Spike. I’m through now. You might just give me a brush down, though. No, not that. That’s a hairbrush. Try the big black one.”

“Dis is a boid of a dude suit,” observed Spike, pausing in his labours.

===Chinese===
「两万英镑。」

「哎呀！要我帮您拾掇衣裳吗，老大？」

「不用了，谢谢，斯派克。我穿戴好了。不过你可以替我把身上刷一刷。不，不是那把。那是梳头刷。拿那把黑色的大毛刷。」

「这身公子哥儿的行头真是俊透了，」斯派克干着活，停下来品评道。

===Original===
“Glad you like it, Spike. Rather chic, I think.”

“It’s de limit. Excuse me, how much did it set you back, boss?”

“Something like twelve guineas, I believe. I could look up the bill and let you know.”

“What’s dat—guineas? Is that more dan a pound?”

“A shilling more. Why these higher mathematics?”

===Chinese===
「你喜欢就好，斯派克。我自己觉得相当别致。」

「真是绝了。恕我多嘴，这身花了您多少，老大？」

「记得是十二几尼（guinea）上下。我可以查查账单再告诉你。」

「几尼是啥——比一镑还多吗？」

「多一先令。怎么，你要用上高等数学了？」

===Original===
Spike resumed his brushing.

“What a lot of dude suits youse could get,” he observed meditatively, “if you had dem jools.” He became suddenly animated. He waved the clothes brush. “Oh, you boss!” he cried. “What’s eatin’ you? Aw, it’s a shame not to. Come along, you boss. Say, what’s doin’? Why ain’t you sittin’ in at de game? Oh, you boss!”

Whatever reply Jimmy might have made to this impassioned appeal was checked by a sudden bang on the door. Almost immediately the handle turned.

“Gee!” cried Spike. “It’s de cop.”

Jimmy smiled pleasantly.

“Come in, Mr. McEachern,” he said, “come in. Journeys end in lovers meeting. You know my friend Mr. Mullins, I think? Shut the door and sit down, and let’s talk of many things.”

===Chinese===
斯派克接着刷他的衣裳。

「要是您有了那串珠宝，」他若有所思地说，「能置办多少身这样的漂亮行头啊。」他忽然来了劲头，挥着那把衣刷嚷起来：「我的老大哎！您这是犯的哪门子轴？放着不干简直造孽。来嘛，我的老大哎。喂，这是唱的哪一出？您干嘛不坐上桌入伙呀？我的老大哎！」

吉米对这番慷慨激昂的呼吁本来要作何回答，已被门上突然一声巨响截住了。紧接着，门把几乎同时转动了。

「哎呀！」斯派克嚷道。「警察来啦。」

吉米愉快地微微一笑。

「请进，麦克伊彻恩先生，」他说，「请进。『行尽天涯，情人相会』嘛（语出《第十二夜》）。这位马林斯先生，我的朋友，您想必认识？关上门，请坐，咱们来聊聊许许多多的事情。」

## XIV Check, and a Countermove / 将军与还手

===Original===
Mr. McEachern stood in the doorway, breathing heavily. As the result of a long connection with evildoers, the ex-policeman was somewhat prone to harbour suspicions of those round about him, and at the present moment his mind was aflame. Indeed, a more trusting man might have been excused for feeling a little doubtful as to the intentions of Jimmy and Spike. When McEachern had heard that Lord Dreever had brought home a casual London acquaintance, he had suspected as a possible drawback to the visit the existence of hidden motives on the part of the unknown. Lord Dreever, he had felt, was precisely the sort of youth to whom the professional bunco-steerer would attach himself with shouts of joy. Never, he had assured himself, had there been a softer proposition than his lordship since bunco-steering became a profession.

When he found that the strange visitor was Jimmy Pitt, his suspicions had increased a thousandfold.

And when, going to his room to get ready for dinner, he had nearly run into Spike Mullins in the corridor, his frame of mind had been that of a man to whom a sudden ray of light reveals the fact that he is on the brink of a black precipice. Jimmy and Spike had burgled his house together in New York; and here they were, together again, at Dreever Castle. To say that the thing struck McEachern as sinister is to put the matter badly. There was once a gentleman who remarked that he smelt a rat and saw it floating in the air. Ex-Constable McEachern smelt a regiment of rats, and the air seemed to him positively congested with them.

===Chinese===
麦克伊彻恩先生站在门口，呼呼地喘着粗气。这位退休警察由于长年同不法之徒打交道的缘故，对身边的人不免惯于疑神疑鬼，此刻他满腹疑云更是烧成了一片。说实在的，换一个轻信些的人，对吉米和斯派克的居心略微起一点疑，也情有可原。当初听说德利弗勋爵从伦敦随手领回来一个萍水之交，麦克伊彻恩便疑心这位不明来历的人物别有用心，恐怕会给这趟做客平添变数。照他的判断，德利弗勋爵正是那种职业老千（bunco-steerer）见了会欢天喜地扑上来攀住的年轻人。他早已给自己下了断语：自从行骗成了一门行当，世上还不曾出过比勋爵大人更肥的冤大头。

等他发现这位陌生来客竟是吉米·皮特，他的疑心顿时翻了千倍。

而等到他回房换晚礼服、在走廊里险些同斯派克·马林斯撞个满怀的时候，他当时的心境，就像一个被一线亮光突然照见自己正站在漆黑悬崖边上的人。吉米和斯派克当年在纽约合伙光顾过他的宅子；如今这两个人又凑到了一处——还是在德里弗城堡。说这件事让麦克伊彻恩觉得可疑，那是把话说轻了。从前有位绅士，说自己嗅出了老鼠的气味，还看见它就飘在半空。退休警员麦克伊彻恩嗅出的是整整一团队老鼠，而且在他看来，空气简直叫这些老鼠堵得水泄不通。

===Original===
His first impulse had been to rush to Jimmy's room there and then; but he had learned society's lessons well. Though the heavens might fall, he must not be late for dinner, so he went and dressed, and an obstinate tie put the finishing touches to his wrath.

Jimmy regarded him coolly, without moving from the chair in which he had seated himself. Spike, on the other hand, seemed embarrassed; he stood first on one leg and then on the other, as if he were testing the respective merits of each and would make a definite choice later on.

"You scoundrels!" growled McEachern.

===Chinese===
他的头一个冲动，是当时当地就冲到吉米房里去；可是社交界的功课他学得很到家。天塌下来，也不能误了开饭的钟点，于是他去更了衣，一条犟头犟脑不肯就范的领带，给他这腔怒火画上了最后一笔。

吉米坐在自己落座的椅子上，纹丝不动，冷冷地打量他。斯派克那一头却显出局促来；他先是单腿站立，又换另一条腿，仿佛在逐一品评两条腿的优劣短长，打算日后再作定夺。

「你们这两个恶棍！」麦克伊彻恩咆哮道。

===Original===
Spike, who had been standing for a few moments on his right leg, and seemed at last to have come to a decision, hastily changed to the left, and grinned feebly.

"Say, youse won't want me any more, boss?" he whispered.

"No; you can go, Spike."

"You stay where you are, you redheaded devil!" said McEachern tartly.

"Run along, Spike," said Jimmy.

The Bowery boy looked doubtfully at the huge form of the ex-policeman, which blocked access to the door.

===Chinese===
斯派克原本已在右腿上立了好一阵，眼看终于拿定了主意，慌忙又换到左腿，咧开嘴有气无力地笑了笑。

「喂，老大，您这儿用不着我了吧？」他悄声说。

「不用了，你可以走了，斯派克。」

「你给我待在原地，你这红毛鬼！」麦克伊彻恩厉声说。

「去吧，斯派克。」吉米说。

那鲍里街小子迟疑地望了望退休警察那一大墩子堵在门口的身形。

===Original===
"Would you mind letting my man pass?" said Jimmy.

"You stay—" began McEachern.

Jimmy got up and walked round him to the door, which he opened. Spike shot out like a rabbit released from a trap. He was not lacking in courage, but he disliked embarrassing interviews, and it struck him that Jimmy was the man to handle a situation of this kind. He felt that he himself would only be in the way.

"Now we can talk comfortably," said Jimmy, going back to his chair.

McEachern's deep-set eyes gleamed and his forehead grew red, but he mastered his feelings.

===Chinese===
「劳驾让我的仆人过去一下？」吉米说。

「你给我待——」麦克伊彻恩刚要开口。

吉米站起身，绕过他走到门口，把门拉开。斯派克像一只出套的兔子，嗖地蹿了出去。他并非缺少胆量，只是不喜欢尴尬的当面锣对面鼓，而且他觉得，这种局面正该由吉米这样的人来收拾。他琢磨着，自己留在这儿只会碍手碍脚。

「这会儿咱们可以踏踏实实地谈了。」吉米说着，回到自己椅子上。

麦克伊彻恩深陷的眼窝里凶光一闪，额头涨红了，但他把火性压了下去。

===Original===
"And now—" he said.

He stopped.

"Yes?" asked Jimmy.

"What are you doing here?"

"Nothing at the moment."

"You know what I mean. Why are you here—you and that redheaded devil, Spike Mullins?"

===Chinese===
「那么，现在——」他说。

他停住了。

「怎么？」吉米问。

「你在这儿干什么？」

「眼下什么都不干。」

「你明白我的意思。你为什么会在这儿——你，还有那个红毛鬼，斯派克·马林斯？」

===Original===
He jerked his head in the direction of the door.

"I am here because I was very kindly invited to come by Lord Dreever."

"I know you."

"You have that privilege. Seeing we only met once, it's very good of you to remember me."

"What's your game? What do you mean to do?"

===Chinese===
他朝房门的方向猛一甩头。

「我来这儿，是因为承蒙德利弗勋爵盛情相邀。」

「我认得你。」

「这是您的特权。鉴于咱们统共只见过一面，您居然还记得我，真是难为您了。」

「你打的什么算盘？你打算干什么？」

===Original===
"To do? Well, I shall potter about the garden, you know, and shoot a bit, perhaps, and look at the horses, and think of life, and feed the chickens—I suppose there are chickens somewhere about—and possibly go for an occasional row on the lake. Nothing more. Oh, yes, I believe they want me to act in some theatricals."

"You'll miss those theatricals. You'll leave here tomorrow."

"Tomorrow? But I've only just arrived, dear heart."

"I don't care about that. Out you go tomorrow. I'll give you till tomorrow."

"I congratulate you," said Jimmy. "One of the oldest houses in England."

===Chinese===
「打算干什么？这个嘛，我打算在花园里遛遛弯儿，您知道的，兴许再打打猎，看看马，想想人生，喂喂鸡——我想这左近总该养着几只鸡——偶尔还兴许上湖里划划船。如此而已。哦，对了，听说他们要我在一出业余演剧里客串。」

「那些业余演剧你看不上了。明天你就得离开这儿。」

「明天？可人家这才刚到呀，心肝儿。」

「我不管这个。明天你给我走人。我宽限你到明天。」

「恭喜您哪，」吉米说。「英格兰最古老的宅邸之一。」

===Original===
"What do you mean?"

"I gathered from what you said that you had bought the castle. Isn't that so? If it still belongs to Lord Dreever, don't you think you ought to consult him before revising his list of guests?"

McEachern looked at him steadily. His manner became quieter.

"Oh! you take that tone, do you?"

"I don't know what you mean by 'that tone.' What tone would you take if a comparative stranger ordered you to leave another man's house?"

McEachern's massive jaw protruded truculently in the manner which had scared good behaviour into brawling East Siders.

===Chinese===
「你这话什么意思？」

「听您那口气，我还当这座城堡已经归了您。不是吗？既然它还是德利弗勋爵的家业，您在修订他的宾客名单之前，是不是该先知会他一声？」

麦克伊彻恩定定地逼视着他。口气渐渐缓了下来。

「哦！你倒敢用这种口气，是吧？」

「我不懂您所谓的『这种口气』是什么口气。倘若一个萍水相逢的外人喝令你从别人家里滚出去，你会用什么口气？」

麦克伊彻恩粗大的下巴好勇斗狠地向前一挺，正是当年把纽约东区那帮寻衅斗殴的莽汉吓规矩了的那个架势。

===Original===
"I know your sort," he said. "I'll call your bluff. And you won't get till tomorrow, either—it'll be now."

" 'Why should we wait for the morrow? You are queen of my heart tonight,' " murmured Jimmy encouragingly.

"I'll expose you before them all. I'll tell them everything."

Jimmy shook his head.

"Too melodramatic," he said. "Sort of 'I call on Heaven to judge between this man and me' kind of thing. I shouldn't. What do you propose to tell, anyway?"

===Chinese===
「你们这号人我见得多了，」他说。「我要戳穿你的虚张声势。而且也宽限不到明天——就是现在。」

「『何必等到明朝？今夜你已是我心之王后。』」吉米在旁打气似的低声吟道。

「我要当着他们大家揭发你。我要把一切原原本本都说出来。」

吉米摇了摇头。

「太闹剧了，」他说。「有点儿『我请上天在此人与我之间主持公道』那一路的做派。要我就不这么干。再说，你究竟打算讲些什么呢？」

===Original===
"Will you deny that you were a crook in New York?"

"I will. I was nothing of the kind."

"What?"

"If you'll listen, I can explain."

"Explain!" The other's voice rose again. "You talk about explaining, you scum, when I caught you in my own parlour at three in the morning, you—"

===Chinese===
「你敢不敢否认你在纽约当过窃贼？」

「我否认。我压根儿就不是那号人。」

「什么？」

「您要是肯听，我可以解释。」

「解释！」对方的嗓门又蹿了上去。「凌晨三点，你在我自家的客厅里被我当场拿住，你倒跟我谈起解释来了，你这人渣，你——」

===Original===
The smile faded from Jimmy's face.

"Half a minute," he said.

It might be that the ideal course would be to let the storm expend itself and then to explain quietly the whole matter of Arthur Mifflin and the bet which had led to his one excursion into burglary. But he doubted it. Things—including his temper—had got beyond the stage of quiet explanations. McEachern would most certainly disbelieve his story. What would happen after that he did not know. A scene, probably—a melodramatic denunciation, at the worst, before the other guests; at the best, before Sir Thomas alone. He saw nothing but chaos beyond that. His story was thin to a degree, unless backed by witnesses, and his witnesses were three thousand miles away. Worse, he had not been alone in the policeman's parlour. A man who is burgling a house for a bet does not usually do it in the company of a professional burglar well known to the police.

No; quiet explanations must be postponed. They could do no good, and would probably lead to his spending the night and the next few nights at the local police station. And even if he were spared that fate, it was certain that he would have to leave the castle.

Leave the castle and Molly! He jumped up. The thought had stung him.

===Chinese===
吉米脸上的笑容敛去了。

「等半分钟。」他说。

也许，上策本是任这场风暴自行发完，过后再心平气和地讲清楚亚瑟·米夫林和那场打赌的来龙去脉——正是那场打赌，引得他平生唯一一回下海行窃。可他疑心此路不通。事情——连同他的火气——早已越过了可以心平气和讲道理的阶段。麦克伊彻恩必定不会信他的故事。信不信之后会怎么样，他不知道。多半是一场风波——往最坏里说，当着满堂宾客一顿闹剧式的声讨；往最好里说，也得单当着托马斯爵士。再往后，他看到的只有一团乱麻。他那套故事本身单薄得可以，除非有证人撑腰，而他的证人远在三千里之外。更糟的是，他潜进那警察的客厅时，并非只身一人。为打赌而光顾一幢宅子的人，通常不至于拉上一个警局挂了号、赫赫有名的职业窃贼同行。

不行；平心静气的解释只能往后放。那除了白费口舌以外毫无用处，多半还会害他今晚连同往后几晚都蹲在本地警察局里。就算逃得过那一场劫，他横竖也得离开这座城堡。

离开城堡，离开莫莉！他霍地站了起来。这个念头蜇了他一下。

===Original===
"One moment," he said.

McEachern stopped.

"Well?"

"You're going to tell them that?" asked Jimmy.

"I am."

"Are you also going to tell them why you didn't have me arrested that night?" he said.

===Chinese===
「慢着。」他说。

麦克伊彻恩停住了。

「怎么？」

「你打算跟他们讲那个？」吉米问。

「正是。」

「那你也打算跟他们讲讲，那天夜里你为什么没叫人抓我吗？」他说。

===Original===
McEachern started. Jimmy planted himself in front of him and glared up into his face. It would have been hard to say which of the two was the angrier. The policeman was flushed, and the veins stood out on his forehead. Jimmy was in a white heat of rage. He had turned very pale, and his muscles were quivering. Jimmy in this mood had once cleared a Los Angeles barroom with the leg of a chair in the space of two and a quarter minutes by the clock.

"Are you?" he demanded. "Are you?"

McEachern's hand, hanging at his side, lifted itself hesitatingly. The fingers brushed against Jimmy's shoulder. Jimmy's lips twitched.

"Yes," he said, "do it! Do it, and see what happens! By God! if you put a hand on me I'll finish you. Do you think you can bully me? Do you think I care for your size?"

===Chinese===
麦克伊彻恩猛地一震。吉米钉在他面前，仰头直瞪着他的脸。两个人里到底哪一个更怒，倒难说得很。警察满脸通红，额头青筋绽起。吉米则是怒到白热。他脸色已经变得煞白，浑身筋肉簌簌发颤。吉米处在这种火头上，曾有一回凭着一条椅子腿，掐着钟表，两分十五秒清空了洛杉矶的一间酒吧。

「你讲不讲？」他逼问。「讲不讲？」

麦克伊彻恩垂在身侧的手迟疑地抬了起来。指头擦着了吉米的肩头。吉米的嘴唇抽动了一下。

「好啊，」他说，「动手啊！动啊，看看是什么下场！老天在上！你敢碰我一指头，我就叫你完蛋。你以为你吓唬得住我？你以为我在乎你那身块头？」

===Original===
McEachern dropped his hand. For the first time in his life he had met a man who, instinct told him, was his match and more. He stepped back a pace.

Jimmy put his hands in his pockets and turned away. He walked to the mantelpiece and leaned his back against it.

"You haven't answered my question," he said. "Perhaps you can't!"

McEachern was wiping his forehead and breathing quickly.

"If you like," said Jimmy, "we'll go down to the drawing room now, and you shall tell your story and I'll tell mine. I wonder which they will think the more interesting? Damn you!" he went on, his anger rising once more, "what do you mean by it? You come into my room and bluster and talk big about exposing crooks. What do you call yourself, I wonder? Do you realise what you are? Why, poor Spike's an angel compared with you! He did take chances. He wasn't in a position of trust. You—"

===Chinese===
麦克伊彻恩把手放下了。他平生头一遭遇上这么一个人——本能告诉他，这人跟自己旗鼓相当，甚且过之。他退后了一步。

吉米双手插进口袋，转过身去。他踱到壁炉台前，背靠着它站定。

「你还没回答我的问题呢，」他说。「兴许你答不上来！」

麦克伊彻恩一边擦着额头，一边急促地喘气。

「你要是愿意，」吉米说，「咱们这会儿就下楼去客厅，你讲你的故事，我讲我的。我倒想瞧瞧，他们觉得哪一个更精彩？你这个人渣！」他接着说，火气又蹿了上来，「你这是唱的哪一出？你闯进我的房间，气势汹汹，大吹大擂要揭发窃贼。我倒想请教，您管自己叫个什么？你可明白你自己是个什么货色？跟您一比，可怜的斯派克简直是位天使！他好歹是豁出身家去冒的险。他可没占着受人之托、守信有责的位子。你——」

===Original===
He stopped.

"Hadn't you better get out of here, don't you think?" he said curtly.

Without a word McEachern walked to the door and went out.

Jimmy dropped into a chair with a deep breath. He took up his cigarette case, but before he could light a match the gong sounded from the distance.

He rose and laughed rather shakily. He felt limp. "As an effort to conciliating papa," he said, "I'm afraid that wasn't much of a success."

===Chinese===
他停住了。

「你是不是还是出去的好，你看呢？」他冷冷地说。

麦克伊彻恩一言不发，走到门口，出去了。

吉米长长吐了一口气，跌坐进一把椅子。他拿起烟盒，可还没来得及划火柴，开饭的锣声已经从远处传了过来。

他站起身，笑得有些发颤。他只觉得浑身发软。「作为安抚老丈人的一项努力，」他说，「恐怕算不得怎么成功。」

===Original===
It was not often that Mr. McEachern was visited by ideas—he ran rather to muscle than to brain—but he had one that evening during dinner. His interview with Jimmy had left him furious, but baffled. He knew that his hands were tied. Frontal attack was useless; to drive Jimmy from the castle would be out of the question. All that could be done was to watch him while he was there, for he had never been more convinced of anything in his life than that Jimmy had wormed his way into the house party with felonious intent. The appearance of Lady Julia at dinner wearing the famous rope of diamonds supplied an obvious motive. The necklace had an international reputation. Probably there was not a prominent thief in England or on the Continent who had not marked it down as a possible prey. It had already been tried for once. It was big game—just the sort of lure which would draw the type of criminal he imagined Jimmy to be.

From his seat at the farther end of the table he looked at the jewels as they gleamed on their wearer's neck. They were almost too ostentatious for what was, after all, an informal dinner. It was not a rope of diamonds—it was a collar. There was something Oriental and barbaric in the overwhelming display of jewellery. It was a prize for which a thief would risk much.

The conversation becoming general with the fish, was not of a kind to remove from his mind the impression made by the sight of the gems. It turned on burglary. Lord Dreever began it.

===Chinese===
麦克伊彻恩先生不常有主意登门——他这个人，长的是肌肉，不是脑子——可那天晚饭席上，他得了一个。同吉米那一场会面，让他又恼又憋闷。他明白自己缚手缚脚，施展不开。正面进攻没有用；要把吉米撵出城堡，更是无从谈起。能做的，只有趁他在此逗留的几日盯住他，因为吉米怀着盗窃的居心钻营进了这座乡间宅邸的宾客圈——这件事，他平生没有比对它更深信不疑的了。晚饭时，朱莉娅夫人戴着那串大名鼎鼎的钻石项链露面，恰好提供了一个现成的动机。那条项链名扬国际。放眼英格兰乃至欧洲大陆，只怕没有一位有头有脸的贼没把它记进自己的猎物册。它已经遭人觊觎过一回了。这是头等的大猎物——恰恰是把吉米这一路罪犯——照他所认定的吉米这一路——勾上钩来的那一款饵。

他从长桌另一头的座位上，望着那串珠宝在主人的颈间闪闪发亮。这毕竟只是一顿非正式的家宴，这一串未免张扬得过了头。那哪里是一串钻石项链——那简直是扎人的颈圈。这般铺天盖地的珠宝陈列里，自有一股东方的、蛮荒的气息。这是一份值得窃贼为之大冒其险的彩头。

上了鱼，谈话转为全桌公谈，谈的内容却没能把那串宝石在他心头留下的印象抹去。话题转到了行窃上头。领的头，正是德利弗勋爵。

===Original===
"Oh, I say," he said. "I forgot to tell you, Aunt Julia; No. 6 was burgled the other night."

No. 6A Eaton Square was the family's London house.

"Burgled!" said Sir Thomas.

"Well, broken into," said his lordship, gratified to find that he had got the ear of his entire audience. Even Lady Julia was silent and attentive. "Chap got through the scullery window about one o'clock in the morning."

"And what did you do?" inquired Sir Thomas.

"Oh, I—er—I was out at the time," said Lord Dreever. "But something frightened the feller," he went on hurriedly, "and he made a bolt for it without taking anything."

===Chinese===
「哦，我说，」他说，「忘了讲给您听，朱莉娅姑妈；前儿夜里，六号让人给偷了。」

伊顿广场六号A，是他们家在伦敦的宅子。

「让人偷了！」托马斯爵士说。

「呃，是让人撬进去了，」勋爵大人说，发现自己的话头儿抓住了满座听众，不禁大为受用。连朱莉娅夫人都屏息凝神，一声不响。「有个家伙，清晨一点钟光景，从洗涤间的窗户钻了进来。」

「那你当时做了什么？」托马斯爵士问。

「哦，我——呃——我那会儿正好不在家，」德利弗勋爵说。「不过有什么东西把那家伙给吓着了，」他慌忙接着说，「他什么也没拿，撒腿就溜了。」

===Original===
"Burglary," said a young man whom Jimmy subsequently discovered to be the drama-loving Charteris, leaning back and taking advantage of a pause, "is the hobby of the sportsman and the lifework of the avaricious."

He took a little pencil from his waistcoat pocket and made a rapid note on his cuff.

Everybody seemed to have something to say on the subject. One young lady gave it as her opinion that she would not like to find a burglar under her bed. Somebody also had heard of a fellow whose father had fired at the butler under the impression that he was a housebreaker, and had broken a valuable bust of Socrates. Lord Dreever had known a man at college whose brother wrote lyrics for musical comedy, and had done one about a burglar's best friend being his mother.

"Life," said Charteris, who had had time for reflection, "is a house which we all burgle. We enter it uninvited, take all that we can lay hands on, and go out again."

===Chinese===
「行窃这门营生，」一个年轻人说——吉米后来才晓得，他就是酷爱戏剧的查特里斯——身子往后一靠，趁着话头儿稍歇，接上一句，「是运动家的癖好，是贪财者的毕生事业。」

他从马甲口袋里摸出一支小铅笔，飞快地往袖口上记了一笔。

这个题目上，人人似乎都有话要说。一位小姐发表高见说，她可不乐意在床底下发现一个贼。又有人说认识一位先生，那位先生的父亲把管家错当了撬门贼，一枪轰过去，打碎了一尊值大价钱的苏格拉底胸像。德利弗勋爵在大学里有位相识，那位的兄弟替音乐喜剧写歌词，写过一首，唱的是贼最好的朋友是他妈。

「人生，」查特里斯说，这会儿他已经有工夫琢磨了，「是一幢咱们人人都偷过的房子。咱们不请自来，能拿到手的尽拿到手，然后转身出去。」

===Original===
He scribbled "Life—house—burgle" on his cuff and replaced the pencil.

"This man's brother I was telling you about," said Lord Dreever, "says there's only one rhyme in the English language to 'burglar,' and that's 'gurgler'—unless you count 'pergola.' He says—"

"Personally," said Jimmy, with a glance at McEachern, "I have rather a sympathy for burglars. After all, they are one of the hardest-working classes in existence. They toil while everybody else is asleep. Besides, a burglar is only a practical Socialist. People talk a lot about the redistribution of wealth. The burglar goes out and does it. I have found burglars some of the decentest criminals I have ever met."

"I despise burglars!" ejaculated Lady Julia, with a suddenness which stopped Jimmy's eloquence as if a tap had been turned off. "If I found one coming after my jewels and I had a pistol I'd shoot him."

===Chinese===
他在袖口上潦潦草草写下「人生——房子——偷」几个字，把铅笔收了回去。

「我方才讲的那位的兄弟，」德利弗勋爵说，「他说，英语里能跟『窃贼』（burglar）押上韵的词只有一个，就是『咕噜鬼』（gurgler）——除非你把『藤架』（pergola）也算上。他还说——」

「拿我个人来说，」吉米说着瞟了麦克伊彻恩一眼，「我对窃贼倒颇有几分同情。说到底，他们是世上最勤劳的阶层之一。人人都睡着的时候，他们却在卖力干活。况且，窃贼不过是个实干型的社会主义者。财富重新分配，人们谈了又谈。窃贼出门，把它办成了。据我见识，窃贼要数我平生遇见过的罪犯里头最体面的一类。」

「我瞧不起窃贼！」朱莉娅夫人脱口嚷道，来得那么突然，仿佛一只龙头咔哒拧死，把吉米的滔滔雄辩当场掐断。「要是有贼打我珠宝的主意，让我撞见，我手里又有支手枪，我就崩了他。」

===Original===
Jimmy met McEachern's eye, and smiled kindly at him. The ex-policeman was looking at him with the gaze of a baffled but malignant basilisk.

"I take very good care no one gets a chance at your diamonds, my dear," said Sir Thomas, without a blush. "I have had a steel box made for me," he added to the company in general, "with a special lock—a very ingenious arrangement, quite unbreakable, I imagine."

Jimmy, with Molly's story fresh in his mind, could not check a rapid smile. Mr. McEachern, watching him intently, saw it. To him it was fresh evidence, if any had been wanted, of Jimmy's intentions, and of his confidence of success. McEachern's brow darkened. During the rest of the meal tense thought rendered him more silent even than was his wont at the dinner table. The difficulty of his position was, he saw, great. Jimmy, to be foiled, must be watched, and how could he watch him?

It was not until the coffee arrived that he found an answer to the question. With his first cigarette came the idea. That night, in his room, before going to bed, he wrote a letter. It was an unusual letter, but singularly enough, almost identical with one Sir Thomas Blunt had written that very morning.

It was addressed to the Manager of Dodson's Private Inquiry Agency, of Bishopsgate Street, E.C., and ran as follows:

===Chinese===
吉米迎上麦克伊彻恩的目光，和蔼地朝他微微一笑。这位退休警察回望他的眼神，活像一条一计未成、却愈发歹毒的蛇怪（basilisk）。

「我小心防范着呢，绝不让任何人有机会碰你的钻石，我的宝贝，」托马斯爵士说，脸都不红一下。「我特意定做了一只钢柜，」他接着对满座说，「配一把特制的锁——机关非常精巧，我想是砸不开的。」

莫莉讲的那桩事言犹在耳，吉米嘴角一扬，没能忍住，飞快地笑了一下。麦克伊彻恩先生紧盯着他，看见了。在他看来，这便是如假包换的新证据——就算原先还缺证据的话——证明吉米居心叵测，而且自信稳操胜券。麦克伊彻恩眉头阴了下来。席上剩下的时间，紧张的思虑叫他比往常在饭桌上还要沉默。他看得明白，自己的处境难得很。要挫败吉米，就得盯住他，可他怎么盯得住呢？

直到咖啡端上来，他才找到了这个问题的答案。点起头一支烟，主意跟着就来了。当天夜里，他在自己房里，就寝之前，写了一封信。这是一封少见的信，可说来也巧，它同托马斯·布伦特爵士那天早晨写的那一封几乎如出一辙。

信是写给主教门大街（Bishopsgate Street, E.C.）多德森私家侦探社经理的，全文如下：

===Original===
> **Sir**—On receipt of this, kindly send down one of your smartest men. Instruct him to stay at the village inn in the character of American seeing sights of England and anxious to inspect Dreever Castle. I will meet him in the village and recognise him as old New York friend, and will then give him further instructions.
> Yours faithfully,
> J. McEachern
> P.S. Kindly not send a rube, but a really smart man.

===Chinese===
> **敬启者**——接信之后，烦即遣派贵社最精明干员一名南下。嘱其以游览英国名胜、急欲一睹德里弗城堡之美国游客身份，下榻村中旅店。余将于村中与彼相会，认作纽约旧友，届时再面授机宜。
> 谨启
> J. 麦克伊彻恩
> 又及：切勿遣乡巴佬，务派真正精明之人。

===Original===
This brief but pregnant letter cost him some pains in its composition. He was not a ready writer, but he completed it at last to his satisfaction. There was a crisp purity in the style which pleased him. He sealed up the envelope and slipped it into his pocket. He felt more at ease now. Such was the friendship that had sprung up between Sir Thomas Blunt and himself as the result of the jewel episode in Paris that he could count with certainty on the successful working of his scheme. The grateful knight would not be likely to allow any old New York friend of his preserver to languish at the village inn. The sleuthhound would at once be installed at the castle, where, unsuspected by Jimmy, he would keep an eye on the course of events. Any looking after that Mr. James Pitt might require might safely be left in the hands of this expert.

With considerable fervour Mr. McEachern congratulated himself on his astuteness. With Jimmy above stairs and Spike below, the sleuthhound would have his hands full.

===Chinese===
这封信言简而意深，起草起来却颇费了他一番工夫。他不是什么提笔就来的人物，但好歹总算写成，自己也觉满意。文笔有一种干脆爽利的纯净，令他颇为受用。他把信封好，塞进了衣袋。这一来，他心里踏实多了。巴黎珠宝那一桩风波，让他跟托马斯·布伦特爵士之间生出了交情；凭这层交情，他大可十拿九稳地指望自己的计划顺顺当当地运转起来。那位感恩戴德的爵士老爷，总不至于坐视救命恩人的纽约故交在村中小店里受冷落。那条侦探猎犬即刻便会在城堡里安营下寨，在那里，趁着吉米毫不生疑，盯住事态的每一步走向。詹姆斯·皮特先生需要什么样的「照看」，尽可以放心交给这位行家一手包办。

麦克伊彻恩先生满怀热忱地祝贺起自己的精明来。楼上一个吉米，底下一个斯派克，那位侦探猎犬可有得忙了。

## XV Mr. McEachern Intervenes / 麦克伊彻恩先生干预

===Original===
Life at the castle during the first few days of his visit filled Jimmy with a curious blend of emotions, mainly unpleasant. Fate, in its pro-Jimmy capacity, seemed to be taking a rest. In the first place, the part allotted to him was not that of Lord Herbert, the character who talked to Molly most of the time. The instant Charteris learned from Lord Dreever that Jimmy had at one time actually been on the stage professionally, he decided that Lord Herbert offered too little scope for the new man’s talents.

“Absolutely no good to you, my dear chap,” he said. “It’s just a small dude part. He’s simply got to be a silly ass.”

Jimmy pleaded that he could be a sillier ass than anybody living; but Charteris was firm.

“No,” he said. “You must be Captain Browne—true acting part, the biggest in the piece, full of fat lines. Spennie was to have played it, and we were in for the worst frost in the history of the stage. Now you’ve come it’s all right. Spennie’s the ideal of Lord Herbert. He’s simply got to be himself. We’ve got a success now, my boy. Rehearsal after lunch. Don’t be late.”

===Chinese===
吉米来做客的头几天，城堡里的日子在他心里搅起一股稀奇古怪的混杂滋味，其中不愉快的居多。命运那副偏向吉米的老脾气，看来是歇工去了。头一桩，派给他的角色就不是赫伯特勋爵——那位同莫莉说话最多的人物。查特里斯从德利弗勋爵嘴里一听说吉米当年居然正经在戏台上卖过艺，当即断定：赫伯特勋爵这个角色，施展不开这位新添人马的才华。

「这个角色对你全无用处，我亲爱的老哥，」他说。「不过是个公子哥儿的小配角。他只消当一个傻头傻脑的笨蛋就成。」

吉米央告说，论当笨蛋他能赛过世上一切活人；可查特里斯不为所动。

「不成，」他说。「你得演布朗上尉（Captain Browne）——真正见表演功夫的角色，全戏分量最重的一个，满纸肥美的台词。这角色原本派定了斯宾尼，那等着咱们的就是戏剧史上顶顶冻人的一场大冷场了。如今你一来，万事大吉。斯宾尼演赫伯特勋爵再合适不过。他只消做他自己就行。咱们这出戏如今是稳操胜券了，老弟。午饭后排练。别迟到。」

===Original===
And he had gone off to beat up the rest of the company.
