From that moment Jimmy’s troubles began. Charteris was a young man in whom a passion for the stage was ineradicably implanted. It mattered nothing to him during these days that the sun shone, that it was pleasant on the lake, and that Jimmy would have given five pounds a minute to be allowed to get Molly to himself for half an hour every afternoon. All he knew or cared about was that the local nobility and gentry were due to arrive at the castle a week from that day, and that very few of the company even knew their lines. Having hustled Jimmy into the part of Captain Browne, he gave his energy free play. He conducted rehearsals with a vigour which occasionally almost welded the rabble he was coaching into something approaching coherency. He painted scenery and left it about—wet—and people sat on it; he nailed up horseshoes for luck, and they fell on people. But nothing daunted him; he never rested.

“Mr. Charteris,” said Lady Julia rather frigidly, after one energetic rehearsal, “is indefatigable. He whirled me about!”

===Chinese===
说罢他便动身去把剧组其余人等一一驱赶到场。

从那一刻起，吉米的苦日子开了张。查特里斯这个年轻人，骨子里种着一股根除不了的舞台热。这些天里，太阳照不照、湖上惬意不惬意，还有吉米那桩情愿——每分钟掏五英镑，只求每天下午准他同莫莉单独厮守半个钟头——在他全然无关痛痒。他唯一知道也唯一上心的是：当地的贵族乡绅一个礼拜后就驾临城堡，而剧组里没几个人背得出自己的台词。把吉米一股脑儿塞进布朗上尉的角色之后，他便把一身干劲撒开来使。他指挥排演的那股生龙活虎劲儿，偶尔几乎能把他调教的这帮乌合之众焊接成一个勉强连贯的整体。他画布景，画完随手乱撂——还是湿的——于是有人坐了上去；他为了讨吉利往高处钉马蹄铁，马蹄铁便掉下来砸人。可什么也挫不败他；他从不歇气。

「查特里斯先生，」一场生龙活虎的排演过后，朱莉娅夫人相当冷冰冰地说，「真是不知疲倦。他把我旋得团团转！」

===Original===
It was, perhaps, his greatest triumph, properly considered, that he had induced Lady Julia to take a part in his piece; but to the born organiser of amateur theatricals no miracle of this kind is impossible, and Charteris was one of the most inveterate organisers in the country. There had been some talk—late at night in the billiard room—of his being about to write in a comic footman role for Sir Thomas, but it had fallen through; not, it was felt, because Charteris could not have hypnotised him into undertaking it, but rather because Sir Thomas was histrionically unfit.

Mainly as a result of the producer’s energy Jimmy found himself one of a crowd, and disliked the sensation. He had not experienced much difficulty in mastering the scenes in which he appeared; but unfortunately those who appeared with him had. It occurred to Jimmy daily, after he had finished “running through the lines” with a series of agitated amateurs, male and female, that for all practical purposes he might just as well have gone to Japan. In this confused welter of rehearsers his opportunities of talking with Molly were infinitesimal. And worse, she did not appear to mind. She was cheerful, and apparently quite content to be engulfed in a crowd. Probably, he thought with some melancholy, if she met his eye, and noted in it a distracted gleam, she put it down to the same cause which made other eyes in the company gleam distractedly during that week.

Jimmy began to take a thoroughly jaundiced view of amateur theatricals, and of these amateur theatricals in particular. He felt that in the electric flame department of the infernal regions there should be a special gridiron, reserved exclusively for the man who invented these performances, so diametrically opposed to the true spirit of civilisation. At the close of each day he cursed Charteris with unfailing regularity.

===Chinese===
若平心而论，他此生最伟大的胜利，恐怕要数说动朱莉娅夫人在他的戏里分任一角；不过在天生搞业余演剧的组织家眼里，这路奇迹没有办不到的，而查特里斯正是全国积习最深的组织家之一。曾有过一阵风声——深夜在台球室里——说他打算给托马斯爵士现编一个滑稽男仆的角色，但此事终究告吹；大伙儿的看法是，倒不是查特里斯没有本事把他催眠得应承下来，而是托马斯爵士在戏剧这一道上实属不堪造就。

主要多亏这位导演的干劲，吉米发现自己在人堆里泯然众人，而这种滋味他很不喜欢。背熟自己出场的几场戏，他没费多大事；不幸的是，同他配戏的那些人费了大事。每天跟一串男女紧张兮兮的业余票友「过一遍台词」下来，吉米都忍不住想，从实际效果讲，他索性去了日本也不过如此。在这锅排练人马的浑汤里，他同莫莉说话的机会微乎其微。更糟的是，她似乎毫不在意。她兴致勃勃，显然十分甘愿叫人堆没顶吞没。他带着几分忧郁想，多半即便她迎上他的目光、瞧见里头一星心猿意马的光，她也只当是那一礼拜里叫剧组旁人的眼神同样失魂落魄的那个缘故闹的。

吉米开始对业余演剧深恶痛绝，尤其对眼下这一摊。他觉得，地狱里专管电火的那一科，理应特设一面烤架，独家留给发明这路演出的人物——这种玩艺儿同文明的真精神实在是南辕北辙。每天收工，他诅咒查特里斯的功课从不缺席。

===Original===
There was another thing that disturbed him. That he should be unable to talk with Molly was an evil, but a negative evil. It was supplemented by one that was positive. Even in the midst of the chaos of rehearsals he could not help noticing that Molly and Lord Dreever were very much together. Also—and this was even more sinister—he observed that both Sir Thomas Blunt and Mr. McEachern were making determined efforts to foster this state of affairs.

Of this he had sufficient proof one evening when, after scheming and plotting in a way that had made the great efforts of Machiavelli and Richelieu seem like the work of raw novices, he had cut Molly out from the throng and carried her off for the alleged purpose of helping him feed the chickens. There were, as he had suspected, chickens attached to the castle. They lived in a little world of noise and smells at the back of the stables. Bearing an iron pot full of a poisonous-looking mash, and accompanied by Molly, he had felt, for perhaps a minute and a half, like a successful general. It is difficult to be romantic when you are laden with chickenfeed in an unwieldy iron pot, but he had resolved that that portion of the proceedings should be brief—the birds should dine that evening on the quick-lunch principle—then to the more fitting surroundings of the rose garden. There was plenty of time before the hour of the sounding of the dressing gong. Perhaps even a row on the lake—

“What-ho!” said a voice.

===Chinese===
还有一桩事搅得他心神不宁。见不着莫莉说话固然是一桩恶，却是消极的恶；给它作伴的还有一桩积极的。哪怕在排练的兵荒马乱之中，他也禁不住留意到，莫莉同德利弗勋爵走得非常之近。而且——这一层还要更加不祥——他看出托马斯·布伦特爵士和麦克伊彻恩先生两位都在下死力气撮合这桩局面。

对这一点，一天傍晚他拿到了铁证：他机关算尽、运筹帷幄，直弄得马基雅维利（Machiavelli）与黎塞留（Richelieu）的毕生伟业都好似生手学徒的涂鸦，终于把莫莉从人堆里截了出来，携之而去，名目是请她帮他喂鸡。城堡后头果然如他所料附设着鸡群，栖身在马厩后面一个小小的声响与气味的王国里。他端着一口铁锅，锅里盛满看着就有毒的糊糊饲料，莫莉伴在身侧——有那么约莫一分半钟，他感觉自己像一位旗开得胜的将军。抱着一锅笨重的鸡食，浪漫是不大浪漫得起来的；但他早已拿定主意，这一段程序务求速战速决——当晚众鸡就按快餐原则用膳——随后转移到玫瑰园那种更相称的环境里去。离更衣锣敲响的时辰还富余得很。说不定还能上湖上荡一荡桨——

「嗬，好哇！」一个声音说。

===Original===
Behind them, with a propitiatory smile on his face, stood his lordship of Dreever.

“My uncle told me I should find you out here. What have you got there, Pitt? Is this what you feed them on? I say, you know, queer coves, hens! I wouldn’t touch the stuff for a fortune. What? Looks to me poisonous.”

He met Jimmy’s eye and stopped. There was that in Jimmy’s eye that would have stopped an avalanche. His lordship twiddled his fingers in pink embarrassment.

“Oh, look!” said Molly. “There’s a poor little chicken out there in the cold. It hasn’t had a morsel. Give me the spoon, Mr. Pitt. Here, chick, chick! Don’t be silly, I’m not going to hurt you. I’ve brought you your dinner.”

===Chinese===
他们身后，站着德利弗勋爵大人，脸上堆着一副赔小心的笑。

「叔父对我说，出来准能在这儿找到你们。皮特，你那锅里是什么？就拿这个喂它们？我说，你知道吧，母鸡这种家伙真是古怪！给我多大的家当我也不碰这玩意儿。什么？我看有毒。」

他迎上吉米的目光，住了口。吉米眼里的那股神气，足以叫一场雪崩半路刹住。勋爵大人手指捻来捻去，窘得满脸绯红。

「哎呀，快看！」莫莉说。「那儿有只可怜的小鸡，孤零零待在冷地方，一口还没吃着呢。把勺子给我，皮特先生。来，小鸡，小鸡！别犯傻，我不会伤你的。你的晚饭我给你送来啦。」

===Original===
She moved off in pursuit of the solitary fowl, which had edged nervously away. Lord Dreever bent towards Jimmy.

“Frightfully sorry, Pitt, old man,” he whispered feverishly. “Didn’t want to come. Couldn’t help it. He sent me out.” He half looked over his shoulder. “And,” he added rapidly, as Molly came back, “the old boy’s at his bedroom window now, watching us through his opera glasses!”

The return journey to the house was performed in silence—on Jimmy’s part in thoughtful silence. He thought hard, and had been thinking ever since.

===Chinese===
她拔步追赶那只落单的鸡去了——那只鸡早已惴惴地蹭开了几步。德利弗勋爵朝吉米俯过身来。

「实在万分对不起，皮特，老哥，」他烧火一般急急地耳语。「我不想来。由不得我。是他派我出来的。」他半侧过头朝肩膀后望了望。「还有，」莫莉走回来时，他飞快补上一句，「老头儿这会儿正在他卧室窗口，举着观剧镜盯咱们呢！」

回屋的一路是在沉默中走完的——在吉米这一边，是沉思的沉默。他使劲地想，并且从那时起一路想到了如今。

===Original===
He had material for thought. That Lord Dreever was as clay in his uncle’s hands he was aware. He had not known his lordship long, but he had known him long enough to realise that a backbone had been carelessly omitted from his composition. What his uncle directed that would he do. The situation looked bad to Jimmy. The order, he knew, had gone out that Lord Dreever was to marry money, and Molly was an heiress. He did not know how much Mr. McEachern had amassed in his dealings with New York crime, but it could not but be something considerable. Things looked black.

Then he had a reaction. He was taking too much for granted. Lord Dreever might be hounded into proposing to Molly, but what earthly reason was there for supposing that Molly would accept him? He declined even for an instant to look upon Spennie’s title in the light of a lure. Molly was not the girl to marry for a title. He endeavoured to examine impartially his lordship’s other claims. He was a pleasant fellow, with—to judge on short acquaintanceship—an undeniably amiable disposition. That much must be conceded. But against this must be placed the equally undeniable fact that he was also, as he would have put it himself, a most frightful ass. He was weak. He had no character. Altogether, the examination made Jimmy more cheerful. He could not see the light-haired one, even with Sir Thomas Blunt shoving behind, as it were, accomplishing the knight’s ends. Shove he never so wisely, Sir Thomas could never make a Romeo out of Spennie Dreever.

It was while sitting in the billiard room one night after dinner, watching his rival play a hundred up with the silent Hargate, that Jimmy came definitely to this conclusion. He had stopped to watch more because he wished to study his man at close range than because the game was anything out of the common as an exposition of billiards. As a matter of fact, it would have been hard to imagine a worse game. Lord Dreever, who was conceding twenty, was poor, and his opponent an obvious beginner. Again, as he looked on, Jimmy was possessed of an idea that he had met Hargate before. But once more he searched his memory and drew blank. He did not give the thing much thought, being intent on his diagnosis of Lord Dreever, who, by a fluky series of cannons, had wobbled into the forties, and was now a few points ahead of his opponent.

===Chinese===
他有的是可供思考的材料。德利弗勋爵是他叔父手里的一团泥，这一点他清楚。这位勋爵大人他认识得不久，可也认识到足以看明白：造他的时候，一根脊梁骨叫人漫不经心地漏装了。叔父指到哪里，他便打到哪里。这局面在吉米看来很不对劲。他知道，命令已经下来：德利弗勋爵要娶一门带钱的亲事，而莫莉正是一位女继承人。麦克伊彻恩先生同纽约的罪案打交道的买卖里攒下了多少家底，他不得而知，但那数目决计小不了。形势显得一片漆黑。

跟着他又转了一个念头。他把事情想得太想当然了。德利弗勋爵或许能被撵着去同莫莉求婚，可天底下有什么理由认定莫莉就会应允他？哪怕一瞬之间，他也不肯把斯宾尼的头衔看作香饵。莫莉不是那种为了头衔出嫁的姑娘。他试着不偏不倚地审视一番勋爵大人其余的长处。这人讨人喜欢，而且——凭短浅的交情判断——脾气随和得无可否认。这一层总得认账。可与之相对，还有一桩同样无可否认的事实：照他自己乐意用的说法，他还是个顶顶不折不扣的大笨蛋。他软弱。他没有主心骨。通盘一算，吉米心头亮堂了些。他实在看不出那位浅发郎有成事的希望——哪怕有托马斯·布伦特爵士在后面推着，姑且算是替这位骑士大人成全好事。任他托马斯爵士推送得再怎么高明，他也决计不能把斯宾尼·德利弗点化成一位罗密欧。

一天晚饭后，吉米坐在台球室里，看他的情敌同沉默寡言的哈盖特打一百分一盘的球，正是在那儿，他实实在在把这个结论敲定了。他停下来观战，与其说这盘球在台球技艺上有什么可看之处，不如说他想近距离端详端详自己这位对手。说实在的，再难想象一盘更糟的球了。让二十分的德利弗勋爵球艺低劣，他的对手则分明是个生手。再有，观战之际，吉米心头掠过一个念头：从前在哪儿见过哈盖特。可他又一次搜刮记忆，一无所获。这茬他没有多想，只顾专心给德利弗勋爵下诊断——勋爵大人靠一连串纯属瞎撞的连撞双球，跌跌撞撞晃进了四十分，眼下还比对手领先几分。

===Original===
Presently, having summed his lordship up to his satisfaction, and grown bored with the game, Jimmy strolled out of the room. He paused outside the door for a moment, wondering what to do. There was bridge in the smoking room, but he did not feel inclined for bridge. From the drawing room there came sounds of music. He turned in that direction, then stopped again. He came to the conclusion that he did not feel sociable. He wanted to think. A cigar on the terrace would meet his needs.

He went up to his room for his cigar-case. The window was open. He leaned out. There was almost a full moon, and it was very light out of doors. His eye was caught by a movement at the farther end of the terrace, where the shadow was. A girl came out of the shadow, walking slowly. …

Not since early boyhood had Jimmy descended stairs with such a rare burst of speed. He negotiated the nasty turn at the end of the first flight at quite a suicidal pace. Fate, however, had apparently wakened up again and resumed business, for he did not break his neck. A few moments later he was out on the terrace, bearing a cloak which he had snatched up en route in the hall.

===Chinese===
不多时，吉米把勋爵大人掂量到了心满意足的程度，对那盘球也腻味了，便踱出屋去。他在门外停了一小会儿，琢磨着干什么好。吸烟室里有桥牌局，他却没那份兴致。客厅里飘出音乐声。他朝那边转了过去，随即又站住了。他得出的结论是：他不想应酬。他要想想心事。到露台上抽一支雪茄，正对他的需求。

他回房去拿雪茄盒。窗子开着。他探身向外。月亮将近满圆，户外亮堂得很。露台尽头的暗影里有一个动静，勾住了他的眼睛。一位姑娘从暗影里走了出来，步子缓缓的。……

打幼年时代起，吉米还不曾以这样一股千载难逢的疾劲冲下过楼梯。第一段楼梯尽头那个刁钻的急拐弯，他简直是以自杀的速度绕过去的。好在命运显然已经睡醒，重新开张营业，因此他没有摔断脖子。片刻之后，他已经站在露台上了，手里还拎着一件半路从门厅里顺手抓来的斗篷。

===Original===
“I thought you might be cold,” he said, breathing quickly.

“Oh, thank you,” said Molly. “How kind of you!” He put it round her shoulders. “Have you been running?”

“I came downstairs rather fast.”

“Were you afraid the boogaboos would get you?” she laughed. “I was thinking of when I was a small child. I was always afraid of them. I used to race downstairs when I had to go to my room in the dark, unless I could persuade someone to hold my hand all the way there and back.”

===Chinese===
「我想你兴许会冷，」他说，喘息未定。

「哦，谢谢你，」莫莉说。「你真好！」他把斗篷围上她的肩头。「你是跑着来的？」

「我下楼下得急了点儿。」

「你是怕叫布加布鬼（boogaboos）逮了去？」她笑道。「我是想起自己小时候了。我老怕那种东西。天黑了下到自己屋里去，我总是一路飞跑——除非能央动什么人牵着我的手，去也奉陪，回也奉陪。」

===Original===
Her spirits had risen with Jimmy’s arrival. Things had been happening that worried her. She had gone out onto the terrace to be alone. When she heard his footsteps she had dreaded the advent of some garrulous fellow guest, full of small talk. Jimmy, somehow, was a comfort—he did not disturb the atmosphere. Little as they had seen of each other, something in him—she could not say what—had drawn her to him. He was a man, she felt instinctively, she could trust.

They walked on in silence. Words were pouring into Jimmy’s mind, but he could not frame them. He seemed to have lost the power of coherent thought.

Molly said nothing. It was not a night for conversation. The moon had turned terrace and garden into a fairyland of black and silver. It was a night to look and listen and think.

===Chinese===
吉米一到，她的兴致便高涨起来。这阵子净出些叫她心烦的事。她跑上露台来，本想一个人清静清静。听见脚步声时，她只怕来的是哪位絮絮叨叨、满肚子闲话的男女同客。吉米呢，不知怎么，是一种安慰——他并不搅扰这方气氛。两人相见的时候虽然少，他身上有一样东西——她说不出是什么——把她往他那边拽。她本能地觉得，这是个她可以信靠的男人。

两人默默走着。千言万语往吉米脑子里灌，他却铸不成句子。他仿佛把条理分明地思想的本领丢了个干净。

莫莉一声不响。这不是个谈话的夜晚。月亮把露台和园子化作了一片黑银两色的仙境。这是该看、该听、该想心事的一夜。

===Original===
They walked slowly up and down. As they turned for the second time Molly’s thoughts formed themselves into a question. Twice she was on the point of asking it, but each time she checked herself. It was an impossible question. She had no right to put it, and he had no right to answer. Yet something was driving her on to ask it.

It came out suddenly, without warning.

“Mr. Pitt, what do you think of Lord Dreever?”

Jimmy started. No question could have chimed in more aptly with his thoughts. Even as she spoke he was struggling to keep himself from asking her the same thing.

===Chinese===
他们缓缓地来回踱步。走到第二回转身的时候，莫莉的心思凝成了一个问句。有两回她话已到口边，可两回都自己收住了。这是个问不得的问题。她没有权利问，他也没有权利答。然而有一样东西驱着她，非问不可。

问句冷不防脱口而出，毫无预兆。

「皮特先生，您觉得德利弗勋爵这个人怎么样？」

吉米吓了一跳。再没有哪个问题能同他心里的念头扣得这样严丝合缝了。她说这话的当口，他正拼尽全力压着自己，不把同一个问题问出口。

===Original===
“Oh, I know I ought not to ask,” she went on. “He’s your host and you’re his friend, I know. But—”

Her voice trailed off. The muscles of Jimmy’s back tightened and quivered, but he could find no words.

“I wouldn’t ask anyone else. But you’re—different somehow. I don’t know what I mean—we hardly know each other—but—”

She stopped again, and still he was dumb.

===Chinese===
「哦，我知道我不该问，」她接着说。「他是您的东道主，您是他的朋友，这我知道。可是——」

她的声音渐渐消散。吉米背上的肌肉绷得紧紧的，微微发颤，可他寻不出一句话来。

「换了别人我决不问。可你——不知怎么有点儿不同。我也不懂自己是什么意思——咱们几乎素不相识——可是——」

她又停住了。他依旧哑然。

===Original===
“I feel so alone,” she said very quietly, almost to herself. Something seemed to break in Jimmy’s head. His brain suddenly cleared. He took a step forward.

A huge shadow blackened the white grass. Jimmy wheeled round. It was McEachern.

“I have been looking for you, Molly, my dear. I thought you must have gone to bed.”

===Chinese===
「我觉得好孤单，」她说得极轻，几乎是对着自己说的。吉米脑袋里仿佛有什么东西绷断了。他的脑子豁然清明。他向前迈了一步。

一条巨大的影子罩黑了白色的草地。吉米急转身。是麦克伊彻恩。

「我一直在找你，莫莉，我亲爱的。我想你准是已经安歇了。」

===Original===
He turned to Jimmy and addressed him for the first time since their meeting in the bedroom.

“Will you excuse us, Mr. Pitt?”

Jimmy bowed and walked rapidly towards the house. At the door he stopped and looked back. The two were standing where he had left them.

===Chinese===
他转向吉米，自那次在卧室里相逢以来，头一回开口同他讲话。

「可否请你容我们单独待一会儿，皮特先生？」

吉米一躬身，快步朝屋子走去。到了门口，他停步回头望了望。那两个人还站在他离开他们的地方。

## XVI A Marriage Has Been Arranged / 婚事已定

===Original===
Neither Molly nor her father had moved or spoken while Jimmy was covering the short strip of turf that ended at the stone steps of the house. McEachern stood looking down at her in grim silence. His great body against the dark mass of the castle wall seemed larger than ever in the uncertain light. To Molly there was something sinister and menacing in his attitude. She found herself longing that Jimmy would come back. She was frightened. Why, she could not have said. It was as if some instinct told her that a crisis in her affairs had been reached, and that she needed him. For the first time in her life she felt nervous in her father’s company. Ever since she was a child she had been accustomed to look upon him as her protector, but now she was afraid.

“Father!” she cried.

“What are you doing out here?”

His voice was tense and strained.

===Chinese===
吉米走过那一段通到屋前石阶的短草坪时，莫莉和她的父亲一动没动，一语未发。麦克伊彻恩阴沉沉地沉默着，低头看她。在明灭不定的光线里，他那魁伟的身躯衬着城堡黑压压的墙垣，显得比什么时候都庞大。在莫莉眼里，他这副姿态透着一股不祥的凶气。她发觉自己一心盼着吉米回来。她害怕了。为什么害怕，她说不上来。仿佛有一种本能在告诉她：她的事已经到了一个关口，她需要他。有生以来头一遭，她在父亲身边觉得心惊。打小时候起，她一向把他当作自己的护卫，可如今她怕他。

「爸爸！」她喊道。

「你一个人跑出来做什么？」

他的声音绷得又紧又僵。

===Original===
“I came out because I wanted to think, father dear.”

She thought she knew his moods, but this was one that she had never seen. It frightened her.

“Why did he come out here?”

“Mr. Pitt? He brought me a wrap.”

“What was he saying to you?”

===Chinese===
「我出来是想清静清静，想点儿心事，好爸爸。」

她自以为摸得透他的脾气，可眼前这一种脾气，她从来没有见过。这叫她害怕。

「他跑出来做什么？」

「皮特先生吗？他给我送斗篷来了。」

「他对你说什么来着？」

===Original===
The rain of questions gave Molly a sensation of being battered. She felt dazed and a little mutinous. What had she done that she should be assailed like this?

“He was saying nothing,” she said, rather shortly.

“Nothing! What do you mean? What was he saying? Tell me!”

Molly’s voice shook as she replied.

“He was saying nothing,” she repeated. “Do you think I’m not telling you the truth, father? He had not spoken a word for ever so long. We just walked up and down. I was thinking, and I suppose he was, too. At any rate, he said nothing. I—I think you might believe me.”

===Chinese===
这一阵急雨般的盘问，打得莫莉只觉自己像挨了拳脚。她头晕目眩，还有点儿想犟嘴。她到底做了什么，要受这样的逼供？

「他什么也没说，」她答道，口气相当冲。

「什么也没说！你这是什么意思？他到底说了什么？讲给我听！」

莫莉回答的时候，声音直打颤。

「他什么也没说，」她又说了一遍。「爸爸，你当我在跟你扯谎吗？好半天他一个字也没开口。我们只是来回踱踱步。我在想心事，想必他也是。反正他什么都没说。我——我想你总该信得过我。」

===Original===
She began to cry quietly. Her father had never been like this before. It hurt her.

McEachern’s manner changed in a flash. In the shock of finding Jimmy and Molly together on the terrace he had forgotten himself. He had had reason to be suspicious. Sir Thomas Blunt, from whom he had just parted, had told him a certain piece of news which had disturbed him. The discovery of Jimmy with Molly had lent an added significance to that piece of news. He saw that he had been rough. In a moment he was by her side, his great arm round her shoulder, petting and comforting her as he had done when she was a child. He believed her word without question, and his relief made him very tender. Gradually the sobs ceased. She leaned against his arm.

“I’m tired, father,” she whispered.

“Poor little girl. We’ll sit down.”

There was a seat at the end of the terrace. He picked her up as if she had been a baby and carried her to it. She gave a little cry.

===Chinese===
她低低地哭起来。父亲从前从来没有这副样子。这叫她心口作痛。

麦克伊彻恩的态度霎时间变了。骤然撞见吉米和莫莉在露台上做伴，他一时忘乎所以。他的疑心原是事出有因。刚刚同他分手的托马斯·布伦特爵士，告诉过他一则消息，叫他心绪大乱。此刻撞见吉米同莫莉待在一处，更给那则消息平添了一层分量。他看出自己方才太粗鲁了。转眼间他已到了她身旁，一条粗胳膊搂住她的肩，像她小时候那样哄她、安慰她。她的话他深信不疑，一块石头落了地，人也变得温柔起来。啜泣渐渐止住了。她偎在他的臂弯上。

「我累了，爸爸，」她低声说。

「可怜的小宝贝。咱们坐下来。」

露台尽头放着一张椅子。他像抱婴孩似的把她抱起来，一直抱到椅子跟前。她轻轻叫了一声。

===Original===
“I didn’t mean I was too tired to walk,” she said, laughing tremulously. “How strong you are, father! If I were naughty you could take me up and shake me till I was good, couldn’t you?”

“Of course; and send you to bed, too, so you be careful, young woman.”

He lowered her to the seat. Molly drew the cloak closer round her and shivered.

“Cold, dear?”

“No.”

“You shivered.”

===Chinese===
「我又没说累得走不动啦，」她笑得颤颤的。「爸爸，你力气真大！我要是淘气，你尽可以把我拎起来摇，一直摇到我服帖为止，是不是？」

「当然啦；还要打发你上床睡觉呢。所以你放规矩点儿，小丫头。」

他把她放到椅上。莫莉把斗篷往身上裹紧了些，打了个寒噤。

「冷吗，亲爱的？」

「不冷。」

「你打寒噤了。」

===Original===
“It was nothing; yes, it was,” she went on quickly.

“It was. Father, will you promise me something?”

“Of course. What?”

“Don’t ever, ever be angry with me like that again, will you? I couldn’t bear it—really I couldn’t. I know it’s stupid of me, but it hurt. You don’t know how it hurt.”

“But my dear—”

===Chinese===
「没什么；不对，是真打了，」她急急改口。

「是真的。爸爸，你肯答应我一件事吗？」

「当然。什么事？」

「往后再别、千万别再那样对我发脾气了，好不好？我受不了——真个受不了。我知道这么想傻气，可是它伤人。你不知道它有多伤人。」

「可是我亲爱的——」

===Original===
“Oh, I know it’s stupid. But—”

“But, my darling, it wasn’t so. I was angry, but it wasn’t with you.”

“With—. Were you angry with Mr. Pitt?”

McEachern saw that he had travelled too far. He had intended that Jimmy’s existence should be forgotten for the time being—he had other things to discuss; but it was too late now. He must go forward.

===Chinese===
「哦，我知道这念头傻。可是——」

「可是我的宝贝儿，事情不是那样的。我是发了火，可不是冲着你。」

「冲——你是冲皮特先生发的火？」

麦克伊彻恩明白，自己已经走得太远了。他本打算叫吉米这个人暂时从话头里隐去——他另有事要商量；可如今为时已晚。他只能往前闯。

===Original===
“I didn’t like to see you out here alone with Mr. Pitt, dear,” he said. “I was afraid—”

He saw that he must go still farther forward. It was more than awkward. He wished to hint at the undesirability of an entanglement with Jimmy without admitting the possibility of it. Not being a man of nimble brain, he found this somewhat beyond his powers.

“I don’t like him,” he said briefly. “He’s crooked.”

Molly’s eyes opened wide. The colour had gone from her face.

===Chinese===
「我不乐意看见你同皮特先生单独待在这儿，亲爱的，」他说。「我担心——」

他明白自己还得往前闯得更远。这实在难堪极了。他想点一点同吉米搅在一处如何不好，却又不能承认这种事真有发生的可能。他脑子素来不灵便，觉得这差事实在超出了自己的能耐。

「我不喜欢那个人，」他斩钉截铁地说。「他手脚不干净。」

莫莉的眼睛睁得老大。她脸上的血色褪得干干净净。

===Original===
“Crooked, father?”

McEachern perceived that he had travelled very much too far, almost to disaster. He longed to denounce Jimmy, but he was gagged. If Molly were to ask the question that Jimmy had asked in the bedroom—that fatal, unanswerable question! The price was too great to pay.

He spoke cautiously, vaguely, feeling his way.

“I couldn’t explain to you, my dear—you wouldn’t understand. You must remember, my dear, that out in New York I was in a position to know a great many queer characters—crooks, Molly. I was working among them.”

“But, father, that night at our house you didn’t know Mr. Pitt. He had to tell you his name.”

===Chinese===
「手脚不干净，爸爸？」

麦克伊彻恩发觉自己走得实在太远，几乎闯到了灾祸跟前。他恨不得把吉米的底细数落个痛快，无奈嘴上像堵了棉花。万一莫莉问出吉米在卧室里问过的那个问题——那个致命的、答不上来的问题！这个代价太大，付不起。

他说话小心翼翼、含含糊糊，一路摸索着往前挪。

「这事我没法向你解释，我的亲爱的——你不会懂的。你得记住，亲爱的，我在纽约那地方，那个位分叫我知道了许多古怪人物——都是贼，莫莉。我当年就在他们中间当差。」

「可是，爸爸，那天晚上在咱们家里，你并不认识皮特先生呀。还得他自己报名字。」

===Original===
“I didn’t know him—then,” said her father slowly; “but—but—” He paused. “But I made inquiries,” he concluded, with a rush, “and found out things.”

He permitted himself a long, silent breath of relief. He saw his way now.

“Inquiries?” said Molly. “Why?”

“Why?”

“Why did you suspect him?”

===Chinese===
「我那时候不认识他，」她父亲缓缓地说；「可是——可是——」他顿了顿。「可我后来打听过，」他急急地收尾，「打听出了些事情。」

他容自己无声地长长舒了一口气。他看出路子该怎么走了。

「打听过？」莫莉说。「为什么？」

「为什么？」

「你为什么疑心他？」

===Original===
A moment earlier the question might have confused McEachern, but not now. He was equal to it. He took it in his stride.

“It’s hard to say, my dear. A man who has had as much to do with crooks as I have recognises them when he sees them.”

“Did you think Mr. Pitt looked—looked like that?” Her voice was very small. There was a drawn pinched expression on her face. She was paler than ever.

He could not divine her thoughts. He could not know what his words had done—how they had shown her in a flash what Jimmy was to her, and lit up her mind like a flame, revealing the secret hidden there. She knew now. The feeling of comradeship, the instinctive trust, the sense of dependence—they no longer perplexed her. They were signs which she could read.

And he was crooked!

===Chinese===
换在方才，这个问题兴许会把麦克伊彻恩问住，此刻却难不倒他。他应付裕如，稳稳当当地一步跨了过去。

「这难说，我的亲爱的。像我这样同贼打过这许多交道的人，一见面就认得出他们。」

「你觉得皮特先生看上去——看上去像那种人？」她的声音小得可怜。她脸上绷出一副憔悴紧窄的神气，人比先前更苍白了。

他猜不透她的心思。他无从知道自己这几句话已经做了什么——怎样一霎时让她看明白了吉米于她算什么，又怎样像一团火照亮了她的心，把藏在里面的秘密照了个透亮。如今她懂了。那种同心的情谊、那种本能的信赖、那种相依相靠的感觉——它们再也不叫她困惑了。那是她读得懂的记号。

而他手脚不干净！

===Original===
McEachern proceeded. Relief made him buoyant.

“I did, my dear. I can read him like a book. I’ve met scores of his sort. Broadway is full of them. Good clothes and a pleasant manner don’t make an honest man. I’ve run up against a mighty high-toned bunch of crooks in my day. It’s a long time since I gave up thinking that it was only the ones with the low foreheads and the thick ears that needed watching. It’s the innocent Willies who look as if all they could do was to lead the cotillon. This man Pitt’s one of them. I’m not guessing, mind you—I know. I know his line, and all about him. I’m watching him. He’s here on some game. How did he get here? Why, he scraped acquaintance with Lord Dreever in a London restaurant. It’s the commonest trick on the list. If I hadn’t happened to be here when he came I suppose he’d have made his haul by now. Why, he came all prepared for it! Have you seen an ugly, grinning, redheaded scoundrel hanging about the place? His valet, so he says. Valet! Do you know who that is? That’s one of the most notorious yeggmen on the other side. There isn’t a policeman in New York who doesn’t know Spike Mullins. Even if I knew nothing of this Pitt that would be enough. What’s an innocent man going round the country with Spike Mullins for, unless they are standing in together at some game? That’s who Mr. Pitt is, my dear, and that’s why, maybe, I seemed a little put out when I came upon you and him out here alone together. See as little of him as you can. In a large party like this it won’t be difficult to avoid him.”

Molly sat staring out across the garden. At first every word had been a stab. Several times she had been on the point of crying out that she could bear it no longer, but gradually a numbness succeeded the pain. She found herself listening apathetically.

McEachern talked on. He left the subject of Jimmy, comfortably conscious that, even if there had ever existed in Molly’s heart any budding feeling of the kind he had suspected, it must now be dead. He steered the conversation away until it ran easily among commonplaces. He talked of New York, of the preparations for the theatricals. Molly answered composedly. She was still pale, and a certain listlessness in her manner might have been noticed by a more observant man than Mr. McEachern. Beyond that there was nothing to show that her heart had been born and killed but a few minutes before. Women have the Red Indian instinct; and Molly had grown to womanhood in those few minutes.

===Chinese===
麦克伊彻恩往下说。心头的宽慰叫他来了劲。

「我正是这么个看法，我的亲爱的。我看那个人，就像看一本摊开的书。他那一号人，我见过几十个。百老汇满街都是。衣裳穿得考究、态度生得讨喜，未必就凑得出一个正经人。想当年我也撞上过好一帮子气派十足的贼。我早就不再那么想了——以为要留神的只有那些塌脑门、肥耳朵的家伙。要留神的偏偏是那些一脸无辜的威利少爷，瞧上去除了领一场科蒂永舞（cotillon），别的什么也干不来。这位皮特先生就是那路人。你听着，我不是瞎猜——我是知道。他的行当、他的底细，我全清楚。我正盯他的梢呢。他到这儿来，身上揣着某种把戏。他怎么到这儿来的？哼，在伦敦一家饭馆里同德利弗勋爵攀上了交情。这是贼谱上顶常见的一手。他来的时候要不是我碰巧在场，我估摸如今他早已得手了。哼，他来的时节装备得停停当当！你可曾见过一个丑八怪——龇着牙笑嘻嘻、顶着一头红毛——在这宅子左近荡来荡去？据说是他的贴身男仆。男仆！你可知道那是谁？那是大洋彼岸恶名最响的撬保险箱贼（yeggman）之流。纽约城里没有一个警察不认得斯派克·马林斯。就算我对这位皮特一无所知，单凭这一条也尽够了。一个清白人，跑遍四面八方都带着斯派克·马林斯，图的是什么？除非两个人串通一气，合伙做着什么买卖。皮特先生就是这么一号人，我的亲爱的；也正为这个，方才我撞见你同他在这外面单独做伴，兴许脸色才不大好看。你同他见面，能少见就少见。像这样一大家子客人，要避开他并不难。」

莫莉坐在那里，直勾勾地望着园子那头。起初，每一个字都是一刀。有几回她差点儿喊出声，说自己再也受不住了；可是渐渐地，麻木顶替了疼痛。她发觉自己竟木然地听着。

麦克伊彻恩絮絮地讲个不停。吉米这个话头他撂下了，心里舒坦得很：就算莫莉心里当真萌生过他所疑心的那种苗头，此刻也必定死了。他把话锋引开，一路引得它安安稳稳淌进家常琐事里。他讲纽约，讲业余演剧的筹备。莫莉应对从容。她的脸色依旧苍白，那举止间的一股没精打采，换一个比麦克伊彻恩先生眼光尖些的男人，兴许能瞧出来。除此之外，再没有一点痕迹，泄露她的心在几分钟前才初初觉醒，又随即被扑灭。女人有红种印第安人的本能；就在那几分钟里，莫莉长成了妇人。

===Original===
Presently Lord Dreever’s name came up.

It caused a momentary pause, and McEachern took advantage of it. It was the cue for which he had been waiting.

He hesitated for a moment, for the conversation was about to enter upon a difficult phase, and he was not quite sure of himself.

Then he took the plunge.

“I have just been talking to Sir Thomas, my dear,” he said. He tried to speak casually, and as a natural result infused so much meaning into his voice that Molly looked at him in surprise. McEachern coughed confusedly. Diplomacy, he concluded, was not his forte. He abandoned it in favour of directness.

===Chinese===
不多时，德利弗勋爵的名字浮上了话头。

这名字惹起一霎间的冷场，麦克伊彻恩便趁了这个空当。这正是他等候多时的提示。

他迟疑了片刻，因为谈话就要踏进一段难走的路，他心里没有十分的底。

随后他一横心，纵身跳了下去。

「我方才同托马斯爵士谈过来着，我的亲爱的，」他说。他竭力想说得像随口闲谈，结果不出所料，嗓音里反倒灌进了满满的意味，惹得莫莉诧异地望住他。麦克伊彻恩心慌意乱地咳了一声。他断定，耍外交不是他的长处。他索性把它撂开，来直的。

===Original===
“He was telling me that you had refused Lord Dreever this evening.”

“Yes, I did,” said Molly. “How did Sir Thomas know?”

“Lord Dreever told him.”

Molly raised her eyebrows.

===Chinese===
「他告诉我，今天晚上你回绝了德利弗勋爵。」

「是的，我回绝了，」莫莉说。「托马斯爵士怎么会知道？」

「德利弗勋爵告诉他的。」

莫莉扬起了眉毛。

===Original===
“I shouldn’t have thought it was the sort of thing he would talk about,” she said.

“Sir Thomas is his uncle.”

“Of course. So he is,” said Molly dryly. “I forgot. That would account for it, wouldn’t it?”

Mr. McEachern looked at her with some concern. There was a hard ring in her voice which he did not altogether like. His greatest admirer had never called him an intuitive man, and he was quite at a loss to see what was wrong. As a schemer he was perhaps a little naive. He had taken it for granted that Molly was ignorant of the manoeuvres which had been going on, and which had culminated that afternoon in a stammering proposal of marriage from Lord Dreever in the rose garden. This, however, was not the case. The woman incapable of seeing through the machinations of two men of the mental calibre of Sir Thomas Blunt and Mr. McEachern has yet to be born. For some considerable time Molly had been alive to the well-meant plottings of that worthy pair, and had derived little pleasure from the fact. It may be that woman loves to be pursued, but she does not love to be pursued by a crowd.

===Chinese===
「我原以为，这样的事不是他会拿去谈论的，」她说。

「托马斯爵士是他叔父。」

「哦，不错。正是叔父，」莫莉干巴巴地说。「我忘了。这就难怪了，是不是？」

麦克伊彻恩先生不无忧虑地看着她。她嗓音里有种硬邦邦的调子，他听着不很对味。最崇拜他的人也从来不敢说他是凭直觉行事的人，此刻他全然摸不着毛病出在哪儿。作为一个摆布局的人，他恐怕天真了一点儿。他满以为，这一直在进行中的种种运筹——当天下午已在玫瑰园里结出果实的运筹：德利弗勋爵结结巴巴地求了婚——莫莉一概蒙在鼓里。然而事实并非如此。要等一位看不穿托马斯·布伦特爵士与麦克伊彻恩先生这般智力水准的两个人的一串诡计的女人出世，恐怕还得有些年头。好一阵子了，莫莉对那两位可敬先生的好心密谋早有觉察，并且没从这件事里得到多少乐趣。女人也许喜欢被人追求，可她决不喜欢被一群人追猎。

===Original===
Mr. McEachern cleared his throat and began again.

“You shouldn’t decide a question like that too hastily, my dear.”

“I didn’t—not too hastily for Lord Dreever, at any rate, poor dear.”

“It was in your power,” said Mr. McEachern portentously, “to make a man happy.”

“I did,” said Molly, bitterly. “You should have seen his face light up. He could hardly believe it was true for a moment, and then it came home to him, and I thought he would have fallen on my neck. He did his very best to look heartbroken—out of politeness—but it was no good. He whistled most of the way back to the house—all flat, but very cheerfully.”

===Chinese===
麦克伊彻恩先生清了清嗓子，重新开言。

「这样的问题，你不该急忙下断语，我的亲爱的。」

「我没有急忙呀——至少对德利弗勋爵来说，一点儿也不急，可怜的人儿。」

「你原本有本事，」麦克伊彻恩先生煞有介事地说，「叫一个男人幸福的。」

「我是叫他了呀，」莫莉没好气地说。「你该瞧瞧他那张脸顿时亮起来的样子。他一时简直不敢相信是真的，接着才回过味来，我当真怕他要扑上来搂住我的脖子了。他还拿出全副本事装出一副肝肠寸断的样子——出于礼貌——可惜装不成。回屋的一路，他大半工夫都在吹口哨——全都走了调，可吹得欢势着呢。」

===Original===
“My dear! What do you mean?”

Molly had made the discovery earlier in their conversation that her father had moods whose existence she had not suspected. It was his turn now to make a similar discovery regarding herself.

“I mean nothing, father,” she said. “I’m just telling you what happened. He came to me looking like a dog that’s going to be washed—”

“Why, of course; he was nervous, my dear.”

“Of course. He couldn’t know that I was going to refuse him.”

She was breathing quickly. He started to speak, but she went on looking straight before her. Her face was very white in the moonlight.

===Chinese===
「我的亲爱的！你这是什么话？」

方才谈着话，莫莉已经发现，父亲身上竟有她从未疑心其存在的种种脾气。如今轮到他来发现，她身上也有类似的意外了。

「我没什么话，爸爸，」她说。「我只是把经过讲给你听。他来找我的时候，那副样子活像一条要被拖去洗澡的狗——」

「咳，那自然；他紧张嘛，我的亲爱的。」

「自然。他又不能未卜先知，料到我要回绝他。」

她呼吸急促。他开口要说话，她却望着正前方继续说下去。月光里她的脸白得很。

===Original===
“He took me into the rose garden. Was that Sir Thomas’s idea? There couldn’t have been a better setting, I’m sure—the roses looked lovely. Presently I heard him gulp, and I was so sorry for him. I would have refused him then, and put him out of his misery, only I couldn’t very well till he had proposed, could I? So I turned my back and sniffed at a rose, and then he shut his eyes—I couldn’t see him, but I knew he shut his eyes—and began to say his lesson.”

“Molly!”

“He did—he said his lesson. He gabbled it. When he had got as far as ‘Well don’t you know, what I mean is, that’s what I wanted to say, you know,’ I turned round and soothed him. I said, I didn’t love him. He said, ‘No, no, of course not.’ I said he had paid me a great compliment. He said, ‘Not at all,’ looking very anxious, poor darling, as if even then he was afraid of what might come next. But I reassured him, and he cheered up, and we walked back to the house together, as happy as could be.”

McEachern put his hand round her shoulder. She winced, but let it stay. He attempted gruff conciliation.

“My dear, you’ve been imagining things. Of course he isn’t happy. Why, I saw the young fellow—”

===Chinese===
「他把我领进了玫瑰园。那是托马斯爵士出的主意吧？我敢说再找不出更好的布景了——玫瑰开得美极了。不多时，我听见他咽了一口唾沫，心里怪替他难受的。我本想那时候就回绝他，叫他早些脱离苦海，只是他还没求婚，不大好办，是不是？于是我便转过身去，凑着一朵玫瑰闻香，跟着他就闭上了眼睛——我瞧不见他，可我知道他闭上了眼睛——开始背他的功课。」

「莫莉！」

「他真背了——背他的功课。叽里咕噜一气。等他背到『这个，你们知道吧，我是说，我想讲的就是这个，你晓得的』，我就转过身去抚慰他。我说，我不爱他。他说，『不不，当然不爱。』我说，他这一番话把我恭维到了家。他说，『不敢当』，神色慌得很，可怜的宝贝儿，仿佛到了那一步还怕下文不知是什么。不过我给他吃了定心丸，他立刻打起精神，我们俩一块儿走回屋子，快活得不得了。」

麦克伊彻恩伸过手去搂她的肩。她缩了一下，却由它搁着。他试着来一场粗声粗气的和解。

「我的亲爱的，你这是想入非非。他自然不快活喽。嗐，我亲眼见过那小伙子——」

===Original===
Recollecting that the last time he had seen the young fellow—shortly after dinner—the young fellow had been occupied in juggling, with every appearance of mental peace, with two billiard balls and a box of matches, he broke off abruptly.

“Father?”

“My dear?”

“Why do you want me to marry Lord Dreever?”

“I think he’s a fine young fellow,” he said, avoiding her eyes.

“He’s quite nice,” said Molly quietly.

===Chinese===
他蓦地记起，上一回见到那小伙子——晚饭后不久——那小伙子正心安理得、有滋有味地抛着戏法，道具是两颗台球和一盒火柴。他戛然刹住了话头。

「爸爸？」

「哎，我的亲爱的？」

「你为什么想要我嫁给德利弗勋爵？」

「我看他是个挺不错的小伙子，」他说，躲开了她的目光。

「他确实挺好，」莫莉平平静静地说。

===Original===
McEachern had been trying not to say it. He did not wish to say it. If it could have been hinted at, he would have done it, but he was not good at hinting. A lifetime passed in surroundings where the subtlest hint is a drive in the ribs with a truncheon does not leave a man an adept at the art. He had to be blunt or silent.

“He’s the Earl of Dreever, my dear.”

He rushed on, desperately anxious to cover the nakedness of the statement in a comfortable garment of words.

“Why, you see, you’re young, Molly. It’s only natural you shouldn’t look on these things sensibly. You expect too much of a man. You expect this young fellow to be like the heroes of the novels you read. When you’ve lived a little longer, my dear, you’ll see that there’s nothing in it. It isn’t the hero of the novel you want to marry, it’s the man who’ll make you a good husband.”

This remark struck Mr. McEachern as so pithy and profound that he repeated it.

===Chinese===
麦克伊彻恩一直在竭力不说那句话。他不愿说它。倘若能绕着弯子点到，他早就点了，无奈他拙于绕弯子。一个人在那种环境里消磨了一辈子——那里头顶其微妙的暗示，也不过是拿警棍往肋骨上捣一下——这样的履历练不出这门精致功夫。他只有两条路：要么直说，要么闭嘴。

「他是德利弗伯爵呀，我的亲爱的。」

他一鼓作气往下讲，火烧火燎地要给这句赤裸裸的话罩上一件言语织成的舒适外衣。

「喏，是这样，你还年轻，莫莉。你看这些事情不能冷静，这也怪不得你。你把一个男人指望得太高。你指望这个小伙子像你读的小说里的男主角。等你再活上几年，我的亲爱的，你就会明白，那里头什么也没有。你要嫁的不是小说的男主角，是能给你当个好丈夫的人。」

这句话在麦克伊彻恩先生听来精辟之至、深奥之至，于是他又把它重复了一遍。

===Original===
He went on. Molly was sitting quite still, looking into the shrubbery. He assumed she was listening, but whether she was or not he must go on talking. The situation was difficult. Silence would make it more so.

“Now, look at Lord Dreever,” he said. “There’s a young man with one of the oldest titles in England. He could go anywhere and do what he liked, and be excused for whatever he did because of his name; but he doesn’t. He’s got the right stuff in him. He doesn’t go racketing around—”

“His uncle doesn’t allow him enough pocket money,” said Molly, with a jarring little laugh. “Perhaps that’s why.”

There was a pause. McEachern required a few moments in which to marshal his arguments once more. He had been thrown out of his stride.

“Father dear, listen,” she said. “We always used to understand each other so well.” He patted her shoulder affectionately. “You can’t mean what you say. You know I don’t love Lord Dreever, you know he’s only a boy. Don’t you want me to marry a man? I love this old place; but surely you can’t think that it can really matter in a thing like this? You don’t really mean that about the hero of the novel? I’m not stupid, like that. I only want—oh, I can’t put it into words; but don’t you see?”

===Chinese===
他接着往下讲。莫莉端端正正坐着，眼睛望着灌木丛。他只当她是在听；其实听与不听，他也只好往下讲。处境已经够为难了，沉默只会叫它更为难。

「再瞧瞧德利弗勋爵，」他说。「这才是个年轻人——顶着英格兰数一数二的古老头衔。他本来爱到哪里去就到哪里去，爱做什么就做什么，凭他那姓氏，做什么都有人原谅；可他不。他骨子里是好料。他从不胡作非为——」

「他叔父给的零用钱不够他胡作非为的，」莫莉说，短短地干笑一声，听着刺耳。「兴许缘故在这里。」

冷了场。麦克伊彻恩需要一点工夫，把他的论点重新排布一遍。他的步子被打乱了。

「好爸爸，你听我说，」她说。「咱们从前总是你懂我、我懂你的。」他慈爱地拍了拍她的肩。「你嘴里说的不会是心里话。你知道我不爱德利弗勋爵，你知道他还只是个孩子。你不想叫我嫁个男子汉吗？我爱这座老宅子；可是你总不至于当真以为，这样的事情里它真顶得上用场吧？你方才那番小说男主角的话，不是真心吧？我还不至于傻到那个地步。我只是要——哦，我说不成句；可你不明白吗？」

===Original===
Her eyes were fixed appealingly on him. It only needed a word from him—perhaps not even a word—to close the gulf which had opened between them.

He missed the chance. He had had time to think, and his arguments were ready again. With stolid good humour he marched along the line he had mapped out. He was kindly and shrewd and practical, and the gulf gaped wider with every word.

“You mustn’t be rash, my dear—you mustn’t act without thinking in these things. Lord Dreever is only a boy, as you say, but he will grow. You say you don’t love him. Nonsense! You like him, you would go on liking him more and more. And why? Because you could make what you pleased of him. You’ve got character, my dear. With a girl like you to look after him, he would go a long way, a very long way. It’s all there; it only wants bringing out. And think of it, Molly—Countess of Dreever! There’s hardly a better title in England. It would make me very happy, my dear. It’s been my one hope all these years to see you in the place where you ought to be. And now the chance has come. Molly dear, don’t throw it away.”

She had leaned back with closed eyes. A wave of exhaustion had swept over her. She listened in a dull dream. She felt beaten. They were too strong for her. There were too many of them. What did it matter? Why not give in and end it all and win peace? That was all she wanted—peace now. What did it all matter?

“Very well, father,” she said listlessly.

===Chinese===
她的眼睛含着哀恳，一眨不眨地钉住他。只消他一句话——兴许连一句话也不必——就足以把两人之间裂开的那道鸿沟弥合起来。

他错过了这个机会。他方才得了工夫盘算，论点又都各就各位。他一副和和气气的木讷神情，沿着自己预先画好的路线一路进发。他是慈祥的、精明的、讲实际的，而那道鸿沟随他的每一个字越裂越宽。

「这种事你不可莽撞，我的亲爱的——不可不想一想就动手。德利弗勋爵是个孩子，这不错，可孩子会长。你说你不爱他。胡说！你喜欢他，往后还要越喜欢越深。为什么？因为他是一块泥，你爱捏成什么样就捏成什么样。你有主心骨，我的亲爱的。有你这样的姑娘照管他，他前程远大，远大得很。好材料全在那儿摆着，单欠打磨出来。再想想吧，莫莉——德利弗伯爵夫人！英格兰再挑不出几个更响亮的头衔了。这会叫我很快活，我的亲爱的。这些年我唯一的指望，就是看着你坐上你该坐的位子。如今机会来了。莫莉，乖孩子，别把它扔掉。」

她仰靠在椅背上，阖着眼睛。一阵疲惫的大浪把她整个淹没了。她在昏昏沉沉的梦境里听着。她觉得被打败了。他们太强，她敌不过；他们人太多。这有什么要紧？何不索性依了，把一切都了结，换一个清静？她要的只有这个——眼下的清静。这一切又有什么要紧？

「好吧，爸爸，」她有气无力地说。

===Original===
McEachern stopped short.

“You’ll do it, dear?” he cried. “You will?”

“Very well, father.”

He stooped and kissed her.

“My own dear little girl,” he said.

She got up.

===Chinese===
麦克伊彻恩猛地收住话头。

「你肯了，亲爱的？」他嚷道。「当真肯了？」

「好吧，爸爸。」

他俯下身，吻了她。

「我的乖乖好女儿，」他说。

她站起身来。

===Original===
“I’m rather tired, father,” she said. “I think I’ll go in.”

Two minutes later Mr. McEachern was in Sir Thomas Blunt’s study. Five minutes later Sir Thomas pressed the bell.

Saunders appeared.

“Tell his lordship,” said Sir Thomas, “that I wish to see him for a moment. He is in the billiard room, I think.”

===Chinese===
「我挺累的了，爸爸，」她说。「我想进去了。」

两分钟以后，麦克伊彻恩先生已经站在托马斯·布伦特爵士的书房里。五分钟以后，托马斯爵士按响了铃。

进来的是桑德斯。

「请告诉勋爵大人，」托马斯爵士说，「就说我有话要见他，请他来一会儿。我想他这会儿在台球室。」

## XVII Jimmy Remembers Something, and Hears Something Else / 吉米想起一件事，又听见一件事

===Original===
The game between Hargate and Lord Dreever was still in progress when Jimmy returned to the billiard room. A glance at the board showed that the score was seventy—sixty-nine in favour of spot.

“Good game,” said Jimmy. “Who’s spot?”

“I am,” said his lordship, missing an easy cannon. For some reason he appeared in high spirits. “Hargate’s been going great guns. I was eleven ahead a moment ago, but he made a break of twelve.”

Lord Dreever belonged to the class of billiard player to whom a double-figure break is a thing to be noted and greeted with respect.

===Chinese===
吉米回到台球室时，哈盖特同德利弗勋爵的那盘球还在进行。他朝记分牌一瞥，比分是七十比六十九，点位方占先。

「好一盘球，」吉米说。「点位方是谁？」

「我，」勋爵大人应道，一杆手到擒来的连撞双球竟告脱手。不知怎的，他显得兴高采烈。「哈盖特一直打得起劲得很。方才我还领先十一分，他倒好，一杆连下了十二分。」

德利弗勋爵属于台球界的这样一类人物：连杆上了两位数，便属值得郑重记录、肃然起敬的盛事。

===Original===
“Fluky,” muttered the silent Hargate deprecatingly. This was a long speech for him. Since their meeting at Paddington Station Jimmy had seldom heard him utter anything beyond a monosyllable.

“Not a bit of it, dear old son,” said Lord Dreever handsomely. “You’re coming on like a two-year-old. I shan’t be able to give you twenty in a hundred much longer.”

He went to a side table and mixed himself a whisky and soda, singing a brief extract from musical comedy as he did so. There could be no shadow of doubt that he was finding life good. For the past few days, and particularly that afternoon, he had been rather noticeably ill at ease. Jimmy had seen him hanging about the terrace at half past five, and had thought that he looked like a mute at a funeral, but now, only a few hours later, he was beaming on the world and chirping like a bird.

===Chinese===
「瞎猫碰上死耗子，」沉默寡言的哈盖特不以为然地咕哝了一句。对他而言，这话已算得上一篇演说。自帕丁顿车站那回见面以来，吉米难得听见他说出一个音节以上的话。

「哪儿的话，亲爱的老弟，」德利弗勋爵宽宏大量地说。「你长进得跟两岁口的新马一个样。再过些日子，一百分让你二十分的便宜我可给不起了。」

他走到边桌旁，给自己调了一杯威士忌苏打，一边调一边哼着音乐喜剧里的一小段唱词。毋庸置疑，他正觉得人生美好。过去这几天，尤其那天下午，他一直明显地坐立不安。五点半光景，吉米还见他在露台上转来转去，那副模样活像出殡队伍里雇来的哭丧人；可这才过了几个钟头，他已经对着全世界眉开眼笑，小鸟一般啭鸣起来。

===Original===
The game moved jerkily along. Jimmy took a seat and watched. The score mounted slowly. Lord Dreever was bad, but Hargate was worse. At length, in the eighties, his lordship struck a brilliant vein. When he had finished his break his score was ninety-five. Hargate, who had profited by a series of misses on his opponent’s part, had reached ninety-six.

“This is shortening my life,” said Jimmy, leaning forward.

The balls had been left in an ideal position. Even Hargate could not fail to make a cannon. He made it.

A close finish to even the worst game is exciting. Jimmy leaned still farther forward to watch the next stroke. It looked as if Hargate would have to wait for his victory. A good player could have made a cannon as the balls lay, but not Hargate. They were almost in a straight line, with white in the centre.

===Chinese===
那盘球跌跌撞撞地往下进行。吉米找了个座位坐下观战。比分缓缓爬升。德利弗勋爵打得糟，可哈盖特打得更糟。末了，进入八十分区间，勋爵大人忽然掘着一道妙矿，一杆连下收场时计到了九十五分。哈盖特专捡对手失误的便宜，也爬到了九十六分。

「这盘球要折我的寿，」吉米说着，往前探了探身。

台面上的球摆在了再理想不过的位置。这样的连撞双球，连哈盖特也不至于打丢。他打成了。

再蹩脚的一盘球，杀到终盘吃紧处也叫人心跳。吉米把身子探得更前，等着看下一杆。看样子，哈盖特的胜仗还得再等一等。就那个球势，好手本可以再连撞一把双球，哈盖特却办不到。三颗球几乎摆成一条直线，白球位居正中。

===Original===
Hargate swore under his breath. There was nothing to be done. He struck carelessly at white. White rolled against red, seemed to hang for a moment, and shot straight back against spot. The game was over.

“Great Scot! What a fluke!” cried the silent one, becoming quite garrulous at the miracle.

A quiet grin spread itself slowly across Jimmy’s face. He had remembered what he had been trying to remember for over a week.

===Chinese===
哈盖特压着嗓子咒骂了一声。横竖无计可施，他随手一杆打向白球。白球滚去贴上红球，像迟疑了一瞬，随即笔直弹回，撞上点位球。这盘球就此终了。

「我的老天！这一杆撞运了！」那位沉默寡言的先生嚷道，见了这等奇迹，居然滔滔不绝起来。

一抹无声的笑意在吉米脸上缓缓漾开。他总算想起了那个想了一个多礼拜都没想起来的事。

===Original===
At this moment the door opened and Saunders appeared. “Sir Thomas would like to see your lordship in his study,” he said.

“Eh? What does he want?”

“Sir Thomas did not confide in me, your lordship.”

“Eh? What? Oh, no. Well, see you later, you men.”

===Chinese===
正在这时，门开了，桑德斯出现在门口。「托马斯爵士请勋爵大人到书房一叙，」他说。

「咦？他要我做什么？」

「托马斯爵士没有把缘由见告，勋爵大人。」

「咦？什么？哦，没事。那行，回头见，二位。」

===Original===
He rested his cue against the table and put on his coat. Jimmy followed him out of the door, which he shut behind him.

“One second, Dreever,” he said.

“Eh? Halloa! What’s up?”

“Any money on that game?” asked Jimmy.

===Chinese===
他把球杆靠上台球桌，穿好外套。吉米跟着他走出门去，反手把门带上。

「等一步，德利弗，」他说。

「咦？哈啰！出什么事了？」

「那盘球有没有赌钱？」吉米问。

===Original===
“Why, yes; by Jove! now you mention it, there was—an even fiver. And—er—by the way, old man, the fact is, just for the moment, I’m frightfully—. You haven’t such a thing as a fiver anywhere about, have you? The fact is—”

“My dear fellow, of course. I’ll square up with him now, shall I?”

“Fearfully obliged if you would. Thanks, old man. Pay it you tomorrow.”

“No hurry,” said Jimmy; “plenty more in the old oak chest.”

===Chinese===
「咦，有；天哪！经你一提，是有的——整整五镑。还有——呃——对了，老哥，实话实说，我这会儿手头窘得不得了——。你身上不会碰巧带着五镑吧？实话是——」

「亲爱的老伙计，自然有。我这就去替你把账结了，好不好？」

「那可真感激不尽。多谢，老哥。明天准还你。」

「不急，」吉米说；「老橡木柜里还多着呢。」

===Original===
He went back to the room. Hargate was practising cannons. He was on the point of making a stroke when Jimmy opened the door.

“Care for a game?” said Hargate.

“Not just at present,” said Jimmy.

Hargate attempted his cannon and failed badly. Jimmy smiled.

===Chinese===
他回到屋里。哈盖特正在练连撞双球。吉米推门进去的时候，他正要出杆。

「来一盘？」哈盖特说。

「这会儿先不了，」吉米说。

哈盖特试着打他的连撞双球，败得极惨。吉米微微笑了。

===Original===
“Not such a good shot as the last,” he said.

“No.”

“Fine shot, that other.”

“Fluke.”

“I wonder.”

===Chinese===
「这一杆可不如上一杆，」他说。

「是不如。」

「上一杆真是好球。」

「碰上的。」

「我看未必。」

===Original===
Jimmy lit a cigarette.

“Do you know New York at all?” he asked.

“Been there.”

“Ever been in the Strollers’ Club?”

Hargate turned his back; but Jimmy had seen his face and was satisfied.

===Chinese===
吉米点了一支烟。

「你对纽约熟不熟？」他问。

「去过。」

「『闲逛者』俱乐部你进过没有？」

哈盖特转过身去；可吉米已经看清了他脸上的神色，心里有数了。

===Original===
“Don’t know it,” said Hargate.

“Great place,” said Jimmy. “Mostly actors and writers, and so on. The only drawback is that some pick up queer friends.”

Hargate did not reply. He did not seem interested.

“Yes,” went on Jimmy. “For instance, a pal of mine—an actor named Mifflin—introduced a man a year ago as a member’s guest for a fortnight, and this man rooked the fellows of I don’t know how much at billiards. The old game, you know—nursing his man right up to the end, and then finishing with a burst. Of course, when that happens once or twice it may be an accident, but when a man who poses as a novice always manages by a really brilliant shot—”

===Chinese===
「不知道那地方，」哈盖特说。

「好地方，」吉米说。「里头净是演员、作家这路人。唯一的毛病是，有些人会结交些不三不四的朋友。」

哈盖特没有答话。他似乎毫无兴趣。

「是啊，」吉米接着说。「比方说，一年前，我的一个朋友——一个姓米夫林的演员——领了一个人进俱乐部，以会员宾客的名义待了两个礼拜，这家伙在台球桌上宰了那帮会友不知多少钱。老把戏了，你懂的——把肥羊一直养到末了，最后一口气猛收网。当然啦，这种事出个一回两回，兴许是意外；可一个装成生手的人，回回都能靠一杆漂亮绝伦的好球得手——」

===Original===
Hargate turned round.

“They fired this fellow out,” said Jimmy.

“Look here!”

“Yes?”

“What do you mean?”

===Chinese===
哈盖特转过了身。

「那家伙后来叫人轰出去了，」吉米说。

「我说你！」

「嗯？」

「你什么意思？」

===Original===
“It’s a dull yarn,” said Jimmy apologetically. “I’ve been boring you. By the way, Dreever asked me to square up with you for that game, in case he shouldn’t be back. Here you are.”

He held out an empty hand.

“Got it?”

“What are you going to do?” demanded Hargate.

===Chinese===
「讲起来是个乏味的故事，」吉米抱歉地说。「让你听腻了吧。对了，德利弗托我同你把那盘球的账结清，怕他一时半会儿回不来。喏，给。」

他伸出一只空手。

「拿到了？」

「你想干什么？」哈盖特厉声问道。

===Original===
“What am I going to do?” queried Jimmy.

“You know what I mean. If you’ll keep your mouth shut, and stand in, it’s halves. Is that what you’re after?”

Jimmy was delighted. He knew that by rights the proposal should have brought him from his seat, with stern, set face, to wreak vengeance for the insult, but on such occasions he was apt to ignore the conventions. His impulse, when he met a man whose code of behaviour was not the ordinary code, was to chat with him and to extract his point of view. He felt as little animus against Hargate as he had felt against Spike on the occasion of their first meeting.

===Chinese===
「我想干什么？」吉米反问。

「你懂我的意思。只要你闭紧嘴巴，再肯入伙，就五五分账。你图的是这个吧？」

吉米乐了。他明知按规矩，这种提议本该叫他霍地离座，绷起一张严霜面孔，为所受的侮辱痛施报复；可每逢这种场合，他偏偏爱把俗套抛在脑后。遇上一个行事不讲寻常规矩的人，他的本能就是同他攀谈，掏一掏他的心思。他对哈盖特殊无恶感，同他头一回撞见斯派克时如出一辙。

===Original===
“Do you make much at this sort of game?” he asked.

Hargate was relieved. This was businesslike.

“Pots,” he said, with some enthusiasm—“pots I tell you, if you’ll stand in—”

“Bit risky, isn’t it?”

“Not a bit of it. An occasional accident—”

===Chinese===
「你干这路买卖油水大么？」他问。

哈盖特松了口气。这才像谈生意。

「大把，」他颇来了些兴致——「告诉你，大把大把，只要你肯入伙——」

「有点儿冒险吧？」

「一点儿不冒。不过是偶有一桩意外——」

===Original===
“I suppose you’d call me one?”

Hargate grinned.

“It must be pretty tough work,” said Jimmy. “You must have to use a tremendous lot of self-restraint.”

Hargate sighed.

“That’s the worst of it,” he said—“the having to seem a mug at the game. I’ve been patronised sometimes by young fools who thought they were teaching me till I nearly forgot myself and showed them what real billiards was.”

===Chinese===
「我想，你口中的『意外』指的就是我吧？」

哈盖特咧嘴一笑。

「这行当想必相当熬人，」吉米说。「得用上多大的克制功夫啊。」

哈盖特叹了口气。

「苦就苦在这儿，」他说——「不得不在球桌上装冤大头。常有那么些傻小子摆出提携后进的架子，自以为在教我打球，教得我差点儿把持不住，让他们见识见识什么叫真台球。」

===Original===
“There’s always some drawback to the learned professions,” said Jimmy.

“But there’s a heap to make up for it in this one,” said Hargate. “Well, look here; is it a deal? You stand in—”

Jimmy shook his head.

“I guess not,” he said. “It’s good of you, but commercial speculation never was in my line. I’m afraid you must count me out of this.”

“What! You’re going to tell—”

===Chinese===
「干哪一行学问都有几分苦处，」吉米说。

「可这一行里头，补偿也多着呢，」哈盖特说。「怎么样，一句话：成交不成交？你入伙——」

吉米摇了摇头。

「我看就算了，」他说。「承你美意，只是商业投机从来不合我的脾胃。这一票，恐怕你只好把我划出去了。」

「什么！你要去说出去——」

===Original===
“No,” said Jimmy, “I’m not. I’m not a vigilance committee. I won’t tell a soul.”

“Why, then—” began Hargate, relieved.

“Unless, of course,” Jimmy went on, “you play billiards again while you’re here.”

“But, damn it, man! if I don’t, what’s the good? Look here, what am I to do if they ask me to play?”

“Give your wrist as an excuse.”

===Chinese===
「不，」吉米说。「我不去。我不是什么治安纠察队。我一个字也不会往外说。」

「那——既然这样，」哈盖特松了口气，刚要开口。

「当然喽，」吉米接着说，「除非你在这儿还上台球桌。」

「可他娘的，老兄！我要是不打，那还图个什么？我说，人家要是请我打，我怎么办？」

「拿你的手腕当托辞。」

===Original===
“My wrist?”

“Yes. You sprained it tomorrow after breakfast. It was bad luck. I wonder how you came to do it? You didn’t sprain it much, but just enough to stop you playing billiards.”

Hargate reflected.

“Understand?” said Jimmy.

===Chinese===
「我的手腕？」

「不错。你明天早饭后把它扭了。真是不走运。说来奇怪，你怎么就扭着了呢？扭得不算重，可偏偏就重到打不了台球。」

哈盖特想了想。

「明白了？」吉米说。

===Original===
“Oh, very well,” said Hargate sullenly. “But,” he burst out, “if I ever get a chance to get even with you—”

“You won’t,” said Jimmy. “Dismiss the rosy dream. Get even! You don’t know me! There’s not a flaw in my armour. I’m a sort of modern edition of the Stainless Knight. Tennyson drew Galahad from me. I move through life with almost a sickening absence of sin. But hush! We are observed—at least, we shall be in another minute—somebody is coming down the passage. You do understand, don’t you? Sprained wrist is the watchword.”

The handle turned. It was Lord Dreever, back again from his interview.

===Chinese===
「哦，再明白不过，」哈盖特悻悻地说。「可是，」他忍不住迸出来，「我要是有朝一日逮着机会找你算账——」

「你逮不着，」吉米说。「收起你那场美梦吧。找我算账！你根本不了解我！我这一身铠甲，寻不出一丝瑕隙。我是无瑕骑士的某种近代重印版。丁尼生（Tennyson）笔下的加拉哈德（Galahad），原型便是我。我行走世间，一身清白简直腻歪得叫人作呕。可是嘘！有人在看咱们——至少，再过一分钟就要看了——有人正顺着走廊过来。你总该明白了吧？口令就一个：扭了手腕。」

门柄转动了。进来的是德利弗勋爵，召见完了回来了。

===Original===
“Halloa, Dreever!” said Jimmy. “We’ve missed you. Hargate has been doing his best to amuse me with acrobatic tricks. But you’re too reckless, Hargate, old man. Mark my words, one of these days you’ll be spraining your wrist. You should be more careful. What, going? Good night. Pleasant fellow, Hargate,” he added, as the footsteps retreated down the passage. “Well, my lad, what’s the matter with you? You look depressed.”

Lord Dreever flung himself onto the lounge and groaned hollowly.

“Damn! Damn!! Damn!!!” he observed.

His glassy eye met Jimmy’s and wandered away again.

===Chinese===
「哈啰，德利弗！」吉米说。「你不在，我们可想你了。哈盖特方才使尽解数，拿杂耍把戏给我解闷。不过你也太不留神了，哈盖特，老哥。记住我的话，早晚有一天你要把手腕扭了的。你得多加小心。怎么，这就走？晚安。哈盖特这人挺可爱，」他补上一句，这时脚步声已沿走廊远去。「好了，我的老弟，你怎么啦？瞧你一脸的愁苦。」

德利弗勋爵一头扑进长椅，声如空瓮地呻吟起来。

「该死！该死！！该死！！！」他说。

他那双呆滞的眼睛撞上吉米的目光，又游移开去。

===Original===
“What on earth’s the matter?” demanded Jimmy. “You go out of here carolling like a songbird, and you come back moaning like a lost soul. What’s happened?”

“Give me a brandy and soda, Pitt, old man, there’s a good chap. I’m in a fearful hole.”

“Why? What’s the matter?”

“I’m engaged,” groaned his lordship.

===Chinese===
「究竟是怎么回事？」吉米追问。「你出门的时候，百灵鸟似的婉转歌唱；回屋的时候，孤魂野鬼似的哀声叹气。出了什么事？」

「给我来杯白兰地苏打，皮特，老哥，做个好人。我这一下掉进了可怕的大窟窿。」

「怎么？到底什么事？」

「我订婚了，」勋爵大人呻吟道。

===Original===
“Engaged? I wish you’d explain. What on earth’s wrong with you! Don’t you want to be engaged? What’s your—”

He broke off as a sudden, awful suspicion dawned upon him. “Who is she?” he cried.

He gripped the stricken peer’s shoulder and shook it savagely. Unfortunately he selected the precise moment when the latter was in the act of calming his quivering nerve centres with a gulp of brandy and soda, and for a space of some two minutes it seemed as if the engagement would be broken off by the premature extinction of the Dreever line. A long and painful fit of coughing, however, ended with his lordship still alive and on the road to recovery.

===Chinese===
「订婚了？你倒是给我说明白。你到底是什么毛病！订婚难道不好？你那——」

话到一半，一个突然而至的可怕疑云在他心头亮起。「她是谁？」他失声喊道。

他一把攥住这位罹难勋爵的肩头，狠狠摇撼。不巧的是，他挑的正是对方正用一大口白兰地苏打安抚颤抖的神经中枢的那一刻。约莫有两分钟光景，看那情形，这门亲事眼看要以德利弗一脉香火过早告绝的方式吹台了。所幸一阵漫长而痛苦的咳呛过后，勋爵大人尚在人世，且已走上康复之途。

===Original===
He eyed Jimmy reproachfully, but Jimmy was in no mood for apologies.

“Who is she?” he kept demanding. “What’s her name?”

“Might have killed me,” grumbled the convalescent.

“Who is she?”

===Chinese===
他眼含责备地瞟着吉米，可吉米毫无赔不是的心思。

「她是谁？」他只是一迭声地追问。「她叫什么？」

「差点儿要了我的命，」这位康复期病人咕哝道。

「她是谁？」

===Original===
“What? Why, Miss McEachern.”

Jimmy had known what the answer would be; but it was scarcely less of a shock for that reason.

“Miss McEachern?” he echoed.

Lord Dreever nodded a sombre nod.

===Chinese===
「什么？哦，麦克伊彻恩小姐呀。」

答案是什么，吉米早就料到了；可这丝毫没有减轻那一记震动。

「麦克伊彻恩小姐？」他复述了一遍。

德利弗勋爵阴郁地点了点头。

===Original===
“You’re engaged to her?”

Another sombre nod.

“I don’t believe it,” said Jimmy.

“I wish I didn’t,” said his lordship wistfully, ignoring the slight rudeness of the remark. “But worse luck, it’s true.”

===Chinese===
「你同她订了婚？」

又是一记阴郁的点头。

「我不信，」吉米说。

「我也巴不得不信，」勋爵大人怅然道，毫不理会这话里那点失礼。「可叹的是，偏偏是真的。」

===Original===
For the first time since the disclosure of the name Jimmy’s attention was directed to the remarkable demeanour of his successful rival.

“You don’t seem over-pleased,” he said.

“Pleased! Have a fiver each way on ‘pleased’! No, I’m not exactly leaping with joy.”

“Then what the devil is it all about? What do you mean? What’s the idea? If you don’t want to marry Miss McEachern, why did you propose to her?”

===Chinese===
自从那个名字揭出来，这还是头一遭，吉米的注意力转到了他那位得了手的情敌那副异乎寻常的做派上。

「你看上去高兴得有限，」他说。

「高兴！『高兴』这匹马，你尽管拿五镑去两头押注！不，我算不上欢天喜地。」

「那这到底算怎么一回事？你什么意思？打的什么算盘？你既然不想娶麦克伊彻恩小姐，干吗要去求婚？」

===Original===
Lord Dreever closed his eyes.

“Dear old boy, don’t. It’s my uncle.”

“Your uncle?”

“Didn’t I explain it all to you?—about him wanting me to marry? You know—I told you the whole thing.”

Jimmy stared at him in silence.

===Chinese===
德利弗勋爵闭上了双眼。

「亲爱的老哥，别问了。都怪我叔父。」

「你叔父？」

「我不是全都跟你讲过了么？——他要我娶亲的事？你记得的——来龙去脉我原原本本都跟你说过。」

吉米一言不发地瞪着他。

===Original===
“Do you mean to say—” he said slowly.

He stopped. It was a profanation to put the thing into words.

“What, old man?”

Jimmy gulped.

===Chinese===
「你的意思是说——」他一字一顿地说。

他住了口。这种事说出口，便是一种亵渎。

「什么呀，老哥？」

吉米喉头一哽。

===Original===
“Do you mean to say you want to marry Miss McEachern simply because she has money?” he said.

It was not the first time that he had heard of a case of a British peer marrying for such a reason, but it was the first time that the thing had filled him with horror. In some circumstances things come home more forcibly to us.

“It’s not me, old man,” murmured his lordship—“it’s my uncle.”

===Chinese===
「你的意思是说，你想娶麦克伊彻恩小姐，仅仅因为她有钱？」他说。

英国贵族为这等缘故成亲的事例，他并非头一回听说；可这件事头一回叫他满心惊骇。有些境况之下，事情贴到咱们自己身上来，感受便格外切肤。

「不是我，老哥，」勋爵大人喃喃道——「是我叔父。」

===Original===
“Your uncle? Good heavens!” Jimmy clenched his hands despairingly. “Do you mean to say that you let your uncle order you about in a thing like this? Do you mean to say you’re such a—such a—such a gelatine-backboned worm—”

“Old man, I say!” protested his lordship, wounded.

“I’d call you a wretched knock-kneed skunk, only I don’t want to be fulsome. I hate flattering a man to his face.”

Lord Dreever, deeply pained, half rose from his seat.

===Chinese===
「你叔父？我的天！」吉米绝望地攥紧了双手。「你的意思是说，这种大事你也任凭叔父呼来喝去？你的意思是说，你竟是这么一个——这么一个——这么一个脊梁骨拿果冻做的软体虫——」

「我说，老哥！」勋爵大人抗声道，大为受伤。

「我本想管你叫一声可怜巴巴的罗圈腿臭鼬，只是怕那样说得太甜。当面恭维人，我不干。」

德利弗勋爵痛心疾首，从座位上欠起了半个身子。

===Original===
“Don’t get up,” urged Jimmy smoothly; “I couldn’t trust myself.”

His lordship subsided hastily. He was feeling alarmed. He had never seen this side of Jimmy’s character. At first he had been merely aggrieved and disappointed. He had expected sympathy. Now the matter had become more serious. Jimmy was pacing the room like a young and hungry tiger. At present, it was true, there was a billiard table between them; but his lordship felt that he could have done with good, stout bars. He nestled in his seat with the earnest concentration of a limpet on a rock. It would be deuced bad form, of course, for Jimmy to assault his host, but could Jimmy be trusted to remember the niceties of etiquette?

“Why the deuce she accepted you I can’t think,” said Jimmy, half to himself, stopping suddenly and glaring across the table.

===Chinese===
「别起身，」吉米柔声相劝；「我怕我管不住自己。」

勋爵大人慌忙矮了回去。他心里发毛。吉米性格里的这一面，他从来没有见过。起初他不过是委屈、失望，原指望换得几分同情。如今事态严重起来了。吉米正像一头饿着肚子的幼虎，在屋里踱来踱去。不错，眼下两人之间还隔着一张台球桌；可勋爵大人觉得，纵然再添几根结实的粗栏杆也不嫌多余。他把自己深深蜷进座位，那份专注劲儿，活像钉在岩石上的一枚帽贝。客人殴打东道主，自然是失礼到家的行径；可吉米那家伙，敢指望他记得这些礼节的细枝末节么？

「她到底见了什么鬼才应了你，我实在想不出，」吉米半是对自己咕哝，猛地站定，隔着球桌瞪了过去。

===Original===
Lord Dreever felt relieved. This was not polite, perhaps, but at least it was not violent.

“That’s what beats me, too, old man,” he said. “Between you and me, it’s a jolly rum business. This afternoon—”

“What about this afternoon?”

“Why, she wouldn’t have me at any price.”

===Chinese===
德利弗勋爵松了口气。这话或许不算客气，可至少不算动武。

「这也正是叫我发懵的地方，老哥，」他说。「咱们关起门来说句体己话，这桩事古怪透了。今天下午——」

「今天下午怎么了？」

「嗐，她说什么也不肯要我。」

===Original===
“You asked her this afternoon?”

“Yes; and it was all right then. She refused me like a bird, wouldn’t hear of it, came pretty near laughing in my face; and then tonight,” he went on, his voice squeaky at the thought of his wrongs, “my uncle sends for me and says she’s changed her mind and is waiting for me in the morning room. I go there and she tells me in about three words that she’s been thinking it over and that the whole fearful thing is on again. I call it jolly rough on a chap. I felt such a frightful ass, you know, I didn’t know what to do—whether to kiss her, I mean—”

Jimmy snorted violently.

===Chinese===
「你今天下午向她求了婚？」

「是啊；那时候一切都正常。她一口回绝，干脆利落，听都不肯听，差点儿当面笑出声来；可到了今晚，」他接着说，一想到自己的冤屈，嗓音都发尖了，「我叔父把我叫了去，说她改了主意，正在起居室里等我。我过去，她三言两语便告诉我，她考虑过了，那桩天大的事又算数了。我说，这待一个老实人也忒狠了。我简直窘成个大傻瓜，你知道，我都不知道该怎么办——我是说，该不该吻她——」

吉米重重地哼了一声。

===Original===
“Eh?” said his lordship blankly.

“Go on,” said Jimmy, between his teeth.

“I felt a fearful fool, you know. I just said ‘Right-O!’ or something—dashed if I know what I did say—and legged it. It’s a jolly rum business, the whole thing. It isn’t as if she wanted me—I could see that with half an eye—she doesn’t care a hang for me. It’s my belief, old man,” he said solemnly, “that she’s been badgered into it. I believe my uncle’s been at her.”

===Chinese===
「咦？」勋爵大人茫然道。

「说下去，」吉米从牙缝里挤出来。

「我窘得够呛，你知道。我就说了句『行嘞！』什么的——天晓得我当时到底说了什么——撒腿就溜了。整个这件事透着古怪。她又不是想要我——这一点我一眼就看了出来——她压根儿不把我放在心上。老哥，」他一本正经地说，「依我看，她是被人死缠硬磨，磨得没奈何才应下的。我疑心是我叔父去磨的她。」

===Original===
Jimmy laughed shortly.

“My dear man, you seem to think your uncle’s persuasive influence is universal. I guess it’s confined to you.”

“Well, anyhow, I believe that’s what’s happened. What do you say?”

“Why say anything? There doesn’t seem to be much need.”

===Chinese===
吉米干笑了一声。

「我亲爱的老兄，你似乎把你叔父的游说神通当成四海皆准了。我看它只在你一个人身上灵验。」

「反正，我认定事情就是这么回事。你说呢？」

「还有什么可说的？看来不必多此一举。」

===Original===
He poured some brandy into a glass and added a little soda.

“You take it pretty stiff,” observed his lordship, with a touch of envy.

“On occasions,” said Jimmy, emptying his glass.

===Chinese===
他往玻璃杯里倒了些白兰地，又添了一点苏打水。

「你这杯调得可真冲，」勋爵大人说，话里带着一丝艳羡。

「偶尔为之，」吉米说着，把杯中酒一干而尽。

## XVIII The Lochinvar Method / 洛钦瓦尔之法

===Original===
As Jimmy sat smoking a last cigarette in his bedroom before going to bed that night Spike Mullins came in. Jimmy had been thinking things over. He was one of those men who are at their best in a losing game. Imminent disaster always had the effect of keying him up and putting an edge on his mind. The news he had heard that night had left him with undiminished determination, but conscious that a change of method would be needed. He must stake all on a single throw now. Young Lochinvar rather than Romeo must be his model. He declined to believe himself incapable of getting anything that he wanted as badly as he wanted Molly. He also declined to believe that she was really attached to Lord Dreever. He suspected the hand of McEachern in the affair, though the suspicion did not clear up the mystery by any means. Molly was a girl of character, not a feminine counterpart of his lordship, content meekly to do what she was told in a matter of this kind. The whole thing puzzled him.

"Well, Spike?" he said.

He was not too pleased at the interruption. He was thinking, and he wanted to be alone.

===Chinese===
那天夜里，吉米在卧室里就寝前抽着临睡的最后一支香烟，斯派克·马林斯走了进来。吉米正把种种心事翻来覆去地掂量。他是那样一种人：牌局越输，越见精神。大祸临头总能把他拧紧一道发条，叫脑子磨出锋刃来。当晚听到的消息并没有折损他的决心分毫，只是叫他明白：法子非换不可了。如今他必须孤注一掷，把一切押在一次投掷上。他要奉为楷模的，不该是罗密欧，而该是年轻的洛钦瓦尔（Lochinvar）。他绝不肯相信，凡是让他像渴望莫莉这般渴望的东西，自己竟会到不了手；他也绝不肯相信她当真心系德利弗勋爵。他疑心这桩事里有麦克伊彻恩的手笔，尽管这疑心丝毫无助于解开谜团。莫莉是个有主见的姑娘，并不是勋爵大人第二的女性翻版，会在这等事情上人家说什么便乖乖照办。这整桩事叫他大惑不解。

「怎么，斯派克？」他说。

他对这一打搅不很痛快。他正想着心事，想独自清静。

===Original===
Something appeared to have disturbed Spike. His bearing was excited.

"Say, boss! Guess what. You know dat guy dat come dis afternoon?—de guy from de village, dat came wit old man McEachern."

"Galer?" said Jimmy. "What about him?"

There had been an addition to the guests at the castle that afternoon. Mr. McEachern, walking in the village, had happened upon an old New York acquaintance of his, who, touring England, had reached Dreever and was anxious to see the historic castle. Mr. McEachern had brought him thither, introduced him to Sir Thomas, and now Mr. Samuel Galer was occupying a room on the same floor as Jimmy's. He had appeared at dinner that night, a short, wooden-faced man, with no more conversation than Hargate. Jimmy had not paid any particular attention to him.

===Chinese===
看来有什么事搅得斯派克不宁。他一副兴奋的样子。

「喂，老大！你猜怎么着。你知道今儿下午来的那个家伙不？——跟麦克伊彻恩老头儿一道来的，村里来的那位。」

「盖勒？」吉米说。「他怎么了？」

当天下午，城堡的客人名册上添了一员。麦克伊彻恩先生在村里散步，撞见一位纽约旧相识：此人游历英国，行抵德里弗，急欲一睹这座历史悠久的名堡。麦克伊彻恩先生便把他领了来，引见给托马斯爵士；于是眼下塞缪尔·盖勒先生便下榻在跟吉米同一层楼的房间。当晚席上他露了面：矮个子，一张木头脸，话不比哈盖特多一句。吉米没有特别留意他。

===Original===
"What about him?" he said.

"He's a 'sleut,' boss."

"A what?"

"A 'sleut.' "

===Chinese===
「他怎么了？」他说。

「他是个『暗探』，老大。」

「一个什么？」

「一个『暗探』。」

===Original===
"A detective?"

"Dat's right. A fly cop."

"What makes you think that?"

===Chinese===
「侦探？」

「没错儿。一个精得很的便衣。」

「你凭什么这么想？」

===Original===
"T'ink! Why, I can tell dem by deir eyes and deir feet, and de whole of dem. I could pick out a fly cop from a bunch of a t'ousand. He's sure a 'nough 'sleut' all right, all right. I seen him rubbering at you, boss."

"At me! Why at me? Why, of course, I see now. Our friend McEachern has got him in to spy on us."

"Dat's right, boss."

"Of course you may be mistaken."

"Not me, boss. And, say, he ain't de only one."

"What, more detectives? They'll have to put up 'House Full' boards at this rate. Who's the other?"

===Chinese===
「凭着想！哎呀，凭他们的眼睛、他们的脚，还有他们浑身上下，我一瞧便知。便衣就是埋进一千个人堆里，我也挑得出他来。他保准是个『暗探』，错不了，错不了。我亲眼瞧见他冲你直伸脖子张望呢，老大。」

「张望我！干吗张望我？哎，可不是，这下我明白了。咱们那位麦克伊彻恩朋友把他弄进来，监视咱们来了。」

「一点不错，老大。」

「当然，你也兴许看走了眼。」

「看走眼的绝不会是我，老大。还有，喂，他可不是独一个。」

「什么，还有侦探？照这个势头，他们非得挂出『客满』的牌子来不可了。另一个是谁？」

===Original===
"A mug what's down in de soivants' hall. I wasn't so sure of him at foist, but now I'm onto his curves. He's a sleut all right. He's vally to Sir Tummas, dis second mug is; but he ain't no vally! He's come to see no one don't get busy wit de jools. Say, what do youse t'ink of dem jools, boss?"

"Finest I ever saw."

"Yes, dat's right. A hundred thousand plunks dey set him back. Dey're de limit, ain't dey? Say, won't you really—"

"Spike, I'm surprised at you! Do you know you're getting a regular Mephistopheles, Spike? Suppose I hadn't an iron will, what would happen? You really must select your subjects of conversation more carefully. You're bad company for the likes of me."

===Chinese===
「一个待在下房仆人厅里的家伙。头里我还不敢把他看死，这会儿可摸透他的路数了。他是个暗探，错不了。这第二号家伙是汤玛斯爵士的贴身跟班；可他才不是什么跟班！他是来盯着，不叫哪个人朝那批珠宝下手的。喂，你觉得那批珠宝怎么样，老大？」

「我平生所见最好的一批。」

「可不是嘛。花了他十万块大洋呢。真是绝世的宝贝，是不是？喂，你当真不——」

「斯派克，你真叫我吃惊！你可知道，你快变成一个地地道道的梅菲斯特（Mephistopheles）了，斯派克？假若我没有一副铁打的意志，会出什么样的事？你说话真得挑拣挑拣题目才是。对我这路人来说，你可是个危险的伴儿。」

===Original===
Spike shuffled despondently.

"But, boss—"

Jimmy shook his head.

"It can't be done, my lad."

"But it can, boss," protested Spike. "It's dead easy. I've been up to de room, and I seen de box what de jools is kept in. Why, it's de softest ever! We could get dem as easy as pullin' de plug out of a bottle. Why, say, dere's never been such a peach of a place for gettin' hold of de stuff as dis house. Dat's right, boss. Why, look what I got dis afternoon, just snoopin' round and not really trying to get busy at all. It was just lying about."

===Chinese===
斯派克垂头丧气地蹭着脚步。

「可是，老大——」

吉米摇了摇头。

「不能干，小伙子。」

「可它干得成啊，老大，」斯派克抗议道。「容易得要死。我上那屋里去过了，瞧见了装珠宝的那口匣子。哎呀，这份松快劲儿，打头一份！咱们要得手，容易得跟拔瓶塞一样。哎呀喂，要说到拿货的宝地，打有这行当以来就没见过第二家，就是这座宅子。真格的，老大。你瞧瞧我今儿下午弄着的东西——我不过四处溜达探探路，压根儿没真打算动手。它就那么白白搁在那儿。」

===Original===
He plunged his hand into his pocket and drew it out again. As he unclosed his fingers Jimmy caught the gleam of precious stones.

"What the—" he gasped.

Spike was looking at his treasure-trove with an air of affectionate proprietorship.

"Where on earth did you get those?" asked Jimmy.

"Out of one of de rooms. Dey belonged to one of de loidies. It was de easiest old t'ing ever, boss. I just went in when dere was nobody around, and dere dey was on de toible. I never butted into anyt'ing so soft."

===Chinese===
他把手往口袋里一插，又抽了出来。他手指一张开，吉米瞥见了宝石的闪光。

「这是——」他倒抽一口冷气。

斯派克端详着这批意外之财，一副深情脉脉的小主人神气。

「你到底打哪儿弄来这些的？」吉米问。

「打一间屋里拿的。原是哪位太太小姐的东西。再省事不过的老买卖了，老大。我拣四下没人的当口走进去，瞧，它们就在桌上摆着。我这辈子没撞见过这么松快的事。」

===Original===
"Spike!"

"Yes, boss."

"Do you remember the room you took them from?"

"Sure. It was de foist on de—"

"Then just listen to me for a moment, my bright boy. When we're at breakfast tomorrow you want to go to that room and put those things back—all of them, mind you—just where you found them. Do you understand?"

Spike's jaw had fallen.

===Chinese===
「斯派克！」

「哎，老大。」

「你还记得自己是打哪间屋里拿的吗？」

「记得。是头一间——」

「那就好生听我说一会儿，我的机灵鬼。明儿咱们吃早饭的当口，你要上那间屋去，把那些东西放回去——全部，听清楚了——一件一件，搁回你原来拿它们的地方。明白了吗？」

斯派克的下巴颏儿耷拉了下来。

===Original===
"Put dem back, boss?" he faltered.

"Every single one of them."

"Boss!" said Spike plaintively.

"Remember—every single one of them, just where it belongs. See?"

"Very well, boss."

The dejection in his voice would have moved the sternest to pity. Gloom had enveloped Spike's spirit. The sunlight had gone out of his life.

===Chinese===
「放回去，老大？」他结结巴巴地说。

「一件一件，全都放。」

「老大！」斯派克好不委屈地说。

「记住——每一件都放，搁回原处。听见没有？」

「好吧，老大。」

他话音里的沮丧，足以叫铁面人也动恻隐之心。阴云裹住了斯派克的心灵。阳光从他生活里熄灭了。

===Original===
It had also gone out of the lives of a good many other people at the castle. This was mainly due to the growing shadow of the day of the theatricals.

For pure discomfort there are few things in the world that can compete with the final rehearsals of an amateur theatrical performance at a country house. Every day the atmosphere becomes more heavily charged with restlessness and depression. The producer of the piece, especially if he is also the author of it, develops a sort of intermittent insanity. He plucks at his moustache, if he has one; at his hair, if he has not. He mutters to himself. He gives vent to occasional despairing cries. The soothing suavity which marked his demeanour in the earlier rehearsals disappears.

He no longer says with a winning smile, "Splendid, old man, splendid! Couldn't be better. But I think we'll take that over just once more, if you don't mind." Instead, he rolls his eyes and snaps out, "Once more, please. This'll never do. At this rate we might just as well cut out the show altogether. What's that? No, it won't be all right on the night! Now, then, once more; and do pull yourselves together this time." After which the scene is sulkily resumed; and conversation, when the parties concerned meet subsequently, is cold and strained.

Matters had reached this stage at the castle. Everybody was thoroughly tired of the piece, and, but for the thought of the disappointment which (presumably) would rack the neighbouring nobility and gentry if it were not to be produced, would have resigned their places without a twinge of regret. People who had schemed to get the best and longest parts were wishing now that they had been content with First Footman or Giles, a villager.

===Chinese===
在那座城堡里，阳光也从好多旁人的生活里熄灭了。这主要是演剧之日的阴影步步进逼所致。

若论纯粹的苦楚，世上少有什么东西能同乡下宅邸里业余演出的末几场排练一争高下。日复一日，空气里的烦躁与低沉越积越厚。那出戏的导演——尤其若他还是编剧——会染上一种间歇性的癫狂。他揪自己的小胡子，若有胡子的话；揪自己的头发，若没有胡子的话。他喃喃自语。他不时迸出一两声绝望的哀鸣。早先排练时他举止间那副抚慰人的温和文雅，消失得无影无踪。

他不再满面春风、笑容可掬地说：「好极了，老哥，好极了！不能再好了。不过这一段，你若不介意，咱们再来一遍。」取而代之的是翻着白眼，劈头喝道：「再来一遍。这样根本不行。照这个排法，咱们索性把整出戏砍了拉倒。你说什么？不，到正式演出那晚它顺不了！来，再来一遍；这回可务必打起精神。」话音一落，那一场戏便没好气地从头再来；而有关人等日后狭路相逢时，谈话总是冷淡而僵硬。

城堡里的事态正是发展到了这般田地。人人都对那出戏腻味透了；若不是想到一旦停演，四邻的贵族乡绅（想必）会何等痛心疾首，人人都巴不得毫无悔意地撂挑子。当初机关算尽要抢分量最重、台词最长的角色的人，如今只恨自己当初不曾安分，演个一号男仆，或是贾尔斯（Giles）——一个村民。

===Original===
"I'll never run an amateur show again as long as I live," confided Charteris to Jimmy, almost tearfully. "It's not good enough. Most of them aren't word-perfect yet."

"It'll be all right—"

"Oh, don't say it'll be all right on the night."

"I wasn't going to," said Jimmy. "I was going to say it'll be all right after the night. People will soon forget how badly the thing went."

"You're a nice, comforting sort of man, aren't you?" said Charteris.

"Why worry?" said Jimmy. "If you go on like this, it'll be Westminster Abbey for you in your prime. You'll be getting brain fever."

===Chinese===
「我这辈子再不办业余演剧了，」查特里斯几乎带着哭音向吉米吐露心曲。「实在不像话。大多数人到现在台词还背不熟。」

「会顺当的——」

「哦，千万别来『到那一晚自然会顺』那一套。」

「我没打算说那个，」吉米说。「我打算说的是：过了那一晚自然就顺了。人们很快就会忘掉这出戏演得有多糟。」

「你可真是个会安慰人的大好人，是不是？」查特里斯说。

「愁什么呢？」吉米说。「你再这么下去，正当盛年就得给送进威斯敏斯特教堂了。你这是要闹脑膜炎的先兆。」

===Original===
Jimmy himself was one of the few who were feeling reasonably cheerful. He was deriving a keen amusement at present from the manoeuvres of Mr. Samuel Galer, of New York. This lynx-eyed man, having been instructed by Mr. McEachern to watch Jimmy, was doing so with a thoroughness which would have aroused the suspicions of a babe. If Jimmy went to the billiard room after dinner, Mr. Galer was there to keep him company. If, during the course of the day, he had occasion to fetch a handkerchief or a cigarette case from his bedroom, he was sure, on emerging, to stumble upon Mr. Galer in the corridor. The employees of Dodson's Private Inquiry Agency believed in earning their salaries.

Occasionally, after these encounters, Jimmy would come upon Sir Thomas Blunt's valet, the other man in whom Spike's trained eye had discerned the distinguishing marks of the sleuth. He was usually somewhere round the corner at these moments, and when collided with apologised with great politeness. Jimmy decided that he must have come under suspicion in this case vicariously, through Spike. Spike, in the servants' hall, would, of course, stand out conspicuously enough to catch the eye of a detective on the look out for sin among the servants; and he himself, as Spike's employer, had been marked down as a possible confederate.

It tickled him to think that both these giant brains should be so greatly exercised on his account.

===Chinese===
吉米自己却是少数几个心情还算舒畅的人。眼下他正从纽约来的塞缪尔·盖勒先生的种种战术里获得莫大的乐趣。这位眼利如猞猁的人物，既奉了麦克伊彻恩先生之命盯吉米的梢，便监视得滴水不漏，那份彻底劲儿足以叫吃奶的娃娃都起疑心。晚饭后吉米若去台球室，盖勒先生准在场奉陪。白天里他若需要回卧室取一块手帕或一只香烟盒，出门时保险会在走廊里撞见盖勒先生。多德森私家侦探社的雇员们笃信一条：薪水一分不能白拿。

偶尔，这样的遭遇战过后，吉米还会撞见托马斯·布伦特爵士的贴身男仆——斯派克那双受过训练的眼睛认出侦探标志性印记的另一个人。这些时候他多半正在哪个拐角附近，撞上了便满有礼貌地赔不是。吉米断定，自己在这桩案子里受怀疑，必是托斯派克之福，连带遭的殃。斯派克在下房仆人厅里，自然惹眼得很，足够叫一个专在仆役堆里搜寻罪案的侦探一眼盯上；而他本人，作为斯派克的东家，也就被圈定为可能的同伙。

想到这两颗巨脑都在为他一个人如此绞尽脑汁，他乐得心痒。

===Original===
He had been watching Molly closely during these days. So far no announcement of the engagement had been made. It struck him that possibly it was being reserved for public mention on the night of the theatricals. The whole county would be at the castle then. There could be no more fitting moment. He sounded Lord Dreever, and the latter said moodily that he was probably right.

"There's going to be a dance of sorts after the show," he said, "and it'll be done then, I suppose. No getting out of it after that—it'll be all over the county. Trust my uncle for that! He'll get on a table and shout it, shouldn't wonder, and it'll be in the Morning Post next day and Katie'll see it. Only two days more! Oh, Lord!"

Jimmy deduced that Katie was the Savoy girl, concerning whom his lordship had vouchsafed no particulars save that she was a ripper and hadn't a penny.

Only two days! Like the Battle of Waterloo, it was going to be a close-run affair. More than ever now he realised how much she meant to him, and there were moments when it seemed to him that she, too, had begun to understand. That night on the terrace seemed somehow to have changed their relationship. He thought he had got closer to her—they were in touch. Before she had been frank, cheerful, unembarrassed; now he noticed a constraint in her manner, a curious shyness. There was a barrier between them, but it was not the old barrier. He had ceased to be one of a crowd.

But it was a race against time. The first day slipped by, a blank, and the second, till now it was but a matter of hours. The last afternoon had come.

===Chinese===
这些天他一直密切留意着莫莉。订婚的消息至今没有公布。他隐隐觉得，这消息说不定正被留着，要等演剧那晚才当众宣布。到那时全郡的人都会聚在城堡。再挑不出更合适的时机了。他去探德利弗勋爵的口风，后者闷闷不乐地说，他多半猜得不错。

「演出之后还有个凑合事的舞会，」他说，「我想就在那时候办了。过后再想躲，躲不成了——全郡都得传遍。这事有我叔父在，保管万无一失！他没准儿还会跳到桌子上大声嚷嚷呢，第二天就登上《晨邮报》（Morning Post），凯蒂也就看见了。只剩两天了！哦，天哪！」

吉米由此断定，凯蒂就是萨伏伊那位姑娘——关于她，勋爵大人不曾吐露半点细节，只说她是顶呱呱的好姑娘，身无分文。

只剩两天了！这一仗将像滑铁卢之战一样，胜负只争毫厘。如今他前所未有地明白她于他意味着什么；而且有些时刻，他觉得她似乎也开始懂了。露台上的那一夜，不知怎么似乎改变了两人之间的关系。他觉得自己同她贴近了——两人有了心照不宣的感应。从前她坦率、爽朗、落落大方；如今他察觉她举止间有一种拘谨，一种说不出的羞怯。两人之间隔着一道屏障，却已不是原先那道。他不再是芸芸众生中的一个了。

然而这是一场同时间的赛跑。第一天溜走了，白纸一张；第二天也溜走了，直到如今，剩下的不过几个钟头。最后一个下午到了。

===Original===
Not even Mr. Samuel Galer, of Dodson's Private Inquiry Agency, could have kept a more unflagging watch than did Jimmy during those hours. There was no rehearsal that afternoon, and the members of the company, in various stages of nervous collapse, strayed distractedly about the grounds. First one, then another, would seize upon Molly, while Jimmy, watching from afar, cursed their pertinacity.

At last she wandered off alone, and Jimmy quitting his ambush, followed.

She walked in the direction of the lake. It had been a terribly hot, oppressive afternoon. There was thunder in the air. Through the trees the lake glistened invitingly.

===Chinese===
在那几个钟头里，就连多德森私家侦探社的塞缪尔·盖勒先生，也监不出比吉米更不懈的梢。那天下午没有排练，剧组成员们神经崩溃的程度不等，心慌意乱地在庭园里游荡。先是一个，接着又一个，总有人逮住莫莉不放；吉米远远看着，把他们这份死缠不放咒了个遍。

终于她独自一人走开了。吉米撤出埋伏，跟了上去。

她朝湖的方向走去。这个下午闷热得厉害，压得人喘不过气。空气里憋着一场雷雨。湖水穿过树影闪闪发光，引人入胜。

===Original===
She was standing at the water's edge when Jimmy came up. Her back was turned. She was rocking with her foot a Canadian canoe that lay alongside the bank. She started as he spoke. His feet on the soft turf had made no sound.

"Can I take you out on the lake?" he said.

She did not answer for a moment. She was plainly confused.

"I'm sorry," she said. "I—I'm waiting for Lord Dreever."

===Chinese===
吉米走近时，她正站在水边。背朝着他。她用脚摇着一艘加拿大式独木舟，小舟就傍在岸边。他一开口，她吓了一跳。他的脚踩在软软的草皮上，不曾出一点声息。

「我陪你上湖上划划，好吗？」他说。

她一时没有答话。她分明是心乱了。

「对不起，」她说。「我——我在等德利弗勋爵。」

===Original===
Jimmy saw that she was nervous. There was tension in the air. She was looking away from him, out across the lake, and her face was flushed.

"Won't you?" he said.

"I'm sorry," she said again.

===Chinese===
吉米看出她紧张。空气里绷着一根弦。她掉开目光，望着湖的对岸，脸上飞着红。

「不行吗？」他说。

「对不起，」她又说了一遍。

===Original===
Jimmy looked over his shoulder. Down the lower terrace was approaching the long form of his lordship. He walked with pensive jerkiness, not as one hurrying to a welcome tryst. As Jimmy looked up he vanished behind the great clump of laurels which stood on the lowest terrace. In another minute he would appear round them.

Gently, but with extreme despatch, Jimmy placed a hand on either side of Molly's waist. The next moment he had swung her off her feet and lowered her carefully onto the cushions in the bow of the canoe.

Then, jumping in himself with a force that made the boat rock, he loosed the mooring rope, seized the paddle and pushed off.

===Chinese===
吉米回头一望。下层露台那头，勋爵大人长长的身影正走过来。他走得像满腹心事，一颠一颠，全不像赶赴一桩称心幽会的人。吉米抬眼望时，他已隐没在最低一层露台上那一大丛月桂树后面。再过一分钟，他就要绕过树丛出现了。

吉米轻轻的，却又干净利落之至，两手往莫莉腰际两侧一分。下一刻，他已把她整个人带离了地面，稳稳当当安放进船头的坐垫里。

随后他自己纵身跳进船里，那一跳震得小船直晃；他解开缆绳，一把抓起桨，撑离了岸。

## XIX On the Lake / 湖上

===Original===
In making love, as in every other branch of life, consistency is the quality most to be aimed at. To hedge is fatal. A man must choose the line of action which he judges to be best suited to his temperament, and hold to it without deviation. If Lochinvar snatches the maiden up onto his saddlebow he must continue in that vein. He must not fancy that, having accomplished the feat, he can resume the episode on lines of devotional humility. Prehistoric man, who conducted his courtship with a club, never fell into the error of apologising when his bride complained of headache.

Jimmy did not apologise. The idea did not enter his mind. He was feeling prehistoric. His heart was beating fast and his mind was in a whirl, but the one definite thought that came to him during the first few seconds of the journey was that he ought to have done this earlier. This was the right way—pick her up and carry her off and leave uncles and fathers and butter-haired peers of the realm to look after themselves. This was the way—alone together in their own little world of water with nobody to interrupt and nobody to overhear. He should have done it before. He had wasted precious, golden time hanging about while futile men chatted to her of things that could not possibly be of interest. But he had done the right thing at last—he had got her. She must listen to him now. She could not help listening. They were the only inhabitants of this new world.

He looked back over his shoulder at the world they had left. The last of the Dreevers had rounded the clump of laurels, and was standing at the edge of the water, gazing perplexedly after the retreating canoe.

===Chinese===
