"I see it," he wheezed. "You're having a joke with me! So this is what you were hatching as I came downstairs! Don't tell me! If you had really thrown him over you wouldn't have been laughing together like that. It's no good, my dear. I might have been taken in if I had not seen you, but I did."

===Chinese===
「嗯？」托马斯爵士说。

「我反复想过了，得出的结论是，我们不——唉，我把婚约解除了。」

托马斯爵士那双一贯凸出的眼睛又朝外凸出了一截。他那红润脸膛的颜色又深了几分。忽然，他咯咯笑了起来。

莫莉惊愕地望着他。托马斯爵士今晚的举止可真是一反常态。

「我明白了，」他呼哧呼哧地说。「你在跟我打趣呢！怪不得我下楼时瞧见你们俩在密谋这个！不用说了！你若真把他甩了，你们才不会那样笑作一团呢。没用的，亲爱的。若不是我亲眼看见，没准儿真叫你们蒙混过去，可我看见了。」

===Original===
"No, no," cried Molly. "You're wrong—you're quite wrong. When you saw us we were just agreeing that we should be very good friends—that was all. I broke off the engagement before that. I—"

She was aware that his lordship had emitted a hollow croak, but she took it as his method of endorsing her statement—not as a warning.

"I wrote Lord Dreever a note this evening," she went on, "telling him that I couldn't possibly—"

She broke off in alarm. With the beginning of her last speech Sir Thomas had begun to swell, until now he looked as if he were in imminent danger of bursting. His face was purple. To Molly's lively imagination his eyes appeared to move slowly out of his head, like a snail's. From the back of his throat came strange noises.

"S-s-so—" he stammered.

===Chinese===
「不，不，」莫莉喊道。「您错了——您大错特错了。您看见我们的时候，我们不过刚说定要做很要好的朋友——仅此而已。在那之前我就解除了婚约。我——」

她听见勋爵大人发出一声空洞的哑叫，却只当那是他在为自己的话作证，没当成警告。

「今晚我写了张便条给德利弗勋爵，」她接着说，「告诉他我实在不能——」

她惊觉不妙，话头猛地刹住。她最后这几句话才起了个头，托马斯爵士就开始膨胀起来，到这会儿，看上去随时都有炸开的危险。他的脸憋成了紫色。在莫莉活跃的想象里，他那双眼珠子仿佛正缓缓地往外挪，活像蜗牛的眼柄。他的喉咙深处发出种种怪响。

「这——这——这么说——」他结结巴巴。

===Original===
He gulped and tried again.

"So this," he said, "so this—so that was what was in that letter, eh?"

Lord Dreever smiled weakly.

"Eh?" yelled Sir Thomas.

His lordship started convulsively.

===Chinese===
他咽了口唾沫，又试了一回。

「这么说，」他说，「这么说——那封信里写的就是这个，嗯？」

德利弗勋爵露出一个绵软无力的微笑。

「嗯？！」托马斯爵士吼道。

勋爵大人惊得浑身一抽。

===Original===
"Er—yes," he said. "Yes, yes—that was it, don't you know!"

Sir Thomas eyed him with a baleful stare. Molly looked from one to the other in bewilderment.

There was a pause, during which Sir Thomas seemed partially to recover command of himself. Doubts as to the propriety of a family row in mid-stairs appeared to occur to him. He moved forward.

"Come with me," he said, with awful curtness.

His lordship followed bonelessly. Molly watched them go, and wondered more than ever. There was something behind this. It was not merely the breaking off of the engagement that had roused Sir Thomas. He was not a just man, but he was just enough to be able to see that the blame was not Lord Dreever's. There had been something more. She was puzzled.

===Chinese===
「呃——是，」他说。「是，是——就是这么回事，您是知道的！」

托马斯爵士恶狠狠地瞪着他。莫莉茫然地看看这个，又看看那个。

停顿了片刻，托马斯爵士似乎勉强收回了几分自制。他似乎想起，在楼梯当中大闹一场家庭风波，体统上恐怕欠妥。他向前走去。

「跟我来，」他说，口气简短得可怕。

勋爵大人骨头散了架似的跟在后头。莫莉目送两人走远，心里越发纳闷。这里头另有文章。叫托马斯爵士动怒的，绝不只是解除婚约这一桩。他不是个公正的人，可也不至于糊涂到看不出过错不在德利弗勋爵身上。必有别的缘故。她给弄糊涂了。

===Original===
In the hall Saunders was standing, weapon in hand, about to beat the gong.

"Not yet!" snapped Sir Thomas. "Wait!"

Dinner had been ordered especially early that night because of the theatricals. The necessity for strict punctuality had been straitly enjoined upon Saunders. At some inconvenience he had ensured strict punctuality. And now—But we all have our cross to bear in this world. Saunders bowed with dignified resignation.

Sir Thomas led the way into his study.

===Chinese===
大厅里，桑德斯正兵刃在手，预备擂响开饭的锣。

「先别敲！」托马斯爵士厉声道。「等着！」

那晚的晚饭特意吩咐提前，为的是赶上业余演剧。严守时刻之必要，已严词叮嘱给桑德斯。他费了好一番周折，方才确保了严守时刻。可如今——然而世人在这世上，各有各的十字架要背。桑德斯庄重而听天由命地鞠了一躬。

托马斯爵士头前带路，进了书房。
===Original===
"Be so good as to close the door," he said.

His lordship was so good.

Sir Thomas backed to the mantelpiece and stood there in the attitude which for generations has been sacred to the elderly Briton—feet well apart, hands clasped beneath his coat tails. His stare raked Lord Dreever like a searchlight.

"Now, sir!" he said.

His lordship wilted before his gaze.

===Chinese===
「劳您的好，把门关上，」他说。

勋爵大人十足劳了这声好。

托马斯爵士退到壁炉台前，摆出了世世代代为年长的不列颠人所奉为神圣的那副姿态——双脚远远岔开，双手在燕尾服后摆底下交握。他的目光像探照灯似的把德利弗勋爵来回扫射。

「好了，先生！」他说。

勋爵大人在他的注视下蔫了下去。

===Original===
"The fact is, uncle—"

"Never mind the facts. I know them! What I require is an explanation."

He spread his feet farther apart. The years had rolled back, and he was plain Thomas Blunt again, of Blunt's Stores, dealing with an erring employee.

"You know what I mean," he went on. "I am not referring to the breaking off of the engagement. What I insist upon learning is your reason for failing to inform me earlier of the contents of that letter."

===Chinese===
「实际情况是，叔父——」

「事实不必讲了。我全知道！我要的是解释。」

他把双脚又岔开了些。岁月倒卷回去，他又变回了那个布伦特百货商店的托马斯·布伦特，正在处置一个犯了过错的店员。

「你明白我的意思，」他接着说。「我不是指解除婚约这件事。我坚决要弄明白的是：那封信的内容，你为何不及早禀告于我。」

===Original===
His lordship said that somehow, don't you know, there didn't seem to be a chance, you know. He had several times been on the point—but—well, somehow—Well, that's how it was.

"No chance?" cried Sir Thomas. "Indeed! Why did you require that money I gave you?"

"Oh—er—I wanted it for something."

"Very possibly. For what?"

"I—the fact is, I owed it to a fellow."

===Chinese===
勋爵大人说，不知怎么搞的，您是知道的，好像总也找不着机会，您知道。他有几回话都到了嘴边——可是——唉，不知怎么搞的——唉，就是这么回事。

「找不着机会？」托马斯爵士喝道。「是嘛！那你方才要我给你那笔钱做什么？」

「哦——呃——我有笔用处。」

「很可能。什么用处？」

「我——实情是，我欠了人家一笔钱。」

===Original===
"Ha! How did you come to owe it?"

His lordship shuffled.

"You have been gambling," boomed Sir Thomas. "Am I right?"

"No, no! I say, no, no. It wasn't gambling—it was a game of skill. We were playing piquet."

"Kindly refrain from quibbling. You lost this money at cards, then, as I supposed. Just so."

===Chinese===
「哈！你怎么欠上的？」

勋爵大人支支吾吾。

「你赌博了，」托马斯爵士声若洪钟。「我说得对不对？」

「不，不！我说，不，不。那不是赌博——是比技巧的游戏。我们打的是皮克牌。」

「请不要强词夺理。那么，你这钱是打牌输掉的，跟我料想的一样。正是如此。」

===Original===
He widened the space between his feet. He intensified his glare. He might have been posing to an illustrator of The Pilgrim's Progress for a picture of "Apollyon straddling right across the way."

"So," he said, "you deliberately concealed from me the contents of that letter, in order that you might extract money from me under false pretences? Don't speak!" (his lordship had gurgled). "You did! Your behaviour was that of a—of a—"

There was a very fair selection of evildoers in all branches of business from which to choose. He gave the preference to the racetrack.

"Of a common welsher," he concluded. "But I won't put up with it. No; not for an instant. I insist upon you returning that money to me here and now. If you have not got it with you, go and fetch it."

His lordship's face betrayed the deepest consternation. He had been prepared for much, but not for this. That he would have to undergo what, in his schooldays, he would have called a "jaw" was inevitable, and he had been ready to go through with it. It might hurt his feelings, possibly, but it would leave his purse intact. A ghastly development of this kind he had not foreseen.

===Chinese===
他把两脚之间的距离又放宽了些。他把瞪视又加了几分火力。他这样子，简直像在给《天路历程》（The Pilgrim's Progress）的插图画家摆姿势，摆的是「亚玻伦（Apollyon）横身挡在路上」那一幅。

「这么说，」他说，「你是故意瞒下那封信的内容，好打着假幌子从我这里骗取钱财？不许说话！」（勋爵大人方才咕哝了一声。）「你就是这么干的！你的行径，是一个——一个——」

各行各业都备有一份相当齐全的恶人名录，任凭挑选。他挑中了赛马场那一行。

「一个下三滥的赖账鬼，」他下了结论。「可这个我咽不下去。不；一刻也咽不下去。我要求你当场就把那笔钱退还给我。身边没带，就去取来。」

勋爵大人脸上露出大难临头的惊惶。他什么都料到了，独独没料到这一出。他本难免要领受一场他念书那会儿会管它叫「挨训」的玩意儿，对此他也做好了硬着头皮受完的准备。那兴许伤他的心，却保得住他的钱包。这样可怖的事态变化，他可没预见。

===Original===
"But, I say, uncle!" he bleated.

Sir Thomas silenced him with a grand gesture.

Ruefully his lordship produced his little all. Sir Thomas took it with a snort and went to the door.

Saunders was still brooding statuesquely over the gong.

===Chinese===
「可是，我说，叔父！」他咩咩哀叫。

托马斯爵士一个堂而皇之的手势叫他住了口。

勋爵大人悲从中来，掏出了他那份小小的全部家当。托马斯爵士哼了一声接过去，朝门口走去。

桑德斯仍旧雕像一般对着那面锣默然肃立。

===Original===
"Sound it!" said Sir Thomas.

Saunders obeyed him with the air of an unleashed hound.

"And now," said Sir Thomas, "go to my dressing room and place these notes in the small drawer of the table."

The butler's calm, expressionless, yet withal observant eye took in at a glance the signs of trouble. Neither the inflated air of Sir Thomas nor the punctured-balloon bearing of Lord Dreever escaped him.

===Chinese===
「敲吧！」托马斯爵士说。

桑德斯遵命照办，那神气像一条挣脱了锁链的猎犬。

「还有，」托马斯爵士说，「上我更衣室去，把这些钞票放回梳妆台的小抽屉里。」

管家那双平静、不露声色却洞察秋毫的眼睛，一眼便把动乱的征兆尽收眼底。托马斯爵士那副充气过度的架势，德利弗勋爵那副泄了气的皮球似的做派，一样也没逃过他的眼。

===Original===
"Something hup," he said to his immortal soul as he moved upstairs. "Been a fair old, rare old row, seems to me."

He reserved his more polished periods for use in public. In conversation with his immortal soul he was wont to unbend somewhat.
===Chinese===
「有事儿喽，」他一边上楼，一边对其不朽的灵魂说。「照我看呐，好家伙，怕是结结实实闹了一场大架。」

他那些更为文雅的辞令，是留着公共场合用的。同不朽的灵魂交谈时，他惯于稍稍放松一点儿。

## XXIV The Treasure-Seeker / 寻宝人

===Original===
Gloom wrapped his lordship about during dinner as with a garment. He owed twenty pounds; his assets amounted to seven shillings and fourpence. He thought, and thought again. Quite an intellectual pallor began to appear on his normally pink cheeks. Saunders silently sympathetic—he hated Sir Thomas as an interloper, and entertained for his lordship, under whose father also he had served, a sort of paternal fondness—was ever at his elbow with the magic bottle; and to Spennie, emptying and re-emptying his glass almost mechanically, wine, the healer, brought an idea. To obtain twenty pounds from any one person of his acquaintance was impossible; to divide the twenty by four and persuade a generous quartet to contribute five pounds apiece was more feasible.

Hope began to stir within him again.

Immediately after dinner he began to flit about the castle like a family spectre of active habits.

===Chinese===
用晚饭的当口，愁云像一件罩袍似的把勋爵大人裹了个严实。他欠着二十镑；全部家当合计七先令四便士。他想了又想。他那向来粉红的腮帮子上，渐渐浮起一层十足思虑性的苍白。桑德斯默默含着同情——他恨托马斯爵士是个鸠占鹊巢的外来户，而对勋爵大人——当年他也在勋爵大人的先父手下当过差——怀着一份父辈似的疼爱——始终捧着那只法宝酒瓶守在他手边；斯宾尼几乎机械地把酒杯干了又斟、斟了又干，酒这位疗伤圣手便给他送来了一个主意。二十镑想从任何一位熟人手里整个儿弄来，是不可能的；把二十镑除以四，说动四位慷慨人士每人凑上五镑，倒还切实可行。

希望又在他胸中蠢蠢欲动。

晚饭一撤，他就像一只手脚勤快的家鬼，开始在城堡里飘飘忽忽地四处游走。

===Original===
“Halloa, Spennie!” said Charteris. “I wanted to see you. It is currently reported that you are in love. At dinner you looked as if you had influenza. What’s your trouble? For goodness’ sake bear up until the show’s over. Don’t go swooning on the stage, or anything. Do you know your lines?”

“The fact is,” said his lordship eagerly, “it’s this way. I happen to want—Can you lend me a fiver?”

“All I have in the world at this moment,” said Charteris, “is eleven shillings and a postage stamp. If the stamp would be of any use to you as a start—No? You know, it’s from small beginnings like that great fortunes are amassed. However—”

===Chinese===
「哈啰，斯宾尼！」查特里斯说。「正想找你呢。眼下外头都传开了，说你恋爱啦。吃晚饭那会儿，你瞧着就像得了流行感冒。你有什么烦心事？行行好，撑到戏演完再说。可别在台上晕过去什么的。你的台词背熟了没有？」

「实不相瞒，」勋爵大人急切地说，「是这么回事。我碰巧正缺——你能借我五镑吗？」

「我眼下在这世上全部的家当，」查特里斯说，「是十一先令外加一枚邮票。要是这枚邮票能给你的大业当个本钱——不要？要知道，宏大的家业，正是打这样的小小起头攒起来的。不过——」

===Original===
Two minutes later Lord Dreever had resumed his hunt.

The path of the borrower is a thorny one, especially if, as in the case of Spennie, his reputation as a payer-back is not of the best.

Spennie, in his time, had extracted small loans from most of his male acquaintances, rarely repaying the same. He had a tendency to forget that he had borrowed half a crown here to pay a cab fare and ten shillings there to settle up for a dinner; and his memory was not much more retentive of larger sums. This made his friends somewhat wary. The consequence was that the great treasure hunt was a failure from start to finish. He got friendly smiles, he got honeyed apologies, he got earnest assurances of goodwill; but he got no money, except from Jimmy Pitt.

===Chinese===
两分钟之后，德利弗勋爵重又踏上了寻宝之途。

借债人的路上荆棘丛生，尤其倘若是斯宾尼这路人——他还债的名声实在不算好听。

斯宾尼这辈子，从相识的男人们大多数手里都掏过小笔借款，还回去的寥寥无几。他有一样毛病：总记不起自己这里借过半克朗付马车钱、那里借过十先令结饭账；至于更大的数目，他的记性也好不了多少。这叫他的朋友们不能不多留个心眼儿。结果便是，这场盛大的寻宝行动自始至终一败涂地。他得到了亲切的微笑，得到了甜言蜜语的歉意，得到了恳恳切切的友好保证；可他就是没得到钱，只除了吉米·皮特。

===Original===
He had approached Jimmy in the early stages of the hunt and Jimmy, being in the mood when he would have lent anything to anybody, yielded the required five pounds without a murmur.

But what was five pounds? The garment of gloom and the intellectual pallor were once more prominent when his lordship repaired to his room to don the loud tweeds which, as Lord Herbert, he was to wear in the first act.

There was a good deal to be said against stealing, as a habit; but it cannot be denied that, in certain circumstances, it offers an admirable solution of a financial difficulty, and, if the penalties were not so exceedingly unpleasant, it is probable that it would become far more fashionable than it is.

===Chinese===
寻宝行动刚一开场，他就找上了吉米；吉米正处在那种谁张口都肯借的心境里，二话没说便把要的五镑交了出去。

可五镑算得了什么？等勋爵大人回到房里，换上那身花哨扎眼的花呢装——头一幕里他要扮赫伯特勋爵（Lord Herbert），穿的就是它——愁云的罩袍和思虑的苍白便又都显露出来。

偷窃作为一种习惯，可非议之处着实不少；可也不能不承认，在某些情形下，它确是解决财务难题的一剂妙方；倘若刑罚没有那么叫人难受到极点，只怕这行当会比如今风光得多。

===Original===
His lordship’s mind did not turn immediately to this outlet from his embarrassment. He had never stolen before, and it did not occur to him directly to do so now. There is a conservative strain in all of us. But gradually, as it was borne in upon him that it was the only course possible, unless he were to grovel before Hargate on the morrow and ask for time to pay—an unthinkable alternative—he found himself contemplating the possibility of having to secure the money by unlawful means. By the time he had finished his theatrical toilet, he had definitely decided that this was the only thing to be done.

His plan was simple. He knew where the money was—in the dressing table in Sir Thomas’s room. He had heard Saunders instructed to put it there. What could be easier than to go and get it? Everything was in his favour. Sir Thomas would be downstairs receiving his guests. The coast would be clear. Why, it was like finding the money.

Besides, he reflected, as he worked his way through a bottle of Mumm which he had had the forethought to abstract from the supper table as a nerve-steadier, it was not really stealing. Dash it all, the man had given him the money! It was his own! He had half a mind—he poured himself out another glass of the elixir—to give Sir Thomas a jolly good talking to into the bargain. Yes, dash it all!

He pushed on his cuffs fiercely. The British lion was roused.

===Chinese===
勋爵大人的心思并没有立刻转向这条解困的出路。他从前没偷过，眼下也没能一下子想到这上头。咱们每个人骨子里都有一股守旧劲儿。可渐渐地，当他醒悟过来这是唯一可行的路子——除非他明天到哈盖特面前摇尾乞怜，求他宽限还钱的时日——这个念头想都不必想——他发觉自己已在琢磨用不合法的手段把那笔钱弄到手了。到他化完戏妆的时候，他已经拿定主意：非此不可。

他的计划简单得很。他知道钱在哪儿——在托马斯爵士房里的梳妆台里。他亲耳听见吩咐桑德斯把钱搁在那儿的。走过去把它取来，还有什么更容易的？样样都对他有利。托马斯爵士要在楼下接待宾客。四下无人，道路畅通。嗐，这简直跟白捡一笔钱没什么两样。

再说，他一边把一瓶玛姆香槟（Mumm）往肚里灌——这瓶酒全亏他有先见之明，早从夜宵席上顺了来，好稳住自己的神经——一边寻思：这压根儿算不得偷。真见鬼，那钱是那家伙给他的！本就是他的！他还存着一半心思——说着又给自己斟了一杯这仙酿——索性把托马斯爵士结结实实训上一顿。对，真见鬼！

他狠狠地往上推了推袖口。英国雄狮被唤醒了。

===Original===
A man’s first crime is, as a rule, a shockingly amateurish affair. Now and then, it is true, we find beginners forging with the accuracy of old hands or breaking into houses with the finish of experts. But these are isolated cases. The average tyro lacks generalship altogether. Spennie Dreever may be cited as a typical novice. It did not strike him that inquiries might be instituted by Sir Thomas when he found the money gone, and that suspicion might conceivably fall upon himself. Courage may be born of champagne, but rarely prudence.

The theatricals began at half past eight with a duologue. The audience had been hustled into their seats, happier than is usual in such circumstances, owing to the rumour which had been circulated that the proceedings were to terminate with an informal dance. The castle was singularly well constructed for such a purpose. There was plenty of room and a sufficiency of retreat for those who sat out, in addition to a conservatory large enough to have married off half the couples in the country.

Spennie’s idea had been to establish an alibi by mingling with the throng for a few minutes, and then to get through his burglarious speciality during the duologue, when his absence would not be noticed. It might be that if he disappeared later in the evening people would wonder what had become of him.

He lurked about till the last of the audience had taken their seats. As he was moving off through the hall a hand fell upon his shoulder. Conscience makes cowards of us all. Spennie bit his tongue and leaped three inches into the air.

===Chinese===
人的第一桩罪行，通常都是一桩外行得吓人的勾当。诚然，也时而能见到新手造假币造得老手一样精准，或者撬门入室撬出专家般的成色。可这些只是个别的例子。寻常的新手全无半点将才。斯宾尼·德利弗便可引作一个典型的新手。他压根没想到，托马斯爵士发现钱没了之后会着手追查，而疑心说不定会落到他自己头上。勇气固然可以生于香槟，谨慎却难得与它同来。

业余演剧八点半开锣，头一个节目是双人短剧（duologue）。观众们被连推带搡按到座位上，心里却比这类场合通常的光景高兴得多，因为四处都传遍了风声，说今晚的节目要以一场不拘礼节的舞会收场。城堡为这种用途造得格外出色：地方宽敞，那些坐到一旁歇着不跳的人也有的是退避之处，另外还有一座大得足以为全国半数情侣保媒成亲的温室。

斯宾尼的如意算盘是：先在人群里混上几分钟，好歹算个不在场的凭据；随后趁演双人短剧的当口，干他那行窃的看家本事——那时节没人会留意他不在。要是他晚上再晚些时候才开溜，人家兴许就要纳闷他上哪儿去了。

他一直猫到最后一位观众落了座。正要穿过大厅开溜，一只手落在了他的肩上。良心这东西，把人人都变成懦夫。斯宾尼咬了一下舌头，往半空蹿起了三英寸。

===Original===
“Halloa, Charteris!” he said gaspingly.

Charteris appeared to be in a somewhat overwrought condition. Rehearsals had turned him into a pessimist, and now that the actual moment of production had arrived his nerves were in a thoroughly jumpy condition, especially as the duologue was to begin in two minutes and the obliging person who had undertaken to prompt had disappeared.

“Spennie,” said Charteris, “where are you off to?”

“What—what do you mean? I was just going upstairs.”

“No, you don’t. You’ve got to come and prompt. That fellow Blake has vanished. I’ll wring his neck! Come along!”

===Chinese===
「哈啰，查特里斯！」他上气不接下气地说。

查特里斯看上去处在一种神经过度紧张的状态。一遍遍的排练早把他熬成了个悲观主义者，如今大幕将启的正日子到了，他的神经更是蹦得七零八落，尤其还差两分钟双人短剧就要开演，而那位好心来担纲提词的人却不见了踪影。

「斯宾尼，」查特里斯说，「你上哪儿去？」

「什——什么意思？我正要上楼去。」

「不行，你别想溜。你得来提词。那个叫布莱克（Blake）的家伙开小差了。我要拧断他的脖子！走吧！」

===Original===
Spennie went reluctantly. Halfway through the duologue the official prompter returned, with the remark that he had been having a bit of a smoke on the terrace and that his watch had gone wrong. Leaving him to discuss the point with Charteris, Spennie slipped quietly away.

The delay, however, had had the effect of counteracting the uplifting effects of the Mumm. The British lion required a fresh fillip. He went to his room to administer it. By the time he emerged he was feeling just right for the task in hand. A momentary doubt occurred to him whether it would not be a good thing to go down and pull Sir Thomas’s nose as a preliminary to the proceedings; but he put the temptation aside. Business before pleasure.

With a jaunty, if somewhat unsteady, step he climbed the stairs to the floor above, and made his way down the corridor to Sir Thomas’s room. He switched on the light and went to the dressing table. The drawer was locked, but in his present mood Spennie, like Love, laughed at locksmiths. He grasped the handle and threw his weight into a sudden tug. The drawer came out with a report like a pistol shot.

“There!” said his lordship, wagging his head severely.

===Chinese===
斯宾尼满心不情愿地去了。双人短剧演到一半，正牌提词人回来了，说是他到露台上抽了口烟，手表又走了时不准。斯宾尼把这场官司留给查特里斯同他理论，自己悄悄溜之大吉。

可是这一耽搁，把玛姆香槟提起来的那股豪兴又给抵销掉了。英国雄狮需要再来一针新鲜的强心剂。他回房去补这一剂。等他重新露面时，手头这桩营生，他觉得自己的状态已经调得恰到好处。有那么一瞬，他心里嘀咕：是不是该先下楼去揪一把托马斯爵士的鼻子，给整场行动来个开场礼；可他把这诱惑摁下了。正事在先，作乐在后。

他迈着轻快——虽说有些打晃——的步子登上楼去，顺着走廊来到托马斯爵士的房门前。他摁亮电灯，走到梳妆台跟前。抽屉锁着，可斯宾尼眼下这副心境，正跟爱情一个样，见了锁匠只管嗤笑。他攥住把手，猛一使劲往外拽。抽屉应声而出，砰的一响，赛过手枪开火。

「怎么样！」勋爵大人说着，煞有介事地直晃脑袋。

===Original===
In the drawer lay the four banknotes. The sight of them brought back his grievances with a rush. He would teach Sir Thomas to treat him like a kid. He would show him!

He was removing the notes, frowning fiercely the while, when he heard a cry of surprise from behind him.

He turned, to see Molly. She wore the costume of a stage milkmaid, and her eyes were round with wonder. Leaving her room a few moments earlier, after dressing for her part, she had almost reached the end of the corridor that led to the landing when she observed his lordship, flashed of face and moving like some restive charger, come curveting out of his bedroom in a dazzling suit of tweeds and make his way upstairs. Ever since their mutual encounter with Sir Thomas before dinner she had been hoping for a chance of seeing him alone. She had not failed to notice his depression during the meal, and her good little heart had been troubled by the thought that she must have been responsible for it. She knew that for some reason what she had said about the letter had brought his lordship into his uncle’s bad books, and she wanted to find him and say she was sorry.

===Chinese===
抽屉里躺着那四张钞票。一见此物，满腹的委屈顿时一齐涌上心头。他要教训教训托马斯爵士，叫他知道把他当娃娃看待是什么下场。他要露一手给他瞧瞧！

他正把钞票往外拿，一面狠狠皱着眉头，忽听得身后传来一声惊呼。

他转过身，看见了莫莉。她穿着戏里挤奶姑娘的行头，一双眼睛瞪得溜圆，满是惊愕。片刻之前，她扮好自己的角色出了房门，刚要走到通向楼梯口的走廊尽头，忽见勋爵大人满面红光、行动活像一匹闹性子的战马，穿着一身亮得耀眼的花呢装，从卧房里腾跃而出，一路奔楼梯去了。打晚饭前俩人一道撞见托马斯爵士那回起，她就一直盼着能寻个机会同他单独谈谈。席间他郁郁寡欢，她看在眼里，而且她那颗善良的小心肝一直隐隐作痛，总觉得这愁绪定是自己招来的。她知道，不知为了什么缘故，她那番关于信的话，害得勋爵大人失了叔父的欢心，她想找到他，说一声对不起。

===Original===
Accordingly, she had followed him. His lordship, still in the warhorse vein, had made the pace upstairs too hot, and had disappeared while she was still halfway up. She had arrived at the top just in time to see him turn down the passage into Sir Thomas’s dressing room. She could not think what his object might be. She knew that Sir Thomas was downstairs, so it could not be with the idea of a chat with him that Spennie was seeking the dressing room.

Faint, yet pursuing, she followed on his trail, and arrived in the doorway just as the pistol-report of the burst lock rang out.

She stood looking at him blankly. He was holding a drawer in one hand. Why, she could not imagine.

“Lord Dreever!” she exclaimed.

The sombre determination of his lordship’s face melted into a twisted but kindly smile.

===Chinese===
于是她跟了上去。勋爵大人战马的性子还没退，上楼的脚步叫人追不上，她还爬到半道，人已经不见了。她赶到楼梯口时，恰瞧见他拐进通道，进了托马斯爵士的更衣室。她实在想不出他图的是什么。她知道托马斯爵士人在楼下，斯宾尼奔更衣室去，断不会是打算找他攀谈。

她虽然困乏，仍穷追不舍，循着踪迹跟到门边，恰在崩断的锁头炸出那声枪响之际赶到了门口。

她站在那儿，直愣愣地望着他。他一只手拎着一只抽屉。这是为什么，她想破脑袋也想不出来。

「德利弗勋爵！」她失声道。

勋爵大人脸上那副阴沉的决绝，化开成一个歪歪扭扭却透着和善的笑。

===Original===
“Good!” he said, perhaps a trifle thickly. “Good! Glad you’ve come—we’re pals—you said so—on stairs—b’fore dinner. Very glad you’ve come. Won’t you sit down?”

He waved the drawer benevolently, by way of making her free of the room. The movement disturbed one of the banknotes, which fluttered in Molly’s direction and fell at her feet.

She stooped and picked it up. When she saw what it was her bewilderment increased.

“But—but—” she said.

His lordship beamed upon her with a pebble-beached smile of indescribable goodwill.

“Sit down,” he urged. “We’re pals—no quol with you—you’re good friend. Quol—Uncle Thomas.”

===Chinese===
「好！」他说，口齿兴许略微含混了些。「好！你来啦——好得很——咱们是哥们儿——你说的——楼梯上——晚饭前。你来啦，好得很。不坐下吗？」

他慈眉善目地挥了挥那只抽屉，算是宣布她在这间屋子里行动自由。这一挥惊动了一张钞票，飘飘摇摇朝莫莉飞去，落在她脚边。

她弯腰拾了起来。看清是张什么票子之后，她的惶惑又添了几分。

「可——可是——」她说。

勋爵大人朝她满脸堆笑，那笑容像一片铺满卵石的海滩，盛着难以言喻的善意。

「坐下，」他催道。「咱们是哥们儿——跟你没——没吵头——你是好朋友。吵——吵的是托马斯叔父。」

===Original===
“But, Lord Dreever, what are you doing? What was that noise I heard?”

“Opening drawer,” said his lordship affably.

“But—” She looked again at what she had in her hand. “But this is a five pound note.”

“Five pound note,” said his lordship—“quite right. Three more of them in here.”

Still she could not understand.

===Chinese===
「可是，德利弗勋爵，你这是在干什么？我方才听见的那声响是怎么回事？」

「开抽屉，」勋爵大人和和气气地说。

「可是——」她把手里的东西又端详了一遍。「可这是一张五镑的票子呀。」

「五镑的票子，」勋爵大人说——「一点儿不错。这里头还有仨。」

她仍旧一头雾水。

===Original===
“But—Were you—stealing them?”

His lordship drew himself up.

“No,” he said. “No! Not stealing. No.”

“Then—”

“Like this: before dinner old boy friendly as you please; couldn’t do enough for me. Touched him for twenty of the best and got away with it. So far all well. Then met you on stairs. You let cat out of bag.”

“But why? Surely—”

===Chinese===
「可——你是不是——在偷它们？」

勋爵大人把腰板一挺。

「不，」他说。「不！不是偷。不是。」

「那——」

「是这么回事：晚饭前老哥客客气气，恨不得什么都替我办。我捞了他上好的二十镑，稳稳当当落了袋。到这儿为止万事大吉。后来在楼梯上撞见了你。你把袋子里的猫给放出来了。」

「可为什么要那样？总不会——」

===Original===
His lordship gave the drawer a dignified wave.

“Not blaming you,” he said magnanimously. “Not your fault—misfortune. You didn’t know—about letter.”

“About the letter?” said Molly. “Yes; what was the trouble about the letter? I knew something was wrong directly I had said that I wrote it.”

“Trouble was,” said his lordship, “that old boy thought it was love letter. Didn’t undeceive him.”

“You didn’t tell him? Why?”

===Chinese===
勋爵大人庄严地挥了挥那只抽屉。

「不怪你，」他宽宏大量地说。「不是你的错——是倒霉。你不晓得——信的事。」

「信的事？」莫莉说。「是啊；那封信到底惹出了什么乱子？我一说出信是我写的，就知道坏了事。」

「乱子就出在，」勋爵大人说，「老哥当它是封情书。没给他澄清。」

「你没告诉他？为什么？」

===Original===
His lordship raised his eyebrows.

“Wanted touch him twenty of the best,” he explained simply.

For the life of her Molly could not help laughing.

“Don’t laugh,” protested his lordship, wounded. “No joke—serious—honour at stake.”

He removed the three notes and replaced the drawer.

“Honour of the Dreevers!” he added, pocketing the money.

===Chinese===
勋爵大人扬了扬眉毛。

「想捞他上好的二十镑，」他直统统地解释道。

莫莉说什么也忍不住，笑出了声。

「别笑，」勋爵大人抗议道，一副受了伤的模样。「不是笑话——正经事——名节攸关。」

他把那三张钞票取了出来，把抽屉放回原处。

「德利弗家门的名节！」他补上一句，把钱揣进兜里。

===Original===
“But, Lord Dreever!” she cried. “You can’t! You mustn’t! You can’t be going, really, to take that money? It’s stealing! It isn’t yours!”

His lordship wagged a forefinger very solemnly at her.

“That,” he said, “is where you make error. Mine! Old boy gave them to me.”

“Gave them to you! Then why did you break open the drawer?”

===Chinese===
「哎呀，德利弗勋爵！」她叫起来。「不行！你不能这样！你总不会当真要拿这笔钱吧？这是偷！又不是你的！」

勋爵大人朝她无比庄重地摇着一根食指。

「这个嘛，」他说，「就是你搞岔了的地方。我的！老哥给我的。」

「给你的！那你还撬抽屉干什么？」

===Original===
“Old boy took them back again, when he found out about letter.”

“Then they don’t belong to you?”

“Yes. Error! They do. Moral right.”

Molly wrinkled her forehead in her agitation. Men of Lord Dreever’s type appeal to the motherly instinct of women. As a man his lordship was a negligible quantity—he did not count; but as a wilful child, to be kept out of trouble, he had a claim on Molly.

She spoke soothingly.

===Chinese===
「老哥听说信的事，又把钱收回去了。」

「那这笔钱就不是你的了？」

「是——呸，错啦！是我的。道义上的权利。」

莫莉急得脑门上拧起了褶子。德利弗勋爵这路人，最能唤起女人那点母性的本能。作为男人，勋爵大人是个可以忽略不计的量——他不算个数；可作为一个任性的孩子，一个得看紧了不叫他惹祸的孩子，他在莫莉跟前是有名分的。

她放缓声气，哄劝起来。

===Original===
“But, Lord Dreever—” she began.

“Call me Spennie,” he urged. “We’re pals. You said so—on stairs. Everybody calls me Spennie, even Uncle Thomas. I’m going to pull his nose,” he broke off suddenly, as one recollecting a forgotten appointment.

“Spennie, then,” said Molly. “You mustn’t, Spennie. You mustn’t, really. You—”

“You look rippin’ in that dress,” he said irrelevantly.

===Chinese===
「可是，德利弗勋爵——」她开口道。

「叫我斯宾尼，」他央求道。「咱们是哥们儿。你说的——楼梯上。人人都叫我斯宾尼，连托马斯叔父也叫。我要去揪他鼻子，」他猛地岔开话头，像是记起一场忘了的约会。

「那好，斯宾尼，」莫莉说。「你不能这样，斯宾尼。真的不能。你——」

「你穿这身戏装帅极了，」他风马牛不相及地插了一句。

===Original===
“Thank you, Spennie, dear. But listen.” She spoke as if she were humouring a rebellious infant. “You really mustn’t take that money. You must put it back. See, I’m putting this note back. Give me the others, and I’ll put them in the drawer too. Then we’ll shut the drawer, and nobody will know.”

She took the notes from him, and replaced them in the drawer. He watched her thoughtfully, as if he were pondering the merits of her arguments.

“No,” he said suddenly. “No—must have them—moral right! Old boy—”

She pushed him gently away.

===Chinese===
「谢谢你，好斯宾尼。可你听我说。」她那口气，像在哄一个闹脾气的娃娃。「这笔钱你真的不能拿。得放回去。瞧，我把这张放回去了。把其余的给我，我也搁进抽屉里。然后咱们把抽屉关上，谁也不会知道。」

她从他手里接过钞票，一一放回抽屉。他若有所思地望着她，仿佛在掂量她那些道理有多少斤两。

「不行，」他忽然说。「不行——非拿不可——道义上的权利！老哥——」

她轻轻把他推开。

===Original===
“Yes, yes, I know,” she said. “I know it’s a shame that you can’t have them; but you mustn’t take them. Don’t you see that he would suspect you the moment he found they were gone? And then you’d get into trouble.”

“Something in that,” admitted his lordship.

“Of course there is, Spennie, dear. I’m so glad you see. There they all are, safe again in the drawer. Now we can go downstairs again, and—”

She stopped. She had closed the door earlier in the proceedings, but her quick ear caught the sound of a footstep in the passage outside.

===Chinese===
「对，对，我知道，」她说。「我知道你拿不着它们，是天大的委屈；可你决不能拿。你没瞧出来吗？他一发现钱没了，头一个就会疑心是你。到那时候你就有麻烦了。」

「这话有点道理，」勋爵大人承认。

「当然有道理，好斯宾尼。你能明白，我太高兴了。瞧，全都在这儿，又安安稳稳躺回抽屉里了。这会儿咱们可以下楼去了，还有——」

她猛地收住。方才动手的当口，她已经把门关上了，可她耳朵尖，捕捉到了外头走廊里的一阵脚步声。

===Original===
“Quick!” she whispered, taking his hand and darting to the electric light switch. “Somebody’s coming. We mustn’t be caught here. They’d see the broken drawer, and you’d get into awful trouble. Quick!”

She pushed him behind the curtain where the clothes hung, and switched off the light.

From behind the curtain came the muffled voice of his lordship.

“It’s Uncle Thomas. I’m coming out. Pull his nose.”

===Chinese===
「快！」她压着嗓子说，一把拉起他的手，箭步蹿向电灯开关。「有人来了。可不能叫人在这儿逮住。人家会瞧见弄坏的抽屉，你就有大麻烦了。快！」

她把他往挂着衣裳的帷幕后面一推，摁灭了灯。

帷幕后面闷闷地传出勋爵大人的声音。

「是托马斯叔父。我出去。揪他鼻子。」

===Original===
“Be quiet!”

She sprang to the curtain and slipped noiselessly behind it.

“But, I say—” began his lordship.

“Hush!”

She gripped his arm. He subsided.

===Chinese===
「别出声！」

她一个箭步蹿到帷幕边，悄没声儿地钻了进去。

「哎，我说——」勋爵大人刚要开口。

「嘘！」

她死死攥住他的胳膊。他蔫了下去。

===Original===
The footsteps had halted outside the door. Then the handle turned softly. The door opened and closed again with hardly a sound.

The footsteps passed on into the room.

===Chinese===
脚步声在门外停住了。接着，门把手轻轻地转动了。门开了，又合上，几乎没有一丝声息。

脚步声径直进了屋。

## XXV Explanations and an Interruption / 解释与一场打断

===Original===
Jimmy, like Lord Dreever, had been trapped at the beginning of the duologue, and had not been able to get away till it was nearly over. He had been introduced by Lady Julia to an elderly and adhesive baronet, who had recently spent ten days in New York, and escape had not been won without a struggle. The baronet, on his return to England, had published a book entitled *Modern America and Its People* and it was with regard to the opinions expressed in this volume that he invited Jimmy’s views. He had no wish to see the duologue, and it was only after the loss of much precious time that Jimmy was enabled to tear himself away on the plea of having to dress. He anathematized the authority on *Modern America and Its People* freely as he ran upstairs.

While the duologue was in progress there had been no chance of Sir Thomas taking it into his head to visit his dressing room. He had been, as his valet detective had observed to Mr. Galer, too busy jollying among the swells. It would only be the work of a few moments restoring the necklace to its place. But for the tenacity of the elderly baronet the thing would have been done by this time. But now there was no knowing what might not happen—anybody might come along the passage and see him.

He had one point in his favour: there was no likelihood of the jewels being required by their owner till the conclusion of the theatricals. The part which Lady Julia had been persuaded by Charteris to play mercifully contained no scope for the display of gems.

===Chinese===
吉米同德利弗勋爵一样，双人短剧一开锣就给困住了，直到快收场才脱得开身。朱莉娅夫人把他引见给一位上了年纪、黏性十足的准男爵（baronet）——此人新近在纽约待过十天——吉米费了好一番挣扎方才脱逃。那准男爵回英国以后出了一部书，题为《现代美国及其人民》（Modern America and Its People），他所要征询的，正是吉米对这部大作里诸般高见的看法。他对看双人短剧毫无兴趣，吉米赔上大把宝贵光阴，末了总算借口得去换装，才把自己撕扯脱身。他一路往楼上跑，一路把这位《现代美国及其人民》的权威骂了个畅快淋漓。

双人短剧进行期间，托马斯爵士决计不会一时兴起跑去自己的更衣室，机会正在于此。正像他那位当贴身男仆的侦探对盖勒先生说的，他忙得很——忙着在名流显贵中间打哈哈。把项链放回原处，不过是几分钟的工夫。要不是那位老准男爵黏劲惊人，这事此刻早已办妥。可如今，什么意外都可能冒出来——随便谁打走廊过，都可能撞见他。

有一层对他有利：演出散场之前，珠宝的主人断不至于要用它们。查特里斯好说歹说劝朱莉娅夫人接下的那个角色，谢天谢地，全然没有展示珠光宝气的余地。

===Original===
Before going down to dinner he had locked up the necklace in a drawer. It was still there, Spike having, apparently, been able to resist the temptation of recapturing it. He took it out and went into the corridor. He looked up and down it. There was nobody about. He shut his door and walked quickly in the direction of the dressing room.

He had provided himself with a lamp from a bicycle belonging to one of the grooms. Once inside, having closed the door, he lit this and looked about him.

Spike had given him minute directions as to the position of the jewel box. He found it without difficulty. To his untrained eye it seemed tolerably massive and impregnable, but Spike had evidently known how to open it without much difficulty. The lid was shut, but it came up without an effort when he tried to raise it, and he saw that the lock had been broken.

“Spike’s coming on!” he said.

He was dangling the necklace over the box, preparatory to dropping it in, when there was a quick rustle at the other side of the room. The curtain was plucked aside and Molly came out.

“Jimmy!” she cried.

===Chinese===
下楼用晚饭之前，他把项链锁进了一只抽屉。项链还在那儿——斯派克显然抵制住了再度把它偷走的诱惑。他取出项链，走进走廊，朝两头望了望，四下无人。他关上房门，快步朝更衣室的方向走去。

他随身带了一盏车灯，是从一个马夫的自行车上取下来的。进了屋，关好门，他点亮车灯，打量四周。

斯派克曾就珠宝箱的方位给他交代过毫厘不差的细目，他没费什么周折便找到了。在门外汉眼里，这箱子看着相当敦实、无懈可击；可斯派克显然深谙不费力气开箱之道。箱盖关着，但他伸手一掀便应手而起——锁已经断了。

「斯派克真出息了！」他说。

他正把项链悬在箱口上方，就要往里放，忽听得屋子那头一阵急促的窸窣声。帷幔给一把扯开，莫莉走了出来。

「吉米！」她喊道。

===Original===
Jimmy’s nerves were always in pretty good order, but at the sight of this apparition he certainly jumped.

“Great Scot!” he said.

The curtain again became agitated by some unseen force, violently this time, and from its depths a plaintive voice made itself heard.

“Dash it all,” said the voice, “I’ve stuck!”

There was another upheaval, and his lordship emerged, his yellow locks ruffled and upstanding, his face crimson.

“Caught my head in a coat or something,” he explained at large. “Halloa, Pitt!”

===Chinese===
吉米的神经一向保养得宜，可猛然瞧见这么个幽魂现世，他着实蹦了一下。

「我的老天！」他说。

帷幔又被一股看不见的力道搅动起来，这回来得凶猛，帷幔深处传出哭腔。

「真见鬼，」那声音说，「我卡住了！」

又是一阵地动山摇，勋爵大人钻了出来，一头黄发乱作一团、根根倒竖，满脸涨得通红。

「脑袋让大衣还是什么的东西给卡住了，」他一五一十地解释道。「哈啰，皮特！」

---

===Original===
Pressed rigid against the wall, Molly had listened with growing astonishment to the movements on the other side of the curtain. Her mystification deepened every moment. It seemed to her that the room was still in darkness. She could hear the sound of breathing; and then the light of the lantern caught her eye. Who could this be, and why had he not switched on the electric light?

She strained her ears to catch a sound. For a while she heard nothing except the soft breathing. Then came a voice that she knew well; and, abandoning her hiding place, she came out into the room, and found Jimmy standing with a lamp in his hand over some dark object in the corner of the room.

It was a full minute after Jimmy’s first exclamation of surprise before either of them spoke again. The light of the lamp hurt Molly’s eyes. She put up a hand to shade them. It seemed to her that they had been standing like this for years.

Jimmy had not moved. There was something in his attitude which filled Molly with a vague fear. In the shadow behind the lamp he looked shapeless and inhuman.

===Chinese===
莫莉紧紧贴墙站着，隔着帷幔听那边的动静，心头的惊愕一阵强似一阵。她的困惑分分秒秒都在加深。她觉得那间屋分明还黑着。她听得见呼吸声；随后，一星灯光晃进了她的眼睛。这会是谁？他为什么不拉开电灯？

她竖起耳朵捕捉声息。有一阵子，除了轻轻的呼吸，她什么也没听见。随后，一个她再熟悉不过的声音响了起来；她抛下藏身处，走出帷幔进了屋，只见吉米手提一盏灯，站在屋角一团黑乎乎的东西跟前。

吉米头一声惊叫过后，足足一分钟，两人谁也没再开口。灯光刺得莫莉眼睛生疼，她抬起一只手遮光。她觉得，两人就这么站着，仿佛已经站了经年累月。

吉米一动没动。他立在那里，某种说不清的东西让莫莉隐隐生出恐惧。灯影背后，他显得没了人形，不像血肉之躯。

===Original===
“You’re hurting my eyes,” she said at last.

“I’m sorry,” said Jimmy. “I didn’t think. Is that better?” He turned the light from her face. Something in his voice and the apologetic haste with which he moved the lamp seemed to relax the strain of the situation. The feeling of stunned surprise began to leave her. She found herself thinking coherently again.

The relief was but momentary. Why was Jimmy in the room at that time? Why had he a lamp? What had he been doing? The questions shot from her brain like sparks from an anvil.

The darkness began to tear at her nerves. She felt along the wall for the switch and flooded the room with light.

===Chinese===
「你晃得我眼睛疼，」她终于开了口。

「对不起，」吉米说。「我没留意。这样好些吗？」他把灯光从她脸上挪开。他话音里的某种东西，连同他挪灯时那副赔不是的急切，似乎松开了绷紧的局面。如遭雷击的惊愕开始从她心头退去，她发觉自己的思绪又能连贯地转了。

可这份宽慰只维持了一瞬。吉米那时节怎么会在这屋里？他手里怎么提着灯？他方才在干什么？一个个问题像铁砧上迸出的火星，噼啪砸进她的脑子。

黑暗开始撕扯她的神经。她沿着墙壁摸到开关，让满屋子顿时大放光明。

===Original===
Jimmy laid down the lantern and stood for a moment undecided. He had concealed the necklace behind him. Now he brought it forward and dangled it silently before the eyes of Molly and his lordship. Excellent as were his motives for being in that room with the necklace in his hand, he could not help feeling, as he met Molly’s startled gaze, quite as guilty as if his intentions had been quite different.

His lordship, having by this time pulled himself together to some extent, was the first to speak.

“I say, you know, what ho!” he observed, not without emotion. “What?”

Molly drew back.

“Jimmy! You were—. Oh, you can’t have been!”

“Looks jolly like it!” said his lordship judicially.

===Chinese===
吉米放下灯，迟疑地站了片刻。项链原本藏在他身后。这会儿他把它拿到身前，无声地悬在莫莉和勋爵大人眼前。他手握项链身处此屋，动机本再光明磊落不过；可一迎上莫莉惊惶的目光，他不由得觉得自己活脱脱一副做贼心虚相，仿佛存的心思当真另有其他。

勋爵大人到这会儿多少缓过了神，抢先开了口。

「我说，你们知道吗，哇嗬！」他不无激动地说。「什么？」

莫莉往后一缩。

「吉米！你是在——哦，不会的，你决不会！」

「瞧着可真像那么回事！」勋爵大人以法官断案般的口吻说。

===Original===
“I wasn’t,” said Jimmy. “I was putting them back.”

“Putting them back?”

“Pitt, old man,” said his lordship solemnly, “that sounds a bit thin.”

“Dreever, old man,” said Jimmy, “I know it does. But it’s the truth.”

His lordship’s manner became kindly.

“Now, look here, Pitt, old son,” he said. “There’s nothing to worry about—we’re all pals here—you can pitch it straight to us. We won’t give you away. We—”

===Chinese===
「我不是在偷，」吉米说。「我是在往回放。」

「往回放？」

「皮特，老伙计，」勋爵大人一本正经地说，「这话听着可有点儿悬乎。」

「德利弗，老伙计，」吉米说，「我知道是悬乎。可这是实话。」

勋爵大人的态度变得和蔼起来。

「哎，你听我说，皮特，好老弟，」他说。「没什么可愁的——咱们这儿都是自己人——你只管跟我们交实底。我们不会卖你。我们——」

===Original===
“Be quiet!” cried Molly. “Jimmy!”

Her voice was strained; she spoke with an effort; she was suffering torments. The words her father had said to her on the terrace were pouring back into her mind. She seemed to hear his voice now, cool and confident, warning her against Jimmy, saying that he was crooked. There was a curious whirring in her head. Everything in the room was growing large and misty. She heard Lord Dreever begin to say something that sounded as if someone were speaking at the end of a telephone; and then she was aware that Jimmy was holding her in his arms and calling to Lord Dreever to bring water.

“When a girl goes like that,” said his lordship, with an insufferable air of omniscience, “you want to cut her—”

“Come along!” said Jimmy. “Are you going to be a week getting that water?”

His lordship proceeded to soak a sponge without further parley; but as he carried his dripping burden across the room Molly recovered. She tried weakly to free herself.

Jimmy helped her to a chair. He had dropped the necklace on the floor, and Lord Dreever nearly trod on it.

===Chinese===
「别说了！」莫莉喊道。「吉米！」

她的声音发紧，说话十分吃力，正受着熬煎。父亲在露台上对她说过的话，一股脑儿涌回心头。此刻她仿佛听得见父亲的声音，冷静而笃定，叫她提防吉米，说他心术不正。她脑子里嗡嗡作响，屋里的东西都在涨大，化作一片迷蒙。她听见德利弗勋爵开始说话，那声音像是从电话线那头传来的；随后她发觉，吉米已把她抱进怀里，正喊德利弗勋爵去拿水。

「姑娘晕成那样的时候，」勋爵大人端出一副叫人受不了的无所不知架势说，「你就该剪开她的——」

「快去呀！」吉米说。「你打那水打算打上一个礼拜吗？」

勋爵大人不再多话，动手浸透了一块海绵；可他捧着这湿淋淋的家什穿过屋子时，莫莉已经缓过来了。她软弱地挣了挣，想脱开身。

吉米扶她坐进一把椅子。项链已掉在地上，德利弗勋爵差点一脚踩上去。

===Original===
“What ho!” observed his lordship, picking it up. “Go easy with the jewellery!”

Jimmy was bending over Molly. Neither of them seemed to be aware of his lordship’s presence. Spennie was the sort of person whose existence is apt to be forgotten. Jimmy had had a flash of intuition. For the first time it occurred to him that Mr. McEachern might have hinted to Molly something of his own suspicions.

“Molly, dear,” he said, “it isn’t what you think. I can explain everything. Do you feel better now? Can you listen? I can explain everything.”

“Pitt, old boy,” protested his lordship, “you don’t understand. We aren’t going to give you away. We’re all—”

Jimmy ignored him.

“Molly, listen,” he said.

===Chinese===
「哇嗬！」勋爵大人拾起项链说。「对珠宝物件儿脚下留神呐！」

吉米正俯身对着莫莉，两人似乎都没察觉勋爵大人在场。斯宾尼就是那种人：他在不在场，旁人很容易忘个干净。吉米心头灵光一闪。他头一回想到，麦克伊彻恩先生或许曾对莫莉漏过口风，隐约透露过自己的疑心。

「莫莉，亲爱的，」他说，「事情不是你想的那样。我能把一切解释清楚。你现在好受些了吗？能听我说吗？我能把一切解释清楚。」

「皮特，老兄，」勋爵大人抗议道，「你没明白。我们不会卖你的。我们都是——」

吉米没理会他。

「莫莉，听着，」他说。

===Original===
She sat up.

“Go on, Jimmy,” she said.

“I wasn’t stealing the necklace—I was putting it back. The man who came to the castle with me, Spike Mullins, took it this afternoon and brought it to me.”

Spike Mullins! Molly remembered the name.

“He thinks I am a crook—a sort of Raffles. It was my fault. I was a fool. It all began that night in New York when we met at your house. I had been to the opening performance of a play called *Love, the Cracksman*—one of those burglar plays.”

“Jolly good show!” interpolated his lordship chattily. “It was at the Circle over here. I went twice.”

===Chinese===
她坐直了身子。

「说吧，吉米，」她说。

「我不是在偷项链——我是在把它放回去。跟我一道来城堡的那个人，斯派克·马林斯，今天下午把它偷了出来，送到了我手上。」

斯派克·马林斯！莫莉记得这个名字。

「他当我是贼——一个拉夫尔斯式的人物。这全怪我，是我糊涂。一切都是从纽约那晚起的头，就在你家碰面那回。我先前刚看了一出戏的首演，戏名叫《窃贼之恋》，就是那路窃贼戏。」

「好戏，好戏！」勋爵大人闲话家常似的插进来一句。「就在咱们这边的圆环剧院（the Circle）演的。我看过两遍。」

===Original===
“A friend of mine, a man named Mifflin, had been playing the hero in it, and after the show, at the club, he started in talking about the art of burglary—he’d been studying it—and I said that anybody could burgle a house. And in another minute it somehow happened that I had made a bet that I would do it that night. Heaven knows whether I ever really meant to; but that same night this man Mullins broke into my flat, and I caught him. We got into conversation, and I worked off on him a lot of technical stuff I’d heard from this actor friend of mine, and he jumped to the conclusion that I was an expert. And then it suddenly occurred to me that it would be a good joke on Mifflin if I went out with Mullins and did break into a house. I wasn’t in the mood to think what a fool I was at the time. Well, anyway, we went out, and—well, that’s how it all happened. And then I met Spike in London, down and out, and brought him here.”

He looked at her anxiously. It did not need his lordship’s owlish expression of doubt to tell him how weak his story must sound. He had felt it even as he was telling it. He was bound to admit that if ever a story rang false in every sentence it was this one.

“Pitt, old man,” said his lordship, shaking his head, more in sorrow than in anger, “it won’t do, old top. What’s the point of putting up any old yarn like that? Don’t you see, what I mean is, it’s not as if we minded. Don’t I keep telling you we’re all pals here? I’ve often thought what a jolly good feller old Raffles was—regular sportsman. I don’t blame a chappie for doing the gentleman burglar touch. Seems to me it’s a dashed sporting—”

===Chinese===
「我有个朋友，叫米夫林，在戏里演男主角。散了戏，在俱乐部里，他聊起了行窃的门道——他对这行当下过功夫研究——我说随便谁都能撬一户人家。不大一会儿，不知怎么一来，我竟然打了赌，赌当晚就去行窃。天晓得我当时是不是真有这个意思；可偏偏就在当天夜里，这位马林斯闯进了我的公寓，叫我逮了个正着。我们攀谈起来，我把从那位演员朋友那儿听来的一大堆行话术语现搬现卖吹给了他，他立刻断定我是行家。跟着我灵机一动：要是跟着马林斯真去撬一户人家，拿米夫林开涮，岂不是一桩妙事？当时我可没那份心思去想自己有多蠢。总之，我们俩就出了门，——嗯，事情的来龙去脉就是这样。后来我在伦敦碰见斯派克，他正穷途末路，我就把他带到了这儿。」

他忧心忡忡地望着她。不必借勋爵大人那副猫头鹰似的满脸疑云，他也知道这故事听上去有多站不住。就是讲的时候，他自己心里也直打鼓。他不得不承认：倘若世上真有一篇故事句句透着假，那就是眼前这一篇。

「皮特，老伙计，」勋爵大人摇着头说，与其说动气，不如说伤心。「这可糊弄不了人，老哥。编这么个老掉牙的瞎话顶什么用？你瞧，我是说，我们其实根本不介意。我不是一直跟你讲，咱们这儿都是自己人吗？我时常琢磨，老拉夫尔斯是多大的一个好好先生——地道的运动家。哪位老兄玩一手绅士窃贼的做派，我也不怪他。依我看，这实在见鬼的有气派——」

===Original===
Molly turned on him suddenly, cutting short his views on the ethics of gentlemanly theft in a blaze of indignation.

“What do you mean?” she cried. “Do you think I don’t believe every word Jimmy has said?”

His lordship jumped.

“Well, don’t you know, it seemed to me a bit thin. What I mean is—” He met Molly’s eye.

“Oh, well!” he concluded lamely.

Molly turned to Jimmy.

===Chinese===
莫莉陡然冲他发作，一腔怒火截断了他关于绅士行窃伦理学的高论。

「你这是什么意思？」她嚷道。「你以为吉米说的话我不字字都信吗？」

勋爵大人吓得一蹦。

「这个嘛，你明白，我觉得听着有点儿悬乎。我是说——」他迎上了莫莉的目光。

「哦，得！」他讪讪地收了场。

莫莉转向吉米。

===Original===
“Jimmy, of course I believe you—I believe every word.”

“Molly!”

His lordship looked on, marvelling. The thought crossed his mind that he had lost the ideal wife. A girl who would believe any old yarn a feller cared to—If it hadn’t been for Katie—For a moment he felt almost sad.

Jimmy and Molly were looking at each other in silence. From the expression on their faces his lordship gathered that his existence had once more been forgotten. He saw her hold out her hands to Jimmy. It was embarrassing for a chap! He looked away.

The next moment the door opened and closed again, and she had gone.

===Chinese===
「吉米，我当然信你——一个字都信。」

「莫莉！」

勋爵大人一旁看着，惊叹不已。他心里掠过一个念头：自己错过了一位理想的太太。人家这姑娘，随便哪个家伙爱编什么瞎话她都肯信——若不是为了凯蒂——那一会儿他几乎有些伤感。

吉米和莫莉默默相望。从两人脸上的神情，勋爵大人明白，自己又一次被忘了个干净。他看见她朝吉米伸出了双手。叫一位老兄情何以堪！他把脸扭开了。

下一刻，门开了又阖上，她走了。

===Original===
He looked at Jimmy. Jimmy was still apparently unconscious of his presence.

His lordship coughed.

“Pitt, old man—”

“Halloa!” said Jimmy, coming out of his thoughts with a start. “You still here? By the way”—he eyed Lord Dreever curiously—“I never thought of asking before—what on earth are you doing here? Why were you behind the curtain? Were you playing hide-and-seek?”

His lordship was not one of those who invent circumstantial stories easily on the spur of the moment. He searched rapidly for something that would pass muster, then abandoned the hopeless struggle. After all, why not be frank? He still believed Jimmy to be of the class of the hero of *Love, the Cracksman*. There would be no harm in confiding in him. He was a good fellow, a kindred soul, and would sympathise.

===Chinese===
他望望吉米。吉米显然还没意识到他在场。

勋爵大人咳嗽了一声。

「皮特，老伙计——」

「哈啰！」吉米猛地从思绪里惊醒。「你还在这儿？对了」——他好奇地打量着德利弗勋爵——「我先前一直没想起来问——你到底在这屋里干什么？躲在帷幔后头做什么？玩捉迷藏呢？」

勋爵大人不属于那种能现编现卖、张口就来一套有鼻子有眼的瞎话的人。他飞快地搜寻一套能蒙混过关的说辞，随即放弃了这场无望的挣扎。说到底，干吗不坦白呢？他依旧相信吉米是《窃贼之恋》主人公那一流的角色。跟他交个底没有坏处。他是个好小子，是同类，自会体谅。

===Original===
“It’s like this,” he said. And, having prefaced his narrative with the sound remark that he had been a bit of an ass, he gave Jimmy a summary of recent events.

“What!” said Jimmy. “You taught Hargate piquet? Why, my dear man, he was playing piquet like a professor when you were in short frocks. He’s a wonder at it.”

His lordship stared.

“How’s that?” he said. “You don’t know him, do you?”

“I met him in New York at the Strollers’ Club. A pal of mine, an actor—this fellow Mifflin I mentioned just now—put him up as a guest. He coined money at piquet. And there were some pretty useful players in the place, too. I don’t wonder you found him a promising pupil.”

“Then—then—why, dash it! then he’s a bally sharper?”

===Chinese===
「是这么回事，」他说。他先发了一通掷地有声的高见，承认自己先前干了点儿蠢事，然后才把近来的种种向吉米扼要说了一遍。

「什么！」吉米说。「你教哈盖特皮克牌？嗐，我的老兄，你还穿着娃娃短裙的年纪，人家皮克牌已经打得跟教授一样了。他是这行里的奇才。」

勋爵大人瞪直了眼。

「这是怎么回事？」他说。「你不认识他，对吧？」

「我在纽约「闲逛者」俱乐部认识他的。我的一位演员朋友——就是方才提到的那个米夫林——把他作为客人请了来。他打皮克牌大赚其钱。那地方可不乏相当的高手。难怪你觉得他是个前程远大的门徒。」

「这么——这么说——好家伙！那他就是个见鬼的老千？」

===Original===
“You’re a genius at crisp description,” said Jimmy. “You’ve got him summed up to rights first shot.”

“I shan’t pay him a bally penny.”

“Of course not. If he makes any objection refer him to me.”

His lordship’s relief was extreme. The more overpowering effects of the elixir had passed away, and he saw now what he had not seen in his more exuberant frame of mind—the cloud of suspicion which must hang over him when the loss of the banknotes was discovered.

He wiped his forehead.

“By Jove!” he said. “That’s something off my mind! By George, I feel like a two-year-old! I say, you’re a dashed good sort, Pitt.”

===Chinese===
「你这人一针见血，真是天才，」吉米说。「一枪就给他画准了像。」

「他一个见鬼的铜板也甭想从我这儿拿走。」

「当然不给。他要是敢有异议，叫他来找我。」

勋爵大人如释重负到了极点。那仙酿最烈的那股子劲头已经散去，他现在看清了先前心气正豪时看不见的东西：钞票失窃一旦败露，必然笼罩在他头顶的疑云。

他抹了一把脑门。

「嗬！」他说。「这下我心里落下一块石头！好家伙，我觉得自己像匹两岁的小马驹！我说，皮特，你真是个见鬼的大好人。」

===Original===
“You flatter me,” said Jimmy. “I strive to please.”

“I say, Pitt, that yarn you told us just now—the bet and all that—honestly, you don’t mean to say that was true, was it? I mean—By Jove! I’ve got an idea.”

“We live in stirring times!”

“Did you say your actor pal’s name was Mifflin?” He broke off suddenly before Jimmy could answer. “Great Scot!” he whispered, “What’s that? Good lord! Somebody’s coming!”

He dived behind the curtain like a rabbit. It had only just ceased to shake when the door opened and Sir Thomas Blunt walked in.

===Chinese===
「你过奖了，」吉米说。「在下唯求效力。」

「我说，皮特，你方才跟我们讲的那个瞎话——什么打赌那一套——说句实在话，你该不会是说那都是真事吧？我是说——天哪！我有主意了。」

「咱们活在一个多事之秋啊！」

「你方才说你那位演员朋友叫米夫林？」没等吉米回答，他忽然打住了。「我的老天！」他悄声说，「那是什么？老天爷！有人来了！」

他像只兔子似的往帷幔后头一钻。帷幔刚刚停止颤动，门开了，托马斯·布伦特爵士走了进来。

## XXVI Stirring Times for Sir Thomas / 托马斯爵士的多事之秋

===Original===
For a man whose intentions towards the jewels and their owner were so innocent, and even benevolent, Jimmy was in a singularly compromising position. It would have been difficult, even under more favourable conditions, to have explained to Sir Thomas’s satisfaction his presence in the dressing room. As things stood it was even harder, for his lordship’s last action before seeking cover had been to fling the necklace from him like a burning coal. For the second time in ten minutes it had fallen to the carpet, and it was just as Jimmy straightened himself after picking it up that Sir Thomas got a full view of him.

The knight stood in the doorway, his face expressing the most lively astonishment. His bulging eyes were fixed upon the necklace in Jimmy’s hand. Jimmy could see him struggling to find words to cope with so special a situation, and he felt rather sorry for him. Excitement of this kind was bad for a short-necked man of Sir Thomas’s type.

With kindly tact he endeavoured to help him out.

===Chinese===
单说吉米，他对那串宝石连同宝石的主人所怀的心思，原本清白无邪，甚至可以说是一片好心，可他的处境却偏偏尴尬得罕见。就算条件再有利些，要把人出现在更衣室里的缘由解释得让托马斯爵士满意，也够为难的了。而眼下的情势比这还要糟：勋爵大人临钻进掩护之前最后一个动作，是把项链像一块烧红的炭似的甩了出去。十分钟之内，它已经是第二回落到地毯上了；偏偏就在吉米把它拾起来、直起腰的那一瞬，托马斯爵士把他看了个正着。

爵士老爷站在门口，满脸是再生动不过的惊愕。两只鼓突的眼睛死死盯住吉米手里的项链。吉米看得出，他正在苦苦搜寻字眼，想应付这么一个特别的场面，心里倒有几分替他难受。这种刺激，对托马斯爵士这等短脖子的人物，是大有害处的。

他怀着善意的体贴，设法替他解围。

===Original===
“Good evening,” he said pleasantly.

Sir Thomas stammered. He was gradually nearing speech.

“What—what—what—” he said.

“Out with it,” said Jimmy.

“What—”

“I knew a man once in South Dakota who stammered,” said Jimmy. “He used to chew dog biscuit while he was speaking. It cured him, besides being nutritious. Another good way is to count ten while you’re thinking what to say, and then get it out quick.”

===Chinese===
「晚上好，」他和颜悦色地说。

托马斯爵士结结巴巴。他离把话说出口，已渐渐近了。

「你——你——你——」他说。

「痛快说出来，」吉米说。

「你——」

「从前我在南达科他州（South Dakota）认识一位口吃的先生，」吉米说。「他说话的当口嚼狗饼干。既治好了口吃，又富有营养。另一个好法子是：一边想着要说什么，一边数到十，然后赶紧把它倒出来。」

===Original===
“You—you blackguard!”

Jimmy placed the necklace carefully on the dressing table. Then he turned to Sir Thomas, with his hands in the pockets of his coat. Over the knight’s head he could see the folds of the curtain quivering gently, as if stirred by some zephyr. Evidently the drama of the situation was not lost on Hildebrand Spencer, twelfth Earl of Dreever.

Nor was it lost on Jimmy. This was precisely the sort of situation that appealed to him. He had his plan of action clearly mapped out. He knew that it would be useless to tell the knight the true facts of the case. Sir Thomas was as deficient in simple faith as in Norman blood.

To all appearances this was a tight corner, but Jimmy fancied that he saw his way out of it. Meanwhile, the situation appealed to him. Curiously enough, it was almost identical with the big scene in Act III of *Love, the Cracksman* in which Arthur Mifflin had made such a hit as the debonair burglar.

Jimmy proceeded to give his own idea of what the rendering of a debonair burglar should be. Arthur Mifflin had lit a cigarette, and had shot out smoke rings and repartee alternately. A cigarette would have been a great help here, but Jimmy prepared to do his best without properties.

===Chinese===
「你——你这恶棍！」

吉米小心翼翼地把项链搁到梳妆台上。然后他转过身来，面对托马斯爵士，双手插在外衣口袋里。越过爵士老爷的头顶，他望得见帷幔的褶层在轻轻颤动，仿佛被哪一阵和风拂过。显而易见，这一场面的戏剧意味，并没有逃过希尔德布兰德·斯宾塞（Hildebrand Spencer）——第十二代德利弗伯爵——的眼睛。

也没有逃过吉米的眼睛。这正是他最来劲的那一类场面。他的行动方案早已在心里盘算得一清二楚。他知道，把事情的底细原原本本讲给爵士老爷听是白费口舌。托马斯爵士这人，单纯的信仰固然欠缺，诺曼人的血统也一样匮乏。

表面上看，这是个棘手之极的窘境，但吉米自信已经瞧出了脱身之道。同时，这局面也着实投他所好。说来也巧，它几乎跟《窃贼之恋》（Love, the Cracksman）第三幕的那场重头戏一模一样——亚瑟·米夫林正是凭那场戏里风流倜傥的窃贼一角大出风头的。

吉米当下便按自己的见解，示范起一个风流倜傥的窃贼该怎么演。当年亚瑟·米夫林是点起一支香烟，烟圈和妙语轮番往外抛。这会儿有支香烟可就帮大忙了，但吉米准备不用道具，也勉力演好这场戏。

===Original===
“So—so it’s you, is it?” said Sir Thomas.

“Who told you!”

“Thief! Low thief!”

“Come, now,” protested Jimmy. “Why low? Just because you don’t know me over here, why scorn me? How do you know I haven’t got a big American reputation? For all you can tell, I may be Boston Willie or Sacramento Sam, or someone. Let us preserve the decencies of debate.”

“I had my suspicions of you. I had my suspicions from the first, when I heard that my idiot of a nephew had made a casual friend in London. So this was what you were! A thief, who—”

===Chinese===
「原——原来是你，是不是？」托马斯爵士说。

「谁告诉你的！」

「贼！下流的贼！」

「哎，得了，」吉米抗辩道。「怎么就下流了？单凭您在这边不认得我，何苦小瞧人？您怎么知道我在美国没有响当当的名号？照您所能看出来的，我说不定是波士顿·威利（Boston Willie），或者萨克拉门托·萨姆（Sacramento Sam），或者哪位好汉呢。咱们总得讲点辩论的体统。」

「我早就疑心你了。打一开头我就疑心，一听说我那蠢货侄子在伦敦随手交了个朋友，我就疑心了。原来你就是这么个货色！一个贼，居然——」

===Original===
“I don’t mind personally,” interrupted Jimmy, “but I hope, if ever you mix with cracksmen, you won’t call them thieves. They are frightfully sensitive. You see, there’s a world of difference between the two branches of the profession, and a good deal of snobbish caste prejudice. Let us suppose that you were an actor-manager. How would you enjoy being called a super? You see the idea, don’t you? You’d hurt their feelings. Now, an ordinary thief would probably use violence in a case like this; but violence, except in extreme cases—I hope this won’t be one of them—is contrary, I understand, to cracksmen’s etiquette. On the other hand, Sir Thomas, candour compels me to add that I have you covered.”

There was a pipe in the pocket of his coat. He thrust the stem earnestly against the lining. Sir Thomas eyed the protuberance apprehensively, and turned a little pale. Jimmy was scowling ferociously. Arthur Mifflin’s scowl in Act III had been much admired.

“My gun,” said Jimmy, “is, as you see, in my pocket. I always shoot from the pocket, in spite of the tailor’s bills. The little fellow is loaded and cocked. He’s pointing straight at your diamond solitaire. That fatal spot! No one has ever been hit in the diamond solitaire and survived. My finger is on the trigger, so I should recommend you not to touch that bell you are looking at. There are other reasons why you shouldn’t, but those I will go into presently.”

Sir Thomas’s hand wavered.

“Do, if you like, of course,” said Jimmy agreeably—“it’s your own house—but I shouldn’t. I am a dead shot at a yard and a half. You wouldn’t believe the number of sitting haystacks I’ve picked off at that distance. I simply can’t miss. On second thoughts, I shan’t fire to kill you. Let us be humane on this joyful occasion. I shall just smash your knees—painful, but not fatal.”

===Chinese===
「我个人倒不介意，」吉米打断他。「不过我希望，您往后同撬门贼（cracksmen）打交道的时候，别管他们叫贼。他们心眼儿敏感得吓人。要知道，这一行的两个支脉之间有天壤之别，势利的门户之见还深得很。打个比方，您要是位兼任主演的班主，被人叫作跑龙套的，您作何感想？这里头的讲究，您明白了吧？会伤人家感情的。喏，换了寻常的贼，碰上这种场面多半就要动粗了；可据我所知，除非万不得已——但愿这回不至于——动粗是违背撬门贼行规的。不过话又说回来，托马斯爵士，说老实话，我不得不补上一句：我已经把您瞄准了。」

他外衣口袋里装着一只烟斗。他把烟斗柄一本正经地顶在衣里上。托马斯爵士提心吊胆地端详那处鼓包，脸色微微发白。吉米正恶狠狠地皱着眉头。亚瑟·米夫林第三幕里那副皱眉的模样，当年可是颇受赞赏的。

「我的枪，」吉米说，「您瞧见了，在我口袋里。我向来隔着口袋开枪，哪怕裁缝的账单因此节节攀升。这个小家伙已经上了膛、扳了机。他正笔直对着您那颗独粒钻石。那个致命的位置！还从没有人挨了独粒钻石一枪能活命的。我的手指已经搁在扳机上，所以我要奉劝您，别去碰您正盯着的那个铃。您不该碰它的缘故还有几条，容我马上细说。」

托马斯爵士的手迟疑起来。

「您要摁，当然尽可以摁，」吉米和蔼地说。「府上终归是您自己的家。不过换了我，就不摁。一码半的距离之内，我是神枪手。在那个距离上我撂倒过多少个端坐不动的干草垛，说出来您都不会信。我简直打不偏。转念一想，我也不打算开枪要您的命。这大喜的日子，咱们还是人道些。我只打断您两条腿——疼是疼，可不要命。」

===Original===
He waggled his pipe suggestively. Sir Thomas blanched. His hand fell to his side.

“Great!” said Jimmy. “After all, why should you be in a hurry to break up this very pleasant little meeting? I’m sure I’m not. Let us chat. How are the theatricals going? Was the duologue a success? Wait till you see our show. Three of us knew our lines at the dress rehearsal.”

Sir Thomas had backed away from the bell, but the retreat was merely for the convenience of the moment. He understood that it might be injurious to press the button just then; but he had recovered his composure by this time, and he saw that ultimately the game must be his.

Jimmy was trapped.

His face resumed its normal hue. Automatically his hands began to move towards his coattails and his feet to spread themselves. Jimmy noted with a smile these signs of restored complacency. He hoped ere long to upset that complacency somewhat.

===Chinese===
他意味深长地晃了晃烟斗。托马斯爵士面无人色。那只手垂回了身侧。

「好极了！」吉米说。「说到底，这么一场愉快的小聚，您何苦急着拆散呢？反正我不急。咱们聊聊。业余演剧排得怎么样了？双人短剧成功吗？等着瞧咱们的戏吧。彩排那会儿，咱们有三个人是把台词背熟了的。」

托马斯爵士已经从铃跟前退开了，可这番后退不过是权且方便一时。他明白，这会儿就去摁那按钮，没准儿要吃大亏；但到这时他已恢复了镇定，而且看得分明：到头来，赢家必是他。

吉米成了瓮中之鳖。

他的脸色恢复了平日的红润。两只手自动自发地朝外衣下摆挪过去，两只脚也自动站开了马步。吉米微笑着注意到这些故态复萌的征兆。他指望不久之后能把这份自满搅和掉一点。

===Original===
Sir Thomas addressed himself to making Jimmy’s position clear to him.

“How, may I ask,” he said, “do you propose to leave the castle?”

“Won’t you let me have the motor?” said Jimmy. “But I expect I shan’t be leaving just yet.”

Sir Thomas laughed shortly.

“No,” he said. “No; I fancy not. I am with you there!”

“Great minds,” said Jimmy. “I shouldn’t be surprised if we thought alike on all sorts of subjects. Just think how you came round to my views on ringing the bell. In a flash! But what made you fancy that I intended to leave the castle?”

===Chinese===
托马斯爵士着手让吉米认清他自己的处境。

「冒昧请教一句，」他说，「你打算怎样离开这座城堡？」

「不肯把汽车借我用用吗？」吉米说。「不过我估计，眼下还走不了。」

托马斯爵士短促地一笑。

「是啊，」他说。「是啊；我看也是。这一层我跟你正是一样！」

「英雄所见略同嘛，」吉米说。「各式各样的问题上咱们想法都对得上，我也不会奇怪。想想看，在摁不摁铃这件事上，你一眨眼的工夫就转到我这边的主张上来了！可到底是什么让你以为我打算离开城堡？」

===Original===
“I should hardly have supposed that you would be anxious to stay.”

“On the contrary. It’s the one place I have been in in the last two years that I have felt really satisfied with. Usually I want to move on after a week. But I could stop here forever.”

“I am afraid, Mr. Pitt—by the way, an alias, of course?”

“I fear not,” he said. “If I had chosen an alias, it would have been Tressilyan, or Trevelyan, or something. I call Pitt a poor thing in names. I once knew a man called Ronald Cheylesmore. Lucky fellow!”

Sir Thomas returned to the point on which he had been about to touch.

===Chinese===
「我原想着，你不见得还肯待下去。」

「恰恰相反。两年来我去过的地方里，唯独这儿叫我打心底称心。往常住上一个礼拜我就想挪窝。可这儿我情愿一辈子住下去。」

「只怕，皮特先生——哦对了，顺带问一句，当然是个化名吧？」

「恐怕不是，」他说。「当初要挑化名，我会挑个特雷西利安（Tressilyan），或者特里维廉（Trevelyan）什么的。依我看，皮特这个名字实在寒碜。从前我认识一个人，叫罗纳德·奇尔斯莫尔（Ronald Cheylesmore）。好一个有福气的家伙！」

托马斯爵士兜回他方才正要触及的话题。

===Original===
“I am afraid, Mr. Pitt, that you hardly realise your position.”

“No?” said Jimmy, interested.

“I find you in the act of stealing my wife’s necklace—”

“Would there be any use in telling you that I was not stealing it, but putting it back?”

Sir Thomas raised his eyebrows in silence.

===Chinese===
「只怕，皮特先生，你几乎没有意识到自己的处境。」

「哦？」吉米来了兴致。

「我当场逮住你偷我太太的项链——」

「假如我告诉您，我不是在偷，而是在把它放回原处，这话说给您听管用吗？」

托马斯爵士不置一词，只扬了扬眉毛。

===Original===
“No?” said Jimmy. “I was afraid not. You were saying—”

“I find you in the act of stealing my wife’s necklace,” proceeded Sir Thomas, “and because for the moment you succeed in postponing arrest by threatening me with a revolver—”

An agitated look came into Jimmy’s face.

“Great Scott!” he cried. He felt hastily in his pocket. “Yes,” he said; “as I had begun to fear. I owe you an apology, Sir Thomas,” he went on with manly dignity, producing the briar. “I am entirely to blame. How the mistake arose I cannot imagine, but I find it isn’t a revolver after all.”

Sir Thomas’s cheeks took on a richer tint of purple. He glared dumbly at the pipe.

“In the excitement of the moment, I suppose—” began Jimmy.

===Chinese===
「不中用，是吧？」吉米说。「我就怕不中用。您方才说到——」

「我当场逮住你偷我太太的项链，」托马斯爵士接着说，「而你，凭着拿一把左轮手枪来威胁我，居然把被捕这桩事暂且拖延了下来——」

吉米的脸上闪出一副惶急的神气。

「我的老天！」他叫了一声，急忙往口袋里摸。「可不，」他说；「正如我开始担心的那样。我得向您道歉，托马斯爵士，」他带着男子汉的威仪继续说道，掏出了那只石南木烟斗。「全怪我。这误会是怎么闹出来的，我想破头也想不出，可我发现它压根儿不是一把左轮手枪。」

托马斯爵士的两颊泛起一层更浓的紫色。他哑口无言，直勾勾瞪着那只烟斗。

「想必是一时激动——」吉米刚要开口。

===Original===
Sir Thomas interrupted. The recollection of his needless panic rankled within him.

“You—you—you—”

“Count ten!”

“You—what you propose to gain by this buffoonery I am at a loss—”

“How can you say such savage things?” protested Jimmy. “Not buffoonery! Wit! Esprit! Flow of soul such as circulates daily in the best society.”

Sir Thomas almost leaped towards the bell. With his finger on it, he turned to deliver a final speech.

===Chinese===
托马斯爵士打断了他。想起自己那场毫无必要的虚惊，他心头的火还在烧。

「你——你——你——」

「数到十！」

「你——你这套丑角把戏究竟想捞到什么好处，我实在——」

「您怎么忍心说这么狠的话？」吉米抗议道。「什么丑角把戏！这是机智！是妙语（esprit）！是上等社会里天天流通的那种性灵的泉涌！」

托马斯爵士几乎是纵身扑向那只铃。他把手指按在铃上，转过身来，要发表一篇最后的演说。

===Original===
“I believe you’re insane,” he cried; “but I’ll have no more of it. I have endured this foolery long enough. I’ll—”

“Just one moment,” said Jimmy. “I said just now that there were other reasons besides the revol—well, pipe—why you should not ring that bell. One of them is that all the servants will be in their places in the audience, so that there won’t be anyone to answer it. But that’s not the most convincing reason. Will you listen to one more before getting busy?”

“I see your game. Don’t imagine for a moment that you trick me.”

“Nothing could be further—”

“You fancy you can gain time by talking, and find some way of escape—”

“But I don’t want to escape. Don’t you realise that in about ten minutes I am due to play an important part in a great drama on the stage?”

===Chinese===
「我看你是个疯子，」他叫道；「可我不奉陪了。这套胡闹我忍得够久的了。我要——」

「且慢一慢，」吉米说。「我方才说过，除了那把左轮——呃，烟斗——之外，还有几条别的缘故，叫您不该去摁那只铃。其中一条是：仆人们这会儿全都各就各位坐在观众席里，铃就算响了，也没有人来应。可这还不是最有说服力的一条。动手之前，您肯不肯再听一条？」

「我看穿了你的把戏。别想糊弄我，一刻也别想。」

「绝无此意——」

「你以为靠耍嘴皮子就能拖时间，好寻出一条脱身的路子——」

「可我不想脱身。您难道不明白，再过十来分钟，台上那出大戏里，有我一个要紧的角色等着上场吗？」

===Original===
“I’ll keep you here, I tell you. You’ll leave this room,” said Sir Thomas grandly, “over my body.”

“Steeplechasing in the home,” murmured Jimmy. “No more dull evenings. But listen—do listen. I won’t keep you a minute, and if you want to push that bell after I’ve finished, you may push it six inches into the wall if you like.”

“Well?” said Sir Thomas shortly.

“Would you like me to lead gently up to what I want to say, gradually preparing you for the reception of the news, or shall I—”

The knight took out his watch.

“I shall give you one minute,” he said.

===Chinese===
「我告诉你，我要把你扣在这儿。你要离开这间屋子，」托马斯爵士威风凛凛地说，「就得从我的身上跨过去。」

「居家越野障碍赛，」吉米咕哝道。「往后再不会有沉闷的夜晚了。可您听我说——务必听我说。耽误不了您一分钟；等我说完了，您还要摁那只铃，您就是把它摁进墙里六英寸深，也随您的便。」

「怎么样？」托马斯爵士没好气地说。

「您是愿意我从从容容引到正题上，一步一步为您迎接这个消息做好铺垫呢，还是我——」

爵士老爷掏出了怀表。

「我给你一分钟，」他说。

===Original===
“Heavens, I must hustle! How many seconds have I got now?”

“If you have anything to say, say it.”

“Very well, then,” said Jimmy. “It’s only this. That necklace is a fraud. The diamonds aren’t diamonds at all. They’re paste!”

===Chinese===
「天哪，我得抓紧！眼下还剩多少秒？」

「有话就说。」

「那好，」吉米说。「就一句话。那串项链是件赝品。那些钻石压根儿不是钻石。是玻璃钻！」

## XXVII A Declaration of Independence / 独立宣言

===Original===
If Jimmy had entertained any doubts concerning the effectiveness of this disclosure, they would have vanished at the sight of the other’s face. Just as the rich hues of a sunset pale slowly into an almost imperceptible green, so did the purple of Sir Thomas’s cheeks become, in stages, first a dull red, then pink, and finally take on a uniform pallor. His mouth hung open. His attitude of righteous defiance had crumpled. Unsuspected creases appeared in his clothes. He had the appearance of one who has been caught in the machinery.

Jimmy was a little puzzled. He had expected to check the enemy, to bring him to reason, but not to demolish him in that way. There was something in this which he did not understand. When Spike had handed him the stones, and his trained eye, after a moment’s searching examination, had made him suspicious, and when, finally, a simple test had proved his suspicions correct, he was comfortably aware that, though found with the necklace on his person, he had knowledge which, communicated to Sir Thomas, would serve him well. He knew that Lady Julia was not the sort of Lady who would bear calmly the announcement that her treasured rope of diamonds was a fraud. He knew enough of her to know that she would demand another necklace, and see that she got it, and that Sir Thomas was not one of those generous and expansive natures which think nothing of an expenditure of twenty thousand pounds.

This was the line of thought which had kept him cheerful during what might otherwise have been a trying interview. He was aware from the first that Sir Thomas would not believe in the purity of his motives; but he was convinced that the knight would be satisfied to secure his silence on the subject of the paste necklace on any terms. He had looked forward to baffled rage, furious denunciation, and a dozen other expressions of emotion, but certainly not to collapse of this kind.
===Chinese===
假如吉米对这番披露的效力还存有什么疑虑，那么一看见对方的脸色，疑虑也就烟消云散了。正像落日的浓艳色泽会慢慢淡成一抹几乎无从察觉的青绿，托马斯爵士两颊的紫色也是一节一节变下去的：先是暗红，继而是粉红，最后成了一色的苍白。他的嘴半张着。那副理直气壮、誓与歹徒周旋到底的架势，一下子垮塌了。衣服上凭空出现了谁也料不到的皱褶。他那模样，活像一个被卷进了机器齿轮的人。

吉米倒有几分纳闷。他料到的是遏住敌人、把他劝得回心转意，却没料到是这般稀里哗啦的摧毁。这里头有点名堂，他还弄不明白。斯派克把石头交到他手里，他那双受过训练的眼睛只搜寻打量了片刻，便起了疑心；最后，一个简单的试验又证实了疑心不差——从那时起，他心里一直踏踏实实：虽然项链是在他身上搜出来的，可他掌握的那点学问，只要向托马斯爵士一透露，就能大大派上用场。他知道，朱莉娅夫人不是那种夫人：听见人家说她视若珍宝的钻石长项链原来是件赝品，还能安之若素。他对她了解得够多，知道她准会另讨一条项链，而且不见到手决不罢休；他也知道，托马斯爵士绝非那种豪阔疏财的性子——那种人花掉两万镑，眼也不会眨一下。

正是这一路盘算，支撑他在这场原本可能相当难熬的会面里始终心平气和。他打一开头就明白，托马斯爵士不会相信他的动机清白；可他确信，爵士老爷只求封住他关于玻璃钻项链的口，无论什么条件都会点头。他预计迎接自己的是受挫的暴怒、劈头盖脸的痛斥，外加十来种别的情绪表演，可万万没料到是这样一种瘫软崩塌。

===Original===
The other had begun to make strange, gurgling noises.

“Mind you,” said Jimmy, “it’s a very good imitation﻿—I’ll say that for it. I didn’t suspect it till I had the thing in my hands. Looking at it﻿—even quite close﻿—I was taken in for a moment.”

Sir Thomas swallowed nervously.

“How did you know?” he muttered.

Again Jimmy was surprised. He had expected indignant denials and demands for proof, excited reiteration of the statement that the stones had cost twenty thousand pounds.

“How did I know?” he repeated. “If you mean what first made me suspect, I couldn’t tell you; it might have been one of a score of things. A jeweller can’t say exactly how he gets on the track of faked stones. He can feel them, he can almost smell them. I worked with a jeweller once; that’s how I got my knowledge of jewels. But if you mean, can I prove what I say about this necklace, that’s easy. There’s no deception; it’s simple. See here. These stones are supposed to be diamonds. Well, the diamond is the hardest stone in existence﻿—nothing will scratch it. Now, I’ve got a little ruby out of a pin which I know is genuine. By rights, then, that ruby ought not to have scratched these stones. You follow that? But it did. It scratched two of them, the only two I tried. If you like I can continue the experiment, but there’s no need. I can tell you straight away what these stones are. I said they were paste, but that wasn’t quite accurate. They’re a stuff called white jargoon. It’s a stuff that’s very easily worked. You work it with the flame of a blowpipe. You don’t want a full description, I suppose? Anyway, what happens is that the blowpipe sets it up like a tonic, gives it increased specific gravity, and a healthy complexion, and all sorts of great things of that kind. Two minutes in the flame of a blowpipe is like a week at the seaside to a bit of white jargoon. Are you satisfied? If it comes to that, I suppose you can hardly be expected to be; convinced is a better word. Are you convinced, or do you hanker after tests like polarised light and refracting liquids?”
===Chinese===
对面那位已经开始发出一种奇怪的、咕噜咕噜的声音。

「别的不说，」吉米道，「这仿制品做得着实不坏——这句公道话我得说。直到把它拿在手里，我才起了疑心。光凭眼睛瞧——哪怕凑得挺近——一时之间我也上了当。」

托马斯爵士神经质地咽了口唾沫。

「你是怎么看出来的？」他咕哝道。

吉米又一次吃惊。他料到的是愤慨的抵赖、讨要凭证，是气势汹汹地反复申明那些石头花了两万镑。

「我怎么看不出来的？」他复述道。「您要问头一样叫我起疑的是什么，我可说不上来；兴许是几十样事由里的随便哪一样。珠宝匠说不清自己是怎么循上假宝石的踪迹的。他摸得出，简直闻得出。我从前在一位珠宝匠手下做过事，这点珠宝上的学问就是这么来的。至于您要问，我说的话能不能证明——那容易。这里头没有戏法，道理简单。您瞧。这些石头按说该是钻石。好，钻石是天下最硬的石头——什么也划不动它。眼下我这儿有一粒从别针上取下的小红宝石，货真价实。那么照理，这粒红宝石是不该在这些石头上划出痕迹来的。您明白这层意思吧？可它偏偏划了。它划伤了两粒——我试过的，也只有这两粒。您要乐意，试验我还可以接着做，不过大可不必。这些石头是什么货色，我可以一句话告诉您。我说它们是玻璃钻，可这话并不十分准确。它们是一种叫白锆石（white jargoon）的东西。这东西极好加工，用吹管的火焰就能摆弄它。全套工艺说明，想来您不要听吧？反正效果是这样：吹管像一剂补药似的把它调理起来——比重增加了，气色红润了，诸如此类的大大好事一大堆。对一粒白锆石来说，在吹管火焰里过两分钟，抵得上上海滨疗养一个礼拜。您满意了吗？真要较起真来，我想您满意也难；说『信服』更妥当些。您信服了没有？还是念念不忘，非要来一套偏振光（polarised light）、折射液（refracting liquids）之类的检验不可？」

===Original===
Sir Thomas had staggered to a chair.

“So that was how you knew!” he said.

“That was﻿—” began Jimmy, when a sudden suspicion flashed across his mind. He scrutinised Sir Thomas’s pallid face keenly.

“Did you know?” he asked.

He wondered that the possibility had not occurred to him earlier.

“By George, I believe you did!” he cried. “You must have done. So that’s how it happened, is it? I don’t wonder it was a shock when I said I knew about the necklace.”
===Chinese===
托马斯爵士跌跌撞撞地靠到一把椅子上。

「原来你是这么知道的！」他说。

「正是——」吉米刚一开口，心头蓦地闪过一丝疑云。他锐利地审视起托马斯爵士那张灰白的脸来。

「莫非您本来就知情？」他问。

他自己也纳闷，这个可能怎么没有早些想到。

「老天爷，我看您就是知道！」他叫道。「您一定知道。事情的底细原来如此，是不是？难怪我一说我知道项链的事，您就吓成那个样子。」

===Original===
“Mr. Pitt!”

“Well?”

“I have something to say to you.”

“I’m listening.”
===Chinese===
「皮特先生！」

「嗯？」

「我有话要跟你说。」

「洗耳恭听。」

===Original===
Sir Thomas tried to rally. There was a touch of the old pomposity in his manner when he spoke.

“Mr. Pitt, I find you in an unpleasant position﻿—”

Jimmy interrupted.

“Don’t you worry about my unpleasant position,” he said. “Fix your attention exclusively upon your own. Let us be frank with one another. You’re in the cart. What do you propose to do about it?”

“I do not understand you,” he began.
===Chinese===
托马斯爵士竭力重整旗鼓。开口的时候，举止间还带出了一丝旧日的官派威风。

「皮特先生，我发现你正处在一个很不体面的境地——」

吉米打断了他。

「我那个不体面的境地，就不劳您操心了，」他说。「您还是把注意力一心一意摆在自己身上吧。咱们彼此都坦白一点。你这下是掉进坑里了。你打算怎么办？」

「我不明白你的意思，」他刚一开口。

===Original===
“No?” said Jimmy. “I’ll try and make my meaning clear. Correct me from time to time if I am wrong. The way I size the thing up is as follows: When you married Lady Julia I gather that it was, so to speak, up to you to some extent. People knew you were a millionaire, and they expected something special in the way of gifts from the bridegroom to the bride. Now you, being of a prudent and economical nature, began to wonder if there wasn’t some way of getting a reputation for lavishness without actually cashing up to any great extent. Am I right?”

Sir Thomas did not answer.

“I am,” said Jimmy. “Well, it occurred to you, naturally enough, that a properly selected gift of jewellery might work the trick. It only needed a little nerve. When you give a present of diamonds to a lady she is not likely to call for polarised light and refracting liquids and the rest of the circus. In ninety-nine cases out of a hundred she will take the thing on trust. Very well. You trotted off to a jeweller and put the thing to him confidentially. I expect you suggested paste; but, being a wily person, he pointed out that paste has a habit of not wearing well. It is pretty enough when it’s new, but quite a small amount of ordinary wear and tear destroys the polish of the surface and the sharpness of the cutting. It gets scratched easily. Having heard this, and reflected that Lady Julia was not likely to keep the necklace under a glass case, you rejected paste as too risky. The genial jeweller then suggested white jargoon, mentioning, as I have done, that after an application or so of the blowpipe its own mother wouldn’t know it. If he was a bit of an antiquary, he probably added that in the eighteenth century jargoon stones were supposed to be actually an inferior sort of diamond. What could be more suitable? ‘Make it jargoon, dear heart,’ you cried joyfully, and all was well. Am I right? I notice that you have not corrected me so far.”
===Chinese===
「哦？」吉米说。「那我来设法把意思说个明白。我说错了，您随时纠正。依我把这桩事掂量的结果，经过是这样的：您娶朱莉娅夫人的时候，据我了解，送礼的事，在某种程度上可以说全凭您做主。人们都知道您是百万富翁，都巴望着新郎官送给新娘的礼物得有点与众不同的名堂。而您呢，天性精明俭省，便开始琢磨：有没有什么法子，既能博得一个挥金如土的名声，又不必真刀真枪掏出多少现钱？我说得对不对？」

托马斯爵士没有作声。

「我是对的，」吉米说。「好，于是您自然而然地想到：一件挑选得当的珠宝礼物，没准儿就能把这戏法变过去。所缺的不过是一点点胆量。您送一位小姐一份钻石礼物，她断不至于嚷着要偏振光、折射液，外加全套马戏班的家什。一百桩里有九十九桩，她凭个信字就把东西收下了。很好。您一溜小跑去找了一位珠宝匠，神神秘秘地把这桩事同他商量。我猜是您先提的玻璃钻；可那位是个老奸巨猾的角色，他指出来，玻璃钻有个毛病：不禁用。新的时候固然花枝招展，可日常穿戴，磨损不用多少工夫，就会毁掉表面的光润和切工的锋棱，还动不动留下划痕。听了这番话，又转念想到朱莉娅夫人不见得会把项链供在玻璃罩子里，您便嫌玻璃钻太冒险，打了回票。那位和蔼可亲的珠宝匠于是一转口，推荐了白锆石，并且像我方才那样言道：吹管上过一两回火，它亲妈也认不出它来。他倘若还称得起半个古物行家，多半又添上一句：远在十八世纪，白锆石还被人当作一种次等的真钻石呢。天底下还有比这更对路的吗？『就用白锆石做吧，亲爱的伙计，』您欢天喜地地喊道，从此天下太平。我说得对不对？我注意到，直到这会儿，您还没纠正过我一个字呢。」

===Original===
Whether Sir Thomas would have replied in the affirmative is uncertain. He was opening his mouth to speak when the curtain at the end of the room heaved, and Lord Dreever burst out like a cannonball in tweeds.

The apparition effectually checked any speech that Sir Thomas might have been intending to make. Lying back in his chair, he goggled silently at the new arrival. Even Jimmy, though knowing that his lordship was in hiding, was taken aback.

His lordship broke the silence.

“Great Scot!” he cried.

Neither Jimmy nor Sir Thomas seemed to consider the observation unsound or inadequate. They permitted it to pass without comment.

“You old scoundrel!” added his lordship, addressing Sir Thomas; “and you’re the man who called me a welsher!” There were signs of a flicker of spirit in the knight’s prominent eyes, but they died away. He made no reply.
===Chinese===
托马斯爵士究竟会不会答一声『是的』，已无从知晓。他正张开嘴要说话，忽见屋子那头的帷幕鼓动起来，德利弗勋爵像一颗穿花呢服的炮弹，从里头冲了出来。

这场显圣，把托马斯爵士原本可能要发的任何言论都有效地堵了回去。他仰靠在椅背上，直瞪着眼，一言不发地打量这位新到的人物。连吉米——虽然明知勋爵大人正藏在里头——也吃了一惊。

勋爵大人打破了沉默。

「我的老天！」他叫道。

吉米和托马斯爵士似乎都不认为这句议论有什么失当或不够。他们容它就这么过去，未加评议。

「你这老恶棍！」勋爵大人接着说，冲的是托马斯爵士；「骂我打赌赖账的，原来就是你这号人！」爵士老爷那双鼓突的眼睛里，闪出一点火气的苗头，可旋即熄灭了。他一言未答。

===Original===
“Great Scot!” moaned his lordship, in a fever of self-pity, “here have I been all these years letting you give me Hades in every shape and form, when all the while﻿—My goodness, if I’d only known earlier!”

He turned to Jimmy.

“Pitt, old man,” he said warmly, “I﻿—dash it﻿—I don’t know what to say. If it hadn’t been for you﻿—I always did like Americans.”

“I’m not one,” said Jimmy; but his lordship went on, unchecked.

“I always thought it bally rot that that fuss happened in﻿—in﻿—wherever it was. If it hadn’t been for fellows like you,” he continued, addressing Sir Thomas once more, “there wouldn’t have been any of that frightful Declaration of Independence business. Would there, Pitt, old man?”
===Chinese===
「我的老天！」勋爵大人悲悲切切地呻吟道，自怜之情如烈火烧身。「这些年，我一直由着你在各式各样的事情上把我折磨得下地狱，谁知你原来——我的老天爷，我要是早点知道就好了！」

他转向吉米。

「皮特，老伙计，」他热乎乎地说，「我——真见鬼——我不知道说什么才好。要不是有你——我一向就喜欢美国人。」

「我不是美国人，」吉米说；可勋爵大人不住口，一径往下讲。

「我一向觉得，那场乱子当年闹在——闹在——闹在随便哪个鬼地方，都是十足见鬼的瞎胡闹。要不是有你这号家伙，」他又掉头冲着托马斯爵士接下去说，「那桩吓死人的『独立宣言』公案，压根儿就不会有。对不对，皮特，老伙计？」

===Original===
These were deep problems too spacious for casual examination. Jimmy shrugged his shoulders.

“Well, I should say Sir Thomas might not have got along with George Washington, anyhow,” he said.

“Of course not. Well”﻿—his lordship moved towards the door﻿—“I’m off downstairs to see what Aunt Julia has to say about it all.”

A shudder, as if from some electric shock, shook Sir Thomas. He leaped to his feet.

“Spencer,” he cried, “I forbid you to say a word to your aunt.”

“Oh!” said his lordship. “You do, do you?”
===Chinese===
这些题目太深奥、太宏大，绝非随口一议所能考究明白。吉米耸了耸肩。

「这个嘛，我看托马斯爵士反正同乔治·华盛顿（George Washington）多半处不来，」他说。

「当然处不来。得啦，」勋爵大人朝门口挪去，「我这就下楼去，听听姑母朱莉娅对这一整套事端怎么说。」

一阵战栗，像过了电似的，透过托马斯爵士全身。他一跃而起。

「斯宾塞，」他叫道，「我不许你对你姑母吐露一个字。」

「哦！」勋爵大人说。「你不许，是吧？」

===Original===
Sir Thomas shivered.

“She would never let me hear the last of it.”

“I bet she wouldn’t. I’ll go and see.”

“Stop!”

“Well?”
===Chinese===
托马斯爵士打了个寒噤。

「她这辈子也不会让我忘掉这件事的。」

「我赌她绝不会。我去瞧瞧。」

「站住！」

「又怎么啦？」

===Original===
Sir Thomas dabbed at his forehead with his handkerchief. He dared not face the vision of Lady Julia in possession of the truth. At one time the fear lest she might discover the harmless little deception he had practised had kept him awake at night, but gradually, as the days went by, and the excellence of the imitation stones had continued to impose upon her and upon everyone else who saw them, the fear had diminished. But it had always been at the back of his mind. Even in her calmer moments his wife was a source of mild terror to him. His imagination reeled at the thought of what depths of aristocratic scorn and indignation she would plumb in a case like this.

“Spencer,” he said, “I insist that you shall not inform your aunt of this!”

“What? You want me to keep my mouth shut? You want me to become an accomplice in this beastly, low-down deception? I like that!”

“The point,” said Jimmy, “is well taken﻿—noblesse oblige, and all that sort of thing. The blood of the Dreevers boils furiously at the idea. Listen! You can hear it sizzling.”
===Chinese===
托马斯爵士拿手帕在额头上一个劲儿地轻揿。他不敢直面那幅图景——朱莉娅夫人掌握了真相时的图景。当年，怕她察觉他玩的那点无害的小骗局，曾搅得他夜里睡不着觉；但日子一天天过去，假石头的精工始终瞒过了她，也瞒过了每一个见过它的人，那份恐惧便渐渐淡了。可它始终蛰伏在他心底。纵然在太太最心平气和的时刻，她也是他一桩小小的恐怖之源。一想到她在这种事上会探到怎样深不见底的贵族式轻蔑与义愤，他的想象力便一阵天旋地转。

「斯宾塞，」他说，「我坚持，这件事你绝不可透露给你姑母！」

「什么？你要我守口如瓶？你要我给这桩卑鄙下流的骗局当帮凶？亏你想得出！」

「这话有理，」吉米说。「位高则任重（noblesse oblige），诸如此类的讲究。德里弗家的血脉一想到这个主意，就沸腾得厉害。听！都能听见它滋滋作响呢。」

===Original===
Lord Dreever moved a step nearer the door.

“Stop!” cried Sir Thomas again. “Spencer!”

“Well?”

“Spencer, my boy, it occurs to me that perhaps I have not always treated you very well.”

“ ‘Perhaps!’ ‘Not always!’ Great Scot! I’ll have a fiver each way on both those. Considering you’ve treated me like a frightful kid practically ever since you’ve known me, I call that pretty rich. Why, what about this very night, when I asked you for a few pounds?”
===Chinese===
德利弗勋爵朝门口又挪近一步。

「站住！」托马斯爵士又喊道。「斯宾塞！」

「又怎么啦？」

「斯宾塞，我的孩子，我忽然想起，我待你，或许一向不大好。」

「『或许』！『一向不大』！我的老天！这两句金言，我各押五镑，两头都下注。想你打认识我那天起，差不多一直把我当个吓人的小娃娃看待，我瞧这话可真够逗的。嗐，就拿今晚来说，我跟你要几个英镑，你是什么嘴脸？」

===Original===
“It was only the thought that you had been gambling﻿—”

“Gambling! How about palming off faked diamonds on Aunt Julia for a gamble?”

“A game of skill, surely,” murmured Jimmy.

“I have been thinking the matter over,” said Sir Thomas, “and if you really need the﻿—Was it not fifty pounds?”

“It was twenty,” said his lordship, “and I don’t need it. Keep it. You’ll want all you can save for a new necklace.”
===Chinese===
「我只是想到你一直在赌钱——」

「赌钱！那把假钻石当赌注，蒙到你姑母朱莉娅头上，又算什么？」

「那可是凭本事的游戏，没跑了，」吉米低声道。

「这事我盘算过了，」托马斯爵士说，「你若真需要那——是五十镑来着？」

「是二十镑，」勋爵大人说。「我也不需要。留着吧。你攒下的每一个子儿，都得留着买一条新项链呢。」

===Original===
His fingers closed on the door handle.

“Spencer﻿—stop!”

“Well?”

“We must talk this over. We must not be hasty.”

He passed the handkerchief over his forehead.
===Chinese===
他的手指已经攥住了门把。

「斯宾塞——站住！」

「又怎么啦？」

「这事咱们必须商量商量。切不可操之过急。」

他拿手帕在额头上抹了一把。

===Original===
“In the past, perhaps,” he resumed, “our relations have not been quite﻿—The fault was mine. I have always endeavoured to do my duty. It is a difficult task to look after a young man of your age﻿—”

His lordship’s sense of his grievances made him eloquent.

“Dash it all!” he cried. “That’s just what I jolly well complain of. Who the dickens wanted you to look after me? Hang it! you’ve kept your eye on me all these years like a frightful policeman! You cut off my allowance right in the middle of my time at the ’Varsity, just when I needed it most, and I had to come and beg for money whenever I wanted to buy a cigarette. I looked a fearful ass I can tell you! Men who knew me used to be dashed funny about it. I’m sick of the whole bally business. You’ve given me a jolly thin time all this while, and now I’m going to get a bit of my own back. Wouldn’t you, Pitt, old man?”

Jimmy, thus suddenly appealed to, admitted that, in his lordship’s place, he might have experienced a momentary temptation to do something of the kind.
===Chinese===
「在过去，或许，」他接着说，「我们的关系不很——过错在我。我一向克尽厥职。照管你这个年纪的年轻人，实在是桩难事——」

勋爵大人满腹的冤屈，此刻替他招来了雄辩。

「真见鬼！」他叫道。「这不恰恰是我一直抱怨的事嘛。哪个人稀罕你来照管？真是的！这些年来你把我盯得死死的，活像个吓人的警察！我大学念到一半，你把我的津贴咔嚓一刀切断，正赶上我最要钱用的时候，往后我想买支烟，都得登门来讨。不瞒你说，我那模样可蠢透了！认识我的人拿这事打趣，风凉话没少说。这一整套见鬼的把戏我受够了。这半天你叫我过的都是寒碜日子，如今我要讨还一点公道。换了你难道不会吗，皮特，老伙计？」

吉米冷不防被点了名，只好承认说，他若处在勋爵大人的地位，一时半刻也难免动这样的念头。

===Original===
“Of course,” said his lordship. “Any fellow would.”

“But, Spencer, let me﻿—”

“You’ve soured my life,” said his lordship, frowning a tense, Byronic frown. “That’s what you’ve done﻿—soured my whole bally life. I’ve had a rotten time. I’ve had to go about touching my friends for money to keep me going. Why, I owe you a fiver, don’t I, Pitt, old man?”
